## XVI A Courier / 第十六章 信使
===Original===

The scouts at Fort Larned, when I arrived there, were commanded by Dick Curtis﻿—an old guide, frontiersman and Indian interpreter. There were some three hundred lodges of Kiowa and Comanche Indians camped near the fort. These Indians had not as yet gone upon the warpath, but were restless and discontented, and their leading chiefs, Satanta, Lone Wolf, Kicking Bird, Satank, Sittamore, and other noted warriors, were rather saucy. The post at the time was garrisoned by only two companies of infantry and one of cavalry.
General Hazen, who was at the post, was endeavoring to pacify the Indians and keep them from going on the warpath. I was appointed as his special scout, and one morning he notified me that he was going to Fort Harker, and wished me to accompany him as far as Fort Zarah, thirty miles distant. The General usually traveled in an ambulance, but this trip he was to make in a six-mule wagon, under the escort of a squad of twenty infantrymen. So, early one morning in August, we started; arriving safely at Fort Zarah at twelve o’clock. General Hazen thought it unnecessary that we should go farther, and he proceeded on his way to Fort Harker without an escort, leaving instructions that we should return to Fort Larned the next day.
After the General had gone I went to the sergeant in command of the squad, and told him that I was going back that very afternoon, instead of waiting till the next morning; and I accordingly saddled up my mule and set out for Fort Larned. I proceeded uninterruptedly until I got about halfway between the two posts, when at Pawnee Rock I was suddenly “jumped” by about forty Indians, who came dashing up to me, extending their hands and saying, “How! How!” They were some of the same Indians who had been hanging around Fort Larned in the morning. I saw that they had on their war-paint, and were evidently now out on the warpath.
My first impulse was to shake hands with them, as they seemed so desirous of it. I accordingly reached out my hand to one of them, who grasped it with a tight grip, and jerked me violently forward; another pulled my mule by the bridle, and in a moment I was completely surrounded. Before I could do anything at all, they had seized my revolvers from the holsters, and I received a blow on the head from a tomahawk which nearly rendered me senseless.

===Chinese===

当我到达拉尼德堡（Fort Larned）时，那里的侦察兵由迪克·柯蒂斯（Dick Curtis）指挥——他是一位老资格向导、边疆拓荒者兼印第安语翻译。当时城堡附近驻扎着大约300顶帐篷的基奥瓦族与科曼奇族印第安人。这些印第安人虽然尚未踏上战途，但已躁动不安、怨声载道，他们的首领萨坦塔（Satanta）、独狼（Lone Wolf）、踢鸟（Kicking Bird）、萨坦克（Satank）、西塔莫尔（Sittamore）等知名勇士更是态度骄横。当时哨所仅驻扎着两个步兵连和一个骑兵连。

驻扎在哨所的哈森将军（General Hazen）正极力安抚印第安人，防止他们掀起战端。我被任命为他的特聘侦察兵。一天早晨，他通知我说要前往哈克堡（Fort Harker），希望我护送他走到30英里外的扎拉堡（Fort Zarah）。将军通常乘坐轻便马车出行，但这次他在由20名步兵组成的小队护送下，改乘一辆六骡大车。于是，在八月的一个清晨，我们启程出发，于中午12点平安抵达扎拉堡。哈森将军认为我们无需再往前送，便在没有护送队的情况下独自前往哈克堡，并留下指令让我们次日返回拉尼德堡。

将军离开后，我找到负责指挥该小队的军士，告诉他我打算当天下午就动身返回，不再等到次日清晨。于是我备好骡子，踏上了回拉尼德堡的路程。一路上平安无事，行至两座哨所中间的波尼岩（Pawnee Rock）附近时，我突然遭到约40名印第安人的“突袭”。他们飞奔冲向我，伸出手喊着：“好！好！”（How! How!）。这些人正是早晨在拉尼德堡周围晃荡的同一伙印第安人。我看到他们脸上涂着战绘，显然已经踏上了战途。

我的第一反应是同他们握手，因为他们显得如此渴望。于是我向其中一人伸出手，他猛地一把死死攥住我的手，用力向前猛拽；另一人一把抓住了我骡子的缰绳，眨眼间我就被团团包围。还没等我做出任何反抗，他们就从枪套里夺走了我的左轮手枪，接着一把印第安战斧狠狠劈在我的头上，几乎将我当场打晕。

===Original===

My gun, which was lying across the saddle, was snatched from its place, and finally the Indian, who had hold of the bridle, started off towards the Arkansas River, leading the mule, which was being lashed by the other Indians who were following.
The savages were all singing, yelling and whooping, as only Indians can do, when they are having their little game all their own way. While looking towards the river I saw, on the opposite side, an immense village moving down along the bank, and then I became convinced that the Indians had left the post and were now starting out on the warpath. My captors crossed the stream with me, and as we waded through the shallow water they continued to lash the mule and myself. Finally they brought me before an important looking body of Indians, who proved to be the chiefs and principal warriors. I soon recognized old Satanta among them, as well as others whom I knew, and I supposed it was all over with me.
The Indians were jabbering away so rapidly among themselves that I could not understand what they were saying. Satanta at last asked me where I had been; and, as good luck would have it, a happy thought struck me. I told him I had been after a herd of cattle or “whoa-haws,” as they called them. It so happened that the Indians had been out of meat for several weeks, as the large herd of cattle which had been promised them had not yet arrived, although expected by them.
The moment that I mentioned that I had been searching for the “whoa-haws,” old Satanta began questioning me in a very eager manner. He asked me where the cattle were, and I replied that they were back only a few miles, and that I had been sent by General Hazen to inform him that the cattle were coming, and that they were intended for his people. This seemed to please the old rascal, who also wanted to know if there were any soldiers with the herd, and my reply was that there were. Thereupon the chiefs held a consultation, and presently Satanta asked me if General Hazen had really said that they should have the cattle. I replied in the affirmative, and added that I had been directed to bring the cattle to them. I followed this up with a very dignified inquiry, asking why his young men had treated me so.

===Chinese===

我横放在马鞍上的步枪也被一把夺走。最后，那名拉着缰绳的印第安人牵着骡子朝阿肯色河走去，跟在后面的其他印第安人则不断用皮鞭抽打着骡子。

这些蛮人一边唱着、叫喊着、高声欢呼着，那种腔调只有印第安人在完全掌控局面时才发得出来。望向河边时，我看到对岸有一座庞大的印第安村落正沿着河岸向下游迁移，这让我确信印第安人已经离开了哨所，此刻正正式踏上战途。捕获我的人押着我穿过河流，当我们涉过浅水时，他们继续狠狠抽打着骡子和我。最后，他们把我押到一群神情威严的印第安人面前，他们显然是酋长和核心勇士。我很快在其中认出了老萨坦塔以及其他几位熟人，心想这下自己彻底完了。

印第安人彼此之间语速飞快地嘀咕着，我根本听不懂他们在说什么。萨坦塔终于开口问我去了哪里；所幸天无绝人之路，我脑海中突然灵光一闪。我告诉他，自己是去寻找一群肉牛，也就是他们口中的“哇-哈”（whoa-haws）。巧的是，印第安人已经好几个星期没吃上肉了，因为原本许诺给他们的一大群肉牛虽然一直在盼，却迟迟没有运到。

一听到我说是在寻找“哇-哈”，老萨坦塔立刻急切地盘问起来。他问我牛群在哪里，我回答说就在后面几英里处，哈森将军派我前来通知他牛群正在运来，就是专门供给他的部族的。这似乎让这个老无赖颇为高兴，他又问牛群周围是否有士兵护送，我回答说有。于是酋长们聚在一起商议了一番，不一会儿萨坦塔问我，哈森将军是否真说过这些牛全归他们。我作了肯定的答复，并补充说自己正是奉命把牛群带给他们的。接着我神情庄重地质问他，为什么他的年轻战士要这般粗暴地对待我。

===Original===

The old wretch intimated that it was only “a freak of the boys”; that the young men had wanted to see if I was brave; in fact, they had only meant to test my bravery, and that the whole thing was a joke.
The veteran liar was now beating me at my own game of lying; but I was very glad of it, as it was in my favor. I did not let him suspect that I doubted his veracity, but I remarked that it was a rough way to treat friends. He immediately ordered his young men to give me back my arms, and scolded them for what they had done. Of course, the sly old dog was now playing it very fine, as he was anxious to get possession of the cattle, with which he believed “there was a heap of soldiers coming.” He had concluded it was not best to fight the soldiers if he could get the cattle peaceably.
Another council was held by the chiefs, and in a few minutes old Satanta came and asked me if I would go over and bring the cattle down to the opposite side of the river, so that they could get them. I replied, “Of course; that’s my instruction from General Hazen.”
Satanta said I must not feel angry at his young men, for they had only been acting in fun. He then inquired if I wished any of his men to accompany me to the cattle herd. I replied that it would be better for me to go alone, and then the soldiers could keep right on to Fort Larned, while I could drive the herd down on the bottom. So, wheeling my mule around, I was soon re-crossing the river, leaving old Satanta in the firm belief that I had told him a straight story, and was going for the cattle, which only existed in my imagination.
I hardly knew what to do, but thought that if I could get the river between the Indians and myself I would have a good three-quarters of a mile the start of them, and could then make a run for Fort Larned, as my mule was a good one.
Thus far my cattle story had panned out all right; but just as I reached the opposite bank of the river, I looked behind and saw that ten or fifteen Indians who had begun to suspect something crooked, were following me. The moment that my mule secured a good foothold on the bank, I urged him into a gentle lope towards the place where, according to my statement, the cattle were to be brought. Upon reaching a little ridge, and riding down the other side out of view, I turned my mule and headed him westward for Fort Larned.

===Chinese===

那个老恶棍暗示说这不过是“年轻人的胡闹”；小伙子们只是想瞧瞧我够不够勇敢；事实上，他们只是想试探一下我的胆量，整件事纯属一场玩笑。

这个老油条骗子此刻在我自己的谎言把戏上反客为主；但我对此感到非常高兴，因为局势正朝对我有利的方向发展。我没有表现出任何怀疑他真诚的意思，只是抱怨说这样对待朋友未免太粗暴了。他随即命令年轻战士把武器还给我，并严厉斥责了他们的所作所为。显而易见，这只狡猾的老狐狸此刻正小心翼翼地周旋，因为他急于得到牛群，而且深信“有大批士兵正随牛群赶来”。他认定如果能和平得到肉牛，最好不要同士兵开战。

酋长们又举行了一次会议，几分钟后，老萨坦塔走过来问我，愿不愿意过河把牛群赶到河对岸，好让他们接收。我回答说：“那是自然，这正是哈森将军给我的指示。”

萨坦塔嘱咐我不要生年轻人的气，说他们不过是闹着玩罢了。接着他又打听我是否需要他的人陪我一起去接牛群。我回答说最好由我独自前去，这样士兵可以直接前往拉尼德堡，而我则可以把牛群赶到河滩洼地。于是，我调转骡头，很快又重新渡过了河，留下老萨坦塔深信我所说的是大实话，正去接那群只存在于我脑海中的牛群。

我一时不知如何是好，但心想只要能把河流隔在印第安人和我之间，我就能领先他们整整四分之三英里，到时候凭借身下这头好骡子，就能全力冲刺逃往拉尼德堡。

到目前为止，我的肉牛谎话进展十分顺利；但当我刚刚踏上对岸河滩时，回头一看，发现有10到15名印第安人似乎起了疑心，正从后面尾随跟了上来。骡蹄一在岸上站稳，我就催促它小跑起来，朝我声称要带牛群过来的方向奔去。一爬上一处小山脊并滑下背弹面脱离他们的视线后，我立刻调转骡头，向西直奔拉尼德堡而去。

===Original===

I let him out for all that he was worth, and when I came out on a little rise of ground, I looked back, and saw the Indian village in plain sight. My pursuers were now on the ridge which I had passed over, and were looking for me in every direction.
Presently they spied me, and seeing that I was running away, they struck out in swift pursuit, and in a few minutes it became painfully evident that they were gaining on me. They kept up the chase as far as Ash Creek, six miles from Fort Larned. I still led them half a mile, as their horses had not gained much during the last half of the race. My mule seemed to have gotten his second wind, and as I was on the old road I had played the whip and spurs on him without much cessation. The Indians likewise had urged their steeds to the utmost.
Finally, upon reaching the dividing ridge between Ash Greek and Pawnee Fork, I saw Fort Larned only four miles away. It was now sundown, and I heard the evening gun at the fort. The troops of the garrison little dreamed that there was a man flying for his life from the Indians and trying to reach the post. The Indians were once more gaining on me, and when I crossed the Pawnee Fork, two miles from the post, two or three of them were only a quarter of a mile behind me. Just as I had gained the opposite bank of the stream I was overjoyed to see some soldiers in a government wagon, only a short distance off. I yelled at the top of my voice, and riding up to them, told them that the Indians were after me.
Denver Jim, a well-known scout, asked how many there were, and upon my informing him that there were about a dozen, he said: “Let’s drive the wagon into the trees, and we’ll lay for ’em.” The team was hurriedly driven in among the trees and low box-elder bushes, and there secreted.
We did not have to wait long for the Indians, who came dashing up, lashing their horses, which were panting and blowing. We let two of them pass by, but we opened a lively fire on the next three or four, killing two at the first crack. The others following, discovered that they had run into an ambush, and whirling off into the brush they turned and ran back in the direction whence they had come. The two who had passed heard the firing and made their escape. We scalped the two that we had killed, and appropriated their arms and equipments; and then catching their horses, we made our way into the post.

===Chinese===

我使出浑身解数狠命催骡狂奔，当我冲上一处小高地回望时，印第安村落历历在目。追击者此刻已经爬上了我刚才越过的那道山脊，正四下搜寻我的踪迹。

很快他们发现了我，见我正在逃跑，便飞马全速追赶上来；没过几分钟，一个令人揪心的情况变得显而易见——他们正在逼近我。他们一直穷追不舍，直到距拉尼德堡6英里的白蜡溪（Ash Creek）。此时我依然领先他们半英里，因为在后半程赛跑中，他们的马匹并没能缩小多少差距。我的骡子似乎缓过了第二口气，加上奔跑在平整的旧驿道上，我不停地挥鞭加刺，骡子马不停蹄。印第安人也同样把他们的坐骑催逼到了极限。

终于，当我登上白蜡溪与波尼河岔（Pawnee Fork）之间的分水岭时，看到拉尼德堡就在仅4英里外。此时正值日落时分，我听到了哨所传来的暮炮声。驻军将士做梦也不会想到，正有一名男子为了活命逃避印第安人的追杀，拼命向哨所狂奔而来。印第安人再次追赶上来，当我穿过距哨所仅2英里的波尼河岔时，有两三个人距我仅有四分之一英里。就在我冲上对岸的一刹那，欣喜若狂地看到不远处正有一辆载着士兵的官办大车。我扯开嗓子高声大喊，策骡冲向他们，高喊印第安人正在追杀我。

知名侦察兵丹佛·吉姆（Denver Jim）问对方有多少人，我告诉他大概有12个人。他说：“快把大车赶进树林里，咱们伏击他们。”大车连忙被赶进树丛和低矮的梣叶槭灌木林中隐藏起来。

我们没等多久，印第安人就飞驰而至，狠命抽打着喘着粗气的坐骑。我们放过了前头的两名骑士，随后对紧跟上来的三四人发起猛烈射击，第一轮枪响就打死了两人。后面的追兵发现中了伏击，急忙调转马头钻入灌木丛，原路仓皇逃窜。先前跑过去的两人听到枪声也仓皇逃脱。我们剥下了被击毙两人的头皮，缴获了他们的武器和装备；随后套上他们的战马，一路回到了哨所。

===Original===

The soldiers had heard us firing, and as we were approaching the fort the drums were being beaten, and the buglers were sounding the call to fall in. The officers had thought that Satanta and his Indians were coming in to capture the fort.
It seems that on the morning of that day, two hours after General Hazen had taken his departure, old Satanta drove into the post in an ambulance, which he had received some months before as a present from the government. He appeared to be angry and bent on mischief. In an interview with Captain Parker, the commanding officer, he asked why General Hazen had left the post without supplying the beef cattle which had been promised to him. The Captain told him that the cattle were surely on the road, but he could not explain why they were detained.
The interview proved to be a stormy one, and Satanta made numerous threats, saying that if he wished, he could capture the whole post with his warriors. Captain Parker, who was a brave man, gave Satanta to understand that he was reckoning beyond his powers, and would find it a more difficult undertaking than he had any idea of, as they were prepared for him at any moment. The interview finally terminated, and Satanta angrily left the officers presence. Going over to the sutler’s store he sold his ambulance to Mr. Tappan the past trader, and with a portion of the proceeds he secretly managed to secure some whisky from some bad men around the fort. There are always to be found around every frontier post some men who will sell whisky to the Indians at any time and under any circumstances, notwithstanding it is a flagrant violation of both civil and military regulations.
Satanta mounted his horse, and taking the whisky with him, he rode rapidly away and proceeded straight to his village. He had not been gone over an hour, when he returned to the vicinity of the post accompanied by his warriors who came in from every direction, to the number of seven or eight hundred. It was evident that the irate old rascal was “on his ear,” so to speak, and it looked as if he intended to carry out his threat of capturing the fort. The garrison at once turned out and prepared to receive the redskins, who, when within half a mile, circled around the fort and fired numerous shots into it, instead of trying to take it by assault.

===Chinese===

士兵们听到了我们的交火声，当我们接近要塞时，战鼓擂响，号手正在吹响集结号。军官们还以为萨坦塔和他的印第安大军前来强攻要塞了。

原来在当天上午，也就是哈森将军出发两个小时后，老萨坦塔坐着一辆轻便马车进了哨所，那是几个月前政府赠送给他的礼物。他显得怒气冲冲，满脑子都是敌意。在同指挥官帕克上尉（Captain Parker）面谈时，他质问为何哈森将军离开哨所时没有兑现给他的肉牛。上尉告诉他，牛群肯定在半路上，但他无法解释究竟为何耽搁了。

这场会面气氛极为剑拔弩张，萨坦塔发出了连番威胁，声称只要他愿意，带上他的勇士就能攻陷整座哨所。英勇果敢的帕克上尉严词警告萨坦塔，说他完全高估了自己的力量，真动手的话会发现这比他想象的要难得多，因为守军随时严阵以待。会谈最终不欢而散，萨坦塔怒气冲冲地离开了军官办公室。他走到随军小卖店，把自己的轻便马车卖给了哨所贸易商塔潘先生（Mr. Tappan），并用其中一部分钱，偷偷从要塞周围的几个地痞无赖手里买到了一些威士忌。在每一个边疆哨所周围，总能找到一些不管任何时间、任何条件都会把威士忌卖给印第安人的恶棍，尽管这公然触犯了民法与军规。

萨坦塔骑上战马，带上威士忌疾驰而去，径直返回了他的村落。他离开还不到一个小时，就带着从四面八方涌来的部族勇士重返哨所附近，人数多达700到800人。很显然，这个暴怒的老恶棍已经“彻底红了眼”，看样子真打算实施他攻克哨所的威胁。守军立即全员出动，准备迎击这群红皮肤战士。印第安人逼近到半英里以内时，绕着要塞四处盘旋游弋，朝要塞内部乱枪齐射，却始终未敢发动正面强攻。

===Original===

While this circular movement was going on, it was observed that the Indian village in the distance was packing up, preparatory to leaving, and it was soon under way. The mounted warriors remained behind some little time, to give their families an opportunity to get away, as they feared that the troops might possibly in some manner intercept them. Finally, they encircled the post several times, fired some farewell rounds, and then galloped away over the prairie to overtake their fast departing village. On their way thither, they surprised and killed a party of woodchoppers down on the Pawnee Fork, as well as some herders who were guarding beef cattle; some seven or eight men in all, were killed, and it was evident that the Indians meant business.
The soldiers with the wagon﻿—whom I had met at the crossing of the Pawnee Fork﻿—had been out for the bodies of the men. Under the circumstances it was no wonder that the garrison, upon hearing the reports of our guns when we fired upon the party whom we ambushed, should have thought the Indians were coming back to give them another “turn.”
We found that all was excitement at the post; double guards had been put on duty, and Captain Parker had all the scouts at his headquarters. He was endeavoring to get someone to take some important dispatches to General Sheridan at Fort Hays. I reported to him at once, and stated where I had met the Indians and how I had escaped from them.
“You was very fortunate, Cody, in thinking of that cattle story; but for that little game your hair would now be an ornament to a Kiowa’s lodge,” said he.
Just then Dick Curtis spoke up and said: “Cody, the Captain is anxious to send some dispatches to General Sheridan, at Fort Hays, and none of the scouts here seem to be very willing to undertake the trip. They say they are not well enough acquainted with the country to find the way at night.”
As a storm was coming up it was quite dark, and the scouts feared that they would lose the way; besides it was a dangerous ride, as a large party of Indians were known to be camped on Walnut Creek, on the direct road to Fort Hays. It was evident that Curtis was trying to induce me to volunteer. I made some evasive answer to Curtis, for I did not care to volunteer after my long day’s ride. But Curtis did not let the matter drop.

===Chinese===

当这种兜圈游弋还在继续时，有人注意到远处的印第安村落正在收拾行装，准备撤离，不久便开拔启程了。骑马的勇士在后面殿后留守了一段时间，好给他们的家眷争取撤离的时间，因为他们担心部队可能以某种方式予以拦截。最终，勇士们绕着哨所盘旋了几圈，打了数枪作为告别，便策马飞奔越过大草原，去追赶迅速离去的村落大队。在追赶途中，他们突袭并杀害了波尼河岔下方的一群伐木工，以及几名看守肉牛的放牧人；前后总共杀害了约7到8人，很显然这群印第安人动了真格。

我先前在波尼河岔渡口遇到的那辆大车上的士兵，本来就是出来收殓遇害人员遗体的。在这种紧张局势下，难怪守军听见我们在伏击那伙追兵时打响的枪声，还以为是印第安人杀了个回马枪，要再给他们来上一轮“围攻”。

我们发现整个哨所已乱成一团、戒备森严；哨兵被加派了双岗，帕克上尉将所有侦察兵召集到了指挥部。他正极力寻找合适人选，把几份紧急公文火速送交驻扎在海斯堡的谢里登将军。我立刻上前向他汇报，详细说明了遭遇印第安人的地点以及自己是如何死里逃生的。

“科迪，能想出那个肉牛的故事，你真是命大；要不是耍了那点小把戏，你头顶上的头发这会儿早就成了基奥瓦人帐篷里的挂饰了。”他说。

就在这时，迪克·柯蒂斯插话说：“科迪，上尉急着派人给海斯堡的谢里登将军送信，可这里的侦察兵没一个愿意接下这趟差事。他们推说对那边的地形不熟，夜里容易迷路。”

当时暴风雨将至，夜色极其昏暗，侦察兵们生怕在荒原上迷失方向；况且这趟差事凶险万分，因为大家都知道有一大群印第安人正驻扎在直通海斯堡必经之路的核桃溪（Walnut Creek）畔。显而易见，柯蒂斯是在试图说动我主动请缨。我对柯蒂斯敷衍了几句，毕竟跑了整整一天，我实在不想再自告奋勇。但柯蒂斯不依不饶，死咬着不放。

===Original===

Said he:
“I wish, Bill, that you were not so tired by your chase of today, for you know the country better than the rest of the boys, and I am certain that you could go through.”
“As far as the ride to Fort Hays is concerned, that alone would matter but little to me,” I said, “but it is a risky piece of work just now, as the country is full of hostile Indians; still if no other scout is willing to volunteer, I will chance it. I’ll go, provided I am furnished with a good horse. I am tired of being chased on a government mule by Indians.” At this Captain Nolan, who had been listening to our conversation, said:
“Bill, you may have the best horse in my company. You can take your choice if you will carry these dispatches. Although it is against regulations to dismount an enlisted man, I have no hesitancy in such a case of urgent necessity as this is, in telling you that you may have any horse you may wish.”
“Captain, your first sergeant has a splendid horse, and that’s the one I want. If he’ll let me ride that horse, I’ll be ready to start in one hour, storm or no storm,” said I.
“Good enough, Bill; you shall have the horse; but are you sure you can find your way on such a dark night as this?”
“I have hunted on nearly every acre of ground between here and Fort Hays, and I can almost keep my route by the bones of the dead buffaloes.” I confidently replied.
“Never fear, Captain, about Cody not finding the way; he is as good in the dark as he is in the daylight,” said Curtis.
An orderly was sent for the horse, and the animal was soon brought up, although the sergeant “kicked” a little against letting him go. After eating a lunch and filling a canteen with brandy, I went to headquarters and put my own saddle and bridle on the horse I was to ride. I then got the dispatches, and by ten o’clock was on the road to Fort Hays, which was sixty-five miles distant across the country. The scouts had all bidden me a hearty goodbye, and wished me success, not knowing when, if ever, they would again gaze upon “my warlike form,” as the poet would say.
It was dark as pitch, but this I rather liked, as there was little probability of any of the redskins seeing me unless I stumbled upon them accidentally. My greatest danger was that my horse might run into a hole and fall down, and in this way get away from me.

===Chinese===

他说：
“比尔，要不是你今天被追得筋疲力尽，我真希望你能去，因为你比其他小伙子都熟悉地形，我相信你一定能冲过去。”
“单就骑马跑一趟海斯堡来说，对我本算不上什么大事，”我说，“但这节骨眼上可是刀尖上舔血的买卖，到处都是充满敌意的印第安人；不过要是没别的侦察兵肯去，我就赌上一把。去可以，但前提是得给我配一匹好马。今天被印第安人骑马追着我骑政府骡子跑，我受够这种窝囊气了。”
一直在旁听我们谈话的诺兰上尉（Captain Nolan）闻言说道：
“比尔，我连里最好的战马随你挑选。只要你肯送这几份公文，随便挑哪匹都行。虽然让一名列兵把坐骑让出来不合条例，但在这种万分紧急的关头，我毫不犹豫地允许你挑走任何一匹中意的马。”
“上尉，您那位军士长手里的战马棒极了，我就要那匹。只要他肯借马，不管有没有暴风雨，我1小时内就能出发。”我说。
“一言为定，比尔；马归你了；但这么黑的夜里，你真有把握找得到路？”
“这儿到海斯堡之间的土地，几乎每一英亩我都去打过猎，我就算靠地上死野牛的骨头，也绝走不丢路。”我胸有成竹地答道。
“上尉，您把心放宽，科迪绝不会迷路；黑夜里他照样跟大白天一样眼明心亮。”柯蒂斯说。
勤务兵被派去牵马，战马很快便被带了过来，尽管军士长为割爱心爱的坐骑小声嘟囔抱怨了一阵。吃过点心、在水壶里灌满白兰地后，我来到指挥部，把自己的鞍具套在了即将骑乘的骏马上。随后我接过公文，到了晚上10点便踏上了前往海斯堡的路程，两地横跨荒原足有65英里。侦察兵们热情地同我握手道别，祝我一路顺风，谁也不知道他们何年何月、甚至是否还能再见到诗中所写的那副“英武身姿”。
夜色漆黑如墨，但我倒挺喜欢这样，因为只要不迎头撞个正着，红皮肤敌人发现我的概率微乎其微。我最大的危险在于战马可能失蹄踩进洞里摔倒，进而从我手中挣脱跑掉。

===Original===

To avoid any such accident, I tied one end of my rawhide lariat to the bridle and the other end to my belt. I didn’t propose to be left on foot, alone out on the prairie.
It was, indeed, a wise precaution that I had taken, for within the next three miles the horse, sure enough, stepped into a prairie-dog’s hole, and down he went, throwing me clear over his head. Springing to his feet, before I could catch hold of the bridle, he galloped away into the darkness; but when he reached the full length of the lariat, he found that he was picketed to Bison William. I brought him up standing, and after finding my gun, which had dropped to the ground, I went up to him and in a moment was in the saddle again, and went on my way rejoicing keeping straight on my course until I came to the ravines leading into Walnut Creek, twenty-five miles from Fort Larned, where the country became rougher, requiring me to travel slower and more carefully, as I feared the horse might fall over the bank, it being difficult to see anything five feet ahead. As a good horse is not very apt to jump over a bank, if left to guide himself, I let mine pick his own way. I was now proceeding as quietly as possible, for I was in the vicinity of a band of Indians who had recently camped in that locality. I thought that I had passed somewhat above the spot, having made a little circuit to the west with that intention; but as bad luck would have it this time, when I came up near the creek I suddenly rode in among a herd of horses. The animals became frightened and ran off in every direction.
I knew at once that I was among Indian horses, and had walked into the wrong pew; so without waiting to apologize, I backed out as quickly as possible. At this moment a dog, not fifty yards away, set up a howl, and then I heard some Indians engaged in conversation;﻿—they were guarding the horses, and had been sleeping. Hearing my horse’s retreating footsteps toward the hills, and thus becoming aware that there had been an enemy in their camp, they mounted their steeds and started for me.
I urged my horse to his full speed, taking the chances of his falling into holes, and guided him up the creek bottom. The Indians followed me as fast as they could by the noise I made, but I soon distanced them; and then crossed the creek.

===Chinese===

为了避免这种意外，我用生牛皮套索的一头紧紧拴在马嚼子上，另一头牢牢系在自己的腰带上。我可不想落得孤身一人徒步被困在大草原上的下场。
事实证明，这的确是一招极为高明的防备。在接下来的3英里之内，战马果然一脚踩进了草原犬鼠洞，猛地向前栽倒，把我整个人凌空甩过了马头。战马一跃而起，还没等我抓稳缰绳，便纵身向黑暗中狂奔而去；但等它跑满了整根套索的长度，才发现自己原来还牢牢拴在“野牛威廉”的腰上。我硬生生把它拉得原地立定，找回跌落在地的步枪后走到它跟前，眨眼间重新翻身上鞍，满心欢喜地继续赶路。我一路笔直前行，直到抵达通往核桃溪的冲沟地带——这里距拉尼德堡已有25英里。此地地势险恶崎岖，周围黑得连眼前5英尺都辨认不清，我生怕马匹失足滑下陡岸，只得放慢速度，格外谨慎地前行。一匹好马如果任由它自己认路，通常是绝不会盲目跳下悬崖的，于是我索性松开缰绳，任由它自行择路。此时我尽可能放轻脚步前行，因为我知道最近有一群印第安人正驻扎在这片区域。我本以为自己有意向西绕了一个小圈，应该已经从他们营地的上游绕过去了；但偏偏运气不佳，当我走到溪边时，突然冷不防闯进了一群马匹之中。牲口们顿时受惊，四散奔逃。
我立刻意识到自己一头扎进了印第安人的马群，走错了庙门；于是连赔礼道歉都顾不上，以最快速度抽身倒退。就在此时，不到50码外的一条猎犬突然狂吠起来，紧接着我听到了几个印第安人的说话声——他们原本负责看管马群，刚才一直在睡大觉。听到我坐骑往山丘方向撤退的蹄声，他们明白营地里钻进了敌人，急忙翻身上马朝我直追过来。
我催马全速狂奔，豁出去不再顾忌马匹踩空摔倒的风险，策马沿着溪流低谷一路向上狂冲。印第安人凭着我弄出的声响在后面紧追不舍，但我很快便将他们甩开，随后纵马渡过了小溪。

===Original===

When I had traveled several miles in a straight course, as I supposed, I took out my compass and by the light of a match saw that I was bearing two points to the east of north. At once changing my course to the direct route, I pushed rapidly on through the darkness towards Smoky Hill River. At about three o’clock in the morning I began traveling more cautiously, as I was afraid of running into another band of Indians. Occasionally I scared up a herd of buffaloes or antelopes, or coyotes, or deer, which would frighten my horse for a moment, but with the exception of these slight alarms I got along all right.
After crossing Smoky Hill River, I felt comparatively safe as this was the last stream I had to cross. Riding on to the northward I struck the old Santa Fe trail, ten miles from Fort Hays, just at break of day.
My horse did not seem much fatigued, and being anxious to make good time and get as near the post as possible before it was fairly daylight as there might be bands of Indians camped along Big Creek, I urged him forward as fast as he could go. As I had not “lost” any Indians, I was not now anxious to make their acquaintance, and shortly after reveille rode into the post. I proceeded directly to General Sheridan’s headquarters, and, was met at the door, by Colonel Moore, aide-de-camp on General Sheridan’s staff who asked me on what business I had come.
“I have dispatches for General Sheridan, and my instructions from Captain Parker, commanding Fort Larned, are that they shall be delivered to the General as soon as possible,” said I.
Colonel Moore invited me into one of the offices, and said he would hand the dispatches to the General as soon as he got up.
“I prefer to give these dispatches to General Sheridan myself, and at once,” was my reply.
The General, who was sleeping in the same building, hearing our voices, called out, “Send the man in with the dispatches.” I was ushered into the General’s presence, and as we had met before he recognized me and said:
“Hello, Cody, is that you?”
“Yes, sir; I have some dispatches here for you, from Captain Parker,” said I, as I handed the package over to him.
He hurriedly read them, and said they were important; and then he asked me all about General Hazen and where he had gone, and about the breaking out of the Kiowas and Comanches.

===Chinese===

当自以为笔直前行了几英里后，我掏出罗盘，借着划着火柴的微光一看，发现自己偏离了正北两个罗盘点，正朝东北方向行进。我立刻调整方向修正为直行路线，穿越沉沉黑夜向烟山河（Smoky Hill River）疾驰。凌晨大约3点左右，我开始更加小心翼翼地行进，生怕再次撞上另一股印第安人。一路上，我偶尔会惊动一群野牛、叉角羚、郊狼或鹿，它们冷不防窜出来让我的坐骑受惊片刻，但除了这些虚惊之外，我一路顺畅无阻。
渡过烟山河后，我心里顿感踏实了许多，因为这是行程中最后一条需要横渡的河流。策马向北前行，天刚蒙蒙亮时，我在距海斯堡10英里处插上了古老的圣菲小道（Santa Fe trail）。
我的战马看起来并未显出太多疲态；而我急于争取时间，想赶在天色彻底大亮前尽可能靠近哨所，因为大溪（Big Creek）沿岸随时可能有印第安人扎营。于是我催马全速狂奔。既然我并未“丢失”任何印第安人，眼下自然毫无兴趣去结识他们。吹过晨号不久，我策马冲进了哨所。我径直前往谢里登将军的指挥部，在门前遇到了将军的副官摩尔上校（Colonel Moore），他向我询问来意。
“我有紧急公文呈交谢里登将军，拉尼德堡指挥官帕克上尉指示我必须以最快速度亲手呈交将军。”我说。
摩尔上校请我进了一间办公室，说等将军起床后会把公文转交给他。
“我宁愿亲自把公文面呈谢里登将军，而且必须是现在。”我回答道。
正在同一栋建筑内就寝的将军听到了我们的谈话声，大声喊道：“让送公文的人进来。”我被引荐到将军面前，因为我们先前打过交道，他一眼认出了我，说道：
“你好啊，科迪，是你吗？”
“是的，长官；我带来了帕克上尉呈交给您的几份紧急公文。”我一边说着，一边将信封递了过去。
他迅速阅毕信件，神色凝重地说内容十分紧急；随后他向我详细打听了哈森将军的去向，以及基奥瓦人和科曼奇人起兵造反的全部细节。

===Original===

I gave him all the information that I possessed, and related the events and adventures of the previous day and night.
“Bill,” said he, “you must have had a pretty lively ride. You certainly had a close call when you ran into the Indians on Walnut Creek. That was a good joke that you played on old Satanta. I suppose you’re pretty tired after your long journey?”
“I am rather weary, General, that’s a fact, as I have been in the saddle since yesterday morning;” was my reply, “but my horse is more tired than I am, and needs attention full as much if not more,” I added. Thereupon the General called an orderly and gave instructions to have my animal well taken care of, and then he said, “Cody, come in and have some breakfast with me.”
“No, thank you, General,” said I, “Hays City is only a mile from here, and I prefer riding over there, as I know about everyone in the town, and want to see some of my friends.”
“Very well; do as you please, and come to the post afterwards as I want to see you,” said he.
Bidding him good morning, and telling him that I would return in a few hours, I rode over to Hays City, and at the Perry House I met many of my old friends who were of course all glad to see me. I took some refreshments and a two hours nap, and afterward returned to Fort Hays, as I was requested.
As I rode up to the headquarters I noticed several scouts in a little group, evidently engaged in conversation on some important matter. Upon inquiry I learned that General Sheridan had informed them that he was desirous of sending a dispatch to Fort Dodge, a distance of ninety-five miles.
The Indians had recently killed two or three men while they were carrying dispatches between Fort Hays and Fort Dodge, and on this account none of the scouts seemed at all anxious to volunteer, although a reward of several hundred dollars was offered to anyone who would carry the dispatches. They had learned of my experiences of the previous day, and asked me if I did not think it would be a dangerous trip. I gave it as my opinion that a man might possibly go through without seeing an Indian, but that the chances were ten to one that he would have an exceedingly lively run and a hard time before he reached his destination, if he ever got there at all.

===Chinese===

我将所知晓的一切毫无保留地汇报给他，并详述了前一天白天与昨夜经历的所有险境。
“比尔，”他说，“你这一趟跑得可真够惊心动魄的。在核桃溪迎头撞上印第安人，能脱身真是死里逃生。你拿老萨坦塔开涮的那出好戏可真够绝的。跑了这么远的路，你现在一定累坏了吧？”
“说实在的，将军，我确实相当疲惫，毕竟我从昨天早晨起就一直骑在马鞍上没下来过；”我回答说，“但我这匹马比我更累，它比我更需要好好照料。”将军随即叫来一名勤务兵，命令他务必将我的坐骑照看妥当，接着他说：“科迪，进屋跟我一起吃点早餐吧。”
“不了，谢谢将军，”我说，“海斯城（Hays City）离这儿只有1英里，我想骑马去那儿，镇上的人我差不多都认识，想去看看几位老朋友。”
“那好，随你便吧；去完之后回哨所一趟，我还有事找你。”他说。
我向他道了声早安，说自己几个小时后就会回来，随后便骑马去了海斯城。在佩里旅馆（Perry House），我遇到了许多老朋友，大家见到我都惊喜万分。我吃了点东西，舒舒服服睡了两个小时，随后按照要求返回了海斯堡。
当我骑马来到指挥部前时，注意到几名侦察兵正聚成一小群，显然在交头接耳讨论某件要紧事。打听之下我才得知，谢里登将军通知他们，正急需派人送一份公文到95英里外的道奇堡。
就在前不久，印第安人刚在海斯堡和道奇堡之间杀害了送公文的两三名人员，因此尽管军方悬赏数百美元重金招募信使，却依然没有任何侦察兵愿意挺身涉险。他们得知了我前一天的惊险经历，纷纷跑来问我是不是觉得这趟路极其凶险。我直言不讳地表达了看法：一个人或许有万分之一的侥幸能一路撞大运碰不上印第安人，但十比一的概率是，在抵达目的地之前，他准会经历一场玩命大逃亡和九死一生的苦头，甚至能不能活着跑到地方都得打个大大的问号。

===Original===

Leaving the scouts to decide among themselves as to who was to go, I reported to General Sheridan, who also informed me that he wished someone to carry dispatches to Fort Dodge. While we were talking, his chief of scouts Dick Parr, entered and stated that none of the scouts had yet volunteered. Upon hearing this I got my “brave” up a little, and said:
“General, if there is no one ready to volunteer, I’ll carry your dispatches myself.”
“I had not thought of asking you to do this duty, Cody, as you are already pretty hard worked. But it is really important that these dispatches should go through,” said the General.
“Well, if you don’t get a courier by four o’clock this afternoon, I’ll be ready for business at that time. All I want is a fresh horse,” said I; “meantime I’ll take a little more rest.”
It was not much of a rest, however, that I got, for I went over to Hays City again and had “a time with the boys.” I came back to the post at the appointed hour, and finding that no one had volunteered, I reported to General Sheridan. He had selected an excellent horse for me, and on handing me the dispatches he said:
“You can start as soon as you wish﻿—the sooner the better; and good luck go with you, my boy.”
In about an hour afterwards I was on the road, and just before dark I crossed Smoky Hill River. I had not yet urged my horse much, as I was saving his strength for the latter end of the route, and for any run that I might have to make in case the “wild-boys” should “jump” me. So far I had not seen a sign of Indians, and as evening came on I felt comparatively safe.
I had no adventures worth relating during the night, and just before daylight I found myself approaching Saw-log Crossing, on the Pawnee Fork, having then ridden about seventy miles.
A company of colored cavalry, commanded by Major Cox, was stationed at this point, and I approached their camp cautiously, for fear that the pickets might fire upon me﻿—as the darkey soldiers were liable to shoot first and cry “halt” afterwards. When within hearing distance I yelled out at the top of my voice, and was answered by one of the pickets. I told him not to shoot, as I was a scout from Fort Hays; and then, calling the sergeant of the guard, I went up to the vidette of the post, who readily recognized me.

===Chinese===

侦察兵们还在为派谁去争论不休，我便直接前往谢里登将军处报到，他也向我表示急需派人送公文到道奇堡。正说着，他的侦察队长迪克·帕尔（Dick Parr）走了进来，报告说还没有一个侦察兵愿意挺身而出。听到这话，我的“胆气”一下子涌了上来，说道：
“将军，要是没人愿意去，我亲自替您把公文送去。”
“科迪，我本没打算派你出这趟公差，因为你已经太劳累了。但这些公文确实必须送达，万分紧急。”将军说。
“这样吧，要是今天下午4点前您还没找到信使，到时候我就听候调遣。我只要一匹体力充沛的战马，”我说，“这会儿我先去稍微歇歇。”
不过，我其实并没怎么正经休息，而是又跑到海斯城跟“弟兄们痛痛快快聚了一阵”。在约定的时间我准时返回哨所，见依然无人请缨，便向谢里登将军报到。他已特意为我挑选了一匹出色的战马，递给我公文时嘱咐道：
“你随时可以启程——越快越好；祝你好运，小伙子。”
大约1小时后，我便策马踏上征途，赶在天黑前渡过了烟山河。一路上我还没怎么催促坐骑，为的是把它的体力保留在后半程，以及防备万一碰上“荒原野小子”突袭时好全力逃命。到目前为止，我还没瞧见任何印第安人的踪迹，随着暮色降临，我心里顿感踏实了不少。
夜间行进一路平安，没有什么值得大书特书的惊险，天亮前不久，我已接近波尼河岔的原木渡口（Saw-log Crossing），此时已连续骑行了约70英里。
由考克斯少校（Major Cox）指挥的一个黑人骑兵连正驻扎在此处。我小心翼翼地向营地靠近，生怕被外围岗哨误开枪击中——因为这些黑人士兵往往习惯先开枪、后喊“站住”。到了声音能传过去的距离，我扯开嗓门大喊了一声，一名哨兵作了回应。我大声招呼他千万别开枪，说我是从海斯堡来的侦察兵；随后在卫兵军士的引导下，我走上前哨，哨兵一眼便认出了我。

===Original===

I entered the camp and proceeded to the tent of Major Cox, to whom I handed a letter from General Sheridan requesting him to give me a fresh horse. He at once complied with the request.
After I had slept an hour and had eaten a lunch, I again jumped into the saddle, and before sunrise I was once more on the road. It was twenty-five miles to Fort Dodge, and I arrived there between nine and ten o’clock, without having seen a single Indian.
After delivering the dispatches to the commanding officer, I met Johnny Austin, chief of scouts at this post, who was an old friend of mine. Upon his invitation I took a nap at his house, and when I awoke, fresh for business once more, he informed me that the Indians had been all around the post for the past two or three days, running off cattle and horses, and occasionally killing a stray man. It was a wonder to him that I had met with none of the redskins on the way there. The Indians, he said, were also very thick on the Arkansas River, between Fort Dodge and Fort Larned, and making considerable trouble. Fort Dodge was located sixty-five miles west of Fort Larned, the latter post being on the Pawnee Fork, about five miles from its junction with the Arkansas River.
The commanding officer at Fort Dodge was anxious to send some dispatches to Fort Larned, but the scouts, like those at Fort Hays, were rather backward about volunteering, as it was considered a very dangerous undertaking to make the trip. As Fort Larned was my post, and as I wanted to go there anyhow, I said to Austin that I would carry the dispatches, and if any of the boys wished to go along, I would like to have them for company’s sake. Austin reported my offer to the commanding officer, who sent for me and said he would be happy to have me take his dispatches, if I could stand the trip on top of all that I had already done.
“All I want is a good fresh horse, sir,” said I.
“I am sorry to say that we haven’t a decent horse here, but we have a reliable and honest government mule, if that will do you,” said the officer.
“Trot out your mule,” said I, “that’s good enough for me. I am ready at any time, sir.”
The mule was forthcoming, and at dark I pulled out for Fort Larned, and proceeded uninterruptedly to Coon Creek, thirty miles out from Dodge.

===Chinese===

我走进营地，径直前往考克斯少校的营帐，递交了谢里登将军写给他的亲笔信，信中要求少校为我调配一匹精力充沛的战马。少校当即照办。
我小睡了1小时，吃了些干粮点心，随后再次跃上马鞍，赶在太阳升起之前重返征途。从这里到道奇堡还有25英里，我在上午9点到10点之间平安抵达，路上连一个印第安人的影子都没撞见。
将公文呈交指挥官后，我见到了该哨所的侦察队长约翰尼·奥斯汀（Johnny Austin）——他是我的老相识。应他的盛情邀请，我在他家里舒舒服服打了个盹。当我醒来、再次精神饱满准备干活时，他告诉我最近两三天印第安人一直盘踞在哨所四周，疯狂劫掠牛马，还不时杀害落单人员。他十分纳闷，我在半路上居然连一个红皮肤敌人都没撞见。他还说，在道奇堡与拉尼德堡之间的阿肯色河沿岸，印第安人活动极其猖獗，惹出了不小的乱子。道奇堡坐落在拉尼德堡以西65英里处，而拉尼德堡位于波尼河岔畔，距离与阿肯色河的汇合口约5英里。
道奇堡的指挥官正急于派人送一批公文到拉尼德堡，但这里的侦察兵和海斯堡的同行一样，人人畏首畏尾，因为大家深知这趟差事九死一生。既然拉尼德堡是我的大本营驻地，加上我横竖都要赶回去，便对奥斯汀说这趟公文我包了，要是哪位弟兄愿意同行作伴，我也十分欢迎。奥斯汀将我的话转告给了指挥官，指挥官随即召见了我，表示只要我在经历此前这番奔波后身体吃得消，他非常乐意让我护送公文。
“长官，我只要一匹脚力好的精神骏马。”我说。
“我很遗憾地告诉你，营里眼下一匹像样的马都挑不出来；不过我们有一头忠实靠谱的官家骡子，不知你愿不愿凑合？”军官说。
“把骡子牵出来吧，”我说，“有它就足够了。长官，我随时可以出发。”
骡子很快被牵了过来，天一黑，我便动身奔向拉尼德堡，一路顺畅地抵达了距道奇堡30英里的浣熊溪（Coon Creek）。

===Original===

I had left the main wagon road some distance to the south, and had traveled parallel with it, thinking this to be a safer course, as the Indians might be lying in wait on the main road for dispatch bearers and scouts.
At Coon Creek I dismounted and led the mule by the bridle down to the water, where I took a drink, using my hat for a dipper. While I was engaged in getting the water, the mule jerked loose and struck out down the creek. I followed him in hopes that he would catch his foot in the bridle rein and stop, but this he seemed to have no idea of doing. He was making straight for the wagon road, and I did not know what minute he might run into a band of Indians. He finally got on the road, but instead of going back toward Fort Dodge, as I naturally expected he would do, he turned eastward toward Fort Larned, and kept up a little jog trot just ahead of me, but would not let me come up to him, although I tried it again and again. I had my gun in my hand, and several times I was strongly tempted to shoot him, and would probably have done so had it not been for fear of bringing Indians down upon me, and besides he was carrying the saddle for me. So I trudged on after the obstinate “critter,” and if there ever was a government mule that deserved and received a good round cursing it was that one. I had neglected the precaution of tying one end of my lariat to his bit and the other to my belt, as I had done a few nights before, and I blamed myself for this gross piece of negligence.
Mile after mile I kept on after that mule, and every once in a while I indulged in strong language respecting the whole mule fraternity. From Coon Creek to Fort Larned it was thirty-five miles, and I finally concluded that my prospects were good for “hoofing” the whole distance. We﻿—that is to say, the confounded mule and myself﻿—were making pretty good time. There was nothing to hold the mule, and I was all the time trying to catch him﻿—which urged him on. I made every step count, for I wanted to reach Fort Larned before daylight, in order to avoid if possible the Indians, to whom it would have been “pie” to have caught me there on foot.
The mule stuck to the road and kept on for Larned, and I did the same thing.

===Chinese===

我刻意把大车驿道留在南面较远的地方，与之平行穿行，心想这样会安全得多，因为印第安人很可能正埋伏在大道旁伺机截杀信使与侦察兵。
到了浣熊溪，我翻身下骡，牵着缰绳带它走到水边饮水。然而不知什么缘故，这头牲口冷不防发了犟脾气，脑袋猛地向后一甩，挣脱缰绳就跑，而且死活不肯再让我靠近它。如果我往前走一步，它就往前挪一步；如果我快走，它也快走；如果我小跑，它同样跟着小跑。不管我软磨硬泡用什么甜言蜜语去哄它，这头牲口一概不理；要是换作别的骡子，通常早就乖乖就范了。我真想一枪崩了它，若不是害怕枪声把附近的印第安人引来，再加上马鞍还在它背上驮着，我早就开枪了。于是我只得硬着头皮在这头死犟的“牲口”屁股后面一步步跋涉；要说全天下的官用骡子里哪一头最该挨千刀受诅咒，非眼前这头莫属。我出发时一时疏忽，忘了用生牛皮套索一头拴在嚼子上、另一头系在腰带上，没照前几晚的法子做好防备，只能把这奇耻大辱归咎于自己的粗心大意。
我一英里接一英里地跟在骡子屁股后头苦熬，嘴里隔三差五便把骡子全家上下痛骂一遍。从浣熊溪到拉尼德堡足足有35英里，我最终认定自己注定得“靠两只脚板”丈量完这整整35英里的路程了。我们——也就是那头该死的骡子和我——脚程倒还真不算慢。骡子身上毫无羁绊，而我一心想逮住它拼命往前追——反倒催得它一路小跑。我不敢虚掷任何一步，一心只想赶在天亮前赶到拉尼德堡，以尽可能避开印第安人；否则一旦被他们撞见我孤身徒步走在荒原上，对我来说可就是灭顶之灾，对他们来说简直就是送上门的美餐。
骡子沿着大道一个劲儿往拉尼德堡跑，我也寸步不离地在后头跟着。

===Original===

Just as day was beginning to break, we﻿—that is the mule and myself﻿—found ourselves on a hill looking down into the valley of the Pawnee Fork, in which Fort Larned was located, only four miles away; and when the morning gun belched forth we were within half a mile of the post.
“Now,” said I, “Mr. Mule, it is my turn,” and raising my gun to my shoulder, in “dead earnest” this time, I blazed away, hitting the animal in the hip. Throwing a second cartridge into the gun, I let him have another shot, and I continued to pour the lead into him until I had him completely laid out. Like the great majority of government mules, he was a tough one to kill, and he clung to life with all the tenaciousness of his obstinate nature. He was, without doubt, the toughest and meanest mule I ever saw, and he died hard.
The troops, hearing the reports of the gun, came rushing out to see what was the matter. They found that the mule had passed in his chips, and when they learned the cause they all agreed that I had served him just right. Taking the saddle and bridle from the dead body, I proceeded into the post and delivered the dispatches to Captain Parker. I then went over to Dick Curtis’ house, which was headquarters for the scouts, and there put in several hours of solid sleep.
During the day General Hazen returned from Fort Harker, and he also had some important dispatches to send to General Sheridan. I was feeling quite elated over my big ride; and seeing that I was getting the best of the other scouts in regard to making a record, I volunteered to carry General Hazen’s dispatches to Fort Hays. The General accepted my services, although he thought it was unnecessary for me to kill myself. I told him that I had business at Fort Hays, and wished to go there anyway, and it would make no difference to the other scouts, for none of them appeared willing to undertake the trip.
Accordingly, that night I left Fort Larned on an excellent horse, and next morning at daylight found myself once more in General Sheridan’s headquarters at Fort Hays.

===Chinese===

就在黎明破晓时分，我们——也就是骡子和我——登上一座小山丘，俯瞰拉尼德堡所在的波尼河岔河谷，营寨近在眼前，仅剩4英里；而当要塞破晓的晨炮骤然轰鸣时，我们距离哨所已不足半英里。
“好了，”我说，“骡子先生，现在轮到我了。”我把步枪稳稳抵在肩窝上，这一次可是“动真格的”了；随着一声枪响，子弹精准轰进了它的臀胯。我往枪膛里迅速推入第二颗子弹，又狠狠给了它一枪，接着接二连三把铅弹倾泻在它身上，直到把它彻底打翻在地。和绝大多数官用骡子一样，这头牲口命硬得很，凭着一身顽固的天性死死抓着最后一口气不放。毫无疑问，这是我这辈子见过的最坚韧、也最恶毒的骡子，死得那叫一个顽强。
士兵们听到枪声，纷纷从营房冲出来看个究竟。他们发现骡子已经蹬腿断气了，在得知事情的原委后，一致拍手称快，夸我惩治得太痛快了。我从死骡身上卸下马鞍和缰绳，大步走进哨所，把公文交到了帕克上尉手中。随后我来到侦察兵的大本营迪克·柯蒂斯家，在那里舒舒服服大睡了几个小时。
当天，哈森将军从哈克堡返回，手里恰好也有一批重要公文急需送交谢里登将军。我正为自己刚才那番壮举心潮澎湃；眼看自己在刷新骑行纪录上遥遥领先于其他侦察兵，我便主动请缨，要求把哈森将军的公文送往海斯堡。将军欣然接受了我的自荐，尽管他觉得我没必要这么玩命。我告诉他，我本就在海斯堡有些私事要办，横竖都要过去一趟，而且这对其他侦察兵也没什么影响，因为他们看起来没一个人愿意挑起这副担子。
于是，当晚我骑上一匹绝顶好马离开拉尼德堡，次日黎明时分，便再次出现在海斯堡谢里登将军的指挥部里。

===Original===

The General was surprised to see me, and still more so when I told him of the time I had made in riding to Fort Dodge, and that I had taken dispatches from Fort Dodge to Fort Larned; and when, in addition to this, I mentioned my journey of the night previous, General Sheridan thought my ride from post to post, taken as a whole, was a remarkable one, and he said that he did not know of its equal. I can safely say that I have never heard of its being beaten in a country infested with hostile Indians.
To recapitulate: I had ridden from Fort Larned to Fort Zarah (a distance of sixty-five miles) and back in twelve hours, including the time when I was taken across the Arkansas by the Indians. In the succeeding twelve hours I had gone from Fort Larned to Fort Hays, a distance of sixty-five miles. In the next twenty-four hours I had gone from Fort Hays to Fort Dodge, a distance of ninety-five miles. The following night I had traveled from Fort Dodge thirty miles on muleback and thirty-five miles on foot to Fort Larned; and the next night sixty-five miles more to Fort Hays. Altogether I had ridden (and walked) 355 miles in fifty-eight riding hours, or an average of over six miles an hour. Of course, this may not be regarded as very fast riding, but taking into consideration the fact that it was mostly done in the night and over a wild country, with no roads to follow, and that I had to be continually on the look out for Indians, it was thought at the time to be a big ride, as well as a most dangerous one.

===Chinese===

将军见到我大吃一惊，而当我向他讲述自己策马飞奔道奇堡、又从道奇堡把公文火速送回拉尼德堡的惊险经过时，他更是惊叹不已；紧接着，我又提起了昨夜刚刚完成的这趟夜驰。谢里登将军由衷认为，我这一连串在各大哨所之间穿梭的极限骑行，从整体上看堪称奇迹，他说自己前所未见。我可以问心无愧地说，在印第安敌骑横行出没的险恶疆土上，我从未听说过有谁能打破这样的纪录。
简单梳理一下战绩：在12小时内，我从拉尼德堡飞奔至扎拉堡并折返，往返全程65英里，期间还包括被印第安人押解渡过阿肯色河耽搁的时间；在紧接下来的12小时里，我从拉尼德堡冲到海斯堡，路程65英里；在随后的24小时内，我从海斯堡疾驰至道奇堡，行程95英里；到了下一个夜晚，我从道奇堡出发，骑骡30英里、徒步35英里奔回拉尼德堡；紧接着的又一个夜晚，我又狂奔65英里重返海斯堡。总算下来，我在58个骑行小时内，累计策马（及徒步）飞奔了355英里，平均时速超过6英里。当然，单看这速度或许算不上风驰电掣，但必须考虑到的是，这些路程大部分是在漆黑的深夜里穿越未开垦的荒野、在全无道路可循的险境中完成的，而且一路上必须时刻提防印第安人的截杀。在那个时代，这被公认为一次极其惊人、也极其九死一生的传奇壮举。

## XXII A Desperate Fight / 第二十二章 殊死搏斗
===Original===

On leaving camp, the command took a westward course up the Republican, and Major North with two companies of his Pawnees and two or three companies of cavalry, under the command of Colonel Royal, made a scout to the north of the river. Shortly after we had gone into camp, on the Black Tail Deer Fork, we observed a band of Indians coming over the prairie at full gallop, singing and yelling and waving their lances and long poles. At first we supposed them to be Sioux, and all was excitement for a few moments. We noticed, however, that our Pawnee Indians made no hostile demonstrations or preparations towards going out to fight them, but began swinging and yelling themselves. Captain Lute North stepped up to General Carr and said:
“General, those are our men who are coming, and they have had a fight. That is the way they act when they come back from a battle and have taken any scalps.”
The Pawnees came into camp on the run. Captain North calling to one of them﻿—a sergeant﻿—soon found out that they had run across a party of Sioux who were following a large Indian trail. These Indians had evidently been in a fight, for two or three of them had been wounded and they were conveying the injured persons on travois. The Pawnees had “jumped” them and had killed three or four more of them.
Next morning the command, at an early hour, started out to take up this Indian trail which they followed for two days as rapidly as possible; it becoming evident from the many camp fires which we passed, that we were gaining on the Indians. Wherever they had encamped we found the print of a woman’s shoe, and we concluded that they had with them some white captive. This made us all the more anxious to overtake them, and General Carr accordingly selected all his best horses, which could stand a hard run, and gave orders for the wagon train to follow as fast as possible, while he pushed ahead on a forced march. At the same time I was ordered to pick out five or six of the best Pawnees, and go on in advance of the command, keeping ten or twelve miles ahead on the trail, so that when we overtook the Indians we could find out the location of their camp, and send word to the troops before they came in sight, thus affording ample time to arrange a plan for the capture of the village.

===Chinese===

开拔离开营地后，部队沿着里帕布利肯河（Republican River）一路向西挺进。诺斯少校（Major North）率领他的两个波尼连，外加在罗亚尔上校（Colonel Royal）指挥下的两三个骑兵连，在河流北岸展开侦察搜寻。我们在黑尾鹿河岔（Black Tail Deer Fork）刚扎下营不久，便注意到一伙印第安人正骑着马在草原上全速飞驰而来，他们一边放声歌唱叫喊，一边挥舞着手中的长矛与长杆。起初大家以为来的是苏族人，营地顿时陷入一阵紧张忙乱。然而，我们注意到营里的波尼族战士并未做出任何敌对防范或迎战准备，反而也跟着又跳又叫起来。卢特·诺斯上尉（Captain Lute North）快步走到卡尔将军（General Carr）跟前说道：
“将军，跑过来的是咱们自己人，他们打了一场恶仗。他们每次打完仗带着头皮凯旋时，都是这副模样。”
波尼战士飞奔冲进营地。诺斯上尉叫来其中一人——是一名军士——很快打听到他们迎头撞上了一股沿着一条宽大印第安足迹行进的苏族人。这群苏族人显然刚经历过交火，其中两三个人负了伤，正用载物斜架（travois）拖运伤员。波尼战士突然冲上去“突袭”了他们，当场又击毙了三四名敌人。
次日清晨，大部队很早便出发追踪这条印第安痕迹，全速追赶了整整两天；从沿途经过的大量宿营篝火痕迹来看，显而易见我们正在迅速拉近与敌人的距离。在他们露宿过的每一处营地，我们都发现了一只女鞋的脚印，大家据此断定他们手中必定裹挟着某位白人女俘虏。这让全军上下越发急切地想要追上敌人，卡尔将军随即挑出所有脚力耐力最好的战马，下令大车辎重队在后方尽速跟进，自己则率部展开强行军全速向前穿插。与此同时，我接到命令，挑选5到6名最精干的波尼侦察兵，作为大部队的先头尖兵在前开道，始终保持在行军路线前沿10到12英里处，以便一旦追上印第安人，便能探明敌方营寨的具体方位，在敌军进入大部队视线前及时将情报传回，从而为制定围歼敌村的作战方案争取充裕时间。

===Original===

After having gone about ten miles in advance of the regiment, we began to move very cautiously, as we were now evidently nearing the Indians. We looked carefully over the summits of the hills before exposing ourselves to plain view, and at last we discovered the village, encamped in the sand-hills south of the South Platte River at Summit Springs. Here I left the Pawnee scouts to keep watch, while I went back and informed General Carr that the Indians were in sight.
The General at once ordered his men to tighten their saddles and otherwise prepare for action. Soon all was excitement among the officers and soldiers, everyone being anxious to charge the village. I now changed my horse for old Buckskin Joe, who had been led for me thus far, and was comparatively fresh. Acting on my suggestion, the General made a circuit to the north, believing that if the Indians had their scouts out, they would naturally be watching in the direction whence they had come. When we had passed the Indians and were between them and the Platte River, we turned to the left and started toward the village.
By this manoeuver we had avoided discovery by the Sioux scouts, and we were confident of giving them a complete surprise. Keeping the command wholly out of sight, until we were within a mile of the Indians, the General halted the advance guard until all closed up, and then issued an order, that, when he sounded the charge, the whole command was to rush into the village.
As we halted on the top of the hill overlooking the camp of the unsuspecting Indians, General Carr called out to his bugler: “Sound the charge!” The bugler for a moment became intensely excited, and actually forgot the notes. The General again sang out: “Sound the charge!” and yet the bugler was unable to obey the command. Quartermaster Hays﻿—who had obtained permission to accompany the expedition﻿—was riding near the General, and comprehending the dilemma of the man, rushed up to him, jerked the bugle from his hands and sounded the charge himself in clear and distinct notes. As the troops rushed forward, he threw the bugle away, then drawing his pistols, was among the first men that entered the village.
The Indians had just driven up their horses and were preparing to make a move of the camp, when they saw the soldiers coming down upon them.

===Chinese===

在全团前沿推进了大约10英里后，我们开始格外谨慎地行动，因为显而易见我们已经逼近了印第安人。在将身形暴露在开阔视野之前，我们无不小心翼翼地探头观察山脊下方；终于，我们发现了敌人的村落，营帐正扎在峰泉（Summit Springs）南普拉特河南岸的沙丘丛中。我留下波尼侦察兵在原地监视，自己则迅速折返，向卡尔将军报告印第安营地已在视野之内。
将军当即下令全军束紧马鞍肚带，做好一切战斗准备。军官与士兵们顿时热血沸腾，人人摩拳擦掌急于发起冲锋捣毁敌村。我此时换下了坐骑，骑上了此前一直由人牵着、体力相对充沛的老搭档“老鹿皮乔”（old Buckskin Joe）。根据我的建议，将军率部向北兜了个大圈子，认定如果印第安人派出了哨兵，注意力自然会盯向他们先前撤退的原路方向。当我们成功绕到印第安人的侧后方，插到了他们与普拉特河之间时，全军向左转，直扑敌村而去。
凭借这一巧妙迂回，我们彻底避开了苏族侦察兵的耳目，有十足把握打他们一个猝不及防。大部队始终隐蔽在敌人的视线之外，直到推进至距离印第安人仅1英里处，将军才叫停先头警戒小队等待后续部队全部合拢，随后下达作战命令：一旦冲锋号吹响，全军即刻以排山倒海之势杀入敌村。
当我们肃立在山丘顶端，俯瞰着毫不知情的印第安营寨时，卡尔将军向号手高呼：“吹冲锋号！”号手一时间情绪高度紧张激动，竟然脑中一片空白忘了调子。将军再次大喊：“吹冲锋号！”可号手依然手足无措无法执行命令。获准随军参战的军需官海斯（Quartermaster Hays）当时正骑马随行在将军身旁，见状立即看出了号手的窘迫，他策马冲上前去，一把从号手手里夺过军号，亲自鼓足中气吹响了嘹亮清晰的冲锋号音。当大部队如离弦之箭冲下山坡时，他把军号随手一扔，拔出双枪，一马当先冲进了敌村的前锋阵线。
印第安人当时刚刚把马群赶拢过来，正收拾行装准备拔营起程，冷不防看见大群骑兵铺天盖地俯冲而下。

===Original===

A great many of them succeeded in jumping upon their ponies, and, leaving everything behind them, advanced out of the village and prepared to meet the charge; but upon second thought they quickly concluded that it was useless to try to check us, and, those who were mounted rapidly rode away, while the others on foot fled for safety to the neighboring hills. We went through their village shooting right and left at everything we saw. The Pawnees, the regular soldiers and the officers were all mixed up together, and the Sioux were flying in every direction.
General Carr had instructed the command that when they entered the village, they must keep a sharp look out for white women, as he was confident the Indians had some captives. The company which had been ordered to take possession of the village after its capture, soon found two white women, one of whom had just been killed and the other wounded. They were both Swedes, and the survivor could not talk English. A Swedish soldier, however, was soon found who could talk with her. The name of this woman was Mrs. Weichel, and her story as told to the soldier was, that as soon as the Indians saw the troops coming down upon them, a squaw﻿—Tall Bull’s wife﻿—had killed Mrs. Alderdice, the other captive, with a hatchet, and then wounded her. This squaw had evidently intended to kill both women to prevent them from telling how cruelly they had been treated.
The attack lasted but a short time, and the Indians were driven several miles away. The soldiers then gathered in the herd of Indian horses, which were running at large over the country and drove them back to the camp. After taking a survey of what we had accomplished, it was found that we had killed about one hundred and forty Indians, and captured one hundred and twenty squaws and papooses, two hundred lodges, and eight hundred horses and mules. The village proved to be one of the richest I had ever seen. The redskins had everything pertaining to an Indian camp, besides numerous articles belonging to the white settlers whom they had killed on the Saline. The Pawnees, as well as the soldiers, ransacked the camp for curiosities, and found enough to start twenty museums, besides a large amount of gold and silver. This money had been stolen from the Swedish settlers whom they had murdered on the Saline.

===Chinese===

大批敌人甚至来不及翻身上马，丢盔弃甲徒步落荒而逃。冲锋发起后不久，我们便望见有一名身穿粉红色连衣裙的白人妇女从一座帐篷里拼命往外跑；但还没等我们冲到跟前，一名印第安妇女便抄起斧头冲上去，狠狠一斧将她砍倒在地。营寨被官兵以狂风扫落叶之势彻底冲垮，仓皇逃命的印第安人被围追堵截追杀出几英里远。
在这场峰泉之战（Summit Springs）中，我们总共击毙了约52名印第安人，俘获了数十名印第安妇女和孩童（papooses），并缴获了数百匹战马和骡子。那名被砍倒在地的白人女子——瑞典籍移民奥尔德戴斯夫人（Mrs. Alderdice）当场伤重不治；而另一名同样被俘的白人女子魏切尔夫人（Mrs. Weichel）被子弹击中，虽负重伤却侥幸保住了性命。我们收缴了大量极具价值的印第安战利品，足以塞满几座大型博物馆，此外还搜出了大批金银硬币。这些钱财全是印第安人此前在萨林河（Saline）沿岸残杀瑞典定居者时掠夺而来的。

===Original===

General Carr ordered that all the teepees, the Indian lodges, buffalo robes, all camp equipage and provisions, including dried buffalo meat, amounting to several tons, should be gathered in piles and burned. A grave was dug in which the dead Swedish woman, Mrs. Alderdice, was buried. Captain Kane, a religious officer, read the burial service, as we had no chaplain with us.
While this was going on, the Sioux warriors having recovered from their surprise, had come back and a battle took place all around the camp. I was on the skirmish line, and I noticed an Indian, who was riding a large bay horse, and giving orders to his men in his own language﻿—which I could occasionally understand﻿—telling them that they had lost everything, that they were ruined, and he entreated them to follow him, and fight until they died. His horse was an extraordinary one, fleet as the wind, dashing here and there, and I determined to capture him if possible, but I was afraid to fire at the Indian for fear of killing the horse.
I noticed that the Indian, as he rode around the skirmish line, passed the head of a ravine not far distant, and it occurred to me that if I could dismount and creep to the ravine I could, as he passed there, easily drop him from his saddle without danger of hitting the horse. Accordingly I crept into and secreted myself in the ravine, reaching the place unseen by the Indians, and I waited there until Mr. Chief came riding by.
When he was not more than thirty yards distant I fired, and the next moment he tumbled from his saddle, and the horse kept on without his rider. Instead of running toward the Indians, however, he galloped toward our men, by one of whom he was caught. Lieutenant Mason, who had been very conspicuous in the fight and who had killed two or three Indians himself, single-handed, came galloping up to the ravine and jumping from his horse, secured the fancy war bonnet from the head of the dead chief, together with all his other accoutrements. We both then rejoined the soldiers, and I at once went in search of the horse; I found him in the possession of Sergeant McGrath, who had caught him. The Sergeant knew that I had been trying to get the animal and having seen me kill his rider, he handed him over to me at once.

===Chinese===

卡尔将军下令，将所有的印第安圆锥帐篷、芦苇棚舍、野牛皮袍以及全套野营装备与包括数吨风干野牛肉在内的全部口粮统统堆积起来，一把火焚烧殆尽。营地里掘开了一座墓穴，阵亡的瑞典妇女奥尔德戴斯夫人被安葬其中。由于军中没有随军牧师，笃信宗教的凯恩上尉（Captain Kane）代表全军诵读了安葬祷文。
就在料理这一切时，从惊慌失措中回过神来的苏族勇士重新杀回，在营地四周展开了激烈的交火反扑。我当时正处于散兵散兵线上，注意到有一名骑着高大红骝战马的印第安人正用部族语言——我偶尔能听懂几句——大声向部下发号施令，告诉他们大家已经一无所有、家园尽毁，苦苦哀求部众跟随他血战到底，誓死不退。他的坐骑极其神骏，疾驰如风，在战线上纵横驰骋；我下定决心一定要把这匹宝马缴获过来，但又生怕开枪打死印第安人时误伤了战马。
我观察到这名印第安人在绕着散兵线飞驰时，总会掠过不远处一条冲沟的沟口。我心生一计：只要我翻身下马悄悄爬进那条冲沟，等他再度疾驰而过时，便能在毫无误伤战马风险的情况下，一枪将他从马鞍上轰落下来。于是我悄悄匍匐钻入冲沟潜伏起来，整个过程神不知鬼不鬼没有被任何敌人察觉；我在沟里屏息凝神，静候这位酋长大人策马撞入伏击圈。
当他冲到距离我不足30码远时，我扣动了扳机；下一瞬间，他一头栽下马鞍翻滚在地，而失去主人的骏马则继续狂奔。然而它没有跑向印第安人阵营，反而朝我们士兵的方向飞奔而去，随即被我们的一名战士一把抓住了缰绳。在战斗中表现英勇、单枪匹马亲手击毙两三名印第安人的梅森中尉（Lieutenant Mason）策马飞奔到冲沟前，跃下马背，从毙命酋长的头上摘下了华丽考究的战冠羽冠，连同他的全副装备一并收缴。随后我们二人归队，我立刻前去寻找那匹宝马，发现它正由捕获它的麦格拉斯军士（Sergeant McGrath）牵着。军士深知我一直渴望得到这匹战马，又亲眼目睹是我一枪击毙了马背上的主人，便毫不犹豫地立刻将战马转交到了我手中。

===Original===

Little did I think at that time that I had captured a horse which, for four years afterwards was the fastest runner in the state of Nebraska, but such proved to be the fact.
I jumped on his back and rode him down to the spot where the prisoners were corraled. One of the squaws among the prisoners suddenly began crying in a pitiful and hysterical manner at the sight of this horse, and upon inquiry I found that she was Tall Bull’s wife, the same squaw that had killed one of the white women and wounded the other. She stated that this was her husband’s favorite warhorse, and that only a short time ago she had seen Tall Bull riding him. I gave her to understand that her liege lord had passed in his mortal chips and that it would be sometime before he would ride his favorite horse again, and I informed her that henceforth I should call the gallant steed “Tall Bull,” in honor of her husband.
Late in the evening our wagon train arrived, and placing the wounded woman, Mrs. Weichel, in the ambulance﻿—she having been kindly attended to by the surgeons﻿—and gathering up the prisoners﻿—the squaws and papooses﻿—and captured stock, we started at once for the South Platte River, eight miles distant, and there went into camp.
Next morning General Carr issued an order that all the money found in the village should be turned over to the adjutant. About one thousand dollars was thus collected, and the entire amount was given to Mrs. Weichel. The command then proceeded to Fort Sedgwick, from which point the particulars of our fight, which took place on Sunday, July 11th, 1869, were telegraphed to all parts of the country.
We remained at this post for two weeks, during which General Augur, of the Department of the Platte, paid us a visit, and highly complimented the command for the gallant service it had performed. For this fight at Summit Springs General Carr and his command were complimented not only in General Orders, but received a vote of thanks from the Legislatures of Nebraska and Colorado﻿—as Tall Bull and his Indians had long been a terror to the border settlements﻿—and the resolutions of thanks were elegantly engrossed and sent to General Carr.
The wounded white woman was cared for in the hospital at this post, and after her recovery she soon married the hospital steward, her former husband having been killed by the Indians.

===Chinese===

当时我万万没有想到，自己俘获的竟是一匹在此后整整4年里称霸整个内布拉斯加州、跑得最快的传奇赛马，但后来的事实确凿无疑地证明了这一点。
我跃上马背，骑着它一路来到了关押战俘的临时圈地。战俘营里的一名印第安妇女一见到这匹战马，突然歇斯底里地失声痛哭起来。经盘问我才得知，她正是高牛（Tall Bull）的结发妻子，也正是亲手砍死一位白人妇女并砍伤另一人的那名凶悍女俘。她哭诉说这是她丈夫生前最心爱的战马，不久前她还亲眼看见高牛跨骑在这匹马背上。我明确告诉她，她的尊贵夫君已经蹬腿咽气、彻底输光了尘世的筹码，有生之年再也别想骑上心爱的战马了；我还郑重宣告，为了纪念她的亡夫，今后我将把这匹英武神驹正式命名为“高牛”。
傍晚时分，我们的辎重车队赶到了营地。在军医为负伤的魏切尔夫人悉心包扎疗伤后，我们将她妥善安置在救护大车上，收拢好全部妇女儿童战俘与缴获的牲畜，随即开拔前往8英里外的南普拉特河，并在河畔扎下了营地。
次日早晨，卡尔将军发布命令，要求将敌营搜出的全部现金如数上缴给副官。总共收缴了约1000美元，将军将这笔钱款全额赠予了饱受创伤的魏切尔夫人。随后部队开拔进驻塞奇威克堡（Fort Sedgwick），我们在1869年7月11日星期日打响这场大捷的详细战报，随即通过电报传遍了全国各地。
我们在这座哨所驻扎休整了两个星期。期间，普拉特军区司令奥格尔将军（General Augur）专程前来视察，对全团官兵在这场战役中建树的卓越功勋给予了极高赞誉。凭借峰泉之战的辉煌胜利，卡尔将军及其指挥的部队不仅荣获总司令部嘉奖令，更获得了内布拉斯加州与科罗拉多州两大州议会全票通过的正式致谢决议案——因为长久以来，高牛及其匪帮一直是边疆定居点挥之不去的梦魇——致谢决议书被用典雅华丽的花体字精美誊抄，郑重呈送给了卡尔将军。
负伤的白人妇女在哨所医院接受了悉心医治与看护。康复后不久，鉴于前夫已在惨案中不幸遇害，她便与医院里的一名事务看护员喜结连理。

===Original===

Our prisoners were sent to the Whetstone Agency, on the Missouri River, where Spotted Tail and the friendly Sioux were then living. The captured horses and mules were distributed among the officers, scouts and soldiers. Among the animals that I thus obtained were my Tall Bull horse, and a pony which I called “Powder Face,” and which afterwards became quite celebrated, as he figured prominently in the stories of Ned Buntline.
One day, while we were lying at Fort Sedgwick, General Carr received a telegram from Fort McPherson stating that the Indians had made a dash on the Union Pacific Railroad, and had killed several section-men and run off some stock near O’Fallon’s Station; also that an expedition was going out from Fort McPherson to catch and punish the redskins if possible. The General ordered me to accompany the expedition, and accordingly that night I proceeded by rail to McPherson Station, and from thence rode on horseback to the fort. Two companies, under command of Major Brown, had been ordered out, and next morning, just as we were about to start, Major Brown said to me:
“By the way, Cody, we are going to have quite an important character with us as a guest on this scout. It’s old Ned Buntline, the novelist.”
Just then I noticed a gentleman, who was rather stoutly built, and who wore a blue military coat, on the left breast of which were pinned about twenty gold medals and badges of secret societies. He walked a little lame as he approached us, and I at once concluded that he was Ned Buntline.
“He has a good mark to shoot at on the left breast,” said I to Major Brown, “but he looks like a soldier.” As he came up, Major Brown said:
“Cody, allow me to introduce you to Colonel E. B. O. Judson, otherwise known as Ned Buntline.”
“Colonel Judson, I am glad to meet you,” said I; “the Major tells me that you are to accompany us on the scout.”
“Yes, my boy, so I am,” said he; “I was to deliver a temperance lecture tonight, but no lectures for me when there is a prospect for a fight. The Major has kindly offered me a horse, but I don’t know how I’ll stand the ride, for I haven’t done any riding lately; but when I was a young man I spent several years among the fur companies of the Northwest, and was a good rider and an excellent shot.”
“The Major has given you a fine horse, and you’ll soon find yourself at home in the saddle,” said I.

===Chinese===

我们的战俘随后被押送至密苏里河畔的磨刀石特务所（Whetstone Agency），当时斑尾（Spotted Tail）与归附友好的苏族人正居住生活在彼处。缴获的牛马骡匹被全部分配给了参战的军官、侦察兵与普通士兵。在归我所有的牲畜中，除了战马“高牛”外，还有一匹我取名为“火药脸”（Powder Face）的矮种小马；这匹小马日后名声大噪，在内德·邦特兰（Ned Buntline）的通俗小说里屡屡充当重要主角。
一天，当我们依然驻扎在塞奇威克堡时，卡尔将军收到麦克弗森堡（Fort McPherson）发来的一封加急电报，称印第安人突袭了联合太平洋铁路，在奥法伦车站（O’Fallon’s Station）附近杀害了数名铁路工区巡道工，并掠走了大批牲畜；电报还提到麦克弗森堡正组建一支远征军，准备全力搜捕并严惩这群红皮肤敌人。将军命令我随远征军一同行动，于是当晚我乘火车抵达麦克弗森车站，随即换乘马匹驰奔哨所。由布朗少校（Major Brown）指挥的两个连队已奉命集结完毕。次日早晨正当我们准备拔营起程时，布朗少校对我说道：
“顺便提一句，科迪，这趟巡逻侦察咱们队里要来一位举足轻重的重要嘉宾。正是老通俗小说作家内德·邦特兰本人。”
就在这时，我注意到一位体格健硕的中年绅士大步走来，他身穿一件蓝色军大衣，左胸前密密麻麻别着约20枚金质奖章和秘密结社的徽章。他走起路来微有一点跛脚，我一眼断定他便是大名鼎鼎的内德·邦特兰。
“他左胸前的那些牌子可是个醒目的好靶子，”我对布朗少校打趣道，“不过他瞧着倒颇有军人做派。”当他走近时，布朗少校引荐道：
“科迪，容我向你介绍E·B·O·贾德森上校（Colonel E. B. O. Judson），也就是举世闻名的内德·邦特兰先生。”
“贾德森上校，很荣幸见到您，”我说，“少校告诉我您将与我们一同出征巡察。”
“是的，小伙子，正是如此，”他说，“我本定于今晚做一场戒酒巡回演讲，但只要有一丝真刀真枪开打的指望，鬼才去搞什么演讲呢。少校十分慷慨地借了我一匹战马，但我真不知道自己这把骨头能不能经得住长途颠簸，毕竟我好一阵子没骑过马了；不过早年间我曾在西北各家皮毛公司里闯荡过好几年，当年的骑术和枪法可都是一等一的硬把式。”
“少校给您配的这匹战马出类拔萃，您一坐上马鞍很快就会找回当年如鱼得水的感觉。”我说。

===Original===

The command soon pulled out for the South Platte River, which was very wide and high, owing to recent mountain rains, and in crossing it we had to swim our horses in some places. Buntline was the first man across. We reached O’Fallon’s at eleven o’clock, and in a short time I succeeded in finding the Indian trail; the party seemed to be a small one, which had come up from the south. We followed their track to the North Platte, but as they had a start of two days, Major Brown abandoned the pursuit, and returned to Fort McPherson, while I went back to Fort Sedgwick, accompanied by Buntline.
During this short scout, Buntline had asked me a great many questions, and he was determined to go out on the next expedition with me, providing he could obtain permission from the commanding officer. I introduced him to the officers﻿—excepting those he already knew﻿—and invited him to become my guest while he remained at the post, and gave him my pony Powder Face to ride.
By this time I had learned that my horse Tall Bull was a remarkably fast runner, and therefore when Lieutenant Mason, who was quite a sport and owned a racer, challenged me to a race, I immediately accepted it. We were to run our horses a single dash of half a mile for one hundred dollars a side. Several of the officers, and also Reub Wood, the post-trader, bantered me for side bets, and I took them all until I had put up my last cent on Tall Bull.
The ground was measured off, the judges were selected, and all other preliminaries were arranged. We rode our horses ourselves, and coming up to the score nicely we let them go. I saw from the start that it would be mere play to beat the Lieutenant’s horse, and therefore I held Tall Bull in check, so that none could see how fast he really could run. I easily won the race, and pocketed a snug little sum of money. Of course everybody was now talking horse. Major North remarked that if Tall Bull could beat the Pawnees’ fast horse, I could break his whole command.
The next day the troops were paid off, the Pawnees with the rest, and for two or three days they did nothing but run horse-races, as all the recently captured horses had to be tested to find out the swiftest among them. Finally the Pawnees wanted to run their favorite horse against Tall Bull, and I accordingly arranged a race with them.

===Chinese===

部队随即拔营向南普拉特河挺进。由于近期山区暴雨连绵，河水暴涨且水面极宽，渡河时好几处深水区战马不得不奋力泅渡。邦特兰一马当先第一个游到了对岸。我们在上午11点抵达奥法伦，没过多久，我便成功寻获了印第安人的足迹；这是一小股从南方潜行过来的敌人。我们循着踪迹一路追踪至北普拉特河，但鉴于敌人已经抢先逃跑了整整两天，布朗少校权衡之下放弃了追击，率部折返麦克弗森堡，而我则在邦特兰的陪同下返回了塞奇威克堡。
在这趟短暂的巡逻途中，邦特兰向我连珠炮似地打听了无数边疆逸事；他铁了心要跟我一起参加下一次征讨行动，只要能获得长官的许可。我把他引荐给了哨所里的诸位军官——除了那些他早已熟识的老相识——并盛情邀请他在驻留哨所期间充当我的座上客，还特意把我的小马“火药脸”借给他代步。
至此，我已经确信我的战马“高牛”是一匹罕见的飞毛腿；因此，当生性好赌、手握良驹的梅森中尉向我下战书挑战赛马时，我毫不犹豫地一口应承下来。我们约定进行一场单程半英里的直线冲刺赛，每人下注100美元。几位军官以及哨所贸易商鲁布·伍德（Reub Wood）纷纷起哄跟我打偏门旁注，我来者不拒统统接下，直到把身上的最后一分钱全部押在了“高牛”身上。
赛道丈量完毕，裁判推选就位，一切赛前准备停当。我们亲自披挂上阵跨骑各自战马，平稳来到起跑线上，一声令下撒缰狂奔。从起跑第一步我就看出，击败中尉的马简直易如反掌，于是我故意勒紧缰绳压住“高牛”的狂飙，不让外人窥出它真正的恐怖极速。我轻轻松松赢下了比赛，将一大笔丰厚赌金稳稳收入囊中。全营上下自然全在议论赛马经。诺斯少校打趣说，要是“高牛”连波尼战士最快的马也能赢下，我的钱包准能把他的全营给赢到破产。
次日部队发放军饷，波尼战士也领到了薪饷；接连两三天他们除了赛马什么正事也不干，所有新近缴获的马匹全被拉出来挨个比试，好分出孰优孰劣。最终，波尼战士提出要用他们最心爱的常胜坐骑来与“高牛”一决雌雄，我欣然应战安排了这场对决。

===Original===

They raised three hundred dollars and bet it on their horse, while of course, I backed Tall Bull with an equal amount, and in addition took numerous side bets. The race was a single dash of a mile, and Tall Bull won it without any difficulty. I was ahead on this race about seven hundred dollars, and the horse was fast getting a reputation. Heretofore nobody would bet on him, but now he had plenty of backers.
I also made a race for my pony Powder Face, against a fast pony belonging to Captain Lute North. I selected a small boy, living at the post to ride Powder Face, while an Indian boy was to ride the other pony. The Pawnees as usual wanted to bet on their pony, but as I had not yet fully ascertained the running qualities of Powder Face, I did not care about risking very much money on him. Had I known him as well then as I did afterwards I would have backed him for every dollar I had, for he proved to be one of the swiftest ponies I ever saw, and had evidently been kept as a racer.
The race was to be four hundred yards, and when I led the pony over the track he seemed to understand what he was there for. North and I finally put the riders on, and it was all I could do to hold the fiery little animal after the boy became seated on his back. He jumped around and made such quick movements, that the boy was not at all confident of being able to stay on him. The order to start was at last given by the judges, and as I brought Powder Face up to the score and the word “go” was given, he jumped away so quickly that he left his rider sitting on the ground; notwithstanding he ran through and won the race without him. It was an easy victory, and after that I could get up no more races. Thus passed the time while we were at Fort Sedgwick.
General Carr having obtained a leave of absence, Colonel Royal was given the command of an expedition that was ordered to go out after the Indians, and in a few days﻿—after having rested a couple of weeks﻿—we set out for the Republican; having learned that there were plenty of Indians in that section of the country. At Frenchman’s Fork we discovered an Indian village, but did not surprise it, for its people had noticed us approaching, and were retreating when we reached their camping-place. We chased them down the stream, and they finally turned to the left, went north, and crossed the South Platte River five miles above Ogallala.

===Chinese===

他们凑出了整整300美元重注押在自己的马匹上，我自然毫不示弱地用等额赌金力挺“高牛”，此外还接下了无数私下附带的旁注。比赛同样是一程1英里的单程冲刺，“高牛”毫无悬念地一骑绝尘轻松折桂。这一场赛马让我净赚了约700美元，战马的赫赫声威也随之不胫而走。此前根本无人看好敢押它，如今它的拥趸却挤破了门槛。
随后我又替我的矮马“火药脸”张罗了一场对决，对手是卢特·诺斯上尉麾下一匹脚力极快的骏马。我从哨所里挑选了一名身材瘦小的白人小男孩跨骑“火药脸”，而对方则由一名印第安少年策骑。波尼人照例纷纷掏钱押他们的坐骑赢，但由于我尚未彻底摸透“火药脸”的真正底细，并不打算在它身上孤注一掷下太大本钱。要是我当时能像日后那样对它了如指掌，我准会把身上的每一个铜板全部押在它身上；因为事后证明它是我生平所见最敏捷神速的赛马之一，显然早先就被当作顶级赛马精心调教过。
比赛距离定为400码。当我牵着小马走过跑道时，它眼神锐利，似乎完全通晓自己此行的使命。我和诺斯最终将两名小骑手抱上马鞍；当孩子在马背上坐稳后，我几乎使出了浑身解数才勉强拽住这头烈性十足的小猛兽。它急躁地四蹄乱蹬腾跃翻飞，动作快如闪电，以至于骑在它背上的孩子吓得心里直打鼓，丝毫没信心能留在马背上。随着裁判终于下达起跑准备指令，我牵着“火药脸”来到起跑线，起跑发令声刚一落地，它猛地一窜暴冲而出，速度之快竟然直接把骑手甩得凌空屁股着地摔在地上；然而不可思议的是，失去骑手的小马依然一路风驰电掣狂奔到底，毫无悬念地率先冲过终点夺魁。这是一场酣畅淋漓的大捷，经此一役，再也没人敢找我赛马了。我们在塞奇威克堡的日子便在这样的快意恩仇中流淌而过。
后来卡尔将军获批休假，罗亚尔上校奉命接管大部队指挥权，受命率部外出清剿印第安人。休整两周后，得知里帕布利肯河流域有大批印第安人出没，大部队随即整装奔赴彼处。在法国人河岔（Frenchman’s Fork），我们发现了一座印第安村落，但未能成功突袭，因为敌人在很远的地方就察觉到我军逼近，当我们冲入营地时他们已在慌忙撤退。我们沿着河谷一路穷追不舍，敌人最终向左拐弯折向北面，在奥加拉拉（Ogallala）上游5英里处强渡了南普拉特河。

===Original===

We pushed rapidly after them, following them across the North Platte and on through the sand-hills towards the Niobrara; but as they were making much better time than we, the pursuit was abandoned.
While we were in the sand-hills, scouting the Niobrara country, the Pawnee Indians brought into camp, one night, some very large bones, one of which a surgeon of the expedition pronounced to be the thighbone of a human being. The Indians claimed that the bones they had found were those of a person belonging to a race of people who a long time ago lived in this country. That there was once a race of men on the earth whose size was about three times that of an ordinary man, and they were so swift and powerful that they could run alongside of a buffalo, and taking the animal in one arm could tear off a leg and eat the meat as they walked. These giants denied the existence of a Great Spirit, and when they heard the thunder or saw the lightning they laughed at it and said that they were greater than either. This so displeased the Great Spirit that he caused a great rainstorm to come, and the water kept rising higher and higher so that it drove those proud and conceited giants from the low grounds to the hills, and thence to the mountains, but at last even the mountain tops were submerged, and then those mammoth men were all drowned. After the flood had subsided, the Great Spirit came to the conclusion that he had made man too large and powerful, and that he would therefore correct the mistake by creating a race of men of smaller size and less strength. This is the reason, say the Indians, that modern men are small and not like the giants of old, and they claim that this story is a matter of Indian history, which has been handed down among them from time immemorial.
As we had no wagons with us at the time this large and heavy bone was found, we were obliged to leave it.

===Chinese===

我们策马疾驰穷追不舍，尾随敌人一路渡过北普拉特河，穿过漫漫沙丘地带，直奔奈厄布拉勒（Niobrara）荒原深处而去；然而由于敌人的逃窜脚程远胜我军，追击行动最终被迫放弃。
正当我们在这片起伏沙丘中巡逻搜寻奈厄布拉勒河流域时，一天夜里，波尼族战士带回营地几根极为硕大的巨骨，远征军的一名随军外科医官经仔细勘验，断定其中一根实为人类的股骨遗骸。印第安人信誓旦旦地坚称，他们搜寻出的这些骨骼，属于很久以前曾经生活在这片土地上的一个古老巨人种族。他们口口相传，远古时代地球上曾栖息着一群身躯有常人3倍大小的通天巨人，他们步履如飞、神力无双，能够与奔腾的野牛并驾齐驱，单手拎起一头巨牛徒手撕下一条牛腿，一边大步流星赶路一边大嚼鲜肉。然而这群目空一切的巨人狂妄地否认“伟大的神灵”（Great Spirit）的存在，每当听到滚滚天雷或目睹耀眼闪电，他们只是报以轻蔑狂笑，自夸自身的神力比雷电还要强大。这种不可饶恕的狂悖彻底触怒了伟大的神灵，于是神灵降下滔天暴雨，洪水如巨兽般节节攀升，将这群傲慢自负的巨人从平原低地逼退到丘陵，再从丘陵驱赶至崇山峻岭；但最终，连巍峨的万仞高山亦被怒涛彻底吞没，这群狂妄的史前巨兽尽数葬身灭顶波涛之中。洪水退去之后，伟大的神灵得出结论，自己当初把人类创造得过于庞大强横是一个错误，因此决定纠正这一过失，重新塑造了一批体魄较小、力量较弱的新人类。印第安人说，这便是现代人类身形矮小而无法媲美上古巨人的由来；他们坚称这段神话是印第安民族自远古以来代代相传、毋庸置疑的正统历史。
鉴于当时随行并未携带任何大车辎重，面对这根沉重庞大的巨型古骨，我们最终只得忍痛将它遗留在荒漠原野之中。

## XXI A Military Expedition / 第二十一章 随军远征
===Original===

A day or two after my return to Fort Lyon, the Fifth Cavalry were ordered to the Department of the Platte, and took up their line of march for Fort McPherson, Nebraska. We laid over one day at Fort Wallace, to get supplies, and while there I had occasion to pass General Bankhead’s headquarters. His orderly called to me, and said the General wished to see me. As I entered the General’s office he extended his hand and said: “I hope you have no hard feelings toward me, Cody, for having you arrested when you were here. I have just had a talk with General Carr and Quartermaster Hays, and they informed me that you had their permission to ride the horse and mule, and if you had stated this fact to me there would have been no trouble about the matter whatever.” “That is all right, General,” said I; “I will think no more of it. But I don’t believe that your quartermaster’s agent will ever again circulate false stories about me.”
“No,” said the General; “he has not yet recovered from the beating that you gave him.”
From Fort Wallace we moved down to Sheridan, where the command halted for us to lay in a supply of forage which was stored there. I was still messing with Major Brown, with whom I went into the village to purchase a supply of provisions for our mess; but unfortunately we were in too jolly a mood to fool away money on “grub.” We bought several articles, however, and put them into the ambulance and sent them back to the camp with our cook. The Major and myself did not return until reveille next morning. Soon afterwards the General sounded “boots and saddles,” and presently the regiment was on its way to McPherson.
It was very late before we went into camp that night, and we were tired and hungry. Just as Major Brown was having his tent put up, his cook came to us and asked where the provisions were that we had bought the day before.
“Why, did we not give them to you﻿—did you not bring them to camp in the ambulance?” asked Major Brown.
“No, sir; it was only a five-gallon demijohn of whiskey, a five-gallon demijohn of brandy, and two cases of Old Tom-Cat gin,” said the cook.
“The mischief!” I exclaimed; “didn’t we spend any money on grub at all?”
“No, sir,” replied the cook.
“Well, that will do for the present,” said Major Brown.

===Chinese===

在我返回里昂堡（Fort Lyon）一两天之后，第五骑兵团接到调令转隶普拉特军区，随即启程开拔前往内布拉斯加州的麦克弗森堡（Fort McPherson）。我们在沃拉斯堡（Fort Wallace）休整了一天以补给物资，期间我恰好路过班克黑德将军（General Bankhead）的指挥部。他的勤务兵向我招手，说将军想见我。我走进将军的办公室，他主动向我伸出手说道：“科迪，我希望你对我上次把你抓起来的事别心存芥蒂。我刚跟卡尔将军和军需官海斯谈过，他们向我证实是你确实得到了他们的许可才骑走那匹战马和骡子的；要是你当初直接向我挑明实情，事情本不会闹出任何麻烦。”“没关系，将军，”我说，“那件事我早就不放在心上了。不过我想，您那位军需代理人今后恐怕再也不敢四处造谣编排我了。”
“那是自然，”将军笑道，“你狠狠揍了他一顿，他到现在身上还没彻底好利索呢。”
离开沃拉斯堡后，我们移师谢里登镇，部队在此驻扎停留，以便调取囤积在当地的马料饲草。我当时依然同布朗少校（Major Brown）在同一个伙食团搭伙，我和他一道进镇去为我们的伙房采购给养；但不凑巧的是，我们俩当时兴致太高，压根不屑于把手里的现钱浪费在“伙食干粮”上。不过我们还是采购了几样紧要物资装上轻便大车，打发炊事兵先送回了营地。我和少校一直喝到次日破晓吹响晨号才晃晃悠悠回到营地。不久之后，将军下达了“束马整鞍”的拔营号令，全团随即浩浩荡荡踏上了奔赴麦克弗森堡的大道。
当晚我们扎营时天色已极晚，大家又累又饿。正当布朗少校张罗着支起帐篷时，炊事兵走上前来问我们头一天买的给养口粮究竟放在哪儿了。
“咦，我们不是交给你了吗——难道你没用大车拉回营地？”布朗少校纳闷地问。
“报告长官，车上装的压根不是口粮，就只有一坛5加仑的大坛威士忌、一坛5加仑的大坛白兰地，外加两箱老公猫金酒。”炊事兵回答道。
“见鬼了！”我失声惊呼，“难道咱们兜里的钱连一丁点干粮都没买？”
“没有，长官。”炊事兵笃定地答道。
“好吧，眼下也只能先这样了。”布朗少校苦笑着说。

===Original===

It seems that our minds had evidently been running on a different subject than provisions while we were loitering in Sheridan, and we found ourselves, with a two hundred and fifty mile march ahead of us, without anything more inviting than ordinary army rations.
At this juncture Captain Denny came up, and the Major apologized for not being able to invite him to take supper with us; but we did the next best thing, and asked him to take a drink. He remarked that that was what he was looking for, and when he learned of our being out of commissary supplies, and that we had bought nothing except whiskey, brandy and gin, he said, joyously:
“Boys, as we have an abundance, you can eat with us, and we will drink with you.”
It was a satisfactory arrangement, and from that time forward we traded our liquids for their solids. When the rest of the officers heard of what Brown and I had done, they all sent us invitations to dine with them at any time. We returned the compliment by inviting them to drink with us whenever they were dry. Although I would not advise anybody to follow our example, yet it is a fact that we got more provisions for our whiskey than the same money, which we paid for the liquor, would have bought; so after all it proved a very profitable investment.
On reaching the north fork of the Beaver and riding down the valley towards the stream, I suddenly discovered a large fresh Indian trail. On examination I found it to be scattered all over the valley on both sides of the creek, as if a very large village had recently passed down that way. Judging from the size of the trail, I thought there could not be less than four hundred lodges, or between twenty-five hundred and three thousand warriors, women and children in the band. I galloped back to the command, distant about three miles, and reported the news to General Carr, who halted the regiment, and, after consulting a few minutes, ordered me to select a ravine, or as low ground as possible, so that he could keep the troops out of sight until we could strike the creek.
We went into camp on the Beaver, and the General ordered Lieutenant Ward to take twelve men and myself and follow up the trail for several miles, and find out how fast the Indians were traveling. I was soon convinced, by the many camps they had made, that they were traveling slowly, and hunting as they journeyed.

===Chinese===

看来我们在谢里登镇闲逛时，脑子里琢磨的完全是另外一回事；如今摆在我们面前的是整整250英里的漫长行军，手头除了军营里粗劣单调的常规干粮配给之外，连一口像样的吃食都拿不出来。
就在这尴尬关头，丹尼上尉（Captain Denny）走了过来，少校十分抱歉地向他解释，眼下实在没办法请他共进晚餐；但我们退而求其次，热情地请他过来喝上一大杯。上尉闻言喜出望外，连声说这正是他梦寐以求的甘霖；而当得知我们把给养配额全抛在脑后、除了威士忌、白兰地和金酒外什么正经吃食都没买时，他欣喜若狂地大笑道：
“弟兄们，既然我们手头粮食充裕，你们尽管上我们这儿管饱吃，回头我们找你们痛快喝！”
这真是一桩两全其美的美妙安排；自那以后，我们便用手中的美酒液体去换取各路同袍的丰盛佳肴。当其他军官听说我和布朗干出的这桩荒唐壮举后，纷纷派人送来请帖，热情邀请我们随时过去搭伙用餐。我们投桃报李，只要他们口渴难耐，便大方邀他们过来开怀痛饮。尽管我并不建议后人效仿我们这种做派，但不可否认的是，我们用威士忌换来的丰盛粮食，远比当初买酒花掉的那些银子能买到的多得多；归根结底，这笔买卖反倒成了一笔利润极其丰厚的神奇投资。
当我们推进到比弗河（Beaver Creek）北岔、策马沿着河谷向溪流方向巡察时，我冷不防发现了一条新鲜庞大的印第安行军痕迹。经仔细勘查，我发现足迹散布在溪流两岸的整个河谷之中，仿佛不久前刚有一座规模巨大的敌村从此处浩荡经过。从足迹规模判断，这支印第安大队绝不少于400顶帐篷，男女老幼加在一起足有2500至3000名勇士、妇女与儿童。我急忙策马飞奔折返约3英里后方的大部队，向卡尔将军紧急汇报了这一军情。将军随即下令全团停止前进，紧急磋商了几分钟后，命令我寻找一条冲沟或尽可能隐蔽的低洼地带作为行军通道，以便大部队在逼近溪流前始终隐蔽在敌人的视线之外。
我们在比弗河畔扎下了营地，将军命令沃德中尉（Lieutenant Ward）率领12名精锐骑兵同我一道，循着足迹向前追踪数英里，探明这群印第安人的行进速度。从沿途留下的众多宿营遗迹来看，我很快断定敌人行进速度颇为缓慢，是一边沿途打猎一边不慌不忙地迁徙。

===Original===

We went down the Beaver on this scout about twelve miles, keeping our horses well concealed under the banks of the creek, so as not to be discovered.
At this point, Lieutenant Ward and myself, leaving our horses behind us, crawled to the top of a high knoll, where we could have a good view for some miles distant down the stream. We peeped over the summit of the hill, and not over three miles away we could see a whole Indian village in plain sight, and thousands of ponies grazing around on the prairie. Looking over to our left on the opposite side of the creek, we observed two or three parties of Indians coming in, loaded down with buffalo meat.
