“This is no place for us, Lieutenant,” said I; “I think we have important business at the camp to attend to as soon as possible.”
“I agree with you,” said he, “and the quicker we get there the better it will be for us.”
We quickly descended the hill and joined the men below. Lieutenant Ward hurriedly wrote a note to General Carr, and handing it to a corporal, ordered him to make all possible haste back to the command and deliver the message. The man started off on a gallop, and Lieutenant Ward said: “We will march slowly back until we meet the troops, as I think the General will soon be here, for he will start immediately upon receiving my note.”
In a few minutes we heard two or three shots in the direction in which our dispatch courier had gone, and soon after we saw him come flying around the bend of the creek, pursued by four or five Indians. The Lieutenant, with his squad of soldiers and myself, at once charged upon them, when they turned and ran across the stream.
“This will not do,” said Lieutenant Ward, “the whole Indian village will now know that soldiers are nearby.
“Lieutenant, give me that note, and I will take it to the General,” said I.
He gladly handed me the dispatch, and spurring my horse I dashed up the creek. After having ridden a short distance, I observed another party of Indians also going to the village with meat; but instead of waiting for them to fire upon me, I gave them a shot at long range. Seeing one man firing at them so boldly, it surprised them, and they did not know what to make of it. While they were thus considering, I got between them and our camp.

===Chinese===

我们一路顺畅追踪了数英里，登上了一处俯瞰河谷的高地。从这里向远处眺望，我们望见溪畔树丛中隐隐升起缕缕青烟，证实印第安营地就在不远处。沃德中尉派我携带公文返回营地向卡尔将军复命，自己则率部留在高地监视敌人动向。
我策马飞奔返回营地，半路上冷不防迎面撞上了一队满载猎获野牛肉、正向营地返程的印第安猎手。我并没有坐以待毙等他们先开火，而是在远距离上果断抬枪射击。看见一名孤身骑兵竟然如此勇猛凶悍地率先发难开火，印第安人顿时大惊失色，一时摸不清虚实。正当他们面面相觑犹豫不决之际，我已纵马敏捷地插到了他们与我们营地之间的开阔通道上。

===Original===

By this time they had recovered from their surprise, and, cutting their buffalo meat loose from their horses, they came after me at the top of their speed; but as their steeds were tired out, it did not take me long to leave them far in the rear.
I reached the command in less than an hour, delivered the dispatch to General Carr, and informed him of what I had seen. He instantly had the bugler sound “boots and saddles,” and all the troops﻿—with the exception of two companies, which we left to guard the train﻿—were soon galloping in the direction of the Indian camp.
We had ridden about three miles when we met Lieutenant Ward, who was coming slowly towards us. He reported that he had run into a party of Indian buffalo-hunters, and had killed one of the number, and had had one of his horses wounded. We immediately pushed forward and after marching about five miles came within sight of hundreds of mounted Indians advancing up the creek to meet us. They formed a complete line in front of us. General Carr, being desirous of striking their village, ordered the troops to charge, break through their line, and keep straight on. This movement would, no doubt, have been successfully accomplished had it not been for the rattlebrained and daredevil French Lieutenant Schinosky, commanding Company B, who, misunderstanding General Carr’s orders, charged upon some Indians at the left, while the rest of the command dashed through the enemy’s line, and was keeping straight on, when it was observed that Schinosky and his company were surrounded by four or five hundred redskins. The General, to save the company, was obliged to sound a halt and charge back to the rescue. The company, during this short fight, had several men and quite a number of horses killed.
All this took up valuable time, and night was coming on. The Indians were fighting desperately to keep us from reaching their village, which being informed by couriers of what was taking place, was packing up and getting away. During that afternoon it was all we could do to hold our own in fighting the mounted warriors, who were in our front and contesting every inch of the ground.

===Chinese===

此时他们终于从惊愕中回过神来，急忙割断绑在马背上的沉重野牛肉，策马以最快速度朝我穷追猛打；但由于他们的坐骑经过长途狩猎早已疲惫不堪，没过多久便被我远远甩在了身后。
不到1小时，我便风驰电掣冲回大部队阵营，将公文郑重呈交卡尔将军，并详述了亲眼所见的敌情。将军当机立断，命令号手吹响“束马整鞍”的战斗警报，除留下两个连负责留守守卫大车辎重外，其余全团官兵全速跃马扬鞭，直扑印第安营地而去。
我们策马狂奔了大约3英里，迎面遇到了正缓缓撤回的沃德中尉。他报告说自己刚才迎头撞上了一股印第安猎手，当场击毙了其中一人，自己麾下也有一匹战马挂彩。全军立刻加速推进，向前疾驰了约5英里后，只见眼前漫山遍野出现了数百名骑着战马的印第安勇士，正沿着溪流河谷正面迎击压上。他们在我们正面排成了一条绵密完整的战斗横队。卡尔将军一心想捣毁敌村，断然下令全军发起冲锋，中央突破撕开敌军阵线长驱直入。若不是指挥B连的法国籍中尉席诺斯基（Lieutenant Schinosky）头脑发热且生性鲁莽亡命，这一战术本可完美奏效；然而席诺斯基误解了卡尔将军的命令，擅自带着部下脱离大队冲向了左翼的一小撮印第安人，而主力大部队此时已一举突破敌阵继续全速向前穿插。就在此时，有人注意到席诺斯基和他的整个连队已被400到500名红皮肤敌人团团包围在核心。为了解救该连免遭全歼，将军万不得已只得下令全军急停，掉转兵锋回师救援。在这场短兵相接的混战中，该连多名战士阵亡，大批战马倒毙。
这番折腾耽搁了极其宝贵的战机，夜幕悄然降临。印第安人为了掩护家眷营地拼死顽抗，而得到信使通报的后方敌村则趁机打包行装仓皇撤退。在整个下午的激战中，面对正面殊死抵抗、寸土必争的印第安马队，我们竭尽全力才勉强稳住战线。

===Original===

The General had left word for our wagon train to follow up with its escort of two companies, but as it had not made its appearance he entertained some fears that it had been surrounded, and to prevent the possible loss of the supply train we had to go back and look for it. About 9 o’clock that evening we found it, and went into camp for the night.
Next morning we passed down the creek and there was not an Indian to be seen. They had all disappeared and gone on with their village. Two miles further on we came to where a village had been located, and here we found nearly everything belonging or pertaining to an Indian camp, which had been left in the great hurry to get away. These articles were all gathered up and burned. We then pushed out on the trail as fast as possible. It led us to the northeast towards the Republican; but as the Indians had a night the start of us we entertained but little hope of overtaking them that day. Upon reaching the Republican in the afternoon the General called a halt, and as the trail was running more to the east, he concluded to send his wagon train on to Fort McPherson by the most direct route, while he would follow on the trail of the redskins.
Next morning at daylight we again pulled out and were evidently gaining rapidly on the Indians for we could occasionally see them in the distance. About 11 o’clock that day while Major Babcock was ahead of the main command with his company, and while we were crossing a deep ravine, we were surprised by about three hundred warriors who commenced a lively fire upon us. Galloping out of the ravine on to the rough prairie the men dismounted and returned the fire. We soon succeeded in driving the enemy before us, and were so close upon them at one time, that they abandoned and threw away nearly all their lodges and camp equipages, and everything that had any considerable weight. They left behind them their played-out horses, and for miles we could see Indian furniture strewn along in every direction. The trail became divided, and the Indians scattered in small bodies, all over the prairie. As night was approaching and our horses were about giving out, a halt was called. A company was detailed to collect all the Indian horses running loose over the country, and to burn the other Indian property.

===Chinese===

将军此前曾下达指令，让两个连护送的大车辎重队随后跟进；但此时后方迟迟不见车队踪影，将军不禁深恐辎重队中途遭到敌人伏击包围，为了确保至关重要的粮秣弹药不落入敌手，大部队不得不放弃追击折返搜寻。当天晚上大约9点左右，我们终于找到了大车队，随即就地扎营过夜。
次日清晨，我们沿着溪流向下游挺进，沿途竟连一个印第安人的影子也没瞧见。敌人早已全员撤离，带着整座村落逃之夭夭。向前推进了2英里后，我们抵达了敌军此前的扎营地，只见现场丢弃了大量属于印第安营地的生活器具和辎重物资，显然是在仓皇逃命时遗弃的。官兵们将这些缴获物品统统搜集起来，一把火付之一炬。随后我们循着敌军逃窜的痕迹以最快速度向前追击。足迹向东北方向延伸直指里帕布利肯河；但鉴于印第安人整整抢先了一个夜晚的脚程，我们对当天能否追上敌人并未抱太大指望。下午抵达里帕布利肯河畔时，将军下令全军暂停休整；眼见敌踪开始大幅向东折转，他决定派遣大车辎重队取道最近的捷径直插麦克弗森堡，自己则率骑兵轻装顺着红皮肤敌人的痕迹继续咬住不放。
次日黎明破晓时分，全军再次拔营追击，显而易见我们正在飞速缩短差距，因为远处已偶尔能依稀望见敌人的移动黑影。当天上午大约11点左右，正当巴布考克少校（Major Babcock）率领部下先头连队在前开路、大部队穿过一条陡峭深沟时，我们冷不防遭到约300名印第安勇士的伏击，密集的子弹居高临下倾泻而下。骑兵们冲出峡沟登上开阔坎坷的草原高地，翻身下马展开猛烈还击。我们很快将敌人压制并驱散；有几次我们追得如此之近，以至于敌人惊慌失措地扔下了几乎所有的帐篷、营盘装备以及任何有分量的辎重。他们沿途丢弃了累瘫倒地的疲马，数英里的原野上四处散落着狼藉一片的印第安器皿。敌人的足迹随后四分五裂，大批印第安人化整为零，三五成群逃散在茫茫大草原各处。眼见夜色将暮，我们的战马也已筋疲力竭濒临极限，指挥官随即下令鸣金收兵。一个连被抽调负责搜拢散落在草原上的无主印第安马匹，并将敌人的其余物资残骸焚烧销毁。

===Original===

The command being nearly out of rations I was sent to the nearest point, Old Fort Kearney, about sixty miles distant for supplies.
Shortly after we reached Fort McPherson, which continued to be the headquarters of the Fifth Cavalry for some time. We remained there for ten days, fitting out for a new expedition to the Republican river country, and were reinforced by three companies of the celebrated Pawnee Indian scouts, commanded by Major Frank North; his officers being Captain Lute North, brother of the Major, Captain Cushing, his brother-in-law, Captain Morse, and Lieutenants Beecher, Matthews and Kislandberry. General Carr recommended at this time to General Augur, who was in command of the Department, that I be made chief of scouts in the Department of the Platte, and informed me that in this position I would receive higher wages than I had been getting in the Department of the Missouri. This appointment I had not asked for.
I made the acquaintance of Major Frank North,2 and I found him, and his officers, perfect gentlemen, and we were all good friends from the very start. The Pawnee scouts had made quite a reputation for themselves as they had performed brave and valuable services, in fighting against the Sioux, whose bitter enemies they were; being thoroughly acquainted with the Republican and Beaver country, I was glad that they were to be with the expedition, and they did good service.
During our stay at Fort McPherson I made the acquaintance of Lieutenant George P. Belden, known as the “White Chief,” whose life was written by Colonel Brisbin, U.S. army. I found him to be an intelligent, dashing fellow, a splendid rider and an excellent shot. An hour after our introduction he challenged me for a rifle match, the preliminaries of which were soon arranged. We were to shoot ten shots each for fifty dollars, at two hundred yards, off hand. Belden was to use a Henry rifle, while I was to shoot my old “Lucretia.” This match I won and then Belden proposed to shoot a one hundred yard match, as I was shooting over his distance. In this match Belden was victorious. We were now even, and we stopped right there.
While we were at this post General Augur and several of his officers, and also Thomas Duncan, Brevet Brigadier and Lieutenant Colonel of the Fifth Cavalry, paid us a visit for the purpose of reviewing the command.

===Chinese===

由于全军口粮行将告罄，我奉命驰奔距离最近的补给点——约60英里外的大本营老卡尼堡（Old Fort Kearney）紧急调拨军粮。
完成补给后不久，大部队进驻麦克弗森堡，该堡在此后相当长一段时间内充当了第五骑兵团的驻防司令部。我们在那里休整补给整整10天，为进军里帕布利肯河流域筹备一次全新的远征大行动，期间还获得了声名远扬的波尼族侦察兵大队的强势增援；该部由弗兰克·诺斯少校（Major Frank North）亲自统领，麾下军官包括少校的胞弟卢特·诺斯上尉、姐夫库欣上尉、莫尔斯上尉，以及比彻中尉、马修斯中尉和基斯兰伯里中尉。此时，卡尔将军郑重向军区司令奥格尔将军推荐，由我出任普拉特军区的首席侦察兵队长，并告诉我这个职位的薪俸将远高于我此前在密苏里军区拿到的报酬。这项极具分量的任命其实我事先从未主动开过口。
在哨所里我结识了弗兰克·诺斯少校，发现他和麾下的诸位军官无一不是真正的绅士典范，我们从一开始便成了无话不谈的挚友。波尼族侦察兵在边疆享有极高威望，在与世仇苏族人的多次殊死血战中建立了赫赫战功；由于他们对里帕布利肯河与比弗河流域的山川地理洞若观火，得知他们将随同远征，我感到格外踏实，而他们在随后的战事中确实立下了汗马功劳。
在麦克弗森堡驻留期间，我还有幸结识了被誉为“白人酋长”的乔治·P·贝尔登中尉（Lieutenant George P. Belden），陆军的布里斯宾上校曾为他专门撰写过传记。我发现他是一位谈吐不凡、英气逼人的硬汉，骑术超群且枪法精绝。在我们相识短短一小时后，他便向我提出进行一场步枪射击较量，比赛细则很快敲定。我们约定在200码距离上采用立姿无依托射击，各打10发，赌注50美元。贝尔登使用一柄亨利步枪，而我则端出我那支寸步不离的老伙计“老鲁克丽莎”（Lucretia）。这场较量最终由我拔得头筹；随后贝尔登提议在100码距离上再比一场，认为刚才的距离超出了他的习惯射程。在这第二场对决中贝尔登旗开得胜。双方至此打成了平手，我们便适可而止握手言欢。
在此期间，奥格尔将军携军区参谋军官，以及第五骑兵团名誉准将兼陆军中校托马斯·邓肯（Thomas Duncan）亲临哨所视察，对全团官兵举行了庄严阅兵。

===Original===

The regiment turned out in fine style and showed themselves to be well drilled soldiers, thoroughly understanding military tactics. The Pawnee scouts were also reviewed and it was very amusing to see them in their full regulation uniform. They had been furnished a regular cavalry uniform and on this parade some of them had their heavy overcoats on, others their large black hats, with all the brass accoutrements attached; some of them were minus pantaloons and only wore a breechclout. Others wore regulation pantaloons but no shirts on and were bareheaded; others again had the seat of the pantaloons cut out, leaving only leggins; some of them wore brass spurs, but had no boots or moccasins on. They seemed to understand the drill remarkably well for Indians. The commands, of course, were given to them in their own language by Major North, who could talk it as well as any full-blooded Pawnee. The Indians were well mounted and felt proud and elated because they had been made United States soldiers. Major North, has had for years complete power over these Indians and can do more with them than any man living. That evening after the parade was over the officers and quite a number of ladies visited a grand Indian dance given by the Pawnees, and of all the Indians I have seen, their dances excel those of any other tribe.
Next day the command started; when encamped, several days after, on the Republican River near the mouth of the Beaver, we heard the whoops of Indians, followed by shots in the vicinity of the mule herd, which had been taken down to water. One of the herders came dashing into camp with an arrow sticking into him. My horse was close at hand, and, mounting him bareback, I at once dashed off after the mule herd, which had been stampeded. I supposed certainly that I would be the first man on the ground. I was mistaken, however, for the Pawnee Indians, unlike regular soldiers, had not waited to receive orders from their officers, but had jumped on their ponies without bridles or saddles, and placing ropes in their mouths, had dashed off in the direction whence the shots had come, and had got there ahead of me. It proved to be a party of about fifty Sioux, who had endeavored to stampede our mules, and it took them by surprise to see their inveterate enemies﻿—the Pawnees﻿—coming at full gallop towards them.

===Chinese===

全团以昂扬威武的军容列队受阅，展示出训练有素、深谙操典战术的精锐气派。波尼族侦察兵也同样接受了检阅，看着他们身穿全套正规军装的滑稽模样，现场充满了忍俊不禁的欢快气氛。军方给他们每人配发了一套正规骑兵军装；在阅兵场上，有的人披着厚重的大衣，有的人戴着插满黄铜饰件的大黑军帽；有的下身干脆连军裤都不穿，仅裹着一条简陋的遮阴布；有的穿戴整齐军裤却光着膀子赤着脑袋；还有人竟然把军裤的臀部剪掉，硬生生改装成了土著皮护腿；有些人在光脚板上绑着黄铜马刺，脚上却连双皮靴或鹿皮软鞋都没穿。不过作为印第安土著，他们对步兵操典的领悟与执行却出奇地标准严整。号令自然全由能讲一口地道波尼土话的诺斯少校亲自用土著语言传达。战士们坐骑神骏，为自己正式晋升为合众国正规军人而自豪万分。多年以来，诺斯少校在这些波尼人心中拥有至高无上的威望，全天下没有任何人能像他那样自如地驾驭这群彪悍的战士。当晚阅兵结束后，军官们携眷属一道观摩了波尼战士献上的一场盛大印第安战舞；在我生平所见的所有土著部族中，波尼人的战舞风采冠绝群雄。
次日大部队开拔出征；数日后在比弗河注入里帕布利肯河的汇合口附近扎营时，营外突然传来了印第安人的刺耳战号声，紧接着牵去河边饮水的骡群周围爆发出密集的枪声。一名负责放牧的战士身上插着一支利箭，策马亡命冲进营地报警。我的战马恰好就在手边，我光着马背纵身一跃跃上马背，发疯似地朝炸了群的骡马方向猛冲，心想自己准是第一个冲到现场的人。然而我完全料错了——波尼战士根本不像正规军那样苦等长官的军令，他们连嚼子和马鞍都顾不上套，仅在马嘴里勒上一根缰绳，听到枪响的一瞬间便翻身跃上马背，风驰电掣般杀向交火点，竟然比我还抢先一步冲到了战场。赶来偷袭的是约50名企图劫掠骡群的苏族勇士，当迎面撞见宿敌波尼战士如潮水般全速冲杀而至时，他们顿时吓得魂飞魄散。

===Original===

They were not aware that the Pawnees were with the command, and as they knew that it would take regular soldiers sometime to turn out, they thought they would have ample opportunity to secure the herd before the troops could give chase.
We had a running fight of fifteen miles, and several of the enemy were killed. During this chase I was mounted on an excellent horse, which Colonel Royal had picked out for me, and for the first mile or two I was in advance of the Pawnees. Presently a Pawnee shot by me like an arrow and I could not help admiring the horse that he was riding. Seeing that he possessed rare running qualities, I determined if possible to get possession of the animal in some way. It was a large buckskin or yellow horse, and I took a careful view of him so that I would know him when I returned to camp.
After the chase was over I rode up to Major North and inquired about the buckskin horse.
“Oh yes,” said the Major, “that is one of our favorite steeds.”
“What chance is there to trade for him?” I asked.
“It is a government horse,” said he, “and the Indian who is riding him is very much attached to the animal.”
“I have fallen in love with the horse myself,” said I, “and I would like to know if you have any objections to my trading for him if I can arrange it satisfactorily with the Indian?”
He said: “None whatever, and I will help you to do it; you can give the Indian another horse in his place.”
A few days after this, I persuaded the Indian, by making him several presents, to trade horses with me, and in this way I became the owner of the buckskin steed, not as my own property, however, but as a government horse that I could ride. I gave him the name of “Buckskin Joe” and he proved to be a second Brigham. That horse I rode on and off during the summers of 1869, 1870, 1871 and 1872, and he was the horse that the Grand Duke Alexis rode on his buffalo hunt. In the winter of 1872, after I had left Fort McPherson, Buckskin Joe was condemned and sold at public sale, and was bought by Dave Perry, at North Platte, who in 1877 presented him to me, and I still own him. He is now at my ranch on the Dismal River, stone blind, but I shall keep him until he dies.
The command scouted several days up the Beaver and Prairie Dog Rivers, occasionally having running fights with war parties of Indians, but did not succeed in getting them into a general battle.

===Chinese===

苏族人做梦也没料到大军之中竟有波尼人随行；他们深知正规军集结出动向来拖泥带水，原以为自己有充裕的时间在骑兵追来之前将骡群席卷一空。
我们展开了一场长达15英里的狂奔追逐战，当场击毙了数名敌寇。在这场追击中，我骑着罗亚尔上校特意为我挑选的骏马，在前头一两英里内始终一马当先跑在波尼人的前沿。忽然，一名波尼战士跨骑一匹神骏烈马如离弦之箭般从我身边疾驰呼啸而过，我不禁对那匹坐骑叹为观止。见它具有如此超凡绝伦的飞奔耐力，我暗下决心，无论如何也得想办法把这匹宝马弄到手。那是一匹身形高大、毛色金黄的鹿皮黄马，我将它的模样死死印在脑海里，以便返回营地后能一眼辨认出来。
追击战结束后，我找到诺斯少校，向他打听那匹黄马的底细。
“噢，你说它啊，”少校笑道，“那可是我们队里数一数二的心头肉。”
“有没有可能通过交易把它换过来？”我急切地问。
“那是官家配发的马匹，”他说，“骑它的那个印第安战士对这匹牲口宝贝得不行，感情深得很。”
“可我自己对它一见钟情，”我说，“要是能私下跟那个印第安弟兄谈妥一桩两全其美的买卖，您长官意下如何，不反对吧？”
他爽快地说：“绝不反对，我甚至可以帮你在中间撮合；你大可以挑另一匹好马给他顶替。”
几天后，在奉送了几件极具诱惑力的小礼物后，我成功说动了那个印第安战士跟我换马；就这样，我成了这匹神骏黄马的新主人——当然，并非归为我的私有财产，而是作为一匹我可以全权策骑的官配军马。我给它正式赐名为“老鹿皮乔”（Buckskin Joe），它日后证明自己是继传奇猎马“布里格姆”之后的又一位旷世神驹。在1869、1870、1871与1872年的夏天，我骑着它南征北战屡建奇功；而俄国皇室亚历克西大公（Grand Duke Alexis）在草原猎杀野牛时，跨骑的正是这匹传奇名驹。1872年严冬，在我离开麦克弗森堡之后，老鹿皮乔因退役报废被军方公开竞价拍卖，北普拉特的戴夫·佩里（Dave Perry）将其买下，并在1877年将它重新作为厚礼赠还予我，至今它依然属于我。如今它正颐养天年生活在我位于凄凉河（Dismal River）畔的牧场里，双目虽已全盲，但我会悉心饲养它直至终老。
大部队随后在比弗河与草原犬鼠河（Prairie Dog River）流域进行了数日的搜山巡察，期间与小股印第安战队多次发生追逐交火，但狡猾的敌人始终避免与我主力大军展开全面决战。

===Original===

At the end of twenty days we found ourselves back on the Republican.
Hitherto the Pawnees had not taken much interest in me, but while at this camp I gained their respect and admiration by showing them how I killed buffaloes. Although the Pawnees were excellent buffalo killers, for Indians, I have never seen one of them who could kill more than four or five in one run. A number of them generally surround the herd and then dash in upon them, and in this way each one kills from one to four buffaloes. I had gone out in company with Major North and some of the officers, and saw them make a “surround.” Twenty of the Pawnees circled a herd and succeeded in killing only thirty-two.
While they were cutting up the animals another herd appeared in sight. The Indians were preparing to surround it, when I asked Major North to keep them back and let me show them what I could do. He accordingly informed the Indians of my wish and they readily consented to let me have the opportunity. I had learned that Buckskin Joe was an excellent buffalo horse, and felt confident that I would astonish the natives; galloping in among the buffaloes, I certainly did so by killing thirty-six in less than a half-mile run. At nearly every shot I killed a buffalo, stringing the dead animals out on the prairie, not over fifty feet apart. This manner of killing was greatly admired by the Indians who called me a big chief, and from that time on, I stood high in their estimation.

===Chinese===

在出征满20天后，我们回师重返里帕布利肯河畔的大营。
在此之前，营里的波尼战士对我并未显露出多少特别的敬意；但正是在这次驻营期间，我凭借当场展示猎杀野牛的绝技，一举赢得了这群骄傲勇士由衷的钦佩与折服。尽管作为印第安土著，波尼人在捕猎野牛上个个身手矫健，但我生平从未见过他们当中有谁能在一轮冲刺中独力猎杀4到5头以上。他们的传统战法通常是纠集一帮人将牛群团团包围，随后一拥而上纵马冲杀，凭这种办法每人能捕杀1到4头。当时我和诺斯少校及几名军官一同出猎，亲眼目睹了他们进行的一次“合围包抄”。20名波尼勇士围堵冲杀了一大群野牛，最终仅仅合力猎杀了32头。
正当他们忙着剥皮肢解猎物时，地平线上又涌现出一大群野牛。印第安人摩拳擦掌正准备再次合围，我上前恳请诺斯少校拦住他们，让我独自下场露上一手。少校随即向波尼战士说明了我的意图，他们欣然同意给我这个一展身手的机会。我早已深知老鹿皮乔是一匹天下无双的猎牛神驹，自信满满定能让这帮土著瞠目结舌。我策马如旋风般杀入狂奔的牛群腹地，战果果然轰动全场——在不到半英里的全速冲刺中，我单枪匹马连续轰毙了整整36头巨牛。几乎每一声枪响便有一头野牛应声栽倒，倒毙的巨兽尸首在草原上整整齐齐排成一条长线，彼此相隔不超过50英尺。这种登峰造极的神乎其技让全场波尼战士顶礼膜拜，他们心悦诚服地齐声高呼我为“大酋长”；自那一刻起，我在他们心目中树立了无与伦比的至高威望。

## XIII A Millionaire / 第十三章 百万富翁
===Original===

Soon after returning to Fort Hays, I was sent with dispatches to Fort Harker. After delivering the messages, I visited the town of Ellsworth, about three miles west of Fort Harker, and there I met a man named William Rose, a contractor on the Kansas Pacific Railroad, who had a contract for grading near Fort Hays. He had had his stock stolen by the Indians, and had come to Ellsworth to buy more.
During the course of our conversation, Mr. Rose incidentally remarked that he had some idea of laying out a town on the west side of Big Creek, about one mile from the fort, where the railroad was to cross. He asked my opinion of the contemplated enterprise, and I told him that I thought it was “a big thing.” He then proposed taking me as a partner in the scheme, and suggested that after we got the town laid out and thrown open to the public, we should establish a store and saloon there.
Thinking it would be a grand thing to be half-owner of a town, I at once accepted his proposition. We bought a stock of such articles as are usually found in a frontier store, and transported them to the place on Big Creek, where we were to found our town. We hired a railroad engineer to survey the site and stake it off into lots; and we gave the new town the ancient and historical name of Rome. To a “starter,” we donated lots to anyone who would build on them, but reserved the corner lots and others which were best located for ourselves. These reserved lots we valued at fifty dollars each.
Our modern Rome, like all mushroom towns along the line of a new railroad, sprang up as if by magic, and in less than one month we had two hundred frame and log houses, three or four stores, several saloons, and one good hotel. Rome was looming up, and Rose and I already considered ourselves millionaires, and thought we “had the world by the tail.” But one day a fine-looking gentleman, calling himself Dr. W. E. Webb, appeared in town, and dropping into our store introduced himself in a very pleasant way.
“Gentlemen, you’ve got a very flourishing little town here. Wouldn’t you like to have a partner in your enterprise?”
“No, thank you,” said I, “we have too good a thing here to whack up with anybody.”

===Chinese===

返回海斯堡（Fort Hays）后不久，我奉命携紧急公文前往哈克堡（Fort Harker）。呈递信件后，我顺道走访了哈克堡以西约3英里处的埃尔斯沃思镇（Ellsworth），并在那里结识了一位名叫威廉·罗斯（William Rose）的汉子。他是堪萨斯太平洋铁路公司（Kansas Pacific Railroad）的承包商，揽下了海斯堡附近一段路基平整工程。他手头的牲口此前被印第安人洗劫一空，这次正是专程跑到埃尔斯沃思来补购大牲畜的。
在彼此交谈期间，罗斯先生随口提起，他打算在距离要塞大约1英里、铁路线即将穿过的比格溪（Big Creek）西岸勘测规划一座全新的市镇。他征求我对这一宏伟构想的意见，我毫不犹豫地告诉他，这绝对是一桩“大有可为的买卖”。他随即提议拉我入伙合伙经营，并建议市镇完成规划并向公众开放招商后，我们两人合开一家杂货店兼小酒馆。
一想到能成为半座城镇的东家，这等天大的好事我自然满口应承。我们采购了一批边疆杂货铺常见的日用百货商品，一路运抵我们在比格溪畔选定的建镇地点。我们聘请了一位铁路工程师勘测地块，并打下木桩划定街区地块；我们将这座新城镇冠以古老而荣耀的历史名号——罗马（Rome）。作为建镇之初的“启动优惠”，任何人只要承诺动工建房，我们便免费赠予地块，唯独把街角地块以及全镇地理位置最好的黄金地段留给我们自己。这些自留地块我们定价为每块50美元。
我们这座现代罗马城就像所有沿新建铁路应运而生的暴发市镇一样，如雨后春笋般奇迹般拔地而起。在不到一个月的时间里，镇上便矗立起两百栋木板房与原木屋，开了三四家杂货铺、好几家酒馆，甚至还有一家气派体面的旅店。罗马城蒸蒸日上、威风凛凛，罗斯和我早已自视腰缠万贯的百万富翁，只觉得自己已经“把整个世界玩弄于股掌之间”。然而有一天，一位仪表堂堂、自称W·E·韦伯医生（Dr. W. E. Webb）的绅士造访小镇，他信步踱进我们的杂货铺，神态自若、极有礼貌地作了自我介绍。
“二位先生，你们在这里建起了一座相当繁华热闹的小镇啊。不知二位是否有兴趣在你们的事业中添一位合伙人呢？”
“不必了，多谢您的好意，”我回答道，“我们这桩买卖油水太大，用不着跟任何人分一杯羹。”

===Original===

My partner agreed with me, but the conversation was continued, and at last the stranger said:
“Gentlemen, I am the agent or prospector of the Kansas Pacific Railroad, and my business is to locate towns for the company along the line.”
“We think we have the only suitable town-site in this immediate locality,” said Mr. Rose, “and as a town is already started, we have saved the company considerable expense.”
“You know as well as I do,” said Dr. Webb, “that the company expects to make money by selling lands and town lots; and as you are not disposed to give the company a show, or share with me, I shall probably have to start another town near you. Competition is the life of trade, you know.”
“Start your town, if you want to. We’ve got the ‘bulge’ on you, and can hold it,” said I, somewhat provoked at his threat.
But we acted too independently and too indiscreetly for our own good Dr. Webb, the very next day after his interview with us, began hauling material to a spot about one mile east of us, where he staked out a new town, which he called Hays City. He took great pains to circulate in our town the story that the railroad company would locate their roundhouses and machine shops at Hays City, and that it was to be *the* town and a splendid business center. A ruinous stampede from our place was the result. People who had built in Rome came to the conclusion that they had built in the wrong place; they began pulling down their buildings and moving them over to Hays City, and in less than three days our once flourishing city had dwindled down to the little store which Rose and I had built.
It was on a bright summer morning that we sat on a pine box in front of our crib, moodily viewing the demolition of the last building. Three days before, we had considered ourselves millionaires; on that morning we looked around and saw that we were reduced to the ragged edge of poverty. Our sanguine expectations of realizing immense fortunes were dashed to the ground, and we felt pretty blue. The new town of Hays had swallowed Rome entirely. Mr. Rose facetiously remarked that he felt like “the last rose of summer,” with all his lovely companions faded and gone, and *he* left blooming alone. I told him I was still there, staunch and true, but he replied that that didn’t help the matter much. Thus ends the brief history of the “Rise, Decline and Fall” of Modern Rome.

===Chinese===

我的合伙人深表赞同，但交谈仍在继续，最后这位不速之客说道：
“二位先生，我是堪萨斯太平洋铁路公司的代理人兼选址特派员，我的本职工作就是为公司沿铁路线选址建镇。”
“我们认为这一带唯一适宜建镇的地盘已经被我们占了，”罗斯先生说，“况且城镇已经初具规模，我们实际上为贵公司省下了一大笔开销。”
“你们心里跟我一样清楚，”韦伯医生说道，“公司指望着靠出售土地和街区地块大发横财；既然你们不打算给公司留口饭吃，也不愿与我分润，那我恐怕只能在你们旁边另起炉灶再建一座新镇了。要知道，竞争才是繁荣商业的生命线。”
“想建你的镇子尽管去建好了。我们早就抢占了先机，牢牢握着‘制高点’，谁也撼动不了。”听到他的要挟，我颇有几分恼火地呛了回去。
但我们未免过于自以为是，行事也太欠考虑了。就在与我们谈话的次日，韦伯医生便开始大举调运建筑建材，运到了我们以东仅约1英里处的一块空地上，在那里圈地打桩划定了一座新镇，命名为海斯城（Hays City）。他不遗余力地在我们镇上大肆宣扬，说铁路公司将把列车转盘库和机械修配厂全部设在海斯城，那里才是铁路干线上真正的核心重镇与未来的繁华商业中心。这一招对我们造成了毁灭性的打击，顷刻间引发了全镇大溃逃。在罗马城盖了房子的居民纷纷认定自己选错了落脚地；大家动手拆除房舍，连夜将木料砖石搬往海斯城。在短短不到三天时间里，我们那座曾盛极一时的都市便土崩瓦解，最后只剩下罗斯和我亲手搭建的那间孤零零的杂货铺。
那是一个晴朗明媚的夏日清晨，我们俩坐在铺子门前的一只松木箱上，闷闷不乐地眼睁睁看着最后一栋建筑被彻底拆除。仅仅三天前，我们还自诩为呼风唤雨的百万富翁；而到了这个早晨，放眼四顾，我们已然沦落到了赤贫的悬崖边缘。坐拥泼天富贵的乐观看法瞬间摔得粉碎，我们俩的心情沮丧到了极点。新建的海斯城将罗马城吞噬得一干二净。罗斯先生自嘲地打趣说，自己如今简直成了“夏日最后的玫瑰”，身边所有娇艳的同伴尽皆凋零逝去，只剩他独自在风中孤芳自赏。我安慰他说我依然坚守阵地、不离不弃，但他苦笑着回答说这于事无补。现代罗马城的“崛起、衰落与崩溃”的短暂历史，至此画上了句号。

===Original===

It having become evident to me that there was very little hope of Rome ever regaining its former splendor and prosperity, I sent my wife and daughter Arta﻿—who had been born at Leavenworth in the latter part of December, 1866﻿—to St. Louis on a visit. They had been living with me for some little time in the rear part of our “store.”
At this time Mr. Rose and myself had a contract under Schumacher, Miller & Co., constructors of the Kansas Pacific, for grading five miles of track westward from Big Creek, and running through the site of Rome. Notwithstanding we had been deserted, we had some small hope that they would not be able to get water at the new town, and that the people would all soon move back to Rome, as we really had the best location. We determined, therefore, to go on with our grading contract, and wait for something better to turn up. It was indeed hard for us, who had been millionaires, to come down to the level of common railroad contractors﻿—but we had to do it, all the same.
We visited the new town of Hays almost daily, to see how it was progressing, and in a short time we became much better acquainted with Dr. Webb, who had reduced us from our late independent to our present dependent position. We found him a perfect gentleman﻿—a whole-souled, genial-hearted fellow, whom everybody liked and respected. Nearly every day, “Doc” and I would take a ride over the prairie together and hunt buffalo.
On one occasion, having ventured about ten miles from the town, we spied a band of Indians not over two miles distant, who were endeavoring to get between us and the town, and thus cut us off. I was mounted on my celebrated horse Brigham, the fleetest steed I ever owned. On several subsequent occasions he saved my life, and he was the horse that I rode when I killed sixty-nine buffaloes in one day. Dr. Webb was riding a beautiful thoroughbred bay, which he had brought with him from the East. Having such splendid horses, we laughed at the idea of a band of Indians overtaking us on a square run, no matter how well they might be mounted; but not caring to be cut off by them, we ran our steeds about three miles towards home, thus getting between the braves and the town. The Indians were then about three-quarters of a mile distant, and we stopped and waved our hats at them, and fired some shots at long range.

===Chinese===

眼见罗马城重现往昔辉煌与繁荣的希望已微乎其微，我便将妻子以及刚出生的女儿阿尔塔（Arta）送往圣路易斯探亲。阿尔塔是1866年12月下旬在莱文沃思（Leavenworth）降生的。在此之前，母女俩一直随我挤住在杂货铺后半截的隔间里。
当时，罗斯先生和我接下了堪萨斯太平洋铁路建造商舒马赫-米勒公司（Schumacher, Miller & Co.）的一份施工分包合同，负责从比格溪向西平整5英里的铁路路基，这段线路恰好穿过罗马城的旧址。尽管遭到全镇居民的遗弃，我们心底仍抱有一线渺茫的希望，觉得海斯城地势缺水，人们迟早会因为缺水而搬回罗马城，毕竟论水源和地利，我们这里才是上上之选。因此，我们决定咬紧牙关把路基平整工程干下去，静观其变等待转机。对于我们这两个昔日的百万富翁而言，一下子跌落回普通铁路苦力承包商的境地，心里确实不是滋味——但生活所迫，我们也只能硬着头皮熬下去。
我们几乎天天都要去新建的海斯城转转，看看那里的建设进展，没过多久，我们便与那位一手将我们从腰缠万贯的独立东家打回底层雇工的元凶——韦伯医生成了熟人。我们发现此人其实是一位无可挑剔的绅士——为人坦荡热忱、性格开朗豪爽，极受大家爱戴与敬重。几乎每天，“医生”都会与我一同策马奔驰在苍茫草原上，四处猎捕野牛。
有一次，我们俩纵马奔驰到距离小镇约10英里处，猛然窥见一伙印第安人正游弋在不足2英里开外，他们正企图迂回插到我们与小镇之间，切断我们的退路。当时我骑着我的名驹“布里格姆”（Brigham），这是我平生拥有过的最神骏迅捷的战马。在此后的许多危急关头，它屡次救过我的性命，而在我创下单日猎杀69头野牛辉煌纪录的那一天，我胯下骑的也正是这匹宝马。韦伯医生则骑着一匹神气活现的纯种红骝马，那是他从东部特意带过来的良驹。仗着彼此胯下的绝世宝马，我们对印第安人在平地上能追上我们这种念头嗤之以鼻，任凭他们的坐骑再精良也绝无可能；但我们也不愿真被他们抄了后路，于是拍马朝着回家的方向飞奔了大约3英里，成功抢占到了印第安勇士与小镇之间的开阔地带。此时印第安人距离我们大约四分之三英里，我们勒住缰绳，摘下帽子朝他们挑衅挥舞，并举枪向他们进行了几轮超远距离射击。

===Original===

There were thirteen in the party, and as they were getting pretty close to us, we struck out for Hays. They came on in pursuit and sent several scattering shots after us, but we easily left them behind. They finally turned and rode off towards the Saline River.
The Doctor thought this glorious sport, and wanted to organize a party to go in pursuit of them, but I induced him to give up this idea, although he did so rather reluctantly. The Doctor soon became quite an expert hunter, and before he had remained on the prairie a year there were but few men in the country who could kill more buffaloes on a hunt than he.
Being aware that Rose and myself felt rather downhearted over our deserted village, the Doctor one day said that, as he had made the proprietors of Rome “howl,” he would give us two lots each in Hays, and did so. We finally came to the conclusion that our old town was dead beyond redemption or revival, and we thereupon devoted our undivided attention to our railroad contract. One day we were pushed for horses to work on our scrapers﻿—so I hitched up Brigham, to see how he would work. He was not much used to that kind of labor, and I was about giving up the idea of making a workhorse of him, when one of the men called to me that there were some buffaloes coming over the hill. As there had been no buffaloes seen anywhere in the vicinity of the camp for several days, we had become rather short of meat. I immediately told one of our men to hitch his horses to a wagon and follow me, as I was going out after the herd, and we would bring back some fresh meat for supper. I had no saddle, as mine had been left at the camp a mile distant, so taking the harness from Brigham, I mounted him bareback and started out after the game, being armed with my celebrated buffalo-killer, “Lucretia Borgia,”﻿—a newly-improved breech-loading needle gun, which I had obtained from the government.
While I was riding toward the buffaloes I observed five horsemen coming out from the fort, who had evidently seen the buffaloes from the post, and were going out for a chase. They proved to be some newly-arrived officers in that part of the country, and when they came up closer, I could see by the shoulder straps that the senior officer was a captain, while the others were lieutenants.
“Hello! my friend,” sang out the captain, “I see you are after the same game we are.”

===Chinese===

对方总共有13人，眼见他们逐渐逼近，我们便策马直奔海斯城。印第安人在身后紧追不舍，零星朝我们放了几记冷枪，但我们轻而易举便将他们甩得连影都看不见。最终他们只能悻悻调转马头，朝萨林河（Saline River）方向策马退去。
医生觉得这场惊险追逐是一场痛快淋漓的绝妙冒险，甚至嚷嚷着要招募一伙人马杀回去乘胜追击，但我好说歹说劝他打消了这个莽撞念头，尽管他显得颇为心有不甘。医生很快成长为一名老练的猎手，在草原上生活还不到一年，当地便鲜有人能在打猎捕杀野牛的本领上胜过他了。
医生心知罗斯和我因罗马城化为荒村废墟而一直耿耿于怀，一天他主动找上门来，说既然是他让罗马城的两位大掌柜“哭爹喊娘”，他愿意补偿我们在海斯城各两处街区地块，并且当场兑现了诺言。我们最终彻底认清现实，明白我们的旧小镇早已回天乏术、绝无复兴可能，于是自此收拢心思，将全副精力投入到铁路路基工程上。有一天，平整路基的刮土铲车急缺挽马——我便把布里格姆套上了挽具，想看看它能不能拉大车干粗活。它显然极不习惯这种笨重苦力，我正打算放弃把它当役畜的念头，忽然工地上一名工人冲我大喊，说有一群野牛正翻过山头漫步过来。由于营地周围一连好几天都没见过半头野牛的踪影，我们手头的鲜肉储备早已见底。我当即吩咐手下一名工人赶快把大车套上马紧随其后，告诉他我打算出马追猎牛群，带回几口新鲜野牛肉给大伙晚上打牙祭。当时我手头没有马鞍，因为我的鞍子留在了1英里外的宿营地，于是我一把卸下布里格姆身上的挽具，光着马背纵身跃上马脊，带着我那支威震四方的猎牛神器“卢克雷蒂娅·博尔吉亚”（Lucretia Borgia）——一支我从军方搞到的最新改良型后膛装弹针击枪——便朝着猎物疾驰而去。
当我策马奔向野牛群时，注意到要塞方向冲出了5名骑手，他们显然也是在哨所里望见了山头的牛群，正赶出来准备大过一把狩猎瘾。他们是刚调派到本地驻防的军官，等他们策马靠得更近些时，我从他们肩章上的军衔辨认出带队的是一位上尉，其余几人则是中尉。
“喂！朋友！”那位上尉老远便扯开嗓门大喊道，“看来咱们盯上的是同一群猎物啊。”

===Original===

“Yes, sir; I saw those buffaloes coming over the hill, and as we were about out of fresh meat I thought I would go and get some,” said I.
They scanned my cheap-looking outfit pretty closely, and as my horse was not very prepossessing in appearance, having on only a blind bridle, and otherwise looking like a workhorse they evidently considered me a green hand at hunting.
“Do you expect to catch those buffaloes on that Gothic steed?” laughingly asked the captain.
“I hope so, by pushing on the reins hard enough,” was my reply.
“You’ll never catch them in the world, my fine fellow,” said the captain. “It requires a fast horse to overtake the animals on these prairies.”
“Does it?” asked I as if I didn’t know it.
“Yes; but come along with us as we are going to kill them more for pleasure than anything else. All we want are the tongues and a piece of tender loin, and you may have all that is left,” said the generous man.
“I am much obliged to you, Captain, and will follow you,” I replied. There were eleven buffaloes in the herd and they were not more than a mile from us. The officers dashed ahead as if they had a sure thing on killing them all before I could come up with them; but I had noticed that the herd was making towards the creek for water, and as I knew buffalo nature, I was perfectly aware that it would be difficult to turn them from their direct course. Thereupon, I started towards the creek to head them off, while the officers came up in the rear and gave chase.
The buffaloes came rushing past me not a hundred yards distant, with the officers about three hundred yards in the rear. Now, thought I, is the time to “get my work in,” as they say; and I pulled the blind-bridle from my horse, who knew as well as I did that we were out for buffaloes﻿—as he was a trained hunter. The moment the bridle was off, he started at the top of his speed, running in ahead of the officers, and with a few jumps he brought me alongside of the rear buffalo. Raising old “Lucretia Borgia” to my shoulder, I fired, and killed the animal at the first shot. My horse then carried me alongside the next one, not ten feet away, and I dropped him at the next fire.
As soon as one buffalo would fall, Brigham would take me so close to the next, that I could almost touch it with my gun.

===Chinese===

“是啊，长官；我瞧见那些野牛翻过了山脊，正好营里快断肉了，我就想着出来猎几头上桌。”我回答道。
他们上上下下仔细打量着我那身寒碜简陋的行头，而我的坐骑外表也实在不敢恭维，头上只套着一副遮眼马笼头，浑身上下怎么看都像是一匹拉大车的钝马役畜，他们显然把我当成了刚涉足狩猎的生手雏儿。
“你该不会指望骑着这么一匹哥特式的笨骨架老马去撵野牛吧？”上尉忍不住嗤笑起来。
“但愿能追上吧，只要缰绳勒得够紧、催得够急就行。”我平静地应承道。
“老弟，凭这匹马你这辈子也休想追上它们，”上尉傲慢地笑道，“在这片大草原上撵野牛，没一匹飞毛腿快马是绝吃不上肉的。”
“是这样吗？”我故作茫然地反问，仿佛对此一无所知。
“可不是嘛！不过你跟在我们后头一道跑吧，我们打野牛纯粹是为了图个乐子。我们只要牛舌和一段嫩牛里脊，剩下的整副牛架子全归你。”这位慷慨的军官夸口说道。
“那可真是太感谢您了，上尉，我就跟在您几位后头。”我顺水推舟答道。牛群里总共有11头野牛，距离我们不足1英里。军官们快马加鞭抢先冲在前面，仿佛十拿九稳能在追上我之前把所有野牛屠戮殆尽；但我早已敏锐地注意到，牛群正径直奔向小溪饮水，凭我对野牛习性的洞悉，我深知这种畜生一旦认准了水源方向，绝难被外力轻易驱赶拐弯。于是，我拍马直插小溪前沿去抄底拦截，而军官们则跟在牛群屁股后头一路狂追。
转瞬间，狂奔的野牛群从距离我不足100码处呼啸穿过，而那帮军官此时还在牛屁股后头300码处苦苦追赶。机不可失，时不再来，我暗忖正是该我“大显身手”的时刻了；我顺手一把扯下了布里格姆脸上的遮眼马笼头——作为一匹身经百战的猎牛神驹，它心里跟我一样明镜似的，知道咱们今天就是来大开杀戒的。笼头一除，它立刻如脱缰闪电般全速爆发狂飙，风驰电掣般从几名军官前方一掠而过，几个纵跃腾挪便将我稳稳送到了跑在最后的那头野牛身侧。我端起老搭档“卢克雷蒂娅·博尔吉亚”顶上肩头，扣动扳机，第一枪便将那头巨兽轰毙在地。紧接着，我的宝马载着我电光石火般咬上第二头野牛，彼此相距不足10英尺，我抬手又是一枪，第二头巨兽应声轰然瘫倒。
只要有一头野牛中弹扑倒，布里格姆便会心领神会地贴上下一头，近得几乎让我能拿枪口直接戳中牛身。

===Original===

In this manner I killed the eleven buffaloes with twelve shots; and, as the last animal dropped, my horse stopped. I jumped to the ground, knowing that he would not leave me﻿—it must be remembered that I had been riding him without bridle, reins or saddle﻿—and turning round as the party of astonished officers rode up, I said to them:
“Now, gentlemen, allow me to present to you all the tongues and tenderloins you wish from these buffaloes.”
Captain Graham, for such I soon learned was his name, replied: “Well, I never saw the like before. Who under the sun are you, anyhow?”
“My name is Cody,” said I.
One of the lieutenants, Thompson by name, who had met me at Fort Harker, then recognized me, and said: “Why, that is Bill Cody, our old scout.” He then introduced me to the other officers, who were Captain Graham, of the Tenth Cavalry, and Lieutenants Reed, Emmick and Ezekiel.
Captain Graham, who was considerable of a horseman, greatly admired Brigham, and said: “That horse of yours has running points.”
“Yes, sir; he has not only got the points, he is a runner and knows how to use the points,” said I.
“So I noticed,” said the captain.
They all finally dismounted, and we continued chatting for some little time upon the different subjects of horses, buffaloes, Indians and hunting. They felt a little sore at not getting a single shot at the buffaloes, but the way I had killed them had, they said, amply repaid them for their disappointment. They had read of such feats in books, but this was the first time they had ever seen anything of the kind with their own eyes. It was the first time, also, that they had ever witnessed or heard of a white man running buffaloes on horseback without a saddle or a bridle.
I told them that Brigham knew nearly as much about the business as I did, and if I had had twenty bridles they would have been of no use to me, as he understood everything, and all that he expected of me was to do the shooting. It is a fact, that Brigham would stop if a buffalo did not fall at the first fire, so as to give me a second chance, but if I did not kill the buffalo then, he would go on, as if to say, “You are no good, and I will not fool away time by giving you more than two shots.” Brigham was the best horse I ever owned or saw for buffalo chasing.

===Chinese===

就这样，我总共射出了12发子弹，干脆利落地击毙了整整11头野牛；当最后一头巨兽重重栽倒在地时，我的坐骑也恰到好处地稳稳刹住了蹄步。我纵身跃下马背，深知它绝不会擅自离我而去——千万别忘了，我当时骑着它可是连鞍子、马嚼子和缰绳一概全无的光背奔袭——我转过身来，面对目瞪口呆策马赶上来的军官们，微笑着招呼道：
“诸位先生，这几头野牛身上的牛舌和里脊肉，各位想要多少尽管自便。”
后来我得知领头的军官名叫格雷厄姆上尉（Captain Graham），他瞠目结舌地叹道：“老天爷，我平生从未见过这等神乎其技。你究竟是何方神圣？”
“我叫科迪（Cody）。”我淡然答道。
其中一位名叫汤普森（Thompson）的中尉曾在哈克堡见过我，此时一眼认出了我，惊呼道：“天哪，这不是比尔·科迪吗，咱们的老侦察兵啊！”他随即将我引荐给其他几名军官，他们分别是第十骑兵团的格雷厄姆上尉，以及里德、埃米克和以西结三位中尉。
格雷厄姆上尉本身是位极精通相马的行家里手，对布里格姆赞不绝口，说道：“你这匹马浑身上下全是飞奔的神骨。”
“正是，长官；它不仅骨相神骏，更是天生的飞毛腿，而且懂得如何把一身神力发挥得淋漓尽致。”我答道。
“我算是亲眼见识到了。”上尉赞叹道。
众人纷纷翻身下马，我们围坐在一起，就马匹、野牛、印第安人与边疆打猎的话题畅聊了许久。军官们对自己连开一枪的机会都没捞着多少有些挂不住脸，但一致表示能亲眼目睹我这等惊天地泣鬼神的猎牛绝技，早已让他们把那一丝遗憾抛到了九霄云外。这类神乎其神的传奇壮举他们过去只在荒诞书本里读到过，这还是第一次亲眼见证活生生的场面。这也是他们平生第一次听说并目睹，竟有白人能在不戴鞍鞯、不执缰绳的光背马背上肆意狂飙追猎野牛。
我告诉他们，布里格姆对捕猎这门营生的门道懂得几乎不比我少，就算给我20副马嚼子也纯属多余，它通晓一切关窍，对我的唯一期望无非就是专心开枪射击而已。事实上确实如此：一枪开出野牛若未当场毙命，布里格姆便会放缓马蹄停顿片刻，好给我补射第二枪的机会；但要是第二枪下去野牛依然不倒，它便会头也不回地径直奔向别处，仿佛在鄙视我说：“你手艺太臭了，我可没闲工夫陪你浪费子弹给第三枪。”论起追猎野牛，布里格姆是我平生拥有过、也见识过的天下第一神驹。

===Original===

Our conversation was interrupted in a little while by the arrival of the wagon which I had ordered out; I loaded the hindquarters of the youngest buffaloes on it, and then cut out the tongues and tender loins, and presented them to the officers, after which I rode towards the fort with them, while the wagon returned to camp.
Captain Graham told me that he expected to be stationed at Fort Hays during the summer, and would probably be sent out on a scouting expedition, and in case he was he would like to have me accompany him as scout and guide. I replied that notwithstanding I was very busy with my railroad contract I would go with him if he was ordered out. I then left the officers and returned to our camp.
That very night the Indians unexpectedly made a raid on the horses, and ran off five or six of our very best work-teams, leaving us in a very crippled condition. At daylight I jumped on old Brigham and rode to Fort Hays, when I reported the affair to the commanding officer; Captain Graham and Lieutenant Emmick were at once ordered out with their company of one hundred colored troops, to pursue the Indians and recover our stock if possible. In an hour we were under way. The darkies had never been in an Indian fight and were anxious to catch the band we were after and “Sweep de red debels from off de face ob de earth.” Captain Graham was a brave, dashing officer, eager to make a record for himself, and it was with difficulty that I could trail fast enough to keep out of the way of the impatient soldiers. Every few moments Captain Graham would ride up to see if the trail was freshening and how soon we should be likely to overtake the thieves.
At last we reached the Saline River, where we found the Indians had only stopped to feed and water the animals, and had then pushed on towards the Solomon. After crossing the Saline they made no effort to conceal their trail, thinking they would not be pursued beyond that point﻿—consequently we were able to make excellent time. We reached the Soloman before sunset, and came to a halt; we surmised that if the Indians were camped on this river, that they had no suspicion of our being in the neighborhood. I advised Captain Graham to remain with the company where it was, while I went ahead on a scout to find the Indians, if they were in the vicinity.

===Chinese===

不久，我吩咐调来的大车赶到了现场，打断了我们的交谈；我将最年轻几头野牛的后腿肉装上大车，随后割下了牛舌与里脊肉，恭敬地赠予了几位军官。随后我策马伴随军官们朝要塞方向走去，大车则返回了我们的营地。
格雷厄姆上尉告诉我，今年夏季他预计将常驻海斯堡，届时极可能会奉命率部出征执行远征侦察任务，一旦成行，他非常希望能聘请我作为随军侦察兵兼向导。我答复他说，虽然手头的铁路合同忙得不可开交，但只要他接到出征调令，我一定义不容辞随行效力。随后我与军官们道别，策马返回了我们的营地。
就在当天夜里，印第安人出其不意地突袭了我们的营地马群，劫走了我们5到6组最壮实的大牲口挽马队，导致我们的施工进度几乎陷入瘫痪。天刚蒙蒙亮，我便翻身跃上老搭档布里格姆，飞马驰往海斯堡向指挥官紧急报案；格雷厄姆上尉与埃米克中尉当即奉命率领由100名黑人士兵组成的一个连队紧急出动，全力追击印第安匪徒夺回被盗牲畜。短短一个小时之内，大部队便已拔营启程。这群黑人士兵生平从未与印第安人真刀真枪交过手，人人摩拳擦掌、求战心切，一心想要追上这伙强盗，“把这帮红皮恶魔从大地上彻底抹去”。格雷厄姆上尉骁勇善战、行事果决，极渴望立下一番功业，以至于我必须竭尽全力放快追踪速度，才不至于被身后急不可耐的行军队伍撞倒。每隔一会儿，格雷厄姆上尉就会策马冲到前沿，询问敌人的足迹是否越来越新鲜，我们究竟何时才能追上这帮盗贼。
最终，我们一路追赶至萨林河畔，发现印第安人仅在此处稍作停歇给牲口饮水喂草，随后便马不停蹄地奔向所罗门河（Solomon River）。在渡过萨林河后，他们自认为官兵绝不可能追过这条界线，因此毫无遮掩痕迹之意——这使我们得以全速行军。在夕阳西下之前，我们赶到了所罗门河畔并就地下令停止前进；我们推测如果印第安人就在此河畔扎营，他们绝对想不到我们已经近在咫尺。我建议格雷厄姆上尉率领全连在原地隐蔽待命，由我独自向前实施单人侦察，探明附近是否确实有印第安人的营寨。

===Original===

After riding some distance down the ravine that led to the river, I left my horse at the foot of a hill; then, creeping to the top, I looked cautiously over the summit upon the Solomon, below. I at once discovered in plain view, not a mile away, a herd of horses grazing, our lost ones among them; very shortly I made out the Indian camp, noted its lay, and how we could best approach it. Reporting to Captain Graham, whose eyes fairly danced with delight at the prospect of surprising and whipping the redskins, we concluded to wait until the moon rose, then get into the timber so as to approach the Indians as closely as possible without being discovered, and finally to make a sudden dash into their camp, and clean them out. We had everything “cut and dried,” as we thought, but, alas! just as we were nearing the point where we were to take the open ground and make our charge, one of the colored gentlemen became so excited that he fired off his gun. We immediately commenced the charge, but the firing of the gun and the noise of our rush through the crackling timber alarmed the Indians, who at once sprang to their horses and were away from us before we reached their late camp. Captain Graham called out “Follow me boys!” which we did for awhile, but in the darkness the Indians made good their escape. The bugle then gave the recall, but some of the darkies did not get back until morning, having, in their fright, allowed their horses to run away with them whithersoever it suited the animal’s pleasure to go.
We followed the trail the next day for awhile, but as it become evident that it would be a long chase to overtake the enemy, and as we had rations only for the day, we commenced the return. Captain Graham was bitterly disappointed in not being able to get the fight when it seemed so near at one time. He roundly cursed the “nigger” who fired the gun, and as a punishment for his carelessness, he was compelled to walk all the way back to Fort Hays.

===Chinese===

顺着通往河流的一条深沟骑行了一段距离后，我将战马留在一座小山脚下；随后，我悄悄匍匐攀爬到山顶，小心翼翼地探头眺望下方的所罗门河谷。放眼望去，在不足1英里开外的一片开阔草地上，一大群马匹赫然正在悠闲吃草，我们被盗的牲口赫然混杂在其中；没过多久，我便辨认出了印第安人的营寨，摸清了营地的布局走向以及最佳突袭路线。我折返回去向格雷厄姆上尉汇报，一听说有希望打敌人一个措手不及并痛歼红皮匪帮，上尉兴奋得双眼直放光芒。我们商定等到月亮升起之后，借助林木掩护潜行接近，在不被察觉的前提下尽可能逼近印第安营寨，最后全军发起雷霆万钧的突然冲锋，将敌人一举全歼。原本一切筹划得“天衣无缝”，谁料天有不测风云！就在我们即将冲出树林、进入开阔地发起冲锋的节骨眼上，其中一名黑人士兵情绪过度紧张激动，竟然走火打响了手中的步枪。我们当即被迫全线发起冲锋，但枪声和大队人马穿过枯枝密林发出的剧烈轰响瞬间惊醒了印第安人，他们像受惊的兔子般飞身跃上马背，还没等我们冲入旧营地，他们早已纵马逃得无影无踪。格雷厄姆上尉厉声高呼：“弟兄们跟我冲！”我们奋力穷追了一阵，但在漆黑夜幕的掩护下，印第安人最终成功逃脱。随后军号吹响了收兵号，但有几名黑人士兵直到次日清晨才狼狈归队——他们在惊恐之下任由惊马漫山遍野狂奔，马爱跑到哪儿就由着它跑到哪儿。
次日我们沿着敌人的撤退痕迹又追踪了一段，但显而易见要追上敌人将是一场漫长的长途追逐，而我们随身携带的干粮仅够维持当天口粮，部队遂开始撤军返航。眼睁睁看着一场唾手可得的大捷化为泡影，格雷厄姆上尉懊恼沮丧至极。他把那个走火放枪的黑人士兵骂了个狗血淋头，为了惩戒他的粗心大意，罚他一路徒步走回了海斯堡。

## XI A Soldier / 第十一章 戎马生涯
===Original===

In the fall of 1861 I made a trip to Fort Larned, Kansas, carrying military dispatches, and in the winter I accompanied George Long through the country, and assisted him in buying horses for the government.
The next spring, 1862, an expedition against the Indians was organized, consisting of a volunteer regiment, the Ninth Kansas, under Colonel Clark. This expedition, which I had joined in the capacity of guide and scout, proceeded to the Kiowa and Comanche country, on the Arkansas River, along which stream we scouted all summer between Fort Lyon and Fort Larned, on the old Santa Fe trail. We had several engagements with the Indians, but they were of no great importance.
In the winter of 1862, I became one of the “Red Legged Scouts,”﻿—a company of scouts commanded by Captain Tuff. Among its members were some of the most noted Kansas Rangers, such as Red Clark, the St. Clair brothers, Jack Harvey, an old pony express-rider named Johnny Fry, and many other well known frontiersmen. Our field of operations was confined mostly to the Arkansas country and southwestern Missouri. We had many a lively skirmish with the bushwhackers and Younger brothers, and when we were not hunting them, we were generally employed in carrying dispatches between Forts Dodge, Gibson, Leavenworth, and other posts. Whenever we were in Leavenworth we had a very festive time. We usually attended all the balls in full force, and “ran things” to suit ourselves. Thus I passed the winter of 1862 and the spring of 1863.
Subsequently I engaged to conduct a small train to Denver for some merchants, and on reaching that place in September, I received a letter stating that my mother was not expected to live. I hastened home, and found her dangerously ill. She grew gradually worse, and at last, on the 22nd of November, 1863, she died. Thus passed away a loving and affectionate mother and a noble, brave, good and loyal woman. That I loved her above all other persons, no one who has read these reminiscences can for a moment doubt.
Previous to this said event my sister Julia had been married to a gentleman named J. A. Goodman, and they now came to reside at our house and take charge of the children, as my mother had desired that they should not be separated. Mr. Goodman became the guardian of the minor children.

===Chinese===

1861年秋，我前往堪萨斯州的拉尼德堡（Fort Larned）递送军用公文；入冬后，我陪同乔治·朗（George Long）走遍乡间，协助他为联邦政府采办军马。
次年春，即1862年，军方组织了一支讨伐印第安人的远征军，由克拉克上校（Colonel Clark）麾下的堪萨斯第九志愿团组成。我以向导兼侦察兵的身份加入了这支远征军，部队开拔进抵阿肯色河沿岸的凯奥瓦人（Kiowa）与科曼奇人（Comanche）聚居地。整个夏天，我们都在古老的圣菲小道（Santa Fe trail）上，在里昂堡与拉尼德堡之间的河谷地带执行巡逻侦察。期间我们与印第安人发生过几次交火，但规模都不大，无关大局。
到了1862年冬季，我成为了“红腿侦察队”（Red Legged Scouts）的一员——这是由塔夫上尉（Captain Tuff）指挥的一支侦察连队。队中云集了多位声名显赫的堪萨斯游骑兵，诸如红发克拉克（Red Clark）、圣克莱尔兄弟（St. Clair brothers）、杰克·哈维（Jack Harvey）、昔日的小马快递骑手约翰尼·弗莱（Johnny Fry），以及许多威震边疆的老拓荒者。我们的作战区域主要局限在阿肯色河流域与密苏里州西南部。我们屡次与南方丛林游击队员（bushwhackers）以及扬格兄弟（Younger brothers）匪帮发生激烈遭遇战；在不搜捕他们的时候，我们通常受雇在道奇堡（Fort Dodge）、吉布森堡（Fort Gibson）、莱文沃思堡（Fort Leavenworth）以及其他军事哨所之间传递紧急公文。每逢进驻莱文沃思，我们都会尽情狂欢作乐。我们通常全员出动出席所有舞会，随心所欲地“主宰一切”。我便是这样度过了1862年的冬天与1863年的春天。
后来，我受雇为几位商人护送一支小型货运大车队前往丹佛。在同年9月抵达丹佛时，我收到了一封家信，信中说我母亲病危，恐已不久于人世。我日夜兼程赶回家中，发现她已病入膏肓。她的病情日渐恶化，最终在1863年11月22日溘然长逝。一位慈爱仁厚、高贵英勇、善良忠诚的伟大母亲就这样离我们而去。我对她的至深爱戴超越了世间所有人，读过这些回忆录的人绝不会对此抱有丝毫怀疑。
在此不幸变故之前，我的姐姐朱莉娅（Julia）已嫁给了一位名叫J·A·古德曼（J. A. Goodman）的绅士；由于母亲生前极愿孩子们不要骨肉分离，他们夫妇便搬到我们家中居住并照料弟弟妹妹。古德曼先生成为了尚未成年的弟妹们的法定监护人。

===Original===

I soon left the home now rendered gloomy by the absence of her whom I had so tenderly loved, and going to Leavenworth I entered upon a dissolute and reckless life﻿—to my shame be it said﻿—and associated with gamblers, drunkards, and bad characters generally. I continued my dissipation about two months, and was becoming a very “hard case.” About this time the Seventh Kansas regiment, known as “Jennison’s Jay-hawkers,” returned from the war, and re-enlisted and reorganized as veterans. Among them I met quite a number of my old comrades and neighbors, who tried to induce me to enlist and go south with them. I had no idea of doing anything of the kind; but one day, after having been under the influence of bad whisky, I awoke to find myself a soldier in the Seventh Kansas. I did not remember how or when I had enlisted, but I saw I was in for it, and that it would not do for me to endeavor to back out.
In the spring of 1864 the regiment was ordered to Tennessee, and we got into Memphis just about the time that General Sturgis was so badly whipped by General Forrest. General A. J. Smith reorganized the army to operate against Forrest, and after marching to Tupalo, Mississippi, we had an engagement with him and defeated him. This kind of fighting was all new to me, being entirely different from any in which I had ever before engaged. I soon became a noncommissioned officer, and was put on detached service as a scout.
After skirmishing around the country with the rest of the army for some little time, our regiment returned to Memphis, but was immediately ordered to Cape Girardeau, in Missouri, as a confederate force under General Price was then raiding that state. The command of which my regiment was a part hurried to the front to intercept Price, and our first fight with him occurred at Pilot Knob. From that time for nearly six weeks we fought or skirmished every day.
I was still acting as a scout, when one day I rode ahead of the command, some considerable distance, to pick up all possible information concerning Price’s movements. I was dressed in gray clothes, or Missouri jeans, and on riding up to a farmhouse and entering, I saw a man, also dressed in gray costume, sitting at a table eating bread and milk. He looked up as I entered, and startled me by saying:
“You little rascal, what are you doing in those ‘secesh’ clothes?”

===Chinese===

失去了深爱的母亲，昔日温馨的家如今变得凄凉压抑，不久我便离开了家门前往莱文沃思。令人汗颜的是，我在那里陷入了一段纸醉金迷、放浪形骸的堕落生活，整日与赌徒、酒鬼以及各类三教九流的无赖之徒厮混在一起。这种放纵荒唐的生活持续了大约两个月，我正迅速沦为一个不可救药的“浑蛋混混”。就在此时，被誉为“詹尼森兜头鸟”（Jennison’s Jay-hawkers）的堪萨斯第七骑兵团从前线征战归来，作为老兵团重新招募并重组部队。在他们中间我偶遇了许多昔日的同袍与邻里故旧，他们力劝我入伍参军，跟随他们一同开赴南方作战。我原本毫无从军的打算；但有一天，在劣质威士忌的酒精麻醉下人事不省，等我清醒过来时，惊愕地发现自己竟已成了堪萨斯第七团的一名正式士兵。我全然记不得自己究竟是何时、又是如何签下的入伍契约，但我明白自己这回是彻底陷进去了，绝不可能再打退堂鼓。
1864年春，该团奉命开赴田纳西州。在我们进驻孟菲斯之际，恰逢斯图尔吉斯将军（General Sturgis）惨败于南方名将福雷斯特将军（General Forrest）手下。A·J·史密斯将军（General A. J. Smith）重新整编大军展开针对福雷斯特的反击行动；在进军密西西比州的图珀洛（Tupelo）后，我们与福雷斯特展开恶战并一举将其击溃。这种大规模正规军会战对我而言全然陌生，与我以往经历过的任何战斗都截然不同。不久我升任士官，并被抽调担任特遣侦察兵。
随大部队在当地展开了一阵围剿清剿后，我们团班师返回孟菲斯，但随即又接到紧急调令开赴密苏里州的吉拉多角（Cape Girardeau），因为此时普赖斯将军（General Price）率领的南军部队正席卷突袭该州。我所属的部队日夜兼程赶往前线拦截普赖斯，我们与他的首场血战在领航峰（Pilot Knob）爆发。自那之后近6个星期的时间里，我们几乎每天都在交火或发生激烈前哨战。
我当时依然充当尖兵侦察员。一天，我只身一人策马在部队前方很远处侦察前行，试图搜集有关普赖斯部动向的一切情报。当时我身着一身灰色便服，也就是密苏里粗斜纹粗布衣裳；当我策马来到一处农庄并推门而入时，赫然看见屋里坐着一名同样身着灰衣的汉子，正坐在桌旁就着牛奶吃面包。我进门时他抬起头来，脱口而出的一句话吓了我一跳：
“你这个小兔崽子，身上穿着这身‘南部分裂分子’的行头是打算干什么？”

===Original===

Judge of my surprise when I recognized in the stranger my old friend and partner, Wild Bill, disguised as a Confederate officer.
“I ask you the same question, sir,” said I without the least hesitation.
“Hush! sit down and have some bread and milk, and we’ll talk it all over afterwards,” said he.
I accepted the invitation and partook of the refreshments. Wild Bill paid the woman of the house, and we went out to the gate where my horse was standing.
“Billy, my boy,” said he, “I am mighty glad to see you. I haven’t seen or heard of you since we got busted on that St. Louis’ horse-race.”
“What are you doing out here?” I asked.
“I am a scout under General McNiel. For the last few days I have been with General Marmaduke’s division of Price’s army, in disguise as a southern officer from Texas, as you see me now,” said he.
“That’s exactly the kind of business that I am out on today,” said I; “and I want to get some information concerning Price’s movements.”
“I’ll give you all that I have;” and he then went on and told me all that he knew regarding Price’s intentions, and the number and condition of his men. He then asked about my mother, and when he learned that she was dead he was greatly surprised and grieved; he thought a great deal of her, for she had treated him almost as one of her own children. He finally took out a package, which he had concealed about his person, and handing it to me he said:
“Here are some letters which I want you to give to General McNiel.”
“All right,” said I as I took them, “but where will I meet you again?”
“Never mind that,” he replied; “I am getting so much valuable information that I propose to stay a little while longer in this disguise.” Thereupon we shook hands and parted.
It is not necessary to say much concerning Price’s raid in general, as that event is a matter of recorded history. I am only relating the incidents in which I was personally interested either as one of the actors or as an observer.
Another interesting and I may say exciting episode happened to me a day or two after my unexpected meeting with Wild Bill. I was riding with the advance guard of our army, and wishing a drink of water, I stopped at a farmhouse. There were no men about the premises, and no one excepting a very fine and intellectual looking lady and her two daughters.

===Chinese===

不难想象我有多么震惊——我立刻认出眼前这位不速之客不是别人，正是我昔日的老友兼老搭档“狂野比尔”（Wild Bill），他此刻正伪装成一名南部邦联军官。
“我也正想问您同样的问题呢，先生。”我毫不迟疑地反驳道。
“嘘！快坐下吃点面包喝口牛奶，待会儿咱们再详谈。”他压低声音说道。
我欣然落座，一同享用了这顿便餐。狂野比尔掏钱付给了女主人，随后我们俩一同走到屋外我的马匹拴着的院门口。
“比利，我的好兄弟，”他说，“真高兴能见到你。自从咱们在圣路易斯那场赛马赌局上输得精光之后，我就再也没听过你的音讯了。”
“你跑到这荒郊野岭来干什么？”我好奇地问。
“我现在是麦克尼尔将军（General McNiel）手下的侦察兵。过去这几天，我一直伪装成一名来自得克萨斯的南军军官潜伏在普赖斯大军的马默杜克将军（General Marmaduke）师部里，正如你现在看到的模样。”他答道。
“我今天执行的任务恰好也是这一路，”我说，“正想打探普赖斯部的兵力调动情报呢。”
“我把我掌握的全都交给你。”接着他竹筒倒豆子般将自己所知晓的有关普赖斯全盘意图、部队兵力与补给状况的情报一五一十告诉了我。随后他问起我母亲的近况，当听说她老人家已经故去时，他震惊万分、悲痛难抑；他生前对我母亲无比敬重爱戴，因为母亲待他几乎就像亲生骨肉一般。最后，他从贴身衣物里摸出一个密件包递到我手中，嘱咐道：
“这里有几封密信，务必亲手交给麦克尼尔将军。”
“包在我身上，”我接过信件说道，“但咱们下回在哪儿碰头？”
“这个先别操心，”他回答，“我正在源源不断获取极其珍贵的核心情报，我打算顶着这身伪装再多潜伏一阵子。”说完，我们互道珍重握手作别。
关于普赖斯突袭战役的全局走向无需在此赘述，正史中早已记载详实。我在此仅仅记录自己作为亲历者或目击者直接卷入的生动片段。
与狂野比尔意外重逢一两天后，我又经历了一场堪称惊心动魄的难忘轶事。当时我正随我军先遣警戒前哨向前推进，口渴难耐想找口水喝，便在路旁一座农舍前勒马停步。宅子里没有任何成年男子，只有一位举止端庄文雅的贵妇和她的两个女儿。

===Original===

They seemed to be almost frightened to death at seeing me﻿—a “yank”﻿—appear before them. I quieted their fears somewhat, and the mother then asked me how far back the army was. When I told her it would be along shortly, she expressed her fears that they would take everything on the premises. They set me out a lunch and treated me rather kindly, so that I really began to sympathize with them; for I knew that the soldiers would ransack their house and confiscate everything they could lay their hands on. At last I resolved to do what I could to protect them. After the generals and the staff officers had passed by, I took it upon myself to be a sentry over the house. When the command came along some of the men rushed up with the intention of entering the place and carrying off all the desirable plunder possible, and then tearing and breaking everything to pieces, as they usually did along the line of march.
“Halt!” I shouted; “I have been placed here by the commanding officer as a guard over this house, and no man must enter it.”
This stopped the first squad; and seeing that my plan was a success, I remained at my post during the passage of the entire command and kept out all intruders.
It seemed as if the ladies could not thank me sufficiently for the protection I had afforded them. They were perfectly aware of the fact that I had acted without orders and entirely on my own responsibility, and therefore they felt the more grateful. They urgently invited me to remain a little while longer and partake of an excellent dinner which they said they were preparing for me. I was pretty hungry about that time, as our rations had been rather slim of late, and a good dinner was a temptation I could not withstand, especially as it was to be served up by such elegant ladies. While I was eating the meal, I was most agreeably entertained by the young ladies, and before I had finished it the last of the rearguard must have been at least two miles from the house.
Suddenly three men entered the room, and I looked up and saw three double-barreled shotguns leveled straight at me. Before I could speak, however, the mother and her daughters sprang between the men and me.
“Father! Boys! Lower your guns! You must not shoot this man,” and similar exclamations, were the cry of all three.

===Chinese===

一见到我——一个货真价实的“联邦北方佬”（yank）——突兀地闯入院中，母女三人吓得几乎魂飞魄散。我好言安抚，平复了她们的恐慌情绪；随后那位母亲颤声询问我北方大军距此还有多远。当我告诉她大部队片刻即至时，她满面愁容地表达了自己的恐惧，生怕大军一到会将庄园里的一切洗劫一空。她们端出了一顿便餐招待我，态度颇为温和友善，以至于我心底油然而生同情之意；因为我深知大队行军官兵必然会把整座宅子翻个底朝天，把目所能及的物资抢夺殆尽。最终我横下一条心，决定竭尽所能护她们周全。等各路将军及其幕僚参谋们策马走过之后，我擅自做主在宅院门口站起岗来充当卫兵。当大部队后续行军纵队开过来时，果然有几拨士兵气势汹汹地冲上前来，企图闯入宅院搜刮一切能带走的战利品，并像他们沿途素来所做的那样把一切砸得稀烂。
“站住！”我厉声断喝，“我奉最高指挥官严令在此驻守警戒此宅，任何人不得擅入一步！”
这声怒喝当场喝退了第一批散兵游勇；眼见狐假虎威的计策奏效，我在整个大军纵队过境期间始终坚守在哨位上，将所有企图趁火打劫的闯入者尽数挡在大门之外。
对于我施以的庇护之恩，母女几人感激涕零、千恩万谢。她们心知肚明我完全是在没有上级命令的情况下擅自承担风险仗义相助，因此心中的感激更深一层。她们极力挽留我多盘桓片刻，盛情邀请我享用一顿她们特意为我精心烹制的丰盛大餐。当时我饥肠辘辘，因为部队近来口粮供给十分匮乏，面对如此丰盛可口的美食与几位优雅女士的殷勤款待，这等诱惑我委实难以抗拒。用餐期间，几位年轻小姐谈笑风生将我招待得极其周到惬意，等我吃完这顿大餐时，后卫部队的殿后尾巴恐怕早已开拔开出宅院两英里开外了。
冷不防，房门砰的一声被推开，三个大汉杀气腾腾闯入房中；我猛一抬头，赫然看见三支黑洞洞的双管猎枪正死死瞄准着我的胸膛。还没等我来得及拔枪或开口，那位母亲和两个女儿便发疯般飞身扑了上来，用血肉之躯死死挡在我与枪口之间。
“爹！弟弟！快把枪放下！千万不能开枪打这位先生！”母女三人惊恐万状地齐声尖叫起来。

===Original===

The guns were lowered, and then the men, who were the father and brothers of the young ladies, were informed of what I had done for them. It appeared that they had been concealed in the woods nearby while the army was passing, and on coming into the house and finding a Yankee there, they determined to shoot him. Upon learning the facts, the old man extended his hand to me, saying:
“I would not harm a hair of your head for the world; but it is best that you stay here no longer, as your command is some distance from here now, and you might be cut off by bushwhackers before reaching it.”
Bidding them all goodbye, and with many thanks from the mother and daughters, I mounted my horse and soon overtook the column, happy in the thought that I had done a good deed, and with no regrets that I had saved from pillage and destruction the home and property of a confederate and his family.
Our command kept crowding against Price and his army until they were pushed into the vicinity of Kansas City, where their further advance was checked by United States troops from Kansas; and then was begun their memorable and extraordinary retreat back into Kansas.
While both armies were drawn up in skirmish line near Fort Scott, Kansas, two men on horseback were seen rapidly leaving the Confederate lines, and suddenly they made a dash towards us. Instantly quick volleys were discharged from the Confederates, who also began a pursuit, and some five hundred shots were fired at the flying men. It was evident that they were trying to reach our lines, but when within about a quarter of a mile of us, one of them fell from his horse to rise no more. He had been fatally shot. His companion galloped on unhurt, and seven companies of our regiment charged out and met him, and checked his pursuers. The fugitive was dressed in Confederate uniform, and as he rode into our lines I recognized him as Wild Bill, the Union scout. He immediately sought Generals Pleasanton and McNiel, with whom he held a consultation. He told them that although Price made a bold showing on the front, by bringing all his men into view, yet he was really a great deal weaker than the appearance of his lines would indicate; and that he was then trying to cross a difficult stream four miles from Fort Scott.

===Chinese===

枪口终于垂了下来，三名男子——正是两位小姐的父亲与兄弟——随后听闻了我刚才冒死护宅的义举。原来，大军过境时他们一直荷枪实弹潜伏在邻近的密林中，等大军走后进屋猛然撞见一名落单的北方兵，便决意立刻开枪取其性命。获悉原委之后，老父亲主动走上前来向我伸出双手，动情地说道：
“就算给我全世界，我也绝不会动您一根汗毛；但您眼下绝不能在此久留了，贵军大部队早已走远，您孤身一人极可能会在追赶归队途中遭到丛林游击队的截杀。”
在母女们千恩万谢的感激声中，我向全家人拱手道别，跃上马背飞驰而去，很快便追赶上了大军行军纵队。一想到自己行了一桩善事，拯救了一个南方邦联家庭的家园与财产免遭劫掠和焚毁，我心头满溢着坦然与欣慰，毫无半分悔意。
我军对普赖斯所部的合围压迫持续步步紧逼，直至将残敌一路压缩赶到了堪萨斯城（Kansas City）郊外，敌人的进一步进犯遭到驻扎堪萨斯各要塞的联邦主力严密遏阻；随后，南军便拉开了其历史上那场惊心动魄、狼狈万状的大溃退序幕，一路仓皇退回堪萨斯境内。
就在两军在堪萨斯州的斯科特堡（Fort Scott）附近拉开散兵线对峙之际，只见南军防线中突然飞驰出两名策马狂奔的骑手，风驰电掣般径直朝我们北方阵营冲杀过来。南军阵地顿时枪声大作，密集的排枪如暴雨般倾泻而出，大队南军骑兵紧随其后穷追不舍，足有500多发子弹呼啸射向这两名逃亡者。显而易见他们正拼死企图冲入我军阵地，但当冲到距离我方防线仅约四分之一英里处时，其中一人中弹栽下马鞍，再也没能站起来，当场气绝身亡。他的同伴则毫发无伤地策马继续狂飙，我团7个连队当即纵马杀出防线迎上前去，猛烈开火阻击打退了追敌。这位逃脱死劫的骑手身穿南军制服，等他策马冲入我军战线时，我一眼认出他正是联邦神级侦察兵狂野比尔。他立刻直奔普莱森顿将军（General Pleasanton）与麦克尼尔将军的大营召开紧急军情密商。他向将军们报告：普赖斯虽在正面战场上倾巢而出摆出一副虚张声势的进攻姿态，但其实际战力远比表面防线虚弱得多；此刻敌人正企图在距离斯科特堡4英里处抢渡一条水流湍急的难渡河流。

===Original===

It was late in the afternoon, but General Pleasanton immediately ordered an advance, and we charged in full force upon the rear of Price’s army, and drove it before us for two hours.
If Wild Bill could have made his successful dash into our lines earlier in the day, the attack would have been made sooner, and greater results might have been expected. The Confederates had suspected him of being a spy for two or three days, and had watched him too closely to allow an opportunity to get away from them sooner. His unfortunate companion who had been shot, was a scout from Springfield, Missouri, whose name I cannot now remember.
From this time on, Wild Bill and myself continued to scout together until Price’s army was driven south of the Arkansas River and the pursuit abandoned. We then returned to Springfield, Missouri, for a rest and for supplies, and Wild Bill and myself spent two weeks there in “having a jolly good time,” as some people would express it.

===Chinese===

此时天色已晚，但普莱森顿将军当机立断下达全线出击命令，我军以排山倒海之势猛扑普赖斯军后卫部队，将溃败的敌军一路狂追猛打整整砍杀了两个小时。
倘若狂野比尔能在当天更早些时候成功突围杀入我方阵线，我军的猛攻必定能提前打响，所取得的战果也必然会辉煌得多。南军早在两三天前便已怀疑他是间谍，对他实施了严密盯防，以至于他根本找不到提早脱身的机会。那位不幸中弹牺牲的同伴是来自密苏里州斯普林菲尔德（Springfield）的一名侦察兵，他的名字我如今已记不起来了。
自那一刻起，狂野比尔与我再度携手搭档，共同担任前锋侦察，直至普赖斯大军被彻底驱逐到阿肯色河以南、全军追击行动告一段落为止。随后我们奉命返回密苏里州的斯普林菲尔德休整并补充给养，狂野比尔与我在那里舒舒服服度过了两周，正如俗话所说，“好好痛快狂欢了一番”。

## XIX A Tough Time / 第十九章 艰苦岁月
===Original===

General Carr soon received orders from General Sheridan that he was to make a winter’s campaign in the Canadian River country, and that we were to proceed to Fort Lyon, on the Arkansas River, in Colorado, and there fit out for the expedition. Leaving Fort Wallace in November, 1868, we arrived at Fort Lyon in the latter part of the month, and outfitted for the coming expedition.
General Penrose had left this post three weeks previously with a command of some three hundred men. He had taken no wagons with him and his supply train was composed only of pack mules. General Carr was ordered to follow with supplies on his trail and overtake him as soon as possible. I was particularly anxious to catch up with Penrose’s command, as my old friend Wild Bill was among his scouts. We followed the trail very easily for the first three days, and then we were caught in Freeze-Out canyon by a fearful snow storm, which compelled us to go into camp for a day. The ground now being covered with snow, we found that it would be almost impossible to follow Penrose’s trail any further, especially as he had left no sign to indicate the direction he was going. General Carr sent for me and said that as it was very important that we should not lose the trail, he wished that I would take some scouts with me, and while the command remained in camp, push on as far as possible and see if I could not discover some traces of Penrose or where he had camped at any time.
Accompanied by four men I started out in the blinding snow storm, taking a southerly direction. We rode twenty-four miles, and upon reaching a tributary of the Cimarron, we scouted up and down the stream for a few miles and finally found one of Penrose’s old camps. It was now late in the afternoon, and as the command would come up the next day, it was not necessary for all of us to return with the information to General Carr. So riding down into a sheltered place in a bend of the creek, we built a fire and broiled some venison from a deer which we had shot during the day, and after eating a substantial meal, I left the four men there, while I returned to bring up the troops.
It was eleven o’clock at night when I got back to the camp. A light was still burning in the General’s tent, he having remained awake, anxiously awaiting my return.

===Chinese===

卡尔将军（General Carr）很快接到了谢里丹将军（General Sheridan）下达的命令，要求他在加拿大河流域（Canadian River）发起一场冬季战役，全军须即刻开拔前往科罗拉多州阿肯色河畔的里昂堡（Fort Lyon），在那里配齐远征所需的全部装备补给。1868年11月，我们告别沃拉斯堡（Fort Wallace），于当月下旬顺利进抵里昂堡，为即将打响的远征做好了全面装配。
大约三周之前，彭罗斯将军（General Penrose）已率领约300人的部队开拔离开了该哨所。由于他并未携带任何货运大车，整个辎重给养队全由驮骡组成。卡尔将军奉命押运大批给养沿其行军路线一路追赶，尽速与其会合。我本人对追赶彭罗斯部格外急切，因为我的老友“狂野比尔”（Wild Bill）正在他的侦察队中效力。前三天我们追踪痕迹十分顺畅，但随后部队在冻死峡谷（Freeze-Out Canyon）遭遇了一场骇人的狂暴暴风雪，全军被迫就地宿营休整了一天。由于大地被皑皑白雪彻底覆盖，大家发现要想继续寻觅彭罗斯的足迹几乎已化为不可能，尤其是他沿途并未留下任何指示行进方向的标志记号。卡尔将军派人传唤我，面授机宜说绝不能跟丢了目标，希望我挑选几名侦察兵，趁主力部队扎营待命之际尽可能向前深入刺探，看看能否搜寻到彭罗斯留下的蛛丝马迹或以往宿营的旧址。
在刺骨盲目的暴风雪中，我带着4名部下向南顶风冒雪开拔。我们一口气策马疾驰了24英里，在抵达锡马龙河（Cimarron）的一条支流后，我们沿着河谷上下搜寻了数英里，终于发现了彭罗斯部队曾经扎营的一处旧营地。此时已近傍晚，鉴于大部队次日便会开拔跟进，我们大可不必全员跑回去向卡尔将军复命。于是我们策马下到小溪弯道处一处避风的山坳，点燃篝火，烤食白天击毙的一头鹿的鹿肉；在美美饱餐了一顿扎实的野味后，我留下那4名弟兄在原地待命，自己则只身策马冒雪返回，引导大部队开拔。
等我赶回大营时，已经是深夜11点钟了。将军的大帐中依旧亮着灯火，他始终未曾入睡，焦急万分地等候着我的归来。

===Original===

He was glad to see me, and was overjoyed at the information I brought, for he had great fears concerning the safety of General Penrose. He roused up his cook and ordered him to get me a good hot supper, all of which I greatly appreciated. I passed the night in the General’s tent, and next morning rose refreshed and prepared for a big day’s work.
The command took up its march next day for the Cimarron, and had a hard tramp of it on account of the snow having drifted to a great depth in many of the ravines, and in some places the teamsters had to shovel their way through. We arrived at the Cimarron at sundown, and went into a nice warm camp. Upon looking around next morning, we found that Penrose, having been unencumbered by wagons, had kept on the west side of the Cimarron, and the country was so rough that it was impossible for us to stay on his trail with our wagons; but knowing that he would certainly follow down the river, General Carr concluded to take the best wagon route along the stream, which I discovered to be on the east side. Before we could make any headway with our wagon train we had to leave the river and get out on the divide. We were very fortunate that day in finding a splendid road for some distance, until we were all at once brought up standing on a high tableland, overlooking a beautiful winding creek that lay far below us in the valley. The question that troubled us, was, how we were to get the wagons down. We were now in the foothills of the Rattoon Mountains, and the bluff we were on was very steep.
“Cody, we’re in a nice fix now,” said General Carr.
“Oh, that’s nothing,” was my reply.
“But you can never take the train down,” said he.
“Never you mind the train, General. You say you are looking for a good camp. How does that beautiful spot down in the valley suit you?” I asked him.
“That will do. I can easily descend with the cavalry, but how to get the wagons down there is a puzzler to me,” said he.
“By the time you’ve located your camp, your wagons shall be there,” said I.
“All right, Cody, I’ll leave it to you, as you seem to want to be boss,” replied he pleasantly. He at once ordered the command to dismount and lead the horses down the mountainside. The wagon train was a mile in the rear, and when it came up, one of the drivers asked: “How are we going down there?”
“Run down, slide down or fall down﻿—any way to get down,” said I.

===Chinese===

见到我平安归来，将军喜出望外；听闻我带回的确切消息，他更是欣喜若狂，因为长久以来他一直对彭罗斯将军一行的安危深感担忧。他当即叫醒炊事兵，命他为我备上一份热气腾腾的丰盛宵夜，对此我感激涕零。当晚我在将军的大帐中安睡，次日清晨醒来神清气爽，做好了大干一场的准备。
次日部队拔营向锡马龙河进发。由于积雪在许多峡谷深沟中堆积得极厚，行军变得异常艰难，有些地段大车车夫不得不挥锹铲雪开路。日落时分我们终于赶到了锡马龙河畔，扎下了一座避风暖和的好营地。次日清晨仔细勘察周围地形后，我们发现由于彭罗斯没有笨重大车的拖累，一直沿着锡马龙河西岸行军；而西岸地形崎岖险峻、怪石嶙峋，我们的大车辎重队根本无法沿着他的足迹前行。但考虑到他必然会沿着河流顺流而下，卡尔将军决定沿着河谷挑选一条最适宜大车通行的路线前进，而我探明这条生路正位于河流东岸。在辎重队取得任何实质性进展之前，我们必须暂时离开河谷，攀登上一道分水岭。当天我们极为幸运，在一段相当长的路程上遇到了一条平坦坦荡的极佳道路，直到全军突然在一处高耸陡峭的台地边缘齐刷刷停住了脚步。在深陷下方的河谷中，一条优美曲折的小溪蜿蜒流淌，尽收眼底。此刻横亘在众人面前的棘手难题是：大车究竟该如何下到悬崖下方？我们此刻正处在拉通山脉（Rattoon Mountains）的山麓丘陵地带，脚下的断崖陡峭得几乎令人眩晕。
“科迪，这回咱们可真是碰上大麻烦了。”卡尔将军苦笑着叹道。
“哎呀，这算不了什么大事。”我平静地答道。
“可你无论如何也把大车弄不下去啊。”他说。
“将军，辎重大车的事您犯不着操心。您刚才说想找个理想的宿营地，您看谷底那片风景如画的风水宝地合不合您的心意？”我反问他。
“那地方再好不过了。我和骑兵下山轻而易举，但大车怎么下得去，我可真是一筹莫展。”他说。
“等您选好扎营的具体地界，您的大车一准儿稳稳停在那儿等您。”我说。
“那好，科迪，全权交给你处置了，反正我看你总是想凡事自己拿大主意。”将军心情愉快地答道。他随即下令全军翻身下马，牵着战马徒步下山。货运大车队当时正落在后方1英里处，等车队赶到悬崖边时，一名车夫惊恐地问道：“咱们到底该怎么下到下面去啊？”
“跑下去、滑下去，或者摔下去——反正能下去就行！”我随口打趣道。

===Original===

“We never can do it; it’s too steep; the wagons will run over the mules,” said another wagon master.
“I guess not; the mules have got to keep out of the way,” was my reply.
Telling Wilson, the chief wagon master, to bring on his mess-wagon, which was at the head of the train, I said I would try the experiment at least. Wilson drove the team and wagon to the brink of the hill, and following my directions he brought out some extra chains with which we locked both wheels on each side, and then rough-locked them. We then started the wagon down the hill. The wheel-horses﻿—or rather the wheel-mules﻿—were good on the hold-back, and we got along finely until we nearly reached the bottom, when the wagon crowded the mules so hard that they started on a run and galloped down into the valley and to the place where General Carr had located his camp. Three other wagons immediately followed in the same way, and in half an hour every wagon was in camp, without the least accident having occurred. It was indeed an exciting sight to see the six-mule teams come straight down the mountain and finally break into a full run. At times it looked as if the wagons would turn a somersault and land on the mules.
This proved to be a lucky march for us as far as gaining on Penrose was concerned, for the route he had taken on the west side of the stream turned out to be a bad one, and we went with our immense wagon train as far in one day as Penrose had in seven. His command had marched on to a plateau or high tableland so steep, that not even a pack mule could descend it, and he was obliged to retrace his steps a long ways, thus losing three days time as we afterwards learned.
While in this camp we had a lively turkey hunt. The trees along the banks of the stream were literally alive with wild turkeys, and after unsaddling the horses between two and three hundred soldiers surrounded a grove of timber and had a grand turkey roundup, killing four or five hundred of the birds, with guns, clubs and stones. Of course, we had turkey in every style after this hunt﻿—roast turkey, boiled turkey, fried turkey, “turkey on toast,” and so on; and we appropriately called this place Camp Turkey.
From this point on, for several days, we had no trouble in following Penrose’s trail, which led us in a southeasterly direction towards the Canadian River. No Indians were seen, nor any signs of them found.

===Chinese===

“咱们绝不可能办得到；坡太陡了，大车非得从后头把骡子活活碾死不可！”另一位车队工头惊呼道。
“我看未必；骡子自己懂得挪步躲开。”我答道。
我吩咐总车队工头威尔逊（Wilson）把排在车队最前面的伙食大车赶上前来，说不管怎样我都要亲自拿它做个试验。威尔逊战战兢兢将骡马和大车赶到悬崖边缘，按照我的吩咐，他找来几根备用粗铁链，我们把大车两侧的前后车轮全都死死锁住，随后又打上了防滑粗锁。接着，我们推动大车顺着山坡向下放行。充当后套车马——确切地说是挽骡——的牲口极善于拉紧挽具往后顶力制动，下坡过程起初异常平稳顺遂，直到快要接近谷底时，沉重的大车惯性逼得骡群承受不住，骡子索性四蹄如飞狂奔起来，拖着大车一路飞驰冲入河谷，径直冲到了卡尔将军选定的宿营地。另外三辆大车立刻如法炮制紧随其后滑下山坡，短短半小时之内，全队每一辆大车都完好无损地稳稳扎进了营地，连一丁点磕碰意外都未曾发生。目睹由6匹骡子牵引的大车直挺挺冲下陡峭山崖并最终全速狂奔冲刺，那景象着实令人屏息惊心；有好几次，沉重的大车看起来险些就要凌空翻个筋斗，直接砸在骡背上。
事实证明，这次大胆的强行下坡对我们尽快追赶彭罗斯而言是一招神来之笔。他在西岸挑选的路线被证明是一条绝路，而我们带着庞大的大车队仅仅走了一天的路程，就顶得上彭罗斯苦苦跋涉七天的进展。他的人马此前误入了一处险峻高耸的石质高原台地，陡峭得连驮骡都无法涉险下山，导致他不得不原路折返极长一段距离，后来我们得知这一失误白白耽误了他整整三天宝贵时间。
在这处营地驻扎期间，我们进行了一场痛快淋漓的围猎野火鸡大赛。溪流两岸的树丛里密密麻麻栖息着数不清的野生火鸡；官兵们卸下马鞍后，两三百名士兵自发将一片树林团团包围，展开了一场声势浩大的火鸡大围捕，大伙动用步枪、棍棒和石块，总共当场击毙了四五百只大鸟。不用说，这场围猎之后我们的餐桌上变着花样全是火鸡大餐——烤火鸡、炖火鸡、炸火鸡、“吐司火鸡”，不一而足；我们顺理成章地将这个营地命名为“火鸡营”（Camp Turkey）。
自此往后的数日里，我们顺畅地追踪着彭罗斯的足迹，痕迹引领着我们一路向东南方向朝加拿大河挺进。沿途既未见半个印第安人的踪影，也没发现任何敌踪迹象。

===Original===

One day, while riding in advance of the command, down San Francisco Creek, I heard someone calling my name from a little bunch of willow brush on the opposite bank, and, upon looking closely at the spot, I saw a negro.
“Sakes alive! Massa Bill, am dat you?” asked the man, whom I recognized as one of the colored soldiers of the Tenth Cavalry. I next heard him say to someone in the brush: “Come out o’ heah. Dar’s Massa Buffalo Bill.” Then he sang out, “Massa Bill, is you got any hawd tack?”
“Nary a hard tack; but the wagons will be along presently, and then you can get all you want,” said I.
“Dat’s de best news I’se heerd foah sixteen long days, Massa Bill,” said he. “Where’s your command? Where’s General Penrose?” I asked.
“I dunno,” said the darkey; “we got lost, and we’s been a starvin’ eber since.”
By this time two other negroes had emerged from their place of concealment. They had deserted Penrose’s command﻿—which was out of rations and nearly in a starving condition﻿—and were trying to make their way back to Fort Lyon. General Carr concluded, from what they could tell him, that General Penrose was somewhere on Polladora Creek; but we could not learn anything definite from the starved “mokes,” for they knew not where they were themselves.
Having learned that General Penrose’s troops were in such bad shape, General Carr ordered Major Brown to start out the next morning with two companies of cavalry and fifty pack-mules loaded with provisions, and to make all possible speed to reach and relieve the suffering soldiers. I accompanied this detachment, and on the third day out we found the half-famished soldiers camped on the Polladora. The camp presented a pitiful sight, indeed. For over two weeks the men had had only quarter rations, and were now nearly starved to death. Over two hundred horses and mules were lying dead, having died from fatigue and starvation. General Penrose, having feared that General Carr would not find him, had sent back a company of the Seventh Cavalry to Fort Lyon for supplies; but no word as yet had been heard from them. The rations which Major Brown brought to the command came none too soon, and were the means of saving many a life.
About the first man I saw after reaching the camp was my old, true and tried friend, Wild Bill. That night we had a jolly reunion around the campfires.

===Chinese===

有一天，当我策马沿着旧金山溪（San Francisco Creek）在大部队前方巡查侦察时，忽然听见对岸一小丛柳树灌木丛里有人在大声呼喊我的名字。定睛仔细一瞧，我从灌木枝叶间辨认出了一名黑人士兵。
“老天爷开眼啊！比尔大爷，真……真的是您老人家吗？”那个汉子颤声问道。我一眼认出他是第十骑兵团的一名黑人士兵。紧接着我听见他扭头冲灌木丛里的同伴喊道：“快滚出来吧！是野牛比尔（Buffalo Bill）大爷到啦！”接着他又扯开破锣嗓子朝我嚷嚷：“比尔大爷，您兜里揣着压缩硬饼干（hard tack）没有啊？”
“一块干粮都没有；不过后头的大车队马上就到，待会儿管够让你们吃到饱。”我回答道。
“这可是我整整十六个苦日子以来听到的顶天大好消息了，比尔大爷！”他感慨万千地叫道。“你们的大部队在哪儿？彭罗斯将军现在人在哪儿？”我急切地打探道。
“俺……俺实在说不上来啊，”黑人苦笑着嘟囔，“咱们大伙彻底走迷了路，打那以后就一直饿得前胸贴后背，天天都在挨饿受冻呢。”
说话间，另外两个黑人也战战兢兢从藏身处钻了出来。他们由于部队给养断绝、几乎全军陷入濒死饥荒，便擅自脱离了彭罗斯的队伍，正一路摸索着企图逃回里昂堡。卡尔将军综合他们提供的支离破碎的情报推断，彭罗斯将军大体应该受困在波利亚多拉溪（Polladora Creek）一带；但这帮饿得神志不清的“黑大个”根本说不出任何准信，因为他们自己连究竟身在何方都浑然不知。
得知彭罗斯将军麾下的官兵已沦落到如此悲惨绝境，卡尔将军当机立断，责令布朗少校（Major Brown）次日清晨率领两个骑兵连与50头驮满粮食给养的驮骡轻装急进，以最快速度赶往救援受难同袍。我奉命随这支救援特遣队同行，行军至第三天，我们终于在波利亚多拉溪畔找到了这群饿得奄奄一息的官兵。营地里的惨状着实令人触目惊心：半个多月以来，士兵们每天只能分到四分之一份口粮，此刻全军几乎快要饿殍遍野。200多匹战马与骡子因极度饥饿与劳累倒毙在地，陈尸荒野。此前由于深恐卡尔将军寻不到自己的下落，彭罗斯将军曾派第七骑兵团的一个连回返里昂堡求援，然而迄今为止音讯全无。布朗少校火线押运送达的军粮犹如雪中送炭，当场挽救了无数濒危将士的宝贵生命。
进驻营地后，我第一眼见到的熟人不是别人，正是我历经生死考验的至交挚友狂野比尔。当天夜里，我们在熊熊燃烧的营火旁举行了一场热烈欢腾的战友重逢聚会。

===Original===

General Carr, upon arriving with his force, took command of all the troops, he being the senior officer and ranking General Penrose. After selecting a good camp, he unloaded the wagons and sent them back to Fort Lyon for fresh supplies. He then picked out five hundred of the best men and horses, and, taking his pack-train with him, he started south for the Canadian River, distant about forty miles, leaving the rest of the troops at the supply camp.
I was ordered to accompany this expedition. We struck the south fork of the Canadian River, or Rio Colorado, at a point a few miles above the old adobe walls, which at one time had composed a fort, and was the place where Kit Carson once had a big Indian fight. We were now within twelve miles of a new supply depot, called Camp Evans, which had been established for the Third Cavalry and Evans’s Expedition from New Mexico. The scouts who had brought in this information also reported that they expected the arrival at Camp Evans of a bull-train from New Mexico with a large quantity of beer for the soldiers. This news was “pie” for Wild Bill and myself, and we determined to lie low for that beer outfit. That very evening it came along, and the beer that was destined for the soldiers at Camp Evans never reached its destination. It went straight down the thirsty throats of General Carr’s command. It appears that the Mexicans living near Fort Union had manufactured the beer, and were taking it through to Camp Evans to sell to the troops, but it struck a lively market without going so far. It was sold to our boys in pint cups, and as the weather was very cold we warmed the beer by putting the ends of our picket-pins heated red-hot into the cups. The result was one of the biggest beer jollifications I ever had the misfortune to attend.
One evening General Carr summoned me to his tent, and said he wished to send some scouts with dispatches to Camp Supply, which were to be forwarded from there to Sheridan. He ordered me to call the scouts together at once at his headquarters, and select the men who were to go. I asked him if I should not go myself, but he replied that he wished me to remain with the command, as he could not spare me. The distance to Camp Supply was about two hundred miles, and owing to the very cold weather it was anything but a pleasant trip. Consequently none of the scouts were anxious to undertake it.

===Chinese===

卡尔将军率主力部队赶到后，鉴于资历与军衔均在彭罗斯将军之上，便顺理成章接管了全军统帅权。在精挑细选了一处理想的大营后，他下令将所有大车卸空，打发车队返航里昂堡重新调运新鲜补给。随后他从全军精挑细选出500名最精悍的骑兵与良驹，携带着随军驮马队一路向南朝着大约40英里外的加拿大河进发，将其余部队留守在大营给养站。
我奉命随这支突击精锐同行。我们在旧土坯废墟堡垒（adobe walls）上游数英里处涉渡了加拿大河南岔（又称科罗拉多河）。那片废墟昔日曾是一座坚固的堡垒，正是大名鼎鼎的基特·卡森（Kit Carson）当年痛击印第安人爆发著名恶战的旧战场。此时我们距离一处名为埃文斯营（Camp Evans）的新建给养转运站仅有12英里远，该营地是专门为第三骑兵团以及来自新墨西哥州的埃文斯远征军设立的。带回这一情报的侦察兵还报告说，一支来自新墨西哥的大车牛车队预计即将抵运埃文斯营，车上载运了供驻军饮用的大批啤酒。这一喜讯对狂野比尔和我而言简直如同“天上掉馅饼”，我们俩当即商定在半道潜伏下来截胡这批甘霖。果不其然，就在当天傍晚，运酒车队摇摇晃晃开了过来，而原本注定送往埃文斯营给士兵享用的啤酒，最终却一滴也没能运抵目的地——它们全数灌进了卡尔将军麾下饥渴难耐的将士们的肚肠里。原来，这批啤酒是由居住在联合堡（Fort Union）附近的墨西哥人私酿的，原本打算运往埃文斯营高价卖给守军，谁料半道上便在我们的营地里撞上了一个无比火爆的现成大买家。啤酒按品脱大铁杯零卖给小伙子们，由于天寒地冻，我们把拴马桩的大铁钎在营火里烧得通红，往铁杯里一插加热啤酒饮用。其结果是引发了我平生不幸躬逢其盛的、规模最为放浪形骸的啤酒狂欢盛宴之一。
一天傍晚，卡尔将军将我传唤至指挥帐篷，表示他打算派遣几名侦察兵携紧急公文送往补给营（Camp Supply），再从那里加急转呈给谢里丹大本营。他命令我立刻在司令部召集全体侦察兵，挑出执行此任务的人选。我请示将军是否由我亲自带队前往，但他坚决表示希望我留在主力身边，因为行军打仗离不开我的襄助。前往补给营的路程足有200英里之遥，加之天气滴水成冰，沿途绝非一趟轻松惬意的美差；因此，侦察兵中没有一个人主动请缨甘愿涉险。

===Original===
