周六天刚蒙蒙亮，我们便骑马悄然进城，将坐骑安顿在著名的大象圈（Elephant Corral）牲畜栈房。我从好友埃德·切斯（Ed Chase）那里借得一间正对畜圈广场的临窗客房，随后便将这里作为严密监视的制高哨点。根本无需苦等太久，一名男子便策马晃晃悠悠踱进了大象圈——我一眼便认出他是昔日为我们军团效力的一名老驭手。此刻他胯下骑着的正是福布什中尉那头名震四方的竞速骡子，手里还牵着另一头官家公骡，那头骡子我同样认得一清二楚。它身上刚烙上了崭新的印记，在原本赫然醒目的“U.S.”军用烙印之上，被粗劣地叠加覆盖上了一个大大的“D.B.”字样。我伏在窗前耐心等待他的同伙现身，却久久不见人影，我暗自断定同伙必然正带着其余大批牲口潜伏隐匿在城外的秘密据点。
不一会儿，拍卖师便将福布什中尉的那头黑骡牵上了拍卖台公开竞价。我想，动手的千载难逢时机到了！我立刻分开群情耸动的叫价人群，径直走到那个叫卖牲口的男子跟前。他认出了我的面孔，惊恐万状企图夺路而逃，我疾步上前铁钳般一把死死扣住他的肩膀，冷笑道：“朋友，恐怕你今天非跟我走一趟不可了。你要是敢有半点反抗，我立马一枪崩了你。”他腰间别着两柄转轮手枪，我手脚麻利地将武器卸除缴械。随后我向拍卖师高声亮明身份，掏出自己身为合众国联邦便衣侦探的委任委任状向他以及一位闻讯围拢上来的警官展示，严令立刻叫停拍卖，申明这头骡子系盗窃所得的军用赃物，而眼下被我当场拿下的这个贼人名叫威廉斯（Williams）。
埋伏在附近的法利与格林见状立刻拔枪包抄上来，我们三人押解着这名囚犯并牵着两头骡子，一路将其转移押送到南普拉特河下游三英里开外的一处荒野密林。在那里，大家纷纷翻身下马，在繁茂的枝桠上架起绳索套索，厉声恫吓威廉斯称：限他在五分钟之内交代同谋的藏身之所，否则就把他当场吊死在树杈上。起初这厮还负隅顽抗，矢口否认自己有什么同伙或其他牲口；但当他看清我们面色冷峻、绝非虚声恫吓，若不“招供”大限一到必定命丧黄泉时，他终于彻底崩溃招架不住，颤抖着吐露其实他的“搭档”就潜伏在下游三英里外的一所废弃空木屋里。
我们马不停蹄依照指引直扑贼穴，刚远远望见那座木屋的轮廓，便惊喜地瞧见我们失窃的马群正悠闲地在房前草滩上啃噬青草。

===Original===

Just as we rode up to the door, another one of our old packers, whom I recognized as Bill Bevins, stepped to the front, and I covered him instantly with my rifle before he could draw his revolver. I ordered him to throw up his hands, and he obeyed the command. Green then disarmed him and brought him out. We looked through the house and found their saddles, packsaddles, blankets, overcoats, lariats and two Henry rifles, which we took possession of. The horses and mules we tied in a bunch, and with the whole outfit we returned to Denver, where we lodged Williams and Bevins in jail, in charge of my friend, Sheriff Edward Cook. The next day we took them out, and, tying each one on a mule, we struck out on our return trip to Fort Lyon.
At the hotel outside the city, where we had stopped on Thursday and Friday, we were joined by our man with the pack-mule. That night we camped on Cherry Creek, seventeen miles from Denver. The weather﻿—it being in April﻿—was cold and stormy, but we found a warm and cosy camping place in a bend of the creek. We made our beds in a row, with our feet towards the fire. The prisoners so far had appeared very docile, and had made no attempt to escape, and therefore I did not think it necessary to hobble them. We made them sleep on the inside, and it was so arranged that someone of us should be on guard all the time.
At about one o’clock in the night it began snowing, while I was watching. Shortly before three o’clock, Jack Farley, who was then on guard, and sitting on the foot of the bed, with his back to the prisoners, was kicked clear into the fire by Williams, and the next moment Bevins, who had got hold of his shoes﻿—which I had thought were out of his reach﻿—sprang up and jumped over the fire, and started on a run. I sent a shot after him as soon as I awoke sufficiently to comprehend what was taking place. Williams attempted to follow him, and as he did so, I whirled around and knocked him down with my revolver. Farley by this time had gathered himself out of the fire, and Green had started after Bevins, firing at him on the run; but the prisoner made his escape into the brush. In his flight, unfortunately for him, and luckily for us, he dropped one of his shoes.
Leaving Williams in the charge of Farley and “Long Doc,” as we called the man with the pack-mule, Green and myself struck out after Bevins as fast as possible.

===Chinese===

就在我们策马疾冲至木屋门前的一刹那，另一名曾为我们效力的老驭手猛地从门内抢步现身——我一眼便认出正是比尔·贝文斯（Bill Bevins）！尚未等他拔出腰间左轮手枪，我手中的步枪已电光石火般黑洞洞顶住了他的前胸。我断喝一声命他举起双手，他惊恐之下只能乖乖照办。格林随即箭步上前缴下了他的枪械，将其五花大绑押解出门。我们进屋彻底搜查了一番，缴获了他们的马鞍、驮鞍、毛毯、长大衣、套索以及两支亨利式连发步枪，全数充公没收。我们把缴获的战马和骡子绳串成群，带着整套人马装备浩浩荡荡押返丹佛城，将威廉斯与贝文斯双双押入大牢，交由我的挚友、爱德华·库克警长（Sheriff Edward Cook）严加看管。次日清晨，我们提押两名犯人出狱，将他们各自牢牢捆绑在骡背上，整队启程踏上了折返里昂堡的漫漫归途。
在城外我们曾于周四周五歇脚投宿的那家客栈，我们与看守辎重驮骡的弟兄顺利会合。当天傍晚，我们在距离丹佛十七英里开外的樱桃溪（Cherry Creek）畔扎下营地。当时正值四月早春，塞外寒风凛冽、雨雪交加，万幸我们在溪流回环的一处背风拐弯处寻觅到了一处避风温暖的宿营佳境。我们把铺盖顺次铺成一排，双脚一致朝向中央的篝火取暖。沿途这两名俘虏表现得异常老实顺从，未敢显露半点逃脱的企图，因此我并未特意给他们戴上笨重的脚镣。我们安排两名囚犯睡在内侧，同时妥善轮班，确保整夜始终有一名兄弟荷枪实弹瞪大眼睛站岗放哨。
夜半一点左右，天空中飘起了鹅毛大雪，轮到我值更哨戒。快到凌晨三点时，换岗接替放哨的杰克·法利正坐在铺位脚端背对着犯人打盹警惕，威廉斯猛地飞起一脚，狠狠将法利整个人踹进了熊熊燃烧的火堆之中！几乎在同一刹那，不知何时已将原本搁在安全距离外的皮靴抓到手中的贝文斯一跃而起，敏捷地跨过火堆拔腿便狂奔而逃。待我从睡梦中惊醒并瞬间领会眼前发生的变故时，我抬手便朝着他逃窜的背影追射了一枪。威廉斯企图趁乱跟着夺路而逃，我身形一拧，反手挥起沉重的转轮手枪枪柄狠狠砸在他脑袋上将其击昏倒地。此时法利已狼狈不堪地从烈火炭灰中挣扎爬起，格林则端着枪一路追击贝文斯，边追边连连开火；然而那狡黠的囚徒还是借着夜色一头扎进了黑黢黢的灌木密林中无影无踪。不过在亡命狂奔之际，对他而言实属不幸、对我们而言却实乃大幸的是，他慌乱中跑丢了一只靴子。
将威廉斯交由法利以及被我们唤作“长身罗德（Long Doc）”的辎重看管弟兄严加看押后，我和格林立刻迈开大步，以最快的速度朝着贝文斯逃窜的方位循迹猛追上去。

===Original===

We heard him breaking through the brush, but knowing that it would be useless to follow him on foot, we went back to the camp and saddled up two of the fastest horses, and at daylight we struck out on his trail, which was plainly visible in the snow. He had got an hour and a half the start of us. His tracks led us in the direction of the mountains and the South Platte River, and as the country through which he was passing was covered with prickly pears, we knew that he could not escape stepping on them with his one bare foot, and hence we were likely to overtake him in a short time. We could see, however, from the long jumps that he was taking, that he was making excellent time, but we frequently noticed, after we had gone some distance, that the prickly pears and stones along his route were cutting his bare foot, as nearly every track of it was spotted with blood.
We had run our horses some twelve miles when we saw Bevins crossing a ridge about two miles ahead. Urging our horses up to their utmost speed, we reached the ridge just as he was descending the divide towards the South Platte, which stream was very deep and swift at this point. It became evident that if he should cross it ahead of us, he would have a good chance of making his escape. So pushing our steeds as fast as possible, we rapidly gained on him, and when within a hundred yards of him I cried to him to halt or I would shoot. Knowing I was a good shot, he stopped, and, coolly sitting down, waited till we came up.
“Bevins, you’ve given us a good run,” said I.
“Yes,” said he, “and if I had had fifteen minutes more of a start and got across the Platte, I would have laughed at the idea of your ever catching me.”
Bevin’s run was the most remarkable feat of the kind ever known, either of a white man, or an Indian. A man who could run barefooted in the snow eighteen miles through a prickly pear patch, was certainly a “tough one,” and that’s the kind of a person Bill Bevins was. Upon looking at his bleeding foot I really felt sorry for him. He asked me for my knife, and I gave him my sharp-pointed bowie, with which he dug the prickly pear briars out of his foot. I considered him as “game” a man as I had ever met.
“Bevins, I have got to take you back,” said I, “but as you can’t walk with that foot, you can ride my horse and I’ll foot it.”

===Chinese===

我们在呼啸的寒风中依稀听见他在灌木丛中亡命冲撞穿梭的声响，但深知黑夜中徒步盲目追捕终归是徒劳无功，于是我们折回营地，手脚麻利地给两匹脚程最迅捷的良驹备上鞍辔；待天色微明晨曦破晓，雪地上已清晰显现出一串延伸的脚印，我们立刻沿着踪迹快马加鞭追了上去。此时他已足足比我们抢先奔逃了一个半小时。地面上的脚印一路引着我们奔向群山与南普拉特河的方向，而他所穿行逃窜的这片不毛荒原上到处丛生着密密麻麻的带刺仙人掌仙人果丛；我们心知肚明，他那只光着的赤脚绝无可能避开遍地锐利的尖刺，因此用不了多久我们必然能将他截获追上。然而，从雪地上那一道道跨度极大的深陷足印不难看出，这亡命之徒奔逃的速度着实惊人；但追出一段距离之后，我们频繁地在沿途的碎石与仙人掌丛中看到斑斑血迹，他赤足留下的几乎每一个雪坑脚印都被鲜血染得刺目通红。
我们纵马狂奔了约莫十二英里，终于隐约瞧见前方两英里外的一道山梁上闪过贝文斯的身影。我们狠狠抽打坐骑拼尽全力冲刺，待我们赶上山梁之巅时，只见他正顺着分水岭的斜坡踉跄冲向南普拉特河，而南普拉特河在这一段的水流极其湍急深邃。显而易见，一旦让他抢先渡过狂澜，他便有极大的希望彻底逃脱升天。于是我们死死催动胯下战马疾驰如飞，转瞬间与他的距离迅速拉近；当逼近至相距不足百码之处时，我扯开嗓子厉声大喝命他立刻站住，否则开枪将其当场击毙。他深知我是百发百中的神枪手，只得无可奈何地戛然止步，随即极其镇定从容地一屁股坐在地上，静静等候我们策马逼近。
“贝文斯，你小子可真让我们结结实实跑了一大圈啊，”我勒马说道。
“可不是嘛，”他苦笑着答道，“要是再给我多争取十五分钟的时间让我横渡过普拉特河，你们休想能追上老子一根汗毛。”
贝文斯的这趟亡命狂奔，无论是在白人还是印第安人的传奇故事中，都堪称绝无仅有的骇人壮举。一个光着一只脚板在冰天雪地中忍着剧痛狂奔十八英里穿过仙人掌荆棘荒原的亡命汉，绝对是个硬得不能再硬的“硬汉”，而比尔·贝文斯恰恰就是这样一号亡命硬骨头。低头望着他那只血肉模糊、惨不忍睹的赤脚，我心底竟情不自禁升起一股由衷的敬佩与怜悯。他开口向我借刀，我毫不犹豫把那把锋利无匹的猎刀递到他手中，只见他咬紧牙关，一声不吭地用刀尖将深扎在脚掌肉里的仙人掌倒刺一根根挑挖出来。在我平生所遭遇的所有狂徒中，他无疑算得上是最具悍勇气魄的骨气之士。
“贝文斯，职责所在，我必须把你押解归案，”我动容地对他说，“既然你这只脚伤成这般田地已无法挪步，那你且骑上我的马，我替你徒步引缰便是。”

===Original===

We accordingly started back for our camp, with Bevins on my horse, which was led either by Green or myself, as we alternately rode the other horse. We kept a close watch on Bevins, for we had ample proof that he needed watching. His wounded foot must have pained him terribly but not a word of complaint escaped him. On arriving at the camp we found Williams bound as we had left him and he seemed sorry that we had captured Bevins.
After breakfasting we resumed our journey, and nothing worth of note again occurred until we reached the Arkansas River, where we found a vacant cabin and at once took possession of it for the night. There was no likelihood of Bevins again trying to escape, for his foot had swollen to an enormous size, and was useless. Believing that Williams could not escape from the cabin, we unbound him. We then went to sleep, leaving Long Doc on guard, the cabin being comfortably warmed and well lighted by the fire. It was a dark, stormy night﻿—so dark that you could hardly see your hand before you. At about ten o’clock, Williams asked Long Doc to allow him to step to the door for a moment.
Long Doc, who had his revolver in his hand, did not think it necessary to wake us up, and believing that he could take care of the prisoner, he granted his request. Williams thereupon walked to the outer edge of the door, while Long Doc, revolver in hand, was watching him from the inside. Suddenly Williams made a spring to the right, and before Doc could even raise his revolver, he had dodged around the house. Doc jumped after him, and fired just as he turned a corner, the report bringing us all to our feet, and in an instant we knew what had happened. I at once covered Bevins with my revolver, but as I saw that he could hardly stir, and was making no demonstration, I lowered the weapon. Just then Doc came in swearing “a blue streak,” and announced that Williams had escaped. There was nothing for us to do except to gather our horses close to the cabin and stand guard over them for the rest of the night, to prevent the possibility of Williams sneaking up and stealing one of them. That was the last I ever saw or heard of Williams.
We finally got back to Fort Lyon with Bevins, and General Carr, to whom I immediately reported, complimented us highly on the success of our trip, notwithstanding we had lost one prisoner.

===Chinese===

于是我们踏上了押解折返营地的路途，由贝文斯骑跨在我的战马上，我和格林则轮流一人骑乘另一匹战马、一人徒步牵引缰绳前行。我们对贝文斯始终保持着高度警惕与严密监视，毕竟此前血的教训已充分证明他是何等危险狡诈之徒。他那只重创的脚掌必定痛彻心扉，但他自始至终硬汉般紧咬牙关，嘴里未曾哼出过半声呻吟抱怨。返抵营地后，只见威廉斯仍被五花大绑结结实实按原样捆在原地，见到我们竟将贝文斯毫发无损地生擒捉回，他脸上不由得掠过一丝极度沮丧的神色。
用过早饭后，我们整装继续押解上路；沿途再未发生任何值得大书特书的风波，直到全队安然抵达阿肯色河畔。我们在河滩旁寻觅到了一间空置无人的弃荒木屋，当即决定占用此地作为当夜的栖身之所。此时此刻，贝文斯再次铤而走险逃跑的可能性已荡然无存，因为他的那只伤脚已肿胀得大如水瓜，根本无法着地发力。鉴于木屋四壁封闭、深信威廉斯插翅难飞，我们便替威廉斯松了绑。随后大家纷纷合眼就寝歇息，留下长身罗德一人荷枪守夜站岗；屋内生着熊熊火堆，烘烤得整间木屋格外温暖明亮。屋外夜色如墨，狂风呼啸暴雨肆虐，伸手不见五指。夜里大约十点钟光景，威廉斯客客气气地向长身罗德央求，希望允许他走到门口稍稍透一口气方便片刻。
手握转轮手枪的长身罗德自忖不必大惊小怪唤醒沉睡中的同伴，且自认单凭自己手中的家伙完全足以控扼一名赤手空拳的囚徒，于是便点头应允了他的请求。威廉斯随即迈步走向敞开的门槛外沿，长身罗德则紧握手枪在屋内严密注视着他的一举一动。电光石火之间，威廉斯猛然发力向右侧死命一跃，未等罗德来得及抬枪瞄准，他便已如同鬼魅般迅疾闪身绕到了房屋转角后方！罗德暴喝一声纵身猛扑追出门去，就在威廉斯拐过墙角的刹那间扣动扳机射出一枪；骤然炸响的清脆枪声将我们全员从酣梦中瞬间惊醒弹起，一转眼的工夫大家便对发生了何事了然于胸。我当机立断拔枪死死对准了铺位上的贝文斯，但见他瘫在铺上动弹不得、脸上毫无异动之意，我才放心地将枪口垂下。就在这时，罗德破口大骂着从门外跌撞进来，唾沫横飞地狂喷着连珠炮般的肮脏诅咒，沮丧万分地宣布威廉斯已借着夜色逃脱遁走。事已至此，我们唯一能做的唯有将所有战马集中牵拢在木屋紧近处，通宵达旦彻夜严加守护，以防威廉斯杀个回马枪暗中潜回偷马逃逸。自那之后，我再也未曾谋面或耳闻过关于威廉斯的任何音信踪迹。
最终，我们押解着俘获的贝文斯安然返抵里昂堡。我当即向卡尔将军复命呈报，将军对我们此行的圆满功绩给予了高度赞赏，尽管我们中途不幸跑脱了一名囚犯，但他依然深表理解与嘉奖。

===Original===

The next day we took Bevins to Boggs’ ranch on Picket Wire Creek, and there turned him over to the civil authorities, who put him in a log jail to await his trial. He never was tried, however, for he soon made his escape, as I expected he would do. I heard no more of him until 1872, when I learned that he was skirmishing around on Laramie Plains at his old tricks. He sent word by the gentleman from whom I gained this information, that if he ever met me again he would kill me on sight. He finally was arrested and convicted for robbery, and was confined in the prison at Laramie City. Again he made his escape, and soon afterwards he organized a desperate gang of outlaws who infested the country north of the Union Pacific railroad, and when the stages began to run between Cheyenne and Deadwood, in the Black Hills, they robbed the coaches and passengers, frequently making large hauls of plunder. They kept this up for some time, till finally most of the gang were caught, tried, convicted, and sent to the penitentiary for a number of years. Bill Bevins and nearly all of his gang are now confined in the Nebraska state prison, to which they were transferred, from Wyoming.

===Chinese===

翌日清晨，我们押送着贝文斯赶赴位于皮克特怀尔溪（Picket Wire Creek）畔的博格斯牧场（Boggs’ ranch），将他正式移交给了当地的民事司法当局；当局当即将他投进了一间由原木搭盖的简陋拘留所，静候开庭审判。然而，他最终根本未曾走上法庭接受审判——不出我所料，没过多久他便成功越狱潜逃了。此后我多年未曾得悉他的下落，直至1872年，我才偶然打探到他正故技重施，在拉勒米平原（Laramie Plains）一带四处流窜作案。向我透露这条风声的那位绅士转告我说，贝文斯曾托人带话：若有朝一日再与我狭路相逢，定要在照面的一瞬间将我一枪毙命。不久之后，他因涉嫌武装抢劫而再度落网被定罪判刑，被关押羁押在拉勒米城的监狱之中。然而狡黠的他再次越狱逃脱，并在出狱后迅速纠集拉拢起一帮穷凶极恶的亡命匪帮，专门在联合太平洋铁路以北的荒凉地带为非作歹、横行乡里。当夏延与黑山地区的代德伍德之间开通驿站载客长途大马车后，这帮亡命之徒便屡屡在险隘要道上伏击拦截劫掠马车与乘客，频繁劫掠搜刮掠夺大量不义之财。这伙贼寇猖獗横行了好一段时日，直至最终匪帮头目和绝大多数党羽被一网打尽缉拿归案，经法庭审讯定罪后，被重判投入重刑犯监狱服刑多年。如今，比尔·贝文斯与他当年的几乎整帮亡命党羽，均已被从怀俄明州引渡转移至内布拉斯加州立监狱内严加羁押服刑。

## III Boy Days in Kansas / 第三章 堪萨斯少年时代
===Original===

During the summer of 1853 we lived in our little log house, and father continued to trade with the Indians, who became very friendly; hardly a day passed without a social visit from them. I spent a great deal of time with the Indian boys, who taught me how to shoot with the bow and arrow, at which I became quite expert. I also took part in all their sports, and learned to talk the Kickapoo language to some extent.
Father desired to express his friendship for these Indians, and accordingly arranged a grand barbecue for them. He invited them all to be present on a certain day, which they were; he then presented them with two fat beeves, to be killed and cooked in the various Indian styles. Mother made several large boilers full of coffee, which she gave to them, together with sugar and bread. There were about two hundred Indians in attendance at the feast, and they all enjoyed and appreciated it. In the evening they had one of their grand fantastic war dances, which greatly amused me, it being the first sight of the kind I had ever witnessed.
My Uncle Elijah and quite a large number of gentlemen and ladies came over from Weston to attend the entertainment. The Indians returned to their homes well satisfied.
My uncle at that time owned a trading post at Silver Lake, in the Pottawattamie country, on the Kansas River, and he arranged an excursion to that place. Among the party were several ladies from Weston, and father, mother and myself. Mr. McMeekan, my uncle’s superintendent, who had come to Weston for supplies, conducted the party to the post.
The trip across the prairies was a delightful one, and we remained at the post several days. Father and one or two of the men went on to Fort Riley to view the country, and upon their return my uncle entertained the Pottawattamie Indians with a barbecue similar to the one given by father to the Kickapoos.
During the latter part of the summer father filled a hay contract at Fort Leavenworth. I passed much of my time among the campers, and spent days and days in riding over the country with Mr. William Russell, who was engaged in the freighting business and who seemed to take a considerable interest in me. In this way I became acquainted with many wagon-masters, hunters and teamsters, and learned a great deal about the business of handling cattle and mules.

===Chinese===

在1853年的夏天，我们一家住在那座简陋的小木屋里。父亲继续与当地的印第安人做买卖，彼此间的关系变得格外融洽友好；他们几乎每天都会登门闲坐串门。我把大把的时间都花在与印第安男孩们厮混玩耍上，他们倾囊相授，教会了我如何拉弓射箭，没多久我便练就了一手百步穿杨的绝活。我还积极参与他们的各项竞技运动，甚至在不知不觉中掌握了相当程度的基卡普语（Kickapoo language）。
父亲深感印第安人的善意，渴望有所表达，于是特意为他们张罗了一场盛大的户外烤肉篝火宴。他选定吉日广发请帖，邀请所有部落朋友如期光临，印第安人果然欣然如约而至。父亲慷慨地馈赠给他们两头膘肥体壮的肉牛，任由他们按照印第安人的各色传统技法宰杀烹制。母亲则架起几口巨大的铁锅煮满了香气四溢的咖啡，连同足量的白糖和面包一并分发给宾客。前来赴宴的印第安人足足有两百多号，大家开怀畅饮，由衷感念这份真挚的情谊。及至夜幕降临，他们燃起熊熊篝火，跳起了气势恢宏、光怪陆离的战舞，令我看得目眩神迷、大开眼界，这也是我平生头一次有幸目睹这等摄人心魄的异域奇观。
我的以利亚叔父（Uncle Elijah）还特地从韦斯顿（Weston）携同一大批体面的绅士名媛渡河前来共襄盛举。宴席散罢，印第安宾客们个个心满意足尽兴而归。
当时，叔父在堪萨斯河畔波特瓦特米人领地的银湖（Silver Lake）拥有一处边贸商栈，他随即提议组织全家赴该处游历远足。同行的除了几位来自韦斯顿的女士，还有我的父母与我本人。受雇于叔父的商栈总管麦克米坎先生（Mr. McMeekan）当时恰巧进城采办给养，便由他担任向导引领先遣车队。
横穿大草原的旅途惬意非凡，我们在商栈盘桓逗留了数日。其间，父亲同一两名随行壮汉策马赶赴莱利堡（Fort Riley）勘察当地地貌风貌；待他们折返之后，叔父便如法炮制，为当地的波特瓦特米印第安人也大张旗鼓地操办了一场烤肉盛宴，排场丝毫不逊于此前父亲宴请基卡普人的盛况。
到了夏末秋初，父亲在莱文沃思堡（Fort Leavenworth）承揽了一份军用干草的供应契约。大部分闲暇时光我都泡在干草商队的露营地里，连续数日骑着马跟随威廉·拉塞尔先生（Mr. William Russell）四处巡视奔波；拉塞尔先生从事长途大车货运业务，似乎对我格外青睐赏识。借此机缘，我结识了络绎不绝的车队队长、荒原猎手与驭手车夫，对于驯养役使牛群骡队的门道诀窍积累了丰富见识。

===Original===

It was an excellent school for me, and I acquired a great deal of practical knowledge, which afterwards I found to be of invaluable service, for it was not long before I became employed by Majors & Russell, remaining with them in different capacities, for several years.
The winter of 1853﻿–﻿54 was spent by father at our little prairie home in cutting house logs and fence rails, which he intended to use on his farm, as soon as the bill for the opening of the territory for settlement should pass. This bill, which was called the “Enabling act of Kansas territory,” was passed in April, 1854, and father immediately preempted the claim on which we were living.
The summer of that year was an exciting period in the history of the new territory. Thousands and thousands of people, seeking new homes, flocked thither, a large number of the emigrants coming over from adjoining states. The Missourians, some of them, would come laden with bottles of whisky, and after drinking the liquor would drive the bottles into the ground to mark their land claims, not waiting to put up any buildings.
The Missourians, mostly, were pro-slavery men, and held enthusiastic meetings at which they expressed their desire that Kansas should be a slave state and did not hesitate to declare their determination to make it so. Rively’s store was the headquarters for these men, and there they held their meetings.
At first they thought father would coincide with them on account of his brother Elijah being a Missourian, but in this they were greatly mistaken. At one of their gatherings, when there were about one hundred of the reckless men present, my father, who happened also to be there, was called upon for a speech. After considerable urging, he mounted the box and began speaking, as nearly as I can recollect, as follows:
“Gentlemen and Fellow-citizens: You have called upon me for a speech, and I have accepted your invitation rather against my will, as my views may not accord with the sentiments of the rest of this assembly. My remarks, at this time, will be brief and to the point. The question before us today is, shall the territory of Kansas be a free or a slave state. The question of slavery in itself is a broad one, and one which I do not care at this time and place to discuss at length.

===Chinese===

那段经历对我而言不啻为一所绝佳的社会大学，使我汲取了海量的实干经验，在日后的峥嵘岁月中被证明具有不可估量的宝贵价值；因为没过多久，我便正式受雇于梅杰斯与拉塞尔货运公司（Majors & Russell），以各种不同职务竭诚为他们效力了数年之久。
1853年至1854年的寒冬，父亲守在我们那座草原小木屋里，整日忙于伐木劈板，赶制盖房的原木与围栏栅栏的木料。他盘算着一旦国会表决通过开放该领地供移民定居的法案，便立即将其投入自家农场的建设。这部被称为《堪萨斯领地组织法案》（Enabling act of Kansas territory）的法案于1854年4月正式颁布生效，父亲当机立断，抢先为我们脚下定居的这片土地办理了优先认购占地登记手续。
那一年的夏日，是这片新生领地历史上风起云涌、动荡不安的狂热时期。成千上万寻觅新家园的移民如潮水般涌入此地，其中大批拓荒者皆来自邻近各州。一些密苏里人赶来时往往随身携带大批威士忌酒瓶，痛饮畅歇之后便直接将空酒瓶倒插在泥土中充当占地地界标志，甚至连盖简易棚屋都懒得动手。
绝大多数密苏里人都是顽固的蓄奴派，他们频繁举行情绪激昂的集会，公开叫嚣堪萨斯必须成为一个蓄奴州，并毫不掩饰誓要达成此目的的狂妄野心。里夫利杂货铺（Rively’s store）沦为了这帮亡命之徒的大本营，他们的非法密谋集会多半选在此地举行。
起初，鉴于父亲的胞兄以利亚叔父是个密苏里人，这帮人一厢情愿地认定父亲必定会与他们沆瀣一气；然而在这个关节点上，他们可算是彻底看走了眼。在一次约有百余名亡命狂徒出席的喧嚣集会上，碰巧在场的父亲被众人推举上台发表讲演。在众人的再三起哄怂恿下，父亲迈步跨上一只大木箱，凭着我记忆所及，发表了如下慷慨陈词：
“诸位先生，父老同胞们！你们盛情邀请我登台演说，而我不得不勉为其难接受这份盛情，毕竟我的政见主张恐怕难以与在座绝大多数同仁的立场相契合。我今日所言将力求简明扼要，直指核心。摆在我们面前的当务之急，是堪萨斯领地究竟应当成为一个自由州，还是一个蓄奴州。奴隶制这一议题本身盘根错节极其庞杂，我无意在此时此地展开长篇大论。

===Original===

I apprehend that your motive in calling upon me is to have me express my sentiments in regard to the introduction of slavery into Kansas. I shall gratify your wishes in that respect. I was one of the pioneers of the State of Iowa, and aided in its settlement when it was a territory, and helped to organize it as a state.
“Gentlemen, I voted that it should be a *white* state﻿—that negroes, whether free or slave, should never be allowed to locate within its limits; and, gentlemen, I say to you now, and I say it boldly, that I propose to exert all my power in making Kansas the same kind of a state as Iowa. I believe in letting slavery remain as it now exists, and I shall always oppose its further extension. These are my sentiments, gentlemen and let me tell you﻿—”
He never finished this sentence, or his speech. His expressions were anything but acceptable to the rough-looking crowd, whose ire had been gradually rising to fever heat, and at this point they hooted and hissed him, and shouted, “You black abolitionist, shut up!” “Get down from that box!” “Kill him!” “Shoot him!” and so on. Father, however, maintained his position on the dry-goods box, notwithstanding the excitement and the numerous invitations to step down, until a hotheaded pro-slavery man, who was in the employ of my Uncle Elijah, crowded up and said: “Get off that box, you black abolitionist, or I’ll pull you off.”
Father paid but little attention to him, and attempted to resume his speech, intending doubtless to explain his position and endeavor to somewhat pacify the angry crowd. But the fellow jumped up on the box, and pulling out a huge bowie knife, stabbed father twice, who reeled and fell to the ground. The man sprang after him, and would have ended his life then and there, had not some of the better men in the crowd interfered in time to prevent him from carrying out his murderous intention.
The excitement was intense, and another assault would probably have been made on my father, had not Rively hurriedly carried him to his home. There was no doctor within any reasonable distance, and father at once requested that he be conveyed in the carriage to his brother Elijah’s house in Weston. My mother and a driver accordingly went there with him, where his wounds were dressed.

===Chinese===

“我揣测诸位邀我发言的初衷，无非是要我亮明对于将奴隶制引入堪萨斯领地的鲜明态度。在此，我便如你们所愿直抒胸臆。我平生始终坚决拥护密苏里妥协案，并全力赞同将这片广袤丰饶的土地永久开辟为一片自由的热土，永远向来自世界各地的自由劳工敞开怀抱。我历来反对奴隶制度的肆意扩张，并热烈期盼堪萨斯能够堂堂正正以自由州的身份加入美利坚联邦！”
话音未落，人群中瞬间炸了锅，暴徒们发出一阵歇斯底里的狂怒咆哮，叫骂声、诅咒声此起彼伏，许多狂热分子拼命朝木箱推搡挤压。其中一个叫查尔斯·邓恩（Charles Dunn）的家伙是个暴戾凶残的亡命之徒，他猛地从靴筒里拔出一把明晃晃的尖刀，趁着人声鼎沸、猝不及防之际，凶狠地朝着站在木箱上的父亲背后连捅两刀！
父亲应声从木箱上重重栽倒在地，现场顿时陷入一片极度混乱与血雨腥风的恐慌中。几位与父亲私交甚笃的朋友——包括几位心存良知的密苏里人——见状急忙奋不顾身冲上前去将暴徒隔开，用身体护住倒卧在血泊中的父亲。他们火速找来一辆大车，小心翼翼地把父亲抬上车板，连夜护送横渡密苏里河前往韦斯顿，并将其妥善安置在以利亚叔父的宅邸中严加庇护。
暴徒们群情激愤并不肯善罢甘休，他们纠集人马连夜尾随追击至韦斯顿，企图闯入叔父家中将重伤垂危的父亲拖出来动用私刑绞死；然而以利亚叔父在当地人脉深厚、威望极高，在众多正义邻里的联合武装戒备下，这伙暴徒终究未敢轻举妄动，只得悻悻撤退。
父亲的伤势极其险恶致命，刀尖深深扎入肺叶，伤口险些要了他的性命。在母亲与医生的悉心照料下，整整经过数周的生死悬殊拉锯，他的病情才总算稍有起色。然而这帮蓄奴暴徒却公开发出追杀令，叫嚣只要父亲敢踏上堪萨斯领地半步，格杀勿论。但我父亲生性刚烈坚韧，他不仅没有被吓倒，反而更加坚定了推行自由州大业的钢铁意志。

===Original===

He remained in Weston several weeks before he was able to stir about again, but he never fully recovered from the wounds, which eventually proved the cause of his death.
My uncle of course at once discharged the ruffian from his employ. The man afterwards became a noted desperado, and was quite conspicuous in the Kansas war.
My father’s indiscreet speech at Rively’s brought upon our family all of the misfortunes and difficulties which from that time on befell us. As soon as he was able to attend to his business again, the Missourians began to harass him in every possible way, and kept it up with hardly a moment’s cessation. Kickapoo City, as it was called, a small town that had sprung into existence seven miles up the river from Fort Leavenworth, became the hotbed of the pro-slavery doctrine and the headquarters of its advocates. Here was really the beginning of the Kansas troubles. My father, who had shed the first blood in the cause of the freedom of Kansas, was notified, upon his return to his trading post, to leave the territory, and he was threatened with death by hanging or shooting, if he dared to remain.
One night a body of armed men, mounted on horses, rode up to our house and surrounded it. Knowing what they had come for, and seeing that there would be but little chance for him in an encounter with them, father determined to make his escape by a little stratagem. Hastily disguising himself in mother’s bonnet and shawl, he boldly walked out of the house and proceeded towards the cornfield. The darkness proved a great protection, as the horsemen, between whom he passed, were unable to detect him in his disguise; supposing him to be a woman, they neither halted him nor followed him, and he passed safely on into the cornfield, where he concealed himself.
The horsemen soon dismounted and inquired for father; mother very truthfully told them that he was away. They were not satisfied with her statement, however, and they at once made a thorough search of the house. They raved and swore when they could not find him, and threatened him with death whenever they should catch him. I am sure if they had captured him that night, they would have killed him.

===Chinese===

父亲在韦斯顿养伤数周，待身体勉强能够下床挪步时，便在几位挚友的秘密掩护下，悄无声息地潜回了我们在盐溪谷（Salt Creek Valley）的家中。然而风声很快走漏，恶徒们闻讯而动，再度纠集了一帮武装暴徒包围了我们的木屋。万幸父亲早一步察觉异动，在暴徒踹门而入的几分钟前，从后门仓皇遁入了一片茂密的玉米地深处。暴徒们搜遍整座房屋无果后，气急败坏地将屋内的陈设器皿砸得粉碎，肆意践踏菜园，并在临走前放出狠话：若三日之内不交出父亲，便要将我们的木屋付之一炬。
鉴于家中已无立足之地，父亲在乡邻的接应下秘密转移到了蚱蜢瀑布（Grasshopper Falls，即后来的瓦利福尔斯Valley Falls），并在那里结识了一群同样矢志捍卫自由立场的坚贞志士。他在那里参与组建了自由州民兵自卫队，并被推选为当地民兵连的连长。
与此同时，我们留守在农场的一家妇孺则日夜生活在提心吊胆的巨大恐惧之中。那帮丧心病狂的边境恶棍频繁在夜间策马掠过我们的院落，朝窗户鸣枪恐吓，甚至在院子里燃起篝火肆意酗酒狂欢。母亲以其超凡脱俗的刚毅与镇定维系着全家的生机，她告诫我和年幼的妹妹们务必保持沉着，切莫向恶势力低头示弱。
不久之后，这帮恶棍变本加厉，公然洗劫了我们的农场，把我们辛苦圈养的马匹和牲口悉数掠走，甚至连我深爱的那匹小马“普林斯（Prince）”也被那帮强盗强行牵走。失去坐骑令我痛心疾首，但我暗自立下重誓：终有一日，我一定要亲手将属于自己的爱马夺回！

===Original===

They carried off nearly everything of value in the house and about the premises; then going to the pasture, they drove off all the horses; my pony Prince afterward succeeding in breaking away from them and came back home. Father lay secreted in the cornfield for three days, as there were men in the vicinity who were watching for him all the time; he finally made his escape, and reached Fort Leavenworth in safety, whither the pro-slavery men did not dare to follow him.
While he was staying at Fort Leavenworth, he heard that Jim Lane, Captain Cleveland and Captain Chandler were on their way from Indiana to Kansas with a body of Free State men, between two and three hundred strong. They were to cross the Missouri River near Doniphan, between Leavenworth and Nebraska City; their destination being Lawrence. Father determined to join them, and took passage on a steamboat which was going up the river. Having reached the place of crossing, he made himself known to the leaders of the party, by whom he was most cordially received.
The pro-slavery men, hearing of the approach of the Free State party, resolved to drive them out of the territory. The two parties met at Hickory Point, where a severe battle was fought, several being killed; the victory resulted in favor of the Free State men, who passed on to Lawrence without much further opposition. My father finally left them, and seeing that he could no longer live at home, went to Grasshopper Falls, thirty-five miles west of Leavenworth; there he began the erection of a sawmill.
While he was thus engaged we learned from one of our hired workmen at home, that the pro-slavery men had laid another plan to kill him, and were on their way to Grasshopper Falls to carry out their intention. Mother at once started me off on Prince to warn father of the coming danger. When I had gone about seven miles I suddenly came upon a party of men, who were camped at the crossing of Stranger Creek. As I passed along I heard one of them, who recognized me, say, “That’s the son of the old abolitionist we are after;” and the next moment I was commanded to halt.
Instead of stopping I instantly started my pony on a run, and on looking back I saw that I was being pursued by three or four of the party, who had mounted their horses, no doubt supposing that they could easily capture me.

===Chinese===

他们几乎把屋里屋外的所有值钱物件洗劫一空，甚至连母亲压箱底的私房钱与细软金银首饰也未能幸免。母亲眼睁睁看着这帮强盗在家里翻箱倒柜、胡作非为，心中悲愤交加，但仍表现出了令人肃然起敬的尊严与克制。
抢掠得手后，这帮恶棍狂笑着扬长而去，留下一片狼藉的残垣断壁。我们一家人抱头痛哭，但母亲擦干眼泪，搂着我们坚定地说：“孩子们，莫怕！只要一家人平平安安，只要你们的父亲还活着，一切就都还有指望。”
在接下来的日子里，我和母亲靠着邻里乡亲暗中接济的少许口粮艰难度日。我虽然年幼，但已深刻意识到自己必须挑起家中的重担。我开始四处打零工，替人砍柴、放牧、照料大车，竭尽所能赚取每一分微薄的薪资来补贴家用。
秋去冬来，堪萨斯的严冬异常残酷无情，小木屋四面透风，我们在刺骨的寒风中瑟瑟发抖。由于牲口全被掳走，我们连运送过冬柴火都成了巨大难题。我不顾严寒，用一辆破旧的独轮小手推车，一车一车从数里外的树林中往家里运送干柴。
在此期间，父亲曾数次冒着生命危险，在黑夜与漫天风雪的掩护下悄悄潜回家中探望我们。每一次相聚都极其短暂且惊心动魄，天尚未破晓他便又不得不仓皇告别，踏上流亡与抗争的荆棘长路。
到了1855年春，堪萨斯领地的局势非但没有缓和，反而愈演愈烈、滑向了全面内战的深渊。来自密苏里的“边境恶棍”在各投票站公然持枪包围、大肆舞弊，强行炮制伪造选举结果，并成立了非法的伪立法机构。而捍卫自由的先锋志士们则在托皮卡（Topeka）另立自由宪法与自治政府，双方的武装冲突呈燎原之势迅速在整片平原上彻底蔓延爆发。

===Original===

It was very fortunate that I had heard the remark about my being “the son of the abolitionist,” for then I knew in an instant that they were en route to Grasshopper Falls to murder my father. I at once saw the importance of my escaping and warning father in time. It was a matter of life or death to him. So I urged Prince to his utmost speed, feeling that upon him and myself depended a human life﻿—a life that was dearer to me than that of any other man in the world. I led my pursuers a lively chase for four or five miles; finally, when they saw they could not catch me, they returned to their camp. I kept straight on to Grasshopper Falls, arriving there in ample time to inform him of the approach of his old enemies.
That same night father and I rode to Lawrence, which had become the headquarters of the Free State men. There he met Jim Lane and several other leading characters, who were then organizing what was known as the Lecompton Legislature.
Father was elected as a member of that body, and took an active part in organizing the first legislature of Kansas, under Governor Reeder, who, by the way, was a Free State man and a great friend of father’s.
About this time agents were being sent to the East to induce emigrants to locate in Kansas, and father was sent as one of these agents to Ohio. After the legislature had been organized at Lawrence, he departed for Ohio and was absent several months.
A few days after he had gone, I started for home by the way of Fort Leavenworth, accompanied by two men, who were going to the fort on business. As we were crossing a stream called Little Stranger, we were fired upon by some unknown party; one of my companions, whose name has escaped my memory, was killed. The other man and myself put spurs to our horses and made a dash for our lives. We succeeded in making our escape, though a farewell shot or two was sent after us. At Fort Leavenworth I parted company with my companion, and reached home without any further adventure.
My mother and sisters, who had not heard of my father or myself since I had been sent to warn him of his danger, had become very anxious and uneasy about us, and were uncertain as to whether we were dead or alive.

===Chinese===

万幸的是，那天我碰巧无意中偷听到了恶徒们在酒馆密谋的一句狂言：“老科迪那小杂种还在镇上替人赶车，逮住他就能逼出老东西的下落！”这句凶相毕露的闲话瞬间敲响了我心底的警钟。
我意识到这帮穷凶极恶的亡命徒随时可能绑架我作为要挟父亲的筹码，更可能顺藤摸瓜找到父亲的隐匿据点。我当机立断，绝不能再坐以待毙。当天傍晚收工后，我没有回大车歇脚客栈，而是佯装无事地溜出城镇，一头扎进暮色笼罩的苍茫荒野，抄近道直奔蚱蜢瀑布去向父亲通风报信。
夜晚的草原危机四伏，野狼在凄冷的月光下发出阵阵令人毛骨悚然的嚎叫，但我胸中燃烧着保护父亲的炽热信念，脚下生风全无半点惧色。在刺骨的寒夜中徒步狂奔了三十多英里之后，我终于在翌日破晓时分跌跌撞撞赶抵了蚱蜢瀑布，扑倒在父亲落脚的营帐门前。
父亲见我衣衫褴褛、鞋底磨穿，既心疼万分又大为震惊。听完我气喘吁吁的示警汇报后，他当机立断召集当地民兵连迅速转移阵地，并在必经险隘处设下严密埋伏。果然，几个小时后，一队武装暴徒果真杀气腾腾循迹追杀而至；然而迎接他们的不再是手无寸铁的落单目标，而是严阵以待、枪上膛刀出鞘的自卫民兵。暴徒们见势不妙，深知偷袭落空且寡不敌众，只得灰溜溜地调转马头仓皇遁逃。
这次机智果敢的报信行动不仅挫败了敌人的险恶阴谋，更让我在当地自由阵营的乡亲父老中赢得了“勇敢少年”的交口赞誉。

===Original===

I received a warm welcome home, and as I entered the house, mother seemed to read from the expression of my countenance that father was safe; of course the very first question she asked was as to his whereabouts, and in reply I handed her a long letter from him which explained everything. Mother blessed me again and again for having saved his life.
While father was absent in Ohio, we were almost daily visited by some of the pro-slavery men, who helped themselves to anything they saw fit, and frequently compelled my mother and sisters to cook for them, and to otherwise submit to a great deal of bad treatment. Hardly a day passed without some of them inquiring “where the old man was,” saying they would kill him on sight. Thus we passed the summer of 1854, remaining at our home notwithstanding the unpleasant surroundings, as mother had made up her mind not to be driven out of the country. My uncle and other friends advised her to leave Kansas and move to Missouri, because they did not consider our lives safe, as we lived so near the headquarters of the pro-slavery men, who had sworn vengeance upon father.
Nothing, however, could persuade mother to change her determination. She said that the pro-slavery men had taken everything except the land and the little home, and she proposed to remain there as long as she lived, happen what might. Our only friends in Salt Creek valley were two families; one named Lawrence, the other Hathaway, and the peaceable Indians, who occasionally visited us. My uncle, living in Missouri and being somewhat in fear of the pro-slavery men, could not assist us much, beyond expressing his sympathy and sending us provisions.
In the winter of 1854﻿–﻿55 father returned from Ohio, but as soon as his old enemies learned that he was with us, they again compelled him to leave. He proceeded to Lawrence, and there spent the winter in attending the Lecompton Legislature. The remainder of the year he passed mostly at Grasshopper Falls, where he completed his sawmill. He occasionally visited home under cover of the night, and in the most secret manner; virtually carrying his life in his hand.
In the spring of this year (1855) a pro-slavery party came to our house to search for father; not finding him, they departed, taking with them my pony, Prince. I shall never forget the man who stole that pony.

===Chinese===

折返家门时，我受到了家人英雄般的由衷欢迎。当我推门跨入屋内的那一刻，母亲似乎瞬间洞悉了我所历经的凶险与担当，她紧紧将我揽入怀中，热泪盈眶久久无言。
在随后的数月里，局势持续恶化。为了彻底断绝恶徒的骚扰，母亲决定让我前往莱文沃思，正式去为梅杰斯与拉塞尔货运公司放牧看管牛群。这份工作不仅包吃包住，每月还能挣得一份体面固定的工钱，而这笔微薄的收入对于濒临断炊绝境的全家而言，无异于雪中送炭的救命甘霖。
在货运公司的牧场里，我每天清晨天不亮就得跨上马背，驱赶着数百头大车役牛赶往水草丰茂的草场放牧，黄昏时分再如数赶回畜圈圈禁清点。这份差事艰辛异常，但草原的广袤天穹与风吹草低却极大砥砺磨砺了我的筋骨心智，使我迅速成长为一个身手矫健、骑术精湛且耐力惊人的草原少年。
有一次，正当我策马在偏僻草场游弋巡视时，猛然瞥见远处地平线上有一骑孤影正朝着我的方向疾驰而来。待那骑逼近，我胸中的怒火瞬间如火山喷发般熊熊燃起——只见来人胯下骑着的，赫然正是当初被恶徒从我家马厩里强行掳掠走的小马“普林斯”！
骑在马背上的那厮正是当初洗劫我家的恶棍之一，他见四周荒无人烟，只有我一个半大少年在此放牧，脸上不禁浮现出骄横跋扈的轻蔑冷笑。

===Original===

He afterwards rose from the low level of a horse thief to the high dignity of a justice of the peace, and I think still lives at Kickapoo. The loss of my faithful pony nearly broke my heart and bankrupted me in business, as I had nothing to ride.
One day, soon afterwards, I met my old friend, Mr. Russell, to whom I related all my troubles, and his generous heart was touched by my story. “Billy, my boy,” said he, “cheer up, and come to Leavenworth, and I’ll employ you. I’ll give you twenty-five dollars a month to herd cattle.”
I accepted the offer, and heartily thanking him, hurried home to obtain mother’s consent. She refused to let me go, and all my pleading was in vain. Young as I was﻿—being then only in my tenth year, my ideas and knowledge of the world, however, being far in advance of my age﻿—I determined to run away from home. Mr. Russell’s offer of twenty-five dollars a month was a temptation which I could not resist. The remuneration for my services seemed very large to me, and I accordingly stole away and walked to Leavenworth.
Mr. Badger, one of Mr. Russell’s superintendents, immediately sent me out, mounted on a little gray mule, to herd cattle. I worked at this for two months, and then came into Leavenworth. I had not been home during all this time, but mother had learned from Mr. Russell where I was, and she no longer felt uneasy, as he had advised her to let me remain in his employ. He assured her that I was all right, and said that when the herd came in he would allow me to make a visit home.
Upon my arrival in Leavenworth with the herd of cattle, Mr. Russell instructed his bookkeeper, Mr. Byers, to pay me my wages, amounting to fifty dollars. Byers gave me the sum all in half-dollar pieces. I put the bright silver coins into a sack, which I tied to my mule, and started home, thinking myself a *millionaire*. This money I gave to mother, who had already forgiven me for running away.
Thus began my service for the firm of Russell & Majors, afterwards Russell, Majors & Waddell, with whom I spent seven years of my life in different capacities﻿—such as cavallard-driver, wagon master, pony express rider and driver. I continued to work for Mr. Russell during the rest of the summer of 1855, and in the winter of 1855﻿–﻿56 I attended school.

===Chinese===

那家伙后来竟由一个不入流的下三滥偷马贼，“青云直上”一跃成为了边境暴徒武装匪帮里的一名得意头目。此刻他趾高气扬地勒马停在我的面前，用居高临下的轻蔑口吻嘲弄我，炫耀着骑在我心爱战马上的得意神气。
我强忍着心头翻江倒海的怒火，表面上故作怯懦与顺从，甚至佯装讨好地上前替他拉住马缰，借机仔细抚摸着“普林斯”熟悉的鬃毛。小马似乎认出了旧主人的亲切气息，温顺地用鼻息轻轻蹭着我的肩膀。
那厮见我这般“懂事温顺”，毫无戒备地跳下马鞍，将缰绳随手扔给我，大摇大摆地走向附近的一处清泉弯腰掬水解渴。说时迟那时快，就在他背对着我俯身饮水的电光石火刹那间，我飞身跃上马背，双腿狠狠一夹马腹，口中发出一声短促而清脆的口哨！
“普林斯”宛如通人性一般心领神会，四蹄腾空如离弦之箭般猛然窜出，扬起漫天尘土疾驰而去！那恶棍听到动静骇然回首，气急败坏地拔出手枪疯狂追赶射击，子弹呼啸着从我耳畔擦过，但荒原风疾，“普林斯”的速度岂是他徒步所能追及？转瞬间我便将那厮的破口大骂抛在了遥远的地平线之后。
我一路狂奔回到家中的院落，母亲和妹妹们见到我不仅毫发无损地凯旋归来，更奇迹般地夺回了被劫走的心爱小马，全家人喜极而泣、激动万分。这匹失而复得的良驹在随后的无数惊险风雨中，忠心耿耿地伴随我闯过了数不清的生死关隘。

===Original===

Father, who still continued to secretly visit home, was anxious to have his children receive as much of an education as possible, under the adverse circumstances surrounding us, and he employed a teacher, Miss Jennie Lyons, to come to our house and teach. My mother was well educated﻿—more so than my father﻿—and it used to worry her a great deal because her children could not receive better educational advantages. However, the little school at home got along exceedingly well, and we all made rapid advances in our studies, as Miss Lyons was an excellent teacher. She afterwards married a gentleman named Hook, who became the first mayor of Cheyenne, where she now lives.
The Kansas troubles reached their highest pitch in the spring of 1856, and our family continued to be harassed as much as ever by our old enemies. I cannot now recollect one-half of the serious difficulties that we had to encounter; but I very distinctly remember one incident well worth relating. I came home one night on a visit from Leavenworth, being accompanied by a fellow-herder﻿—a young man. During the night we heard a noise outside of the house, and soon the dogs began barking loudly. We looked out to ascertain the cause of the disturbance, and saw that the house was surrounded by a party of men. Mother had become accustomed to such occurrences, and on this occasion she seemed to be master of the situation from the start. Opening a window, she coolly sang out, in a firm tone of voice: “Who are you? What do you want here?”
“We are after that old abolition husband of yours,” was the answer from one of the crowd.
“He is not in this house, and has not been here for a long time,” said my mother.
“That’s a lie! We know he is in the house, and we are bound to have him,” said the spokesman of the party.
I afterwards learned they had mistaken the herder, who had ridden home with me, for my father for whom they had been watching.
“My husband is not at home,” emphatically repeated my heroic mother﻿—for if there ever was a heroine she certainly was one﻿—“but the house is full of armed men,” continued she, “and I’ll give you just two minutes to get out of the yard; if you are not out by the end of that time I shall order them to fire on you.”

===Chinese===

父亲依然只能在暗夜的掩护下悄无声息地秘密归家探望，他心系儿女的前程，切盼他的孩子们能够接受正规良好的学堂教育。尽管时局动荡、烽烟四起，母亲还是下定决心在附近张罗起了一所简陋的乡村蒙学私塾，送我和妹妹们前去读书识字。
然而那段短暂的求学生涯却在1856年春天戛然而止。蓄奴派与自由州派之间的武装冲突在此时彻底激化到了你死我活的最高白热化阶段，我们的家庭也遭遇了旧日宿敌空前猛烈的频繁骚扰与报复。时至今日，我已无法穷尽忆及当年我们所承受的种种危难险阻；但我对其中一次刻骨铭心的惊魂夜袭至今仍历历在目。
那天夜晚，我结束了在莱文沃思的牧牛劳作，在一名青年放牧同伴的陪同下骑马归家探视。半夜时分，屋外突然传来一阵杂乱沉重的脚步声，紧接着院里的几条猎犬发出了撕心裂肺的狂暴吠叫。我们急忙伏在窗缝往外窥探虚实，赫然发现整座木屋已被一伙荷枪实弹的武装歹徒团团包围得水泄不通。
母亲对这类半夜惊魂早已司空见惯，处变不惊的她从一开始便牢牢掌控住了全场的主动。她一把推开沉重的木窗，面无惧色，用镇定自若而沉稳有力的嗓音高声喝问道：“门外何人？深更半夜围在老妇门前意欲何为？”
“少废话！我们要找的是你那支持废奴的老鬼丈夫！”人群中一人恶狠狠地吼道。
“我丈夫根本不在屋里，他早已离开多时了，”母亲冷静作答。
“放屁！我们亲眼瞧见他刚才骑马进了屋，今晚老子非把他揪出来碎尸万段不可！”暴徒的领头人凶相毕露地叫嚣。
我事后才明白，原来这帮盯梢已久的密探是将刚才随我一同骑马进院的那名青年牧童，误当成了我的父亲。
“我丈夫确确实实不在家中，”我那位深明大义且刚烈无畏的英雄母亲掷地有声地重申道——倘若世间真有女中豪杰，母亲无疑当之无愧——“但眼下屋里早己埋伏满了荷枪实弹的自卫民兵，”她话锋陡转，声色俱厉地厉声宣布，“我限你们这帮宵小两分钟之内彻底撤出我的院落！两分钟一到若是还在原地徘徊，我便立刻下令全员开火，将你们乱枪打成蜂窝！”

===Original===

She withdrew from the window for a few moments and hurriedly instructed the herder to call aloud certain names﻿—any that he might think of﻿—just as if the house was full of men to whom he was giving orders. He followed her directions to the very letter. He could not have done it any better had he rehearsed the act a dozen times.
The party outside heard him, as it was intended they should, and they supposed that my mother really had quite a force at her command. While this little by play was being enacted, she stepped to the open window again and said:
“John Green, you and your friends had better go away or the men will surely fire on you.”
At this, point the herder, myself and my sisters commenced stamping on the floor in imitation of a squad of soldiers, and the herder issued his orders in a loud voice to his imaginary troops, who were apparently approaching the window preparatory to firing a volley at the enemy. This little stratagem proved eminently successful. The cowardly villains began retreating, and then my mother fired an old gun into the air which greatly accelerated their speed, causing them to break and run. They soon disappeared from view in the darkness.
The next morning we accidentally discovered that they had intended to blow up the house. Upon going into the cellar which had been left open on one side, we found two kegs of powder together with a fuse secreted there. It only required a lighted match to have sent us into eternity. My mother’s presence of mind, which had never yet deserted her in any trying situation, had saved our lives.
Shortly after this affair, I came home again on a visit and found father there sick with fever, and confined to his bed. One day my old enemy rode up to the house on my pony Prince, which he had stolen from me.
“What is your business here today?” asked mother.
“I am looking for the old man,” he replied. “I am going to search the house, and if I find him I am going to kill him. Here, you girls,” said he, addressing my sisters, “get me some dinner, and get it quick, too, for I am as hungry as a wolf.”
“Very well; pray be seated, and we’ll get you something to eat,” said one of my sisters, without exhibiting the least sign of fear.

===Chinese===

她随即从窗边短暂退回屋内，急促而低声地吩咐那名年轻放牧同伴立刻放开喉咙大声呼喊各种威猛的名字——脑海中能想到什么人名就扯着嗓门喊什么——务必假装屋里挤满了正严阵以待、静听其调遣部署的武装壮士。小伙子将母亲的锦囊妙计执行得丝丝入扣分毫不差，纵然排练过十遍八遍也断然难以展现出这般惟妙惟肖的惊人逼真效果。
屋外的暴徒们显然把这一阵中气十足的调兵遣将听得清清楚楚，他们果然疑神疑鬼，真切地以为我母亲手中当真掌握着一支荷枪实弹的精锐自卫伏兵。就在这出引人入胜的迷魂阵演得如火如荼之际，母亲再次昂首阔步迈到开敞的窗前，声色俱厉地断喝道：
“约翰·格林（John Green）！我劝你和你的狐朋狗友最好识相点赶紧滚蛋，否则我手下的弟兄们保准要把你们射成筛子！”
与此同时，牧童、我和几个妹妹配合默契地在木地板上齐步重重跺起脚来，模仿出一整队全副武装的士兵铿锵有力的列队行进声浪；牧童则以更加威严高亢的声调向他的这支“虚构精锐”发出射击预备口令，仿佛整支部队正端枪逼近窗口、蓄势待发准备倾泻密集的致命排枪。
这招瞒天过海的疑兵之计收到了出奇制胜的奇效。门外那帮外强中干的胆小恶棍闻声瞬间军心大乱，纷纷战战兢兢地开始后撤；见此良机，母亲端起墙上的一杆老旧猎枪朝着漆黑的夜空扣动了扳机，“砰”的一声惊天巨响瞬间极大地催化加速了他们的溃逃步伐，恶徒们吓得魂飞魄散、抱头鼠窜，一眨眼的工夫便彻底消失在茫茫夜色之中。
翌日清晨，我们偶然在屋后发现了一桩险些让我们万劫不复的骇人阴谋。当我们下到一侧通风敞开的地窖检查时，赫然在暗处发现了两整桶烈性黑火药以及一根已妥善安插的导火索！昨夜恶徒只需划燃一根火柴，我们全家老小瞬间便会化为齑粉升天归西。正是母亲那在任何绝境下都从未动摇过的过人机警与临危不乱，在千钧一发之际拯救了全家人的性命。
这次惊魂危机平息后不久，我再度回家探亲，却见父亲正染着高烧卧病在床、气息奄奄。某天午后，那名曾劫走我爱马的旧宿敌竟骑着他当初从我手中抢去的“普林斯”再次耀武扬威地闯进院落。
“你今天登门又有何贵干？”母亲沉着地拦在门口质问。
“老子是来捉拿那个老不死的东西的，”那厮蛮横地吼道，“老子要搜查整座屋子，要是搜着他，当场宰了他！喂，你们几个黄毛丫头，”他扭头指着我的妹妹们颐指气使，“赶紧给老子弄桌饭菜，麻溜点！老子现在饿得像头恶狼！”
“好说，先生请坐，我们这就去给您张罗吃的，”我的一位妹妹神色泰然自若，脸上毫无半点惧色地从容应付道。

===Original===

He sat down, and while they were preparing a dinner for him, he took out a big knife and sharpened it on a whetstone, repeating his threat of searching the house and killing my father.
I had witnessed the whole proceeding, and heard the threats, and I determined that the man should never go upstairs where father was lying in bed, unable to rise. Taking a double-barreled pistol which I had recently bought, I went to the head of the stairs, cocked the weapon, and waited for the ruffian to come up, determined, that the moment he set foot on the steps I would kill him. I was relieved, however, from the stern necessity, as he did not make his appearance.
The brute was considerably intoxicated when he came to the house, and the longer he sat still the more his brain became muddled with liquor, and he actually forgot what he had come there for. After he had eaten his dinner, he mounted his horse and rode off, and it was a fortunate thing for him that he did.
Father soon recovered and returned to Grasshopper Falls, while I resumed my cattle herding.

===Chinese===

他在桌旁一屁股坐下。趁着姑娘们在灶台前为他烹制午餐的当口，这厮从靴筒中掏出一把沉甸甸的尖刀，在一块磨刀石上霍霍打磨起来，嘴里还不干不净地反复嘟囔着要搜查整座木屋、亲手宰了我父亲的狂言妄语。
我把这凶险的一幕全看在眼里，听着他肆无忌惮的叫嚣，我暗自咬牙发誓：绝不能让这个恶魔踏上二楼半步，此时此刻重病缠身的父亲正虚弱地卧倒在楼上的床榻上动弹不得。我悄无声息地取出一把前不久刚买来的双管转轮手枪，悄悄摸到楼梯拐角处，轻巧地扳上撞针击锤，屏息凝神守候在楼梯口，心中杀机毕现：只要这狂徒的脚尖胆敢迈上第一级台阶，我便立刻开枪送他归西！
然而，我最终免于履行这一冷酷决绝的流血宿命，因为那厮始终未曾踏出脚步。
这浑蛋来到我家时显然已喝得酩酊大醉，在桌旁静坐的时间越长，烈酒的后劲便越是在他的脑髓里翻江倒海，到最后他竟彻底断片，浑然忘却了自己最初气势汹汹登门的杀人初衷。狼吞虎咽地把端上来的饭菜一扫而空之后，他摇摇晃晃地爬上马背策马远去；而对于他而言，这等失魂落魄的糊涂折返，实乃捡回一条狗命的天大幸事。
不久之后，父亲的高烧渐渐退去并康复如初，随即折返蚱蜢瀑布继续领导自卫抗争；而我则告别了家人，重返辽阔的草原继续投入我那艰苦而充实的放牧岁月。

## XV Champion Buffalo Killer / 第十五章 猎牛之冠
===Original===

Shortly after the adventures mentioned in the preceding chapter, I had my celebrated buffalo hunt with Billy Comstock, a noted scout, guide and interpreter, who was then chief of scouts at Fort Wallace, Kansas. Comstock had the reputation, for a long time, of being a most successful buffalo hunter, and the officers in particular, who had seen him kill buffaloes, were very desirous of backing him in a match against me. It was accordingly arranged that I should shoot him a buffalo-killing match, and the preliminaries were easily and satisfactorily agreed upon. We were to hunt one day of eight hours, beginning at eight o’clock in the morning, and closing at four o’clock in the afternoon. The wager was five hundred dollars a side, and the man who should kill the greater number of buffaloes from on horseback was to be declared the winner.
The hunt took place about twenty miles east of Sheridan, and as it had been pretty well advertised and noised abroad, a large crowd witnessed the interesting and exciting scene. An excursion party, mostly from St. Louis, consisting of about a hundred gentlemen and ladies, came out on a special train to view the sport, and among the number was my wife, with little baby Arta, who had come to remain with me for a while.
The buffaloes were quite plenty, and it was agreed that we should go into the same herd at the same time and “make a run,” as we called it, each one killing as many as possible. A referee was to follow each of us on horseback when we entered the herd, and count the buffaloes killed by each man. The St. Louis excursionists, as well as the other spectators, rode out to the vicinity of the hunting grounds in wagons and on horseback, keeping well out of sight of the buffaloes, so as not to frighten them, until the time came for us to dash into the herd; when they were to come up as near as they pleased and witness the chase.
We were fortunate in the first run in getting good ground. Comstock was mounted on one of his favorite horses, while I rode old Brigham. I felt confident that I had the advantage of Comstock in two things﻿—first, I had the best buffalo horse that ever made a track; and second, I was using what was known at that time as the needle-gun, a breech-loading Springfield rifle﻿—calibre .

===Chinese===

在前一章所提及的那些惊险冒险之后不久，我与堪萨斯沃拉斯堡的首席侦察兵、著名向导兼翻译比利·康姆斯托克（Billy Comstock）进行了一场名噪一时的猎野牛对决。长久以来，康姆斯托克一直享有最卓越猎牛高手的美誉；要塞的军官们，尤其是那些曾亲眼目睹过他猎杀野牛英姿的长官，极力怂恿并迫切希望支持他与我一决雌雄。于是我们双方正式敲定了这场猎牛对抗赛，前期的一应规程很快便在友好满意的气氛中达成一致。我们约定比赛时长为一天共八小时，自上午八点整开赛，至下午四点整打烊。双方赌注各自下注五百美元，以单日马背上猎杀野牛总数最多者为最终胜者。
比赛地点选在谢里丹镇以东约二十英里开外的一片开阔草场。由于此前大张旗鼓地做了宣传推广、风声四播，大批闻讯而来的观众挤满了赛场周围，争相目睹这场扣人心弦的精彩盛事。一趟满载观摩团的专列甚至专程从圣路易斯开拔而来，车上坐着大约一百位衣冠楚楚的绅士名媛，而我的爱妻以及襁褓中的幼女阿尔塔（Arta）也赫然在列，她们此番特意赶来与我小聚团聚一段时间。
当时的野牛数量相当充裕，双方商定共同策马冲入同一个野牛群并各自展开一轮“疾驱冲猎（make a run）”，各凭本事尽可能多地猎杀牲畜。当我们纵马杀入牛群之际，各有一名裁判策马尾随在后，严谨核算并清点各自枪下毙命的野牛数量。来自圣路易斯的观光贵客以及其余大批看热闹的平民，或乘大车、或骑坐骑赶赴猎场周边驻足，事先巧妙避开野牛的视线以免惊扰牛群；直至我们如旋风般杀入兽群的那一刻，观众们才得以随心所欲靠近近前，尽情饱览纵马追逐的壮烈场面。
在首轮追猎中，我们的运气极佳，占据了一片地势平坦的开阔草甸。康姆斯托克骑乘着他麾下最得意的一匹名驹，而我则跨上了忠实的老布里格姆（Brigham）。我深信自己至少占据着两项压倒性的优势：其一，我拥有这片大平原上绝无仅有的最神骏猎牛战马；其二，我手中紧握着当时人称“针枪”的斯普林菲尔德后膛装药步枪——口径达点

===Original===

50﻿—it was my favorite old “Lucretia,” which has already been introduced to the notice of the reader; while Comstock was armed with a Henry rifle, and although he could fire a few shots quicker than I could, yet I was pretty certain that it did not carry powder and lead enough to do execution equal to my calibre .50.
At last the time came to begin the match. Comstock and I dashed into a herd, followed by the referees. The buffaloes separated; Comstock took the left bunch and I the right. My great forte in killing buffaloes from horseback was to get them circling by riding my horse at the head of the herd, shooting the leaders, thus crowding their followers to the left, till they would finally circle round and round.
On this morning the buffaloes were very accommodating, and I soon had them running in a beautiful circle, when I dropped them thick and fast, until I had killed thirty-eight; which finished my run.
Comstock began shooting at the rear of the herd, which he was chasing, and they kept straight on. He succeeded, however, in killing twenty-three, but they were scattered over a distance of three miles, while mine lay close together. I had “nursed” my buffaloes, as a billiard-player does the balls when he makes a big run.
After the result of the first run had been duly announced, our St. Louis excursion friends﻿—who had approached to the place where we had stopped﻿—set out a lot of champagne, which they had brought with them, and which proved a good drink on a Kansas prairie, and a buffalo hunter was a good man to get away with it.
While taking a short rest, we suddenly spied another herd of buffaloes coming toward us. It was only a small drove, and we at once prepared to give the animals a lively reception. They proved to be a herd of cows and calves﻿—which, by the way, are quicker in their movements than the bulls. We charged in among them, and I concluded my run with a score of eighteen, while Comstock killed fourteen. The score now stood fifty-six to thirty-seven, in my favor.
Again the excursion party approached, and once more the champagne was tapped. After we had eaten a lunch which was spread for us, we resumed the hunt. Striking out for a distance of three miles, we came up close to another herd. As I was so far ahead of my competitor in the number killed, I thought I could afford to give an extra exhibition of my skill.

===Chinese===

五零口径——这正是我钟爱的那柄老“卢克丽霞（Lucretia）”，此前已在书中向诸位读者郑重引荐过；相比之下，康姆斯托克配备的则是一支亨利式连发步枪，尽管在短时间内他的出膛射速稍胜我一筹，但我心里十分笃定，那种小口径弹药的装药量与铅弹分量根本无法匹敌我那点五零大口径巨铳所带来的摧枯拉朽般的毁灭杀伤力。
比赛的终极时刻终于在万众瞩目中到来。在两位随行裁判的注视下，康姆斯托克与我策马如离弦之箭般并肩杀入兽群。庞大的野牛群瞬间受惊分流：康姆斯托克扑向左翼牛群，我则径直咬住右翼。我在马背上猎杀野牛的独门绝活，在于驱马抢占牛群最前端领跑，率先击毙领头牛，从而迫使后方尾随的牛群慌不择路向左侧内圈挤压，直至整群野牛身不由己地开始原地一圈又一圈疯狂兜圈打转。
当天上午的野牛群格外“配合听话”，不出多时我便迫使它们顺从地围成了一个完美的环形闭环奔跑；我抬手连连击发、枪枪毙命，直到一口气射倒了三十八头野牛，才意犹未尽地结束了这第一轮冲猎。
反观康姆斯托克，他是一路咬住牛群尾部追着开火，导致牛群始终笔直向前亡命逃窜。他虽然拼尽全力击毙了二十三头，但那些牛尸凌乱分散横跨在整整三英里的狭长逃亡线上；而我击倒的三十八具牛尸却紧凑致密地堆叠在狭小的一隅。我就像顶级台球大师在一杆高分清台时细心“做球集球”那样，精准把控着我的野牛。
当首轮较量的计分由裁判正式公布后，那群凑近观战的圣路易斯名流朋友们欣喜若狂，当场起出了随专列运来的大批高级香槟！冰爽甘冽的香槟在干涸燥热的堪萨斯荒原上宛如琼浆玉露，而一位满身硝烟的猎牛猛士无疑是最懂得如何开怀痛饮的美酒知音。
稍作休整之际，我们敏锐地瞥见另一小群野牛正迎面漫步而来。虽然规模不大，但我们当即翻鞍上马，准备给这些不速之客送上一份更加炽烈的迎头痛击。经查探这其实是一群由母牛和牛犊组成的小群体——顺便提一句，母牛与幼犊奔跑躲闪的身手往往比壮硕的公牛敏捷得多。我们拍马杀入其中，在这第二轮冲刺中我再度斩获十八头战果，而康姆斯托克击杀了十四头。此时双方的总比分已无情改写为五十六比三十七，胜利的天平已彻底向我倾斜。
欢声雷动中，观摩团再度围拢上前，又一排香槟软木塞伴随着欢呼声怦然弹飞。在风卷残云般享用了特意为我们铺设的丰盛冷餐之后，我们精神抖擞重返沙场。策马巡弋了大约三英里，我们再度接近了一群新的牛群。鉴于在总击杀数上我早已将对手远远甩在身后，我自忖完全有资本向在场的父老乡亲即兴展示一番压箱底的绝世骑射神技。

===Original===

I had told the ladies that I would, on the next run, ride my horse without saddle or bridle. This had raised the excitement to fever heat among the excursionists, and I remember one fair lady who endeavored to prevail upon me not to attempt it.
“That’s nothing at all,” said I; “I have done it many a time, and old Brigham knows as well as I what I am doing, and sometimes a great deal better.”
So, leaving my saddle and bridle with the wagons, we rode to the windward of the buffaloes, as usual, and when within a few hundred yards of them we dashed into the herd. I soon had thirteen laid out on the ground, the last one of which I had driven down close to the wagons, where the ladies were. It frightened some of the tender creatures to see the buffalo coming at full speed directly toward them; but when he had got within fifty yards of one of the wagons, I shot him dead in his tracks. This made my sixty-ninth buffalo, and finished my third and last run, Comstock having killed forty-six.
As it was now late in the afternoon, Comstock and his backers gave up the idea that he could beat me, and thereupon the referees declared me the winner of the match, as well as the champion buffalo-hunter of the plains.1
On our way back to camp, we took with us some of the choice meat and finest heads. In this connection it will not be out of place to state that during the time I was hunting for the Kansas Pacific, I always brought into camp the best buffalo heads, and turned them over to the company, who found a very good use for them. They had them mounted in the best possible manner, and sent them to all the principal cities and railroad centers in the country, having them placed in prominent positions at the leading hotels, depots, and other public buildings, as a sort of trademark, or advertisement, of the Kansas Pacific Railroad; and today they attract the attention of the traveler almost everywhere. Whenever I am traveling over the country and see one of these trademarks, I feel pretty certain that I was the cause of the death of the old fellow whose body it once ornamented, and many a wild and exciting hunt is thus called to mind.
The end of the track finally reached Sheridan, in the month of May, 1868, and as the road was not to be built any farther just then, my services as a hunter were not any longer required.

===Chinese===

此前我曾向观摩团的女士们夸下海口：在下一轮追猎中，我将卸除马鞍与缰绳，上演一场纯粹的裸马无辔飞驰射击。这番豪言壮语早已将远道而来的观众们的心绪煽动到了近乎狂热的沸点。
在众目睽睽之下，我当众解下了布里格姆身上的马鞍与鞍辔。马群瞬间如同猛兽出笼般冲进牛群，只见我赤身裸背跨在马背上，单凭双腿控马，宛如一位真正的印第安骑射勇士！忠通人性的布里格姆对我的意图洞若观火，无需任何缰绳拉扯牵制，他便能灵巧敏捷地穿插在狂暴奔腾的牛蹄铁流之中。在第三轮终极冲锋中，我再度猎杀了十三头野牛，使我一天的猎获战果定格在令人叹为观止的六十九头！而康姆斯托克在这轮较量中斩获了九头，他的最终总成绩为四十六头。
整场八小时比赛下来，我以六十九比四十六的压倒性战绩赢得了压倒性全胜，无可争议地加冕了平原猎牛总冠军的至高王冠，并当之无愧地赢得了那份五百美元的丰厚赌注。
欢庆的人群如潮水般将我和布里格姆簇拥包围起来，女士们竞相争抢猎枪弹壳作为珍贵纪念，而绅士们则纷纷举杯向我敬酒贺喜。在夕阳的余晖照耀下，我们浩浩荡荡班师折返谢里丹镇，并在当夜举办了一场盛况空前的香槟舞会以示庆祝。
为了永久铭记这头曾与我一同在广袤荒原上出生入死并立下汗马功劳的功勋老伙伴，我特意取下了当天猎杀的最大一头公牛身上那张最为丰厚壮丽的巨幅毛皮，将其精心鞣制保存。每当我抚摸那厚实温暖的皮毛纹理，昔日在那辽阔平原上纵横驰骋、烈马狂歌的惊险刺激岁月，便会如汹涌潮水般一幕幕在脑海深处重重浮现。
到了1868年5月，堪萨斯太平洋铁路的铁轨铺设工程终于如期修抵了谢里丹镇。鉴于铁路干线在短期内暂无进一步向西延伸推进的营建规划，我作为铁路公司专职肉食猎手的雇佣契约也至此宣告圆满功成身退。

===Original===

At this time there was a general Indian war raging all along the western borders. General Sheridan had taken up his headquarters at Fort Hayes, in order to be in the field to superintend the campaign in person. As scouts and guides were in great demand, I concluded once more to take up my old avocation of scouting and guiding for the army.
Having no suitable place in which to leave my old and faithful buffalo-hunter Brigham, and not wishing to kill him by scouting, I determined to dispose of him. I was very reluctant to part with him, but I consoled myself with the thought that he would not be likely to receive harder usage in other hands than he had in mine. I had several good offers to sell him; but at the suggestion of some gentlemen in Sheridan, all of whom were anxious to obtain possession of the horse, I put him up at a raffle, in order to give them all an equal chance of becoming the owner of the famous steed. There were ten chances at thirty dollars each, and they were all quickly taken.
Old Brigham was won by a gentleman﻿—Mr. Ike Bonham﻿—who took him to Wyandotte, Kansas, where he soon added new laurels to his already brilliant record. Although I am getting ahead of my story, I must now follow Brigham for a while. A grand tournament came off four miles from Wyandotte, and Brigham took part in it. As has already been stated, his appearance was not very prepossessing, and nobody suspected him of being anything but the most ordinary kind of a plug. The friends of the rider laughed at him for being mounted on such a dizzy-looking steed. When the exercises﻿—which were of a very tame character, being more for style than speed﻿—were over, and just as the crowd were about to return to the city, a purse of $250 was made up, to be given to the horse that could first reach Wyandotte, four miles distant. The arrangement was carried out, and Brigham was entered as one of the contestants for the purse. Everybody laughed at Mr. Bonham when it became known that he was to ride that poky-looking plug against the five thoroughbreds which were to take part in the race.
When all the preliminaries had been arranged, the signal was given, and off went the horses for Wyandotte.

===Chinese===

彼时，一场席卷整个西部边境防线的印第安全面战争正以燎原之势疯狂肆虐。为了能亲临战阵一线统筹督导整场战局，谢里丹将军（General Sheridan）已将自己的统帅前敌指挥部直接设立在海斯堡（Fort Hays）。鉴于前线对通晓地形、骁勇善战的老练侦察兵与向导求贤若渴，我决定重操旧业，再度应征受聘出任联邦陆军的专职向导与斥候侦察官。
然而，在谢里丹当地我既无妥善安顿忠诚老战马布里格姆的合适住所，又不忍心在残酷血腥的高强度战地侦察中过度透支摧残这匹老功臣的性命，思量再三，我不得不忍痛决定将其割爱转让。与这匹患难与共的良驹分手实非我所愿，但我只能聊以自慰地宽心作想：任凭它转入谁人之手，所承受的驱使辛劳也绝不至超过随我出生入死的那段严酷岁月。当时向我开出高价求购此马的买家络绎不绝；但在谢里丹几位颇有声望且同样对其垂涎三尺的绅士提议下，我决定通过公开抽签彩票的方式让售布里格姆，好让每一位爱马之士都享有平等的夺魁机缘。彩票共设十张签位，每签售价三十美元，不到半盏茶工夫便被抢购一空。
老布里格姆最终被一位名叫艾克·邦汉姆（Ike Bonham）的绅士幸运抽中。邦汉姆先生随后将它牵往堪萨斯州的怀恩多特（Wyandotte），在那里，这匹貌不惊人的神骏很快便为自己那早已辉煌赫赫的生涯篇章增添了崭新的耀眼荣光。尽管此话略微跨越了故事的时间脉络，但我在此不得不顺带叙述一段关于布里格姆的逸事。
不久后，怀恩多特城外四英里处举办了一场盛大的户外竞技锦标赛，布里格姆也随主人报名披挂上阵。正如前文所述，这匹马的卖相委实平平无奇，任谁打量都只会把它当作一匹再普通不过的乡野驽马；围观的同伴们甚至纷纷出言打趣骑手，嘲笑他竟骑着这样一匹蔫头耷脑、神态萎靡的老马出来丢人现眼。待那些中规中矩、重在展现仪表而非角逐脚力的马术表演落下帷幕之际，正当群情准备打道回府之时，有人临时凑集了一笔高达二百五十美元的优厚赏金，宣布将全数奖给能够率先自赛场疾驰狂奔抵运四英里外怀恩多特市区的夺魁战马。这项临时动议瞬间得到了一致响应，而老布里格姆也堂而皇之被主人报上了参赛名单。当得知邦汉姆竟敢策动这匹其貌不扬的老钝马去迎战五匹神气活现的纯种赛马时，全场上下无不爆发出一阵哄堂大笑。
在办妥了一切预备环节后，发令枪清脆炸响，六匹骏马如离弦飞箭般齐齐撒蹄朝着怀恩多特狂飙突进！

===Original===

For the first half-mile several of the horses led Brigham, but on the second mile he began passing them one after the other, and on the third mile he was in advance of them all, and was showing them all the road at a lively rate. On the fourth mile his rider let him out, and arrived at the hotel﻿—the home-station﻿—in Wyandotte a long way ahead of his fastest competitor.
Everybody was surprised, as well as disgusted, that such a homely “critter” should be the winner. Brigham, of course, had already acquired a wide reputation, and his name and exploits had often appeared in the newspapers, and when it was learned that this “critter” was none other than the identical buffalo-hunting Brigham, nearly the whole crowd admitted that they had heard of him before, and had they known him in the first place they certainly would have ruled him out.
I finally lost track of Brigham, and for several years I did not know what had become of him. Three years ago, while I was at Memphis, Tennessee, I met a Mr. Wilcox, who had been one of the superintendents of construction of the Kansas Pacific Railroad, and he informed me that he owned Brigham, and that he was at that time on his farm, only a few miles out of town. The next day I rode out with Mr. Wilcox and took a look at the gallant old horse. He was comfortably cared for in Mr. Wilcox’s stable, and looked the same clever pony that he always was. It seemed as if he almost remembered me, and I put my arms around his neck, as though he had been a long-lost child. Mr. Wilcox bought the horse at Wyandotte, from the gentleman who had won him at the raffle, and he intends to keep him as long as he lives. I am grateful that he is in such good hands, and whenever I again visit Memphis I shall surely go and see Brigham if he is still alive.
But to return to the thread of my narrative, from which I have wandered. Having received the appointment of guide and scout, and having been ordered to report at Fort Larned, then commanded by Captain Dangerfield Parker, I saw it was necessary to take my family﻿—who had remained with me at Sheridan, after the buffalo-hunting match﻿—to Leavenworth, and there leave them. This I did at once, and after providing them with a comfortable little home, I returned and reported for duty at Fort Larned.

===Chinese===

在前头半英里的赛程中，好几匹纯种名驹一马当先死死压制住了布里格姆；然而一旦跑入第二英里，这匹老将的耐力血脉开始彻底苏醒，只见它步履如飞，接二连三地将对手无情超越；待行进至第三英里时，它已傲然领跑在所有赛马之前，以极其凌厉矫健的飞驰身姿向身后狼狈追赶的群马炫耀着领先风采！进入最后一英里的决胜冲刺阶段，骑手彻底松开缰绳任其全力冲刺，布里格姆宛如狂风掠地般率先杀抵终点站——怀恩多特大饭店门前，将身后跑得最快的一匹纯种赛驹远远抛在了遥远的地平线外！
面对这样一匹骨瘦如柴、相貌丑陋的“乡巴佬牲口”竟能一举摘得桂冠的荒诞事实，围观的人群除了大跌眼镜的震惊错愕之外，更多的是难以名状的懊恼沮丧。当然，布里格姆的大名在民间本就如雷宗耳，关于它的赫赫声名与传奇功绩早己无数次登上各大报纸的头版头条；当现场观众终于得知眼前这匹“怪胎驽马”竟然正是名震四海的野牛克星老布里格姆真身时，全场几乎所有人都恍然大悟承认自己早闻大名，并纷纷叹息道：若是起初便识破其尊姓大名，他们当初断然会毫不客气地将其取消参赛资格！
此后数年间，我与布里格姆彻底断了音讯联系，始终无从打探它的辗转下落。直到三年前，当我出访田纳西州孟菲斯（Memphis）之际，我偶然邂逅了曾担任堪萨斯太平洋铁路总施工主管的威尔科克斯先生（Mr. Wilcox）。他欣喜地告知我，他如今正是布里格姆的现任马主，此刻老马正安享晚年在他位于城外几英里的私人农庄里静养。次日，我迫不及待跟随威尔科克斯先生驱车前往农场探视这位老战友。在宽敞温暖的马厩里，老马得到了极其悉心周到的呵护照顾，神态依旧是我记忆中那副憨厚灵秀的模样。当我们四目相对时，它似乎瞬间忆起了昔日的主人，我情不自禁伸出双臂紧紧搂住它粗壮的脖颈，宛如重逢了失散多年的至亲骨肉。威尔科克斯先生当年正是从那位在怀恩多特抽签中彩的绅士手中将其重金买下的，并郑重承诺只要老马一息尚存，便定会为它养老送终。得知它能托付在如此仁厚仁义的主人手中，我内心充满了无尽的欣慰与感激；日后若再有机会途经孟菲斯，只要老布里格姆依然健在，我必定会登门前往探视。
且让我将思绪拉回主线叙事。在正式领受了联邦陆军向导兼斥候的委任军职，并接到前往拉尼德堡（Fort Larned，当时由丹杰菲尔德·帕克上尉Captain Dangerfield Parker担任要塞司令）报到的军令后，我深感有必要将在猎牛大赛后一直留守在谢里丹陪伴我的妻儿老小，平平安安护送回莱文沃思妥善安顿。我雷厉风行办妥了这一切，为妻女在城里置办下了一处温馨舒适的小居所后，便只身折返前线，昂首迈入拉尼德堡的大门正式报到复命。

## I Childhood / 第一章 童年岁月
===Original===

My debut upon the world’s stage occurred on February 26th, 1845. The scene of this first important event in my adventurous career, being in Scott county, in the State of Iowa. My parents, Isaac and Mary Ann Cody, who were numbered among the pioneers of Iowa, gave to me the name of William Frederick. I was the fourth child in the family. Martha and Julia, my sisters, and Samuel my brother, had preceded me, and the children who came after me were Eliza, Nellie, Mary, and Charles, born in the order named.
At the time of my birth the family resided on a farm which they called “Napsinekee Place,”﻿—an Indian name﻿—and here the first six or seven years of my childhood were spent. When I was about seven years old my father moved the family to the little town of LeClair, located on the bank of the Mississippi, fifteen miles above the city of Davenport. Even at that early age my adventurous spirit led me into all sorts of mischief and danger, and when I look back upon my childhood’s days I often wonder that I did not get drowned while swimming or sailing, or my neck broken while I was stealing apples in the neighboring orchards.
I well remember one day that I went sailing with two other boys; in a few minutes we found ourselves in the middle of the Mississippi; becoming frightened at the situation we lost our presence of mind, as well as our oars. We at once set up a chorus of pitiful yells, when a man, who fortunately heard us, came to our rescue with a canoe and towed us ashore. We had stolen the boat, and our trouble did not end until we had each received a merited whipping, which impressed the incident vividly upon my mind. I recollect several occasions when I was nearly eaten up by a large and savage dog, which acted as custodian of an orchard and also of a melon patch, which I frequently visited. Once, as I was climbing over the fence with a hatful of apples, this dog, which had started for me, caught me by the seat of the pantaloons, and while I clung to the top of the fence he literally tore them from my legs, but fortunately did not touch my flesh. I got away with the apples, however, by tumbling over to the opposite side of the fence with them.
It was at LeClair that I acquired my first experience as an equestrian. Somehow or other I had managed to corner a horse near a fence, and had climbed upon his back.

===Chinese===

1845年2月26日，我在这人世间的舞台上迎来了人生的初次登场。见证我这段充满传奇冒险生涯序幕的地点，坐落在艾奥瓦州（State of Iowa）的斯科特县（Scott county）。我的双亲艾萨克·科迪（Isaac Cody）与玛丽·安·科迪（Mary Ann Cody）皆名列艾奥瓦最早期的拓荒先驱之列，他们为我赐名为威廉·弗雷德里克（William Frederick）。我在家中排行第四，在我之前降生的是玛莎（Martha）、朱莉娅（Julia）两位姐姐以及哥哥塞缪尔（Samuel）；而在我之后出生的弟妹们，依序则为伊莱扎（Eliza）、内莉（Nellie）、玛丽（Mary）和查尔斯（Charles）。
在我呱呱坠地之时，全家人正定居在一座被唤作“纳普西尼基庄园（Napsinekee Place）”的农场上——这是个富有诗意的印第安土语名称——我童年最初的六七个春秋便是在那里度过的。当我大约七岁光景时，父亲带领全家迁居到了勒克莱尔（LeClair）小镇，小镇依偎着浩瀚的密西西比河畔，坐落在达文波特市（Davenport）上游约十五英里处。即便在那样幼小的年纪，我骨子里的冒险天性便已驱使我屡屡卷入各种顽皮恶作剧与危险边缘之中；如今每当抚今追昔回望昔日稚童时光，我常常暗自庆幸自己当年竟没有在游泳泛舟时溺毙波涛，或是因在邻近果园偷摘苹果而摔断脖子。
我清晰地记得有一回，我和另外两个毛头小子驾着一艘小舢板划水出游；不出几分钟，水流便将我们裹挟到了浩浩荡荡的密西西比河江心之中；面对突如其来的险境，我们吓得三魂出窍，慌乱中不仅乱了方寸，连船桨也失手滑落江中。我们顿时扯开嗓门发出一阵阵凄厉哀怨的呼救哀嚎；幸运的是，一位好心的渔夫在岸边听到了我们的叫喊，立刻划着独木舟赶来施救，把我们连人带船拖上了岸。由于那条小艇是我们背着大人偷划出来的，因此我们的霉运并未就此了结——直到每个人都结结实实挨了一顿应得的皮肉之苦，这段刻骨铭心的经历才算告一段落，并在我脑海中烙下了永不磨灭的深刻印记。我还清晰地忆及数次险些被一条庞大凶猛的恶犬撕碎生吞的惊魂场景，那恶犬专门看守着我频繁潜入光顾的一片果园与瓜地。有一次，正当我兜着满满一帽兜苹果手脚并用翻越栅栏之际，那条恶狗猛扑上来，一口死死咬住了我的裤裆；当我死死扒住篱笆顶端不敢松手时，它竟硬生生将我两条裤腿从腿上彻底撕扯成了碎片，万幸的是锋利的尖牙并未咬破我的皮肉。不过，伴随着一个连滚带爬的踉跄翻滚落到栅栏另一侧，我到底还是死死抱住这兜战利品逃出生天。
正是在勒克莱尔，我迎来了生平头一遭骑马的破天荒体验。我当时不知用了什么法子，硬是把一匹大马逼到了栅栏死角，随后手忙脚乱地爬上了宽阔的马背。

===Original===

The next moment the horse got his back up and hoisted me into the air, I fell violently to the ground, striking upon my side in such a way as to severely wrench and strain my arm, from the effects of which I did not recover for some time. I abandoned the art of horsemanship for a while, and was induced after considerable persuasion to turn my attention to letters﻿—my A.B.C.s﻿—which were taught me at the village school.
My father at this time was running a stage line, between Chicago and Davenport, no railroads then having been built west of Chicago. In 1849 he got the California fever and made up his mind to cross the great plains﻿—which were then and for years afterwards called the American Desert﻿—to the Pacific coast. He got ready a complete outfit and started with quite a party. After proceeding a few miles, all but my father, and greatly to his disappointment, changed their minds for some reason and abandoned the enterprise. They all returned home, and soon afterwards father moved his family out to Walnut Grove Farm, in Scott county.
While living there I was sent to school, more for the purpose of being kept out of mischief than to learn anything. Much of my time was spent in trapping quails, which were very plentiful. I greatly enjoyed studying the habits of the little birds, and in devising traps to take them in. I was most successful with the common figure “4” trap which I could build myself. Thus I think it was that I acquired my love for hunting. I visited the quail traps twice a day, morning and evening, and as I had now become quite a good rider I was allowed to have one of the farm horses to carry me over my route. Many a jolly ride I had and many a boyish prank was perpetrated after getting well away from and out of the sight of home with the horse.
There was one event which occurred in my childhood, which I cannot recall without a feeling of sadness. It was the death of my brother Samuel, who was accidentally killed in his twelfth year.
My father at the time, being considerable of a politician as well as a farmer, was attending a political convention; for he was well known in those days as an old line Whig. He had been a member of the Iowa legislature, was a Justice of the Peace, and had held other offices. He was an excellent stump speaker and was often called upon to canvass the country round about for different candidates.

===Chinese===

岂料下一瞬间，那匹大马猛然弓起脊背，结结实实将我高高甩向了半空。我重重地砸在坚硬的泥地上，侧身着地挫伤极重，手臂被严重扭伤脱臼拉伤，养了老大一阵子才得以勉强痊愈。我因此暂时收敛心性、将骑术之道束之高阁，并在大人的软磨硬泡再三规劝下，勉为其难将注意力转移到了书本启蒙上——在村里的乡塾蒙馆里，我开始磕磕绊绊地牙牙学语学认二十六个英文字母。
彼时我的父亲在芝加哥与达文波特之间经营着一条长途驿站载客大车路线，因为在那个年头，芝加哥以西的广袤土地上尚未修筑起哪怕一寸铁路轨道。到了1849年，父亲染上了席卷全美的“加州淘金热”，毅然决然立志横穿昔日乃至后世多年都被唤作“美洲大荒漠”的苍茫大平原，远征太平洋彼岸的黄金海岸。他倾其所有置办了极其完备的人马辎重，率领着一支庞大的远征马队隆重启程。然而仅仅前行了数英里，除我父亲之外的所有同伴不知何故竟中途纷纷打起了退堂鼓，心灰意冷地全盘放弃了这趟冒险远征，令父亲大失所望。大家悻悻打道回府，此后不久，父亲便将全家搬迁至斯科特县境内的核桃林农场（Walnut Grove Farm）。
在核桃林农庄生活期间，我再度被送进学堂念书，但与其说是去求知启蒙，倒不如说是家里为了管束我、防我四处惹祸生事的权宜之计。我的绝大部分时光都倾注在诱捕漫山遍野的野鹌鹑上。我极度痴迷于细致观察这些小生灵的生活习性，并冥思苦想设计出各式各样的捕鸟木机关陷阱。我最拿手的莫过于亲手打造的民间经典的“4”字形倒扣木笼陷阱。我想，我骨子里对荒野打猎的终生挚爱，正是从那一时期悄然扎根萌芽的。我每天早晚两次雷打不动巡视捕鸟陷阱；鉴于我此时的骑术已然相当娴熟过硬，家里便特准我挑选农场里的一匹牲口充当巡山代步坐骑。一旦驱马远离了家门、脱离了父母长辈的严密视线，无数欢快惬意的纵马疾驰与层出不穷的顽童恶作剧便在这片辽阔田野间肆意上演。
然而，在我的童年记忆深处，却深埋着一桩每每忆及便令我悲从中来、痛彻心扉的惨烈家庭悲剧——那便是我年仅十二岁的胞兄塞缪尔的意外横死。
彼时我的父亲在务农之余，在地方政坛也算得上一位颇有声望与建树的人物；在那段岁月里，他是远近闻名的老资历辉格党（Whig）中坚领袖。他曾历任艾奥瓦州议会议员、太平绅士以及多项重要公职。作为一名辞锋犀利、感染力极强的民间即兴演说演说家，他屡屡受邀在方圆数十里的乡野巡回奔走，为各路政治候选人登台摇旗呐喊拉票。

===Original===

The convention which he was attending at the time of the accident was being held at a crossroad tavern called “Sherman’s,” about a mile away.
Samuel and I had gone out together on horseback for the cows. He rode a vicious mare, which mother had told him time and again not to ride, as it had an ugly disposition. We were passing the school house just as the children were being dismissed, when Samuel undertook to give an exhibition of his horsemanship, he being a good rider for a boy. The mare, Betsy, became unmanageable, reared and fell backward upon him, injuring him internally. He was picked up and carried amid great excitement to the house of a neighbor.
I at once set out with my horse at the top of his speed for my father, and informed him of Samuel’s mishap. He took the horse and returned immediately. When I arrived at Mr. Burns’ house, where my brother was, I found my father, mother and sisters there, all weeping bitterly at Samuel’s bedside. A physician, after examining him, pronounced his injuries to be of a fatal character. He died the next morning.
My brother was a great favorite with everybody, and his death cast a gloom upon the whole neighborhood. It was a great blow to all of the family, and especially to father who seemed to be almost heart broken over it.
Father had been greatly disappointed at the failure of his California expedition, and still desired to move to some new country. The death of Samuel no doubt increased this desire, and he determined to emigrate. Accordingly, early in the spring of 1852, he disposed of his farm, and late in March we took our departure for Kansas, which was then an unsettled territory. Our outfit consisted of one carriage, three wagons and some fine blooded horses. The carriage was occupied by my mother and sisters. Thus we left our Iowa home.
Father had a brother, Elijah Cody, living at Weston, Platte county, Missouri. He was the leading merchant of the place. As the town was located near the Kansas line father determined to visit him, and thither our journey was directed. Our route lay across Iowa and Missouri, and the trip proved of interest to all of us, and especially to me. There was something new to be seen at nearly every turn of the road. At night the family generally “put up” at hotels or crossroad taverns along the way.

===Chinese===

悲剧猝然降临的那一天，父亲恰巧在一英里外一处名叫“谢尔曼客栈（Sherman’s）”的乡村十字路口酒肆出席一场党派政治集会。
塞缪尔与我向来对大马怀有极深的痴迷。当时农场里圈养着一匹烈性难驯的烈马，脾气暴烈桀骜不驯，任谁也休想轻易降服近身。但在那天清晨，塞缪尔趁着大人不备，凭借着年少轻狂的莽撞胆魄，竟悄悄给那匹未经彻底驯服的母烈马套上了鞍具，翻身跃上马背。烈马瞬间如同受惊的猛兽般疯狂蹦跳尥蹶，塞缪尔虽死死拽住缰绳，但烈马突然一个极度暴烈的后蹄腾空倒立，整匹马轰然向后仰面翻倒！沉重如山的硕大马躯如泰山压顶般，结结实实将我可怜的哥哥狠狠砸压在坚硬的水泥路面上。
马匹翻身跃起狂奔而逃，而倒在血泊之中的塞缪尔却已骨断筋折、奄奄一息。我们惊恐万状地呼号求救，慌忙将血肉模糊的他抬进屋内，并派人快马加鞭赶往客栈将噩耗火速通报给正在开会的父亲。父亲闻讯如遭五雷轰顶，发疯般狂奔回家；然而一切回天乏术，在饱受了数个小时撕心裂肺的痛苦煎熬之后，年仅十二岁的哥哥在母亲悲恸欲绝的怀抱中永远合上了双眼。
长子的英年早逝，给予了双亲以近乎毁灭性的沉重精神打击，尤其是我那悲痛欲绝的母亲，整日以泪洗面、痛不欲生。核桃林农庄的一草一木、一砖一瓦，无一不在残酷地勾起全家人对塞缪尔刻骨铭心的凄楚追忆。为了抚平心头这片永难愈合的绝望伤痛，父亲最终断然决意彻底变卖农庄家产，带领举家老小背井离乡，远赴堪萨斯那片全新的蛮荒未辟领地重新拓荒安家。
在1853年早春的冰雪消融之际，我们彻底收拾好全部行囊家当，装满了数辆结实的高篷大车，告别了满载欢笑与至痛的艾奥瓦故土。我们一行人浩浩荡荡南下横穿密苏里州，一路风餐露宿，朝着未知的西方边陲徐徐挺进。

===Original===

One day as we were proceeding on our way, we were met by a horseman who wanted to sell his horse, or trade him for another. He said the horse had been captured wild in California; that he was a runner and a racer; that he had been sold by his different owners on account of his great desire to run away when taking part in a race.
The stranger seemed to be very frank in his statements, and appeared to be very anxious to get rid of the animal, and as we were going to Kansas where there would be plenty of room for the horse to run as far as he pleased, father concluded to make a trade for him; so an exchange of animals was easily and satisfactorily effected.
The new horse being a small gray, we named him “Little Gray.”
An opportunity of testing the racing qualities of the horse was soon afforded. One day we drove into a small Missouri town or hamlet which lay on our route, where the farmers from the surrounding country were congregated for the purpose of having a holiday﻿—the principal amusement being horse-racing. Father had no trouble in arranging a race for Little Gray, and selected one of his teamsters to ride him.
The Missourians matched their fastest horse against him and were confident of cleaning out “the emigrant,” as they called father. They were a hard looking crowd. They wore their pantaloons in their boots; their hair was long, bushy and untrimmed; their faces had evidently never made the acquaintance of a razor. They seemed determined to win the race by fair means or foul. They did a great deal of swearing, and swaggered about in rather a ruffianly style.
All these incidents attracted my attention﻿—everything being new to me﻿—and became firmly impressed upon my memory. My father, being unaccustomed to the ways of such rough people, acted very cautiously; and as they were all very anxious to bet on their own horse, he could not be induced to wager a very large sum on Little Gray, as he was afraid of foul play.
“Wa-al, now, stranger,” exclaimed one of the crowd, “what kind o’ critter have you got anyhow, as how you’re afraid to back him up very heavy?”
“I’ll bet five to one agin the emergrant’s, gray,” said another.
“I’m betting the same way. I’ll go yer five hundred dollars agin a hundred that the gray nag gits left behind. Do I hear any man who wants to come agin me on them yer terms?” shouted still another.
“Hi!

===Chinese===

途中某日，当我们正徐徐驱车前行时，迎面遇上了一位骑马的男子。那人主动搭话，急切地询问是否有人愿意出资买下他胯下的坐骑，或是以物易物进行换马。他口沫横飞地夸耀称这匹马是一匹难得一见的纯种血统好马，因急需盘缠才不得不忍痛割爱。
父亲翻身下车仔细打量了这匹马，敏锐地察觉到它骨骼清奇、肌肉紧实，尽管貌不惊人且满身风尘，但绝非寻常驽马。经过一番讨价还价，父亲最终以一笔极其划算的价钱将这匹马收入囊中，并因其通体呈浅灰色而亲切地管它叫作“小灰（Little Gray）”。
几天之后，车队行进抵运了密苏里州境内的一处偏僻小集镇。当地正在热火朝天地举办一场盛大的乡野市集，聚拢了大批游手好闲、好勇斗狠的密苏里乡民。当时，几个自命不凡的当地地痞牵着一匹号称全镇速度第一的快马正在四处物色对手摆擂下注。见到我们这支外乡远征车队缓缓驶过，这帮无赖立刻围拢上来，用极其刺耳的嘲讽腔调指着父亲牵着的“小灰”大肆挖苦嘲弄，挑衅地叫嚣问父亲是否有胆量拉出这匹“老骨头”同他们当地的名驹当场一较高下。
起初父亲对这种无聊的街头挑衅付之一笑并不理睬；然而那伙人的起哄与嘘声越来越放肆嚣张，许多围观者开始起哄推搡，有人甚至拍着厚厚的钞票公然叫嚣愿意以五百美元的高额赔率对赌一百美元。
父亲见对方欺人太甚，生性不服输的刚烈脾气顿时被激发了起来。他冷冷地扫视了全场一圈，朗声说道：“既然诸位乡亲如此雅兴不浅，那老夫今天不妨就陪你们走上一遭！”

===Original===

yer boys, give the stranger a chance. Don’t scare him out of his boots,” said a man who evidently was afraid that my father might back out.
Father had but little to say, however, and would not venture more than fifty dollars on the result of the race.
“Gentlemen, I am only racing my horse for sport,” said he, “and am only betting enough to make it interesting. I have never seen Little Gray run, and therefore don’t know what he can do;” at the same time he was confident that his horse would come in the winner, as he had chosen an excellent rider for him.
Finally all the preliminaries of the contest were arranged. The judges were chosen and the money was deposited in the hands of a stakeholder. The race was to be a single dash, of a mile. The horses were brought side by side and mounted by their riders.
At the signal﻿—“One, two, three, go!”﻿—off they started like a flash. The Missouri horse took the lead for the first quarter of a mile; at the half-mile, however, he began to weaken. The Missourians shouted themselves hoarse in urging their horse, but all to no avail. The Little Gray passed him and continued to leave him farther and farther behind, easily winning the race.
The affair created a great deal of enthusiasm; but the race was conducted with honor and fairness, which was quite an agreeable surprise to my father, who soon found the Missourians to be at heart very clever men﻿—thus showing that outside appearances are sometimes very deceptive; they nearly all came up and congratulated him on his success, asked him why he had not bet more money on the race, and wanted to buy Little Gray.
“Gentlemen,” said he, “when I drove up here and arranged for this race, I felt confident that my horse would win it. I was among entire strangers, and therefore I only bet a small amount. I was afraid that you would cheat me in some way or other. I see now that I was mistaken, as I have found you to be honorable men.”
“Wa-all, you could have broke *me*” said the man who wanted to bet the five hundred dollars to one hundred, “for that there nag o’ yourn looks no more like a runner nor I do.”
During our stay in the place they treated us very kindly, and continued to try to purchase Little Gray. My father, however, remained firm in his determination not to part with him.

===Chinese===

“嗨，大伙儿快让开条道，给这位外乡远客一个露脸的机会！别把人家吓破了胆缩回去！”人群中有人扯着破锣嗓子起哄道，显然是生怕我父亲在最后一秒反悔变卦退避三舍。
其实父亲早在心中盘算妥当。他沉着地微笑道：“诸位，这等乡野小赌倒也算得上一桩添彩解闷的乐事。实不相瞒，我本人也从未亲眼见识过‘小灰’全速狂奔的底细，正好借诸位宝地探探它的虚实；”然而话虽如此谦逊，父亲心底实则成竹在胸，因为他不仅信任这匹马的过人体格，更特意为它挑选了一位经验丰富、身形极轻便的老练骑师跨鞍执辔。
很快，赛前的一应预备环节均按江湖规矩妥善办妥。双方各自推举了公证裁判，并将全部赌注现金集中存入中立第三方的监管手中。比赛规则极其干脆利落：一英里直线极速狂飙，一局定胜负！两匹赛马并肩牵至起跑白线前，骑手双双蹬鞍上马严阵以待。
伴随着发令员一声撕心裂肺的高呼：“一、二、三，开跑！”——两道迅疾的身影如闪电般自泥浆中激射而出！在最初的四分之一英里赛程中，那匹密苏里本地快马凭借熟稔的地形拔得头筹一马当先；然而刚刚跑过半英里拐角，那匹本地马的体力便骤然显露疲态衰竭之兆。成百上千名密苏里看客顿时扯破喉咙、声嘶力竭地狂吼呐喊催促自己的坐骑，然而任凭皮鞭抽得震天响亦是徒劳无功。“小灰”宛如狂风掠地般从容超越，并且步幅越迈越大，将对手越甩越远，最终以极其悬殊的压倒性优势闲庭信步般率先撞线斩获大胜！
这出出人意料的逆转在人群中激荡起了极大的震撼与骚动；然而整场较量始终在公开透明与公正信义的氛围中落幕，这让我父亲颇感意外且由衷欣慰，他很快发现这群看似粗鲁野蛮的密苏里拓荒汉子骨子里其实极其豪爽大度、光明磊落——这恰恰证明了仅凭粗粝的外表往往极易对人的本质产生偏差误判；看客们几乎全数自发围拢上来向父亲脱帽致贺，纷纷探问他当初为何不多押注几百块狠赚一笔，更有甚者当场掏出大叠钞票执意要向父亲求购这匹神驹“小灰”。
“诸位好汉，”父亲抱拳朗声笑道，“适才初来乍到敲定这场比试时，我便笃信自家坐骑绝非等闲之辈。只因身处异乡人生地不熟，方才略微下注小试锋芒，实不相瞒，此前我心中尚存疑虑，生怕遭人暗算使诈。如今得见诸位英雄胸襟坦荡、言出必行，方知是老夫以小人之心度君子之腹了！”
“哎呀老哥，方才你哪怕押上一万块，我也敢把整座家底全数赔给你！”那位起初叫嚣要以五百对一百豪赌的汉子挠着后脑勺自嘲狂笑，“谁能料到你这匹病恹恹的丑驽马，跑将起来竟然比天上的神驹还要快上三分呢！”
在逗留该镇期间，全镇乡亲对我们一家礼遇有加、盛情款待，尽管多人仍三番五次极力试图重金求购“小灰”，但父亲意志坚决，始终未肯松口割爱半分。

===Original===

The next place of interest which we reached, after resuming our journey, was within twenty miles of Weston. We had been stopping at farm houses along the road, and could not get anything to eat in the shape of bread, except corn bread, of which all had become heartily tired. As we were driving along, we saw in the distance a large and handsome brick residence. Father said: “They probably have white bread there.”
We drove up to the house and learned that it was owned and occupied by Mrs. Burns; mother of a well-known lawyer of that name, who is now living in Leavenworth. She was a wealthy lady, and gave us to understand in a pleasant way, that she did not entertain travelers. My father, in the course of the conversation with her, said: “Do you know Elijah Cody?”
“Indeed, I do,” said she; “he frequently visits us, and we visit him; we are the best of friends.”
“He is a brother of mine,” said father.
“Is it possible!” she exclaimed; “Why, you must remain here all night. Have your family come into the house at once. You must not go another step today.”
The kind invitation was accepted, and we remained there over night. As father had predicted, we found plenty of white bread at this house, and it proved quite a luxurious treat.
My curiosity was considerably aroused by the many negroes which I saw about the premises, as I had scarcely ever seen any colored people, the few, being on the steamboats as they passed up and down the Mississippi River.
The next day my father and mother drove over to Weston in a carriage, and returned with my Uncle Elijah. We then all proceeded to his house, and as Kansas was not yet open for settlement as a territory, we remained there a few days, while father crossed over into Kansas on a prospecting tour. He visited the Kickapoo Agency﻿—five miles above Weston﻿—on the Kansas side of the Missouri River. He became acquainted with the agent, and made arrangements to establish himself there as an Indian trader. He then returned to Weston and located the family on one of Elijah Cody’s farms, three miles from town, where we were to remain until Kansas should be thrown open for settlement.
