After completing these arrangements, he established a trading post at Salt Creek Valley, in Kansas, four miles from the Kickapoo Agency.

===Chinese===

重整鞍辔继续踏上征途之后，我们抵运的下一个富有深意的重要驿站，已然逼近至距韦斯顿不足二十英里之处。连日来我们一家老小寄宿于沿途的农舍寒舍，顿顿入口的干粮除了一成不变、粗糙难咽的玉米干饼之外别无他物，全家人早就吃得倒尽胃口、苦不堪言。正当马车在泥泞大道上缓缓颠簸之际，远处开阔的林荫尽头赫然映现出一座气派恢宏、精美典雅的红砖双层豪宅官邸。父亲眼睛一亮，不由得打趣道：“像这等体面富庶的人家，家里八成能藏着香喷喷的精面细粮白面包吧！”
车队缓缓驶抵门廊前停驻，经打探得知，这处华宅的主人乃是当地名门伯恩斯夫人（Mrs. Burns）——她的爱子正是如今名震莱文沃思法律界的著名大律师。这位贵妇家资巨万、仪态万方，但她以极其客气婉转且彬彬有礼的优雅言辞明确告知我们：寒舍向来从不破例开门接纳过路行旅客商。父亲在与她寒暄周旋的闲谈间，看似漫不经心地随口问了一句：“夫人，不知您是否知晓以利亚·科迪（Elijah Cody）此人？”
“哎呀，何止是知晓！”夫人脸上瞬间绽放出惊喜交集的灿烂笑靥，“以利亚先生可是我们宅邸常来常往的座上贵客，两家过从甚密，乃是莫逆至交之友啊！”
“实不相瞒，以利亚正是家兄，”父亲微微欠身作答。
“天底下竟有这等巧事！”贵妇失声惊呼起来，“哎呀呀，那你们今夜无论如何必须赏光留宿寒舍！快，快请阖府家眷即刻移步登堂入室！今天你们一步也不准再往前走了！”
面对这等突如其来的盛情难却，我们欣然接受了这番款待，并在这座豪宅中安度了极其舒坦惬意的一宿。正如父亲此前幽默预言的那样，主人家毫不吝啬地为我们端上了堆积如山的松软纯白精粉面包，对于饥肠辘辘的我们而言，这顿丰盛的大餐无异于久旱逢甘霖的人间至味珍馐。
在此期间，穿梭忙碌于宅邸庄园内外的大群黑人仆从极大地激发起我强烈的好奇心；在此之前，我几乎从未亲眼目睹过深色皮肤的有色人种，仅有的模糊记忆也不过是在密西西比河上来回穿梭的巨型蒸汽明轮客轮上偶见的一两抹匆匆身影。
翌日清晨，父亲与母亲共乘一辆精致马车赶赴韦斯顿，并很快将我的以利亚叔父一同接了回来。两兄弟阔别重逢感慨万千，全家随即移驾前往叔父的宅邸安顿。鉴于当时的堪萨斯领地尚未正式对外颁布移民开荒法案，我们便在叔父家宽心盘桓了数日，其间父亲则孤身渡江踏入堪萨斯腹地展开全方位的实地拓荒踏勘。他一路实地考察了位于密苏里河堪萨斯一侧、坐落在韦斯顿上游五英里处的基卡普印第安代办所（Kickapoo Agency）。父亲在当地迅速结识了代办官员，并顺理成章达成了在此地立足开办印第安边贸商栈的一揽子协议。随后他从容折返韦斯顿，将全家老小临时妥善安顿在以利亚叔父名下一座距镇区三英里开外的肥沃庄园里，静候堪萨斯大门正式敞开的那一刻。待一切后方安顿妥帖之后，父亲快马加鞭赶赴堪萨斯境内的盐溪谷（Salt Creek Valley）——该地距基卡普代办所仅四英里之遥——正式挂牌营建起了属于我们自己的全新印第安边贸商栈。

===Original===

One day, after he had been absent some little time, he came home and said that he had bought two ponies for me, and that next morning he would take me over into Kansas. This was pleasant news, as I had been very anxious to go there with him, and the fact that I was now the owner of two ponies made me feel very proud. That night I could not sleep a wink. In the morning I was up long before the sun, and after an early breakfast, father and I started out on our trip. Crossing the Missouri River at the Rialto Ferry, we landed in Kansas and passed along to Fort Leavenworth, four miles distant.

===Chinese===

某天傍晚，在离家外出奔波了好一阵子之后，父亲风尘仆仆推门归家，脸上洋溢着按捺不住的欣喜神色。他兴高采烈地向我宣布，他特意为我买下了整整两匹神气活现的专属矮种小马，并郑重承诺明早便要亲自带我横渡大江、初探神秘莫测的堪萨斯荒原！这无异于天降鸿福的天大喜讯，连日来我朝思暮想无时无刻不在渴望着随同父亲赴前线探险，而如今自己竟然摇身一变、成为了名正言顺坐拥两匹爱驹的少年马主，这更令我胸中充盈着无比自豪与荣耀的狂喜！当夜我兴奋得在床榻上翻来覆去，愣是彻夜未曾合眼哪怕一秒。清晨红日尚未跃出地平线，我便已一骨碌翻身下床披挂整齐；在草草咽下早点之后，父亲与我策马并肩迈向征途。我们在里阿尔托轮渡（Rialto Ferry）渡口顺利横穿奔腾浩荡的密西西比密苏里河，四蹄踏上了向往已久的堪萨斯沃土，并一路意气风发地径直奔向四英里外威名赫赫的莱文沃思堡。

## XXXII Conclusion / 第三十二章 结语
===Original===

After my arrival at North Platte, I found that the ranchmen or cattlemen, had organized a regular annual “roundup,” to take place in the spring of the year.
The word “roundup” is derived from the fact that during the winter months the cattle become scattered over a vast tract of land, and the ranchmen assemble together in the spring to sort out and each secure his own stock. They form a large circle, often of a circumference of two hundred miles, and drive the cattle towards a common centre, where, all the stock being branded, each owner can readily separate his own from the general herd, and then he drives them to his own ranch.
In this cattle driving business is exhibited some most magnificent horsemanship, for the “cowboys,” as they are called, are invariably skillful and fearless horsemen﻿—in fact only a most expert rider could be a cowboy, as it requires the greatest dexterity and daring in the saddle to cut a wild steer out of the herd.
Major North was awaiting me, upon my arrival at North Platte, having with him our own horses and men. Other cattle owners, such as Keith and Barton, Coe and Carter, Jack Pratt, the Walker Brothers, Guy and Sim Lang, Arnold and Ritchie and a great many others with their outfits, were assembled and were ready to start on the roundup.
My old friend Dave Perry, who had presented Buckskin Joe to me, and who resided at North Platte, was most anxious to go with us for pleasure, and Frank North told him he could, and have plenty of fun, provided he would furnish his own horses, provisions and bedding, and do the usual work required of a cowboy. This, Dave was willing to undertake. We found him to be a good fellow in camp, and excellent company.
As there is nothing but hard work on these roundups, having to be in the saddle all day, and standing guard over the cattle at night, rain or shine, I could not possibly find out where the fun came in, that North had promised me. But it was an exciting life, and the days sped rapidly by; in six weeks we found ourselves at our own ranch on Dismal River, the roundup having proved a great success, as we had found all our cattle and driven them home.
This work being over, I proposed to spend a few weeks with my family at North Platte, for the purpose of making their better acquaintance, for my long and continued absence from home made me a comparative stranger under my own rooftree.

===Chinese===

抵达北普拉特（North Platte）后，我发现当地的牧场主与牧牛大亨们正在紧锣密鼓地筹备一年一度在春季如期举行的例行“大围聚（roundup）”。
“大围聚”这一行话源于草原畜牧的现实规律：在漫长严酷的冬季数月里，放牧的牛群往往会漫无边际地四散散落在辽阔无垠的荒原各个角落；到了万物复苏的阳春时节，各家牧场主便联合纠集人马，齐心协力将牛群集中围拢并逐一拣选辨识出属于自家的牲口。他们排开一条横跨大平原的巨大合围圆弧线，周长往往广达两百英里之巨，从四面八方将漫山遍野的牛群一路向着中央集结点徐徐驱赶；待牛群全数聚拢于一处时，由于每头牲口身上皆烙有各家独一无二的专属烙印，每位牧主便能轻而易举地将属于自己的牛只从万头攒动的庞大混合牛群中剥离甄别出来，随后各自驱赶回自家的牧场圈禁。
在这等赶牛大合围的壮观场面中，往往能尽览西部最登峰造极、令人叹为观止的神乎其神马术；因为人称“牛仔（cowboys）”的这群荒原健儿，无一不是技艺绝伦、悍不畏死的绝顶骑术大师——事实上，唯有最出类拔萃的顶级驭马高手方能胜任合格牛仔的头衔，毕竟在颠簸狂暴的马鞍上飞身将一头性情凶悍的野牛犊精准从受惊狂奔的狂暴牛群中硬生生剥离截击出来，需要何等超凡入圣的过人敏捷与视死如归的惊人胆魄！
待我抵运北普拉特之际，诺斯少校（Major North）早已率领着我们自家的马匹人马在当地恭候多时。包括基思与巴顿（Keith and Barton）、科与卡特（Coe and Carter）、杰克·普拉特（Jack Pratt）、沃克兄弟（Walker Brothers）、盖伊与西姆·朗（Guy and Sim Lang）、阿诺德与里奇（Arnold and Ritchie）在内的众多其他实力雄厚的牧场主，亦各自率领精锐装备马队纷至沓来齐聚一堂，摩拳擦掌做好了投身大围聚的万全准备。
我定居在北普拉特的老挚友戴夫·佩里（Dave Perry）——当年正是他将心爱战马“鹿皮乔（Buckskin Joe）”慷慨相赠于我——按捺不住心头的狂热，极度渴望随同我们一道策马出行，去大平原上寻欢猎奇一番。弗兰克·诺斯笑着告知他：同去自无不可且包管趣味无穷，前提是他必须自备马匹、干粮口粮与铺盖卷，并且必须不折不扣承担起一名寻常牛仔所当履行的繁重劳役。戴夫毫不犹豫满口答应。在随后的行军扎营中，我们发现他不仅是个能吃苦耐劳的好小伙，更是一位在篝火旁谈笑风生的绝佳旅伴。
然而，这类大围聚的实况充斥着难以想象的极度艰辛劳碌：整天整夜黏在颠簸生疼的马鞍上风餐露宿，无论狂风暴雨还是烈日当空，入夜后还得荷枪实弹轮番通宵看护暴躁受惊的庞大牛群。我无论如何也琢磨不透，诺斯当初向我打包票许诺的所谓“无上乐趣”究竟藏在哪个角落！不过，这终究是一段热血沸腾、充满激情的动荡岁月，时光在马蹄飞踏中如白驹过隙般匆匆流逝；转眼六周过去，我们押解着庞大的牛群胜利凯旋抵运了我们坐落在迪斯马尔河（Dismal River）畔的自家专属牧场。此番大围聚取得了空前圆满的大捷，我们不仅寻获了全部失散牛只，更毫发无损地将其尽数驱赶回槽。
繁重劳作告一段落后，我打算在北普拉特陪伴家人盘桓数周，借此机会好好同他们热络亲近一番，毕竟常年累月在外奔波征战，已令我在这座属于自己的屋檐之下几乎沦为了一名形同陌路的匆匆过客。

===Original===

One great source of pleasure to me was that my wife was delighted with the home I had given her amid the prairies of the far west. Soon after my arrival, my sisters Nellie and May, came to make us a visit, and a delightful time we all had during their stay. When they left us, I accompanied them to their home in Denver, Colorado, where I passed several days visiting old friends and scenes.
Returning to Ogallala I purchased from Bill Phant, an extensive cattle drover from Texas, a herd of cattle, which I drove to my ranch on the Dismal River, after which I bade my partner and the boys goodbye, and started for the Indian Territory to procure Indians for my Dramatic Combination for the season of 1878﻿–﻿79.
En route to the Territory, I paid a long promised visit to my sisters, Julia﻿—Mrs. J. A. Goodman﻿—and Eliza﻿—Mrs. George M. Myers﻿—who reside in Kansas, the state which the reader will remember was my boyhood home.
Having secured my Indian actors, and along with them Mr. O. A. Burgess, a government interpreter, and Ed A. Burgess, known as the “Boy Chief of the Pawnees,” I started for Baltimore, where I organized my combination, and which was the largest troupe I had yet had on the road; opening in that city at the Opera House, under the management of Hon. John T. Ford, and then started on a southern tour, playing in Washington, Richmond and as far south as Savannah, Georgia, where we were brought to a sudden halt, owing to the yellow fever which was then cruelly raging in the beautiful cities of the “Land of the cotton and the cane.”
While playing in Washington, I suddenly learned from a reporter﻿—Washington newspaper men know everything﻿—that my Indians were to be seized by the Government and sent back to their agency. Finding that there was foundation for the rumor, I at once sought General Carl Shurz, Secretary of the Interior, and asked him if he intended depriving me of my Indian actors. He said that he did, as the Indians were away from their reservation without leave. I answered that I had had Indians with me the year before and nothing had been said about it; but Commissioner Haight replied that the Indians were the “wards of the government,” and were not allowed off of their reservation.

===Chinese===

令我感到无比由衷欣慰与宽怀的是，爱妻对我在这遥远西部大草原中央为她置办营建的温馨家园充满了欢喜与热爱。在我返家后不久，我的两个妹妹内莉（Nellie）与梅（May）亦专程远道前来看望我们，在她们做客逗留的岁月里，全家人沉浸在一片其乐融融、温馨惬意的欢声笑语之中。待到依依惜别之际，我亲自策马护送她们返回科罗拉多州丹佛市的居所，并在丹佛逗留徜徉了数日，流连于昔日的旧友故交与熟悉街景之间。
折返奥加拉拉（Ogallala）之后，我从德克萨斯大牲畜贩子比尔·芬特（Bill Phant）手中斥资收购了一整群健壮的优质牛群，并亲自将其驱赶护送至位于迪斯马尔河畔的自有牧场。在此之后，我依依不舍挥别了我的合伙人与牧场弟兄们，整装启程奔赴印第安领地，旨在为即将拉开序幕的1878年至1879年演季的“巡回戏剧联合剧团”物色招揽一批真正的印第安土著特技演员。
在取道奔赴印第安领地的漫漫旅途中，我顺道登门拜访了我那两位定居在堪萨斯州的胞姐胞妹——朱莉娅（Julia，即J·A·古德曼夫人Mrs. J. A. Goodman）与伊莱扎（Eliza，即乔治·M·迈尔斯夫人Mrs. George M. Myers）——如前文所述，堪萨斯正是见证我少年磨砺的第二故乡，而此番探视也是我许诺已久的一桩夙愿。
在顺利招募到了心仪的印第安特技演员班底，并随团聘请到了联邦政府认证的资深印第安语翻译O·A·伯吉斯先生（Mr. O. A. Burgess）以及被尊称为“波尼族少年酋长”的埃德·A·伯吉斯（Ed A. Burgess）之后，我率队挥师直奔巴尔的摩。我们在那里正式完成了大联合剧团的组建整编，其阵容规模堪称我历年来率队巡演之最；剧团在尊贵的约翰·T·福特（Hon. John T. Ford）阁下麾下的歌剧院盛大开锣首演，随即顺势拉开了波澜壮阔的南方巡演大幕，先后在华盛顿、里士满等地登台献艺，一路凯歌高奏挺进至最南端的佐治亚州萨凡纳（Savannah, Georgia）。然而行抵萨凡纳后，全团的巡演步伐却骤然被迫画上了休止符——当时一场空前惨烈恐怖的黄热病疫情，正无情残酷地肆虐吞噬着这片被誉为“棉花与甘蔗之乡”的南国锦绣名城。
在华盛顿登台献演期间，一位嗅觉灵敏的报界记者——华盛顿的新闻记者向来无所不知、无孔不入——悄悄向我走漏风声，称联邦政府内政部正密谋强行查扣我麾下的印第安演员，并准备将他们强行遣送遣返原籍代办所。经多方核实证实空穴来风绝非虚妄之后，我当机立断径直登门拜会了时任内政部长的卡尔·舒尔茨将军（General Carl Schurz），开门见山严正质问他是否当真蓄意剥夺我合法雇佣印第安演职人员的正当权益。部长神色严肃地给出了肯定答复，言之凿凿宣称这些印第安人系未经军政当局审批擅自脱离保留区管制。我据理力争驳斥道，上一年度我便曾携同印第安班底四处巡回商演，当时当局从未提出过半点异议；然而在一旁的印第安事务局局长海特专员（Commissioner Haight）却板起面孔公事公办地声称：印第安人系“合众国政府法定监护之被监护人”，依律决不允许擅自越出保留区雷池半步！

===Original===

I told the Commissioner that the Indians were frequently off of their reservations out west, as I had a distinct remembrance of meeting them upon several occasions “on the war path,” and furthermore I thought I was benefitting the Indians as well as the government, by taking them all over the United States, and giving them a correct idea of the customs, life, etc., of the pale faces, so that when they returned to their people they could make known all they had seen.
After a conversation with the Secretary of the Interior, the Commissioner concluded to allow me to retain the Indians, by appointing me Indian Agent, provided I would give the necessary bonds, and pledge myself to return them in safety to their agency﻿—which terms I agreed to.
From Savannah, Georgia, having changed my route on account of the yellow fever, I jumped my entire company to Philadelphia, and at once continued on a northeastern tour, having arranged with the well-known author and dramatist, Colonel Prentiss Ingraham, to write a play for me.
The drama entitled *The Knight of the Plains, or Buffalo Bill’s Best Trail*, was first produced at New Haven, Conn.; it has proved a great success, and I expect to play it in England, where I purpose to go next season on a theatrical tour, having been urged to do so by my many friends abroad.
After a successful tour of six weeks on the Pacific Slope, thus ending the season of 1878﻿–﻿79, I am at my home at North Platte, Nebraska, for the summer; and thus ends the account of my career as far as it has gone.

===Chinese===

我毫不客气地当面反唇相讥反驳专员先生道：在辽阔西部的边陲前线，印第安人私自擅离保留区早已是家常便饭见怪不怪，因为在我的深刻戎马记忆中，我曾无数次在他们全副武装踏上“嗜血征战之路”时与他们在崇山峻岭间兵戎相见过招！此外我进一步严正阐明，我率领这群印第安同胞巡游游历全美各大都会，使他们得以亲身体验并切实领略“苍白面孔”文明世界的风土民情、社会生活与昌盛规制，待他们日后满载见闻回归部落乡梓之际，便能化作文明传播的和平桥梁将所见所闻广布族人，这无论对于印第安族群自身还是对于合众国政府的长治久安而言，皆堪称功在千秋的善莫大焉之举！
在与内政部长进行了一番深入密谈磋商之后，局长最终妥协让步，敲定了一套折中方案：特准我继续保留并率领这批印第安演员随团演出，破格委任我本人兼任特别“印第安代办（Indian Agent）”一职，条件是我必须依法签署并缴纳充足巨额的履约保函保证金，以人格担保届时定将全员平平安安毫发无损地护送归还回各自的部落代办所——对此项开明条款，我欣然应允并当场签字画押。
离开佐治亚州萨凡纳后，鉴于南方黄热病疫情严峻，我果断调整了行军巡演路线，率领整个庞大剧团星夜兼程大转移奔赴宾夕法尼亚州的费城，并马不停蹄开启了东北各州的连轴巡演之旅。在此期间，我特邀文坛名宿、著名军旅剧作家普伦蒂斯·英格拉姆上校（Colonel Prentiss Ingraham）为我量身定制打造了一部全新的传奇话剧大作。
这部题为《平原骑士，或野牛比尔的最佳行踪》（The Knight of the Plains, or Buffalo Bill’s Best Trail）的恢宏传奇戏剧，在康涅狄格州的纽黑文（New Haven, Conn.）大剧院隆重举行了世界首演；一经推出便轰动全城大获全胜，赢得了空前绝后的极高盛誉。眼下我正筹备在下一演季率团远渡重洋跨过大西洋，赴英格兰本土展开宏大的戏剧巡回展演，而这也正是大洋彼岸无数殷切期盼的海外挚友多年来对我不遗余力力邀倡议的宏愿。
在顺利终结了在太平洋沿岸地区（Pacific Slope）为期六周的盛大巡回公演之后，波澜壮阔的1878年至1879年演出季至此宣告圆满功成身退。此刻的我，正安坐在内布拉斯加州北普拉特的温馨府邸中恬淡度夏；而我截至目前为止的人生传奇纪事，亦在此暂且告一段落。

## XXXI Dangerous Work / 第三十一章 凶险差事
===Original===

One evening while we were in camp on the Yellowstone at the mouth of Powder River, I was informed that the commanding officers had selected Louis Richard, a half breed, and myself to accompany General Mills on a scouting expedition on the steamer *Far West*, down the Yellowstone as far as Glendive Creek. We were to ride on the pilot house and keep a sharp lookout on both sides of the river for Indian trails that might have crossed the stream. The idea of scouting on a steamboat was indeed a novel one to me, and I anticipated a pleasant trip.
At daylight next morning we reported on board the steamer to General Mills, who had with him four or five companies of his regiment. We were somewhat surprised when he asked us where our horses were, as we had not supposed that horses would be needed if the scouting was to be done on the steamer. He said we might need them before we got back, and thereupon we had the animals brought on board. In a few minutes we were booming down the river, at the rate of about twenty miles an hour.
The steamer *Far West* was commanded by Captain Grant Marsh, whom I found to be a “brick.” I had often heard of him, for he was and is yet one of the best known river captains in the country. He it was who, with his steamer the *Far West*, transported the wounded men from the battle of the Little Big Horn to Fort Abraham Lincoln on the Missouri River, and on that trip he made the fastest steamboat time on record. He was a skillful and experienced pilot, handling his boat with remarkable dexterity.
While Richard and myself were at our stations on the pilot house, the steamer with a full head of steam went flying past islands, around bends, over sand bars, at a rate that was exhilarating. Presently I thought I could see horses grazing in a distant bend of the river and I reported the fact to General Mills, who asked Captain Marsh if he could land the boat near a large tree which he pointed out to him.
“Yes, sir; I can land her there, and make her climb the tree if necessary,” said he.
On reaching the spot designated, General Mills ordered two companies ashore, while Richard and myself were ordered to take our horses off the boat and push out as rapidly as possible to see if there were Indians in the vicinity.

===Chinese===

一天傍晚，当我们在保德河（Powder River）河口附近的黄石河（Yellowstone）畔扎营时，我接到上级通报：指挥官们挑中了混血儿路易斯·理查德（Louis Richard）与我，随同米尔斯将军（General Mills）搭乘“远西号”（Far West）轮船执行一次侦察任务，顺黄石河直下直到格兰戴夫溪（Glendive Creek）。我们的职责是在舵手室顶上放哨，密切监视大河两岸，搜寻可能横渡过河的印第安人（Indians）踪迹。乘着轮船去侦察，对我而言委实新鲜，我也满心以为这会是一趟惬意的差事。
次日破晓，我们登上轮船向米尔斯将军报到，他随行带着麾下军团的四五个连队。将军开口询问我们的战马何在，这倒让我们颇感诧异，因为我们原以为既然坐船侦察，自然用不上马匹。将军却说在返程前很可能会用上马，于是我们便把牲口牵上了船。不过几分钟工夫，轮船便开足马力顺流疾驰，时速约达20英里。
“远西号”轮船由格兰特·马什船长（Captain Grant Marsh）掌舵，我发现他当真是个豪爽过硬的汉子。我早就久仰他的大名，因为无论当年还是眼下，他都是举国闻名的大河领航巨擘。正是这位船长，曾驾驶着他的“远西号”，把小大角（Little Big Horn）战役中的伤员一路运往密苏里河（Missouri River）畔的亚伯拉罕·林肯堡（Fort Abraham Lincoln），并且在那次航行中创下了有史以来最快的汽船航速纪录。他是一位经验老到、技艺绝伦的舵手，驾驭起轮船来游刃有余、精妙无比。
当理查德与我各就各位伫立在舵手室顶上时，轮船加足马力，劈波斩浪掠过一座座江心岛屿、绕过一道道险弯、冲过片片浅滩沙洲，其疾如飞的势头令人心旷神怡。未几，我隐约瞧见远处河湾处有一群马正在吃草，便立即向米尔斯将军报告。将军指着岸边一棵大树，询问马什船长能否在那儿靠岸。
“没问题，长官；我能把船靠过去，真有必要的话，我还能让这艘船爬到树上去！”船长答道。
抵运指定地点后，米尔斯将军下令两个连队登岸，理查德与我则奉命把马匹牵下船，火速向外围推进，探查附近是否有印第安人出没。

===Original===

While we were getting ashore, Captain Marsh remarked that if there was only a good heavy dew on the grass he would shoot the steamer ashore and take us on the scout without the trouble of leaving the boat.
It was a false alarm, however, as the objects we had seen proved to be Indian graves. Quite a large number of braves who had probably been killed in some battle, had been buried on scaffolds, according to the Indian custom, and some of their clothing had been torn loose from the bodies by the wolves and was waving in the air.
On arriving at Glendive Creek we found that Colonel Rice and his company of the Fifth Infantry, who had been sent there by General Mills, had built quite a good little fort with their trowel-bayonets﻿—a weapon which Colonel Rice was the inventor of, and which is, by the way, a very useful implement of war, as it can be used for a shovel in throwing up entrenchments and can be profitably utilized in several other ways. On the day previous to our arrival, Colonel Rice had a fight with a party of Indians, and had killed two or three of them at long range with his Rodman cannon.
The *Far West* was to remain at Glendive over night, and General Mills wished to send dispatches back to General Terry at once. At his request I took the dispatches and rode seventy-five miles that night through the bad lands of the Yellowstone, and reached General Terry’s camp next morning, after having nearly broken my neck a dozen times or more.
There being but little prospect of any more fighting, I determined to go East as soon as possible to organize a new “Dramatic Combination,” and have a new drama written for me, based upon the Sioux war. This I knew would be a paying investment as the Sioux campaign had excited considerable interest. So I started down the river on the steamer Yellowstone en route to Fort Beauford. On the same morning Generals Terry and Crook pulled out for Powder River, to take up the old Indian trail which we had recently left.
The steamer had proceeded down the stream about twenty miles when it was met by another boat on its way up the river, having on board General Whistler and some fresh troops for General Terry’s command. Both boats landed, and almost the first person I met was my old friend and partner, Texas Jack, who had been sent out as a dispatch carrier for the *New York Herald*.

===Chinese===

正当我们涉水登岸之际，马什船长打趣道，草地上哪怕只要有一层浓重的晨露，他就能一口气把轮船开上岸去，省得我们费劲下船跑路，直接载着我们去侦察。
不过这只是一场虚惊，我们先前瞅见的目标原来是印第安人的坟茔。相当多大概是在某场战斗中阵亡的印第安勇士，按照部落习俗被安放在脚手架式的树葬台上收殓；野狼撕扯烂了裹尸的衣物，残破的布条正迎风飘摇。
抵达格兰戴夫溪后，我们发现米尔斯将军先前派驻此地的赖斯上校（Colonel Rice）与其麾下第五步兵团的连队，已经用他们的镘刀刺刀（trowel-bayonets）筑起了一座颇为像样的小堡垒——顺便提一句，这种武器正是赖斯上校发明的，在行军作战中极其好使，既能充当铲子挖掘掩体战壕，还能派上许多其他用场。就在我们到达的前一天，赖斯上校还与一伙印第安人交了火，并用他的罗德曼加农炮（Rodman cannon）进行远程轰击，当场击毙了两三名敌手。
“远西号”轮船当晚在格兰戴夫停泊过夜，米尔斯将军希望能立即派人把快报送回给特里将军（General Terry）。应将军之请，我怀揣公文连夜跨马飞奔，在黄石河沿岸崎岖荒地中穿行了75英里，一路上险些折断脖子十几次，终于在次日清晨赶到了特里将军的营地。
眼见短期内已无大规模激战的苗头，我决定尽早赶往东部，组建一支新的“巡回戏剧联合剧团（Dramatic Combination）”，并请人以眼下的苏族战争（Sioux war）为题材为我量身定做一部新戏。我心知这定是一桩稳赚不赔的好买卖，毕竟苏族战役在全美激起了轰动一时的极大关注。于是我登上了“黄石号”（Yellowstone）轮船顺流而下，准备取道前往博福德堡（Fort Beauford）。就在同一个清晨，特里将军与克鲁克将军（General Crook）亦率部拔营前往保德河，沿我们不久前追踪过的印第安旧踪追寻下去。
轮船顺水航行了约20英里，迎面遇上了另一艘逆流而上的船只，船上载着惠斯勒将军（General Whistler）以及增援特里将军部属的新生部队。两船靠岸停泊，我下船见到的第一批人中，竟就有我的老搭档兼老朋友“德克萨斯·杰克”（Texas Jack），他此行正受聘担任《纽约先驱报》（New York Herald）的随军通信速递员。

===Original===

General Whistler, upon learning that General Terry had left the Yellowstone, asked me to carry to him some important dispatches from General Sheridan, and although I objected, he insisted upon my performing this duty, saying that it would only detain me a few hours longer; as an extra inducement he offered me the use of his own thoroughbred horse, which was on the boat. I finally consented to go, and was soon speeding over the rough and hilly country towards Powder River; and I delivered the dispatches to General Terry that same evening. General Whistler’s horse, although a good animal, was not used to such hard riding, and was far more exhausted by the journey than I was.
After I had taken a lunch, General Terry asked me if I would carry some dispatches back to General Whistler, and I replied that I would. Captain Smith, General Terry’s aide-de-camp, offered me his horse for the trip, and it proved to be an excellent animal; for I rode him that same night forty miles over the bad lands in four hours, and reached General Whistler’s steamboat at one o’clock. During my absence the Indians had made their appearance on the different hills in the vicinity, and the troops from the boat had had several skirmishes with them. When General Whistler had finished reading the dispatches, he said:
“Cody, I want to send information to General Terry concerning the Indians who have been skirmishing around here all day. I have been trying all the evening long to induce someone to carry my dispatches to him, but no one seems willing to undertake the trip, and I have got to fall back on you. It is asking a great deal, I know, as you have just ridden eighty miles; but it is a case of necessity, and if you’ll go, Cody, I’ll see that you are well paid for it.”
“Never mind about the pay,” said I, “but get your dispatches ready, and I’ll start at once.”
In a few minutes he handed me the package, and mounting the same horse which I had ridden from General Terry’s camp, I struck out for my destination. It was two o’clock in the morning when I left the boat, and at eight o’clock I rode into General Terry’s camp, just as he was about to march﻿—having made one hundred and twenty miles in twenty-two hours.

===Chinese===

惠斯勒将军获悉特里将军已离开黄石河后，便请求我替他将几份谢里登将军（General Sheridan）的重要文书转送过去。尽管我出言推辞，但他坚持要我担此重任，声称这不过只会耽搁我几个小时；为了加大说服力，他还把船上属于他自己的一匹纯种良马借给我骑乘。我最终点头应允，旋即策马疾驰穿过丘陵起伏的崎岖荒原奔向保德河，并在当天傍晚将公文呈交给了特里将军。惠斯勒将军的那匹坐骑虽然是匹好马，却受不住这般严酷急行军的折腾，跑完这一趟后反倒比我还要精疲力竭。
待我草草吃过午饭，特里将军询问我是否愿意带一份回信快报送还给惠斯勒将军，我爽快答应。特里将军的副官史密斯上尉（Captain Smith）慷慨地将战马借给我跑这一趟，事实证明这确实是一匹不可多得的千里良驹；当晚我跨着它在荒原上风驰电掣，仅用4小时便横跨40英里险路，于凌晨1点赶回了惠斯勒将军的轮船前。在我离开期间，成群的印第安人曾在周边各处山梁频繁露头，船上的部队已同他们打了好几场小规模前哨交火。惠斯勒将军阅毕文书后，对我说道：
“科迪（Cody），我急需将今天一整天在此处与我们纠缠交火的印第安敌情通报给特里将军。我整晚都在寻人送信，可营里没有一个人敢接这趟苦差事，我只能再仰仗你了。我知道这有些强人所难，你毕竟刚刚连轴跑了80英里路；但事态万分紧急，如果你愿意跑一趟，科迪，我绝不会亏待你，定会付给你极其丰厚的酬金。”
“酬金的事先搁一边，”我回道，“您只管把公文备妥，我即刻启程。”
不过几分钟，他就把密封卷宗交到我手里。我跨上从特里将军营地骑来的那匹原班战马，直奔目的地疾驰而去。离开轮船时正值凌晨2点，待我于早晨8点策马冲入特里将军的大营时，全军恰好正整装待发——在短短22小时内，我已连轴奔驰了整整120英里。

===Original===

General Terry, after reading the dispatches, halted his command, and then rode on and overtook General Crook, with whom he held a council; the result was that Crook’s command moved on in the direction which they had been pursuing, while Terry’s forces marched back to the Yellowstone and crossed the river on steamboats. At the urgent request of General Terry I accompanied the command on a scout in the direction of the Dry Fork of the Missouri, where it was expected we would strike some Indians.
The first march out from the Yellowstone was made in the night, as we wished to get into the hills without being discovered by the Sioux scouts. After marching three days, a little to the east of north, we reached the buffalo range, and discovered fresh signs of Indians, who had evidently been killing buffaloes. General Terry now called on me to carry dispatches to Colonel Rice, who was still camped at the mouth of Glendive Creek, on the Yellowstone﻿—distant about eighty miles from us.
Night had set in with a storm, and a drizzling rain was falling when, at ten o’clock, I started on this ride through a section of country with which I was entirely unacquainted. I traveled through the darkness a distance of about thirty-five miles, and at daylight I rode into a secluded spot at the head of a ravine where stood a bunch of ash trees, and there I concluded to remain till night; for I considered it a dangerous undertaking to cross the wide prairies in broad daylight﻿—especially as my horse was a poor one.
I accordingly unsaddled my animal, and ate a hearty breakfast of bacon and hard tack which I had stored in the saddle-pockets; then, after taking a smoke, I lay down to sleep, with my saddle for a pillow. In a few minutes I was in the land of dreams.
After sleeping some time﻿—I can’t tell how long﻿—I was suddenly awakened by a roaring, rumbling sound. I instantly seized my gun, sprang to my horse, and hurriedly secreted him in the brush. Then I climbed up the steep side of the bank and cautiously looked over the summit; in the distance I saw a large herd of buffaloes which were being chased and fired at by twenty or thirty Indians. Occasionally a buffalo would drop out of the herd, but the Indians kept on until they had killed ten or fifteen. They then turned back, and began to cut up their game.

===Chinese===

特里将军拆阅文书后下令部队原地驻扎，随后亲自策马赶往前头追上克鲁克将军，两人举行了紧急磋商。磋商达成的方案是：克鲁克麾下的部队沿着原先既定路线继续推进，特里将军的人马则折返黄石河并搭乘汽船渡河。在特里将军的殷切请求下，我随同部队一同向密苏里河干岔口（Dry Fork）方向前出侦察，军方估计我们将在那里与一伙印第安人正面相撞。
从黄石河出发的第一程行军选在夜间展开，以便队伍能神不知鬼不觉地扎入丘陵腹地而不被苏族的斥候哨兵察觉。大军朝东北偏北方向行军了3天后，抵运了野牛群生息的牧野地带，沿途发现了印第安人留下的新鲜痕迹，显然对方刚刚在此地猎杀过野牛。此时特里将军又找上我，让我携带加急文书送交给赖斯上校，彼时上校仍驻扎在黄石河畔格兰戴夫溪的河口——距离我们足有约80英里之遥。
入夜后狂风呼啸，细雨绵绵。夜间10点整，我孤身一人策马冲入这片我素未涉足的全然陌生地界。我冒着浓重的夜色疾驰了大约35英里，待黎明破晓时分，我策马来到一处深谷源头的僻静隐蔽处，此处生长着一丛白蜡树。我思忖再三，决定在此潜伏挨到天黑；毕竟在我看来，在大白天毫无掩护地横穿辽阔大平原委实是一场九死一生的冒险，更何况我的坐骑脚力相当平庸。
于是我卸下马鞍，就着马鞍口袋里随身携带的腌肉和干硬饼干饱餐了一顿早点；抽过一袋烟后，我头枕马鞍沉沉睡去。不出几分钟，我便已沉入梦乡。
睡了不知多久——我实在辨不清到底过了多少时辰——一阵轰隆隆滚雷般的剧烈巨响猛然将我惊醒。我刹那间一把抓起身旁的枪支，飞身冲到战马旁，急匆匆将马匹拉进茂密灌木丛中藏匿妥当。紧接着我手脚并用攀上陡峭土坡，小心翼翼探出头向坡顶外张望：只见远方大平原上一大群野牛正狂暴狂奔，身后紧追着20或30名边追边射的印第安人。时不时有野牛中弹扑倒掉队，但这伙印第安人穷追不舍，直到猎杀了10到15头野牛才勒马停手。随后他们掉转马头折返回来，开始动手切割宰剥猎获的猎物。

===Original===

I saddled my horse and tied him to a small tree where I could reach him conveniently in case the Indians should discover me by finding my trail and following it. I then crawled carefully back to the summit of the bluff, and in a concealed position watched the Indians for two hours, during which time they were occupied in cutting up the buffaloes and packing the meat on their ponies. When they had finished this work they rode off in the direction whence they had come and on the line which I had proposed to travel. It appeared evident to me that their camp was located somewhere between me and Glendive Creek, but I had no idea of abandoning the trip on that account.
I waited till nightfall before resuming my journey, and then I bore off to the east for several miles, and by making a semicircle to avoid the Indians, I got back on my original course, and then pushed on rapidly to Colonel Rice’s camp, which I reached just at daylight.
Colonel Rice had been fighting Indians almost every day since he had been encamped at this point, and he was very anxious to notify General Terry of the fact. Of course I was requested to carry his dispatches. After remaining at Glendive a single day I started back to find General Terry, and on the third day out I overhauled him at the head of Deer Creek while on his way to Colonel Rice’s camp. He was not, however, going in the right direction, but bearing too far to the east, and I so informed him. He then asked me to guide the command and I did so.
On arriving at Glendive I bade goodbye to the General and his officers and took passage on the steamer *Far West*, which was on her way down the Missouri. At Bismarck I left the steamer, and proceeded by rail to Rochester, New York, where I met my family.
Mr. J. Clinton Hall, manager of the Rochester Opera House, was very anxious to have me play an engagement at his theatre. I agreed to open the season with him as soon as I had got my drama written; and I did so, meeting with an enthusiastic reception.
My new drama was arranged for the stage by J. V. Arlington, the actor. It was a five-act play, without head or tail, and it made no difference at which act we commenced the performance. Before we had finished the season several newspaper critics, I have been told, went crazy in trying to follow the plot.

===Chinese===

我迅速备好马鞍，把坐骑拴在一棵顺手就能牵到的小树上，防备印第安人若循着我的蹄印追查过来时好随时策马脱身。随后我轻手轻脚爬回断崖顶端，在隐蔽的藏身处足足观察了这伙印第安人2小时。在此期间，他们忙着大卸野牛骨肉，把沉重的鲜肉捆扎在各家的矮马背上。待忙完这桩营生后，他们便调转马头朝原路折返而去——而那恰恰是我计划前行的行军方向。我心里很清楚，他们的宿营大寨必定就横亘在我与格兰戴夫溪之间的必经之路上，但我绝无半点因噎废食放弃使命的打算。
我耐着性子一直熬到夜幕深沉方才重新上路。我先向东面迂回兜出数英里，兜了个大半圆绕开印第安人的地盘，随后切回到原先的既定路线上，马不停蹄火速奔赴赖斯上校的营地，恰好在破晓东方发白之际赶到了大营。
自打在此处扎营以来，赖斯上校几乎天天都在同印第安人交战搏杀，他极度迫切希望将这一战况速报给特里将军。理所当然，送信的重任再次落在了我的肩头。在格兰戴夫休整了短短1天之后，我便踏上折返路程前去寻觅特里将军；在出发后的第3天，我终于在鹿溪（Deer Creek）源头追上了正朝赖斯上校营地进军的将军一行。然而他的行军方位出了差错，队伍偏离航向向东拐得太远，我便据实向他说明。将军当即请我为大军充当向导领路，我欣然领命。
抵运格兰戴夫后，我向将军及其随行军官们挥手作别，随即登上了正顺密苏里河南下的“远西号”轮船。行至俾斯麦（Bismarck）后我离船上岸，改乘火车一路奔赴纽约州罗切斯特（Rochester, New York），与我的家人团聚。
罗切斯特歌剧院（Rochester Opera House）的经理J·克林顿·霍尔（J. Clinton Hall）先生极其热切地盼望我能登上他的剧场登台献艺。我答应他只要新戏本一脱稿便率先来他的剧院开启巡演演季；剧目落成后我如约履诺，首演当即收获了全城观众雷鸣般的狂热追捧。
我的这部新剧本由著名演员J·V·阿灵顿（J. V. Arlington）操刀改编并搬上舞台。这是一部没头没尾的五幕荒诞话剧，整台戏无论从哪一幕开演似乎都毫无二致。听说在这个巡演季尚未落幕之前，城里好几位报馆的戏剧剧评人为了理清戏里的剧情逻辑，硬生生被逼得抓狂发疯。

===Original===

It afforded us, however, ample opportunity to give a noisy, rattling, gunpowder entertainment, and to present a succession of scenes in the late Indian war, all of which seemed to give general satisfaction.
From Rochester I went to New York and played a very successful engagement at the Grand Opera House under the management of Messrs. Poole and Donnelly. Thence my route took me to all the principal cities in the Eastern, Western and Middle States, and I everywhere met with crowded houses. I then went to the Pacific Coast, against the advice of friends who gave it as their opinion that my style of plays would not take very well in California. I opened for an engagement of two weeks at the Bush Street Theatre, in San Francisco, at a season when the theatrical business was dull, and Ben DeBar and the Lingards were playing there to empty seats. I expected to play to a slim audience on the opening night, but instead of that I had a fourteen hundred dollar house. Such was my success that I continued my engagement for five weeks, and the theatre was crowded at every performance. Upon leaving San Francisco I made a circuit of the interior towns and closed the season at Virginia City, Nevada.
On my way East, I met my family at Denver, where they were visiting my sisters Nellie and May who were then residing there.
Some time previously I had made arrangements to go into the cattle business in company with my old friend, Major Frank North, and while I was in California he had built our ranches on the South Fork of the Dismal River, sixty-five miles north of North Platte, in Nebraska. Proceeding to Ogalalla, the headquarters of the Texas cattle drovers, I found Major North there awaiting me, and together we bought, branded and drove to our ranches, our first installment of cattle. This occupied us during the remainder of the summer.
Leaving the cattle in charge of Major North, I visited Red Cloud Agency early in the fall, and secured some Sioux Indians to accompany me on my theatrical tour of 1877﻿–﻿78. Taking my family and the Indians with me, I went directly to Rochester. There I left my oldest daughter, Arta, at a young ladies’ seminary, while my wife and youngest child traveled with me during the season.
I opened at the Bowery Theatre, New York, September 3rd, 1877, with a new Border Drama entitled, *May Cody, or Lost and Won*, from the pen of Major A. S.

===Chinese===

不过，这部戏倒是为我们提供了绝佳的机会，去营造一场枪炮震天、热火朝天的火药实弹视听盛宴，并将刚刚结束的印第安战争中的一幕幕激烈枪战淋漓尽致地搬上舞台，观众们无不看得如痴如醉、大呼过瘾。
离开罗切斯特后，我挥师前往纽约，在普尔与唐纳利先生（Messrs. Poole and Donnelly）执掌的大歌剧院（Grand Opera House）登台公演，场面极其轰动。随后，我的巡演足迹踏遍了东部、西部以及中部各州的所有核心大都市，所到之处场场爆满、座无虚席。紧接着我力排众议奔赴太平洋沿岸（Pacific Coast），此前许多挚友曾纷纷好心劝阻，认为我这种风格的戏剧在加利福尼亚（California）恐怕吃不开。我在旧金山（San Francisco）的布什街剧院（Bush Street Theatre）定下了为期两周的驻场演出，当时正值当地演艺市场一片萧条的淡季，就连名角本·德巴尔（Ben DeBar）以及林加德剧团（the Lingards）在台上卖力献艺时台下都门可罗雀、空座连片。开演首夜我原本也做好了观众寥寥的心理准备，孰料当晚剧院票房竟一举斩获了1400美元的高额进账。演出引发的狂潮如此空前，以至于我顺势将档期延长至5周，且每场演出无不被围得水泄不通。离开旧金山后，我又在内陆各城镇巡回演出了一圈，最终在内华达州弗吉尼亚城（Virginia City, Nevada）为这一季巡演画上了圆满句号。
在回师东部的途中，我与家人在丹佛（Denver）汇合，彼时她们正在探望定居在当地的我的两个妹妹内莉（Nellie）与梅（May）。
在此前不久，我便与老搭档弗兰克·诺斯少校（Major Frank North）商定携手进军畜牧养牛业。当我在加州登台巡演期间，诺斯少校便在内布拉斯加州北普拉特（North Platte, Nebraska）以北65英里处的迪斯马尔河南汊（South Fork of the Dismal River）营建修筑起了我们的自有牧场。我马不停蹄赶往德克萨斯牧牛人的大本营奥加拉拉（Ogalalla），诺斯少校早已在当地恭候多时；我们二人齐心协力采购了大批牛群，逐一打上专属烙印，并将我们进军牧业的首批大牲口一路驱赶回自家的牧场。这项繁重劳役占据了我们整个夏天的剩余时光。
将牧场牛群全权托付给诺斯少校掌管照料后，我于初秋时分专程造访了红云代办所（Red Cloud Agency），成功招揽了一批苏族印第安人随我一同投身1877—1878年的新戏剧巡回公演。我携上爱妻爱子以及这批印第安演员直奔罗切斯特。在那儿，我把大女儿阿塔（Arta）送进了一所女子教会寄宿学校深造，爱妻与最小的幼子则随同我一起踏上漫漫巡演之路。
1877年9月3日，我在纽约鲍厄里剧院（Bowery Theatre）隆重揭幕开演，上演了一部题为《梅·科迪，或得与失》（May Cody, or Lost and Won）的全新边疆传奇大戏，该剧本出自美军少校A·S·

===Original===

Burt, of the United States army. It was founded on the incidents of the “Mountain Meadow Massacre,” and life among the Mormons. It was the best drama I had yet produced, and proved a grand success both financially and artistically. The season of 1877﻿–﻿78 proved to be the most profitable one I had ever had.
In February, 1878, my wife became tired of traveling, and proceeded to North Platte, Nebraska, where, on our farm adjoining the town, she personally superintended the erection of a comfortable family residence, and had it all completed when I reached there, early in May. In this house we are now living, and we hope to make it our home for many years to come.

===Chinese===

伯特（Major A. S. Burt）之手。该剧以轰动一时的“山地草甸大屠杀（Mountain Meadow Massacre）”历史公案以及摩门教徒（Mormons）的生活为创作蓝本。这是我迄今为止制作水准最高的一部舞台力作，无论在商业票房还是艺术造诣上都斩获了无比辉煌的大获全胜。1877—1878年的演出季，亦被证明是我平生获利最丰厚的一季巡演。
到了1878年2月，爱妻对经年累月的舟车劳顿深感倦怠，便先行前往内布拉斯加州北普拉特，在我们紧邻城镇边缘的农庄地上，亲自全程督建了一栋温馨宜居的家庭宅邸；待我于5月初赶抵当地时，整栋宅院已全数修葺齐备落成。如今，我们举家正安居在这座大宅之中，并衷心期盼在未来的漫长岁月里，这里能长长久久成为我们遮风避雨的温暖归宿。

===Original===

> **To General Philip H. Sheridan**,

this book is most respectfully dedicated by
> The Author.

===Chinese===

> 谨以此书，以最崇高之敬意，献给菲利普·H·谢里登将军（General Philip H. Sheridan）。
>
> —— 著者 谨奉

## II Early Influences / 第二章 早年熏陶
===Original===

General Harney was in command at Fort Leavenworth at the time of our visit, and a regiment of cavalry was stationed there. They were having a dress parade when we rode up, and as this was the first time that I had ever seen any soldiers, I thought it was a grand sight. I shall never forget it, especially the manoeuvres on horseback.
After witnessing the parade we resumed our journey. On the way to my father’s trading camp we had to cross over a high hill known as Salt Creek Hill, from the top of which we looked down upon the most beautiful valley I have ever seen. It was about twelve miles long and five miles wide. The different tributaries of Salt Creek came down from the range of hills at the southwest. At the foot of the valley another small river﻿—Plum Creek, also flowed. The bluffs fringed with trees, clad in their full foliage, added greatly to the picturesqueness of the scene.
While this beautiful valley greatly interested me, yet the most novel sight, of an entirely different character, which met my enraptured gaze, was the vast number of white-covered wagons, or “prairie-schooners,” which were encamped along the different streams. I asked my father what they were and where they were going; he explained to me that they were emigrant wagons bound for Utah and California.
At that time the Mormon and California trails ran through this valley, which was always selected as a camping place. There were at least one thousand wagons in the valley, and their white covers lent a pleasing contrast to the green grass. The cattle were quietly grazing near the wagons, while the emigrants were either resting or attending to camp duties.
A large number of the wagons, as I learned from my father, belonged to Majors & Russell, the great government freighters. They had several trains there, each consisting of twenty-five wagons, heavily loaded with government supplies. They were all camped and corraled in a circle.
While we were viewing this scene, a long wagon train came pulling up the hill, bound out from Fort Leavenworth to some distant frontier post. The cattle were wild and the men were whipping them fearfully, the loud reports of the bullwhips sounding like gunshots. They were “doubling-up,” and some of the wagons were being drawn by fifteen yokes of oxen.

===Chinese===

在我们造访莱文沃思堡（Fort Leavenworth）期间，哈尼将军（General Harney）正执掌该堡防务，驻扎着整整一个骑兵团。我们策马赶到时，官兵们恰好在举行盛大的阅兵式；由于这是我平生第一遭亲眼目睹正规军军容，顿觉壮观非凡。那场面令我终生难忘，尤其是骑兵们在马背上精湛卓绝的战术队列操练。
观摩完阅兵式后，我们重整行装继续前行。在前往父亲贸易营地的途中，我们必须翻越一座名为盐溪山（Salt Creek Hill）的巍峨高丘；伫立山巅凭高远眺，展现在我们眼前的，是我平生所见最瑰丽秀美的一座大山谷。山谷长约12英里，宽约5英里。盐溪（Salt Creek）的各条支流从西南方绵延起伏的丘陵群间奔流而下；山谷低洼处，另一条名为李子溪（Plum Creek）的清澈小河亦静静流淌。郁郁葱葱、绿树成荫的悬崖峭壁耸立四周，更为这幅壮美画卷平添了无尽诗意。
尽管这座明秀山谷令我心旷神怡，但真正让我如痴如醉、目不暇接的，却是另一幅截然不同且前所未见的宏大奇景——沿各条溪流两岸鳞次栉比扎营的数不胜数的白色篷车，即人们口中的“大草原篷车（prairie-schooners）”。我好奇地向父亲打听这些车队究竟是何物、又将驶向何方；父亲耐心地向我解释：那些全都是开往犹他（Utah）与加利福尼亚（California）的移民大车队。
在那段岁月里，摩门古道与加利福尼亚移民路线均横贯这座山谷，移民车队向来习惯以此作为宿营中转站。整座山谷里至少聚集了1000辆大篷车，那一片片洁白的篷顶在葱郁碧绿的草甸映衬下显得格外夺目。牛群在车旁悠闲自得地啃食青草，移民们有的在休养生息，有的则在忙碌着营地杂务。
据父亲告知，这其中有很大一部分货车隶属于当时赫赫有名的政府运输巨头梅杰斯与拉塞尔公司（Majors & Russell）。他们在此驻扎着好几支大货运车队，每支车队均由25辆满载政府物资的重型篷车组成，整整齐齐围成防御圆阵扎营。
正当我们凭高俯瞰这片壮景之际，一列漫长的大货车队正吃力地顺着山坡一路往上爬，准备从莱文沃思堡开拔前往某处遥远的边疆边卡哨所。拉车的公牛性情凶悍狂躁，车夫们挥舞沉重的长皮鞭无情狠抽，空中炸响的甩鞭声宛如阵阵枪鸣。由于山陡坡陡，他们只得并组挽力，有些重车甚至动用了整整15轭耕牛并力拖拽。

===Original===

I remember asking my father a great many questions, and he explained to me all about the freighting business across the great plains, and told me about the different government posts.
Pointing over to the army of wagons camped below us, he showed me which were the Mormons’ and which were the Californians’, and said that we must steer clear of the former as the cholera was raging among them. Five hundred had died that spring﻿—1853﻿—and the graveyard was daily increasing its dimensions. The unfortunate people had been overtaken by the dreadful disease, and had been compelled to halt on their journey until it abated.
While we were looking at the Mormons they were holding a funeral service over the remains of some of their number who had died. Their old cemetery is yet indicated by various landmarks, which, however, with the few remaining headboards, are fast disappearing.
We passed on through this “Valley of Death,” as it might then have been very appropriately called, and after riding for some time, my father pointed out a large hill and showed me his camp, which afterwards became our home.
There was another trading-post nearby, which was conducted by Mr. M. P. Rively, who had a store built, partly frame, and partly of logs. We stopped at this establishment for a while, and found perhaps a hundred men, women and children gathered there, engaged in trading and gossipping. The men had huge pistols and knives in their belts; their pantaloons were tucked in their boots; and they wore large broad-rimmed hats.
To me they appeared like a lot of cutthroat pirates who had come ashore for a lark. It was the first time I had ever seen men carrying pistols and knives, and they looked like a very dangerous crowd. Some were buying articles of merchandise; others were talking about the cholera, the various camps, and matters of interest; while others were drinking whisky freely and becoming intoxicated. It was a busy and an exciting scene, and Rively appeared to be doing a rushing trade.
At some little distance from the store I noticed a small party of dark-skinned and rather fantastically dressed people, whom I ascertained were Indians, and as I had never before seen a real live Indian, I was much interested in them. I went over and endeavored to talk to them, but our conversation was very limited.

===Chinese===

我清晰记得自己当时刨根问底向父亲抛出了许多疑问，父亲便将横穿大平原的货运贸易门道一五一十讲给我听，还详尽介绍了分布在各处的政府要塞哨所。
他伸手指向下方扎营的篷车大军，逐一为我指认哪些属于摩门教徒（Mormons），哪些属于前往加利福尼亚的淘金移民。父亲特别叮嘱，我们必须对前者敬而远之，因为可怕的霍乱疫情正在他们的人群中疯狂肆虐。仅在1853年那年春季，便已有整整500人不幸丧生，坟场的地界每天都在急剧扩张。这群苦难的流亡者被凶残的瘟疫迎头痛击，只得在茫茫旅途中被迫驻足停留，苦苦等待疫病平息。
正当我们远远观望时，摩门教徒正在为几位刚刚罹难的同伴举行肃穆的葬礼。时至今日，他们当年的那处荒野墓地依稀仍有若干地标可寻，只是连同那几块残存的朽木墓碑在内，早已在风雨侵蚀中迅速磨灭无存了。
我们策马穿过了这座在当年绝对称得上名副其实的“死亡之谷”；行进了相当一段路程后，父亲抬手指着一座巍峨的大山丘，向我展示了他的宿营地——那里日后便成了我们举家的温暖家园。
在附近不远处，还坐落着另一处由M·P·里夫利先生（Mr. M. P. Rively）经营的贸易驿站，那是一栋半木构架、半原木搭建的商铺货栈。我们在此下马盘桓了片刻，只见约有100名男女老幼簇拥聚集在那里，或热火朝天地以物易物，或围拢闲聊八卦。男人们的腰带上无不插着大号手枪与锋利猎刀，裤脚严严实实塞进皮靴，头上顶着大号宽檐毡帽。
在少不更事的我眼里，这群人活脱脱就像是一帮登岸寻衅作乐的亡命海盗。这是我头一遭亲眼目睹有人随身配带手枪与利刃，看起来端的是一群极度危险的亡命徒。他们有的在采买各色日用杂货；有的在忧心忡忡议论霍乱疫情、周遭各营的动态以及奇闻轶事；还有的在毫无节制地痛饮威士忌，喝得酩酊大醉。整座商铺充斥着喧嚣而动荡的草莽气息，里夫利先生的买卖看起来红火兴旺极了。
在距离商铺稍远的一处角落里，我留意到一小群肤色黝黑、衣着奇装异服的人马，打听之下方知他们原来是真正的印第安人（Indians）。由于我平生从未亲眼见过活生生的印第安土著，顿时对他们产生了莫大的浓厚兴致。我信步走上前去试图同他们搭话交谈，不过彼此的言语交流十分有限。

===Original===

That evening we reached our camp, which was located two miles west of Rively’s. The first thing I did was to hunt up my ponies, and from my father’s description of them, I had no difficulty in finding them. They were lariated in the grass and I immediately ran up to them supposing them to be gentle animals. I was greatly mistaken, however, as they snorted and jumped away from me, and would not allow me to come near them.
My father, who was standing not far distant, informed me that the ponies were not yet broken. I was somewhat disappointed at this; and thereupon he and one of his men caught one of the animals and bridled her, then putting me on her back, led her around, greatly to my delight. I kept petting her so much that she soon allowed me to approach her. She was a beautiful bay, and I named her “Dolly”; the other pony was a sorrel, and I called him “Prince.”
In the evening some Indians visited the camp﻿—which as yet consisted only of tents, though some logs had been cut preparatory to building houses﻿—and exchanged their furs for clothing, sugar and tobacco. Father had not learned their language, and therefore communicated with them by means of signs. We had our supper by the campfire, and that night was the first time I ever camped out and slept upon the ground.
The day had been an eventful one to me, for all the incidents were full of excitement and romance to my youthful mind, and I think no apology is needed for mentioning so many of the little circumstances, which so greatly interested me in my childhood’s days, and which no doubt had a great influence in shaping my course in after years. My love of hunting and scouting, and life on the plains generally, was the result of my early surroundings.
The next morning father visited the Kickapoo Agency, taking me along. He rode a horse, and putting me on my pony “Dolly,” led the animal all the way. He seemed anxious to break me in, as well as the pony, and I greatly enjoyed this, my first day’s ride on a Kansas prairie.
At the Kickapoo village I saw hundreds of Indians, some of whom were living in lodges, but the majority occupied log cabins. The agent resided in a double-hewed log house, one of the apartments of which was used as a school for the Indians. The agency store was opposite this structure.
All the buildings were whitewashed, and looked neat and clean.

===Chinese===

傍晚时分，我们抵达了自家的营地，它位于里夫利商铺以西约2英里处。我赶到后的头一桩要紧事，便是迫不及待前去搜寻属于我的那几匹小马；凭借父亲先前的生动描摹，我不费吹灰之力便在草地上找到了它们。小马正被套马绳拴在草地上吃草，我满心以为它们性情温顺，便兴冲冲一溜小跑迎上前去。哪知我大错特错，小马猛然打着响鼻腾空后跃，根本不准我靠近半步。
站在不远处的父亲笑着告诉我，这两匹小马尚未经过驯化调教。这让我颇感失望与沮丧；于是父亲叫上一名伙计合力套住其中一匹小母马并给它套上嚼子，随后把我抱上马背，牵着马在草地上兜圈子，这令我欣喜若狂。我不住地轻抚拍打它的脖颈安抚它，没过多久它便愿意亲近我了。这是一匹神骏漂亮的枣红马，我为它取名“多莉（Dolly）”；另一匹则是栗色小公马，我唤作“王子（Prince）”。
入夜后，几位印第安人造访了我们的营地——彼时营地里尚且只有帐篷，尽管周围已砍伐堆放了不少准备盖房的原木——他们用带来的兽皮换取衣物、食糖和烟草。父亲不懂他们的部族语言，便全凭手势打手语沟通。我们在篝火旁享用了晚饭；那一夜，是我平生头一次在野外扎营露宿，头枕苍茫大地安眠。
这充实的一天对我而言意义非凡，每一个细节都在我年少的心灵中激荡着惊险与浪漫；我想，详尽记叙这些在我童年岁月里留下深刻烙印的琐碎往事无需赘言致歉，因为正是它们潜移默化奠定了我日后波澜壮阔的人生轨迹。我对荒野狩猎、斥候侦察以及大平原生涯的痴迷挚爱，全皆源于幼年时期的这般耳濡目染。
次日清晨，父亲带上我一道造访了基卡普代办所（Kickapoo Agency）。他跨上一匹大马，把我抱上我的小马“多莉”，一路牵着马缰前行。父亲似乎急于磨练驯化我的骑术，如同调教那匹小马一般；而我平生头一遭在堪萨斯（Kansas）大草原上策马漫游，亦感到痛快畅意极了。
在基卡普村落里，我目睹了数以百计的印第安人，其中一部分仍栖身在传统的兽皮尖顶帐篷里，但绝大多数人都已住进了结实的原木小木屋。代办所长官居住在一栋双开间修整平整的木屋里，其中一间被辟为供印第安孩童读书习字的学校；代办所货栈则正对着这栋建筑。
所有的营房宅屋皆粉刷得雪白，显得整洁而敞亮。

===Original===

The Kickapoos were very friendly Indians, and we spent much of our time among them, looking about and studying their habits.
After a while we returned to our own camp, and just as we arrived there, we saw a drove of horses﻿—there were three or four hundred in all﻿—approaching from the west, over the California trail. They were being driven by seven or eight mounted men, wearing sombreros, and dressed in buckskin, with their lariats dangling from their saddles, and they were followed by two or three pack-mules or horses. They went into camp a little below us on the bank of the stream.
Presently one of the men walked out towards our camp, and my father called to me to come and see a genuine Western man; he was about six feet two inches tall, was well built, and had a light, springy and wiry step. He wore a broad-brimmed California hat, and was dressed in a complete suit of buckskin, beautifully trimmed and beaded. He saluted us, and father invited him to sit down, which he did. After a few moments conversation, he turned to me and said:
“Little one, I see you are working with your ponies. They are wild yet.”
I had been petting Dolly and trying to break her, when my father called me to come and look at the Californian.
“Yes,” I replied, “and one of them never has been ridden.”
“Well, I’ll ride him for you;” and springing lightly to his feet, he continued: “come on. Where is the animal?”
Accordingly we all went to the place where Prince was lariated. The stranger untied the rope from the picket pin, and taking a half-loop around the pony’s nose, he jumped on his back.
In a moment he was flying over the prairie, the untamed steed rearing and pitching every once in a while in his efforts to throw his rider; but the man was not unseated. He was evidently an experienced horseman. I watched his every movement. I was unconsciously taking another lesson in the practical education which has served me so well through my life.
The Californian rode the pony until it was completely mastered, then coming up to me, jumped to the ground, handed me the rope, and said:
“Here’s your pony. He’s all right now.”
I led Prince away, while father and the stranger sat down in the shade of a tent, and began talking about the latter’s horsemanship, which father considered very remarkable.

===Chinese===

基卡普人（Kickapoos）是一群非常和善友好的印第安人，我们大部分闲暇时间都流连在他们中间，四处漫步观察并揣摩他们的生活习俗。
过了一阵子，我们折返回自家营地。就在我们刚刚抵达时，只见加利福尼亚古道上尘土飞扬，一大群烈马——足有300到400匹之巨——正从西边浩浩荡荡开拔而来。驱赶这群马匹的是7或8名骑在马背上的彪形大汉，头戴墨西哥宽檐帽，身裹鹿皮劲装，鞍桥两侧悬挂着盘绕的套马长索，身后还尾随着2或3头驮运辎重的骡马。他们在略低于我们营地的溪流下游河滩处扎下了营盘。
不一会儿，其中一名汉子信步朝我们营地走来；父亲立刻招呼我上前，让我见识一下何为真正的西部硬汉：他身高足有6英尺2英寸（约1.88米），体格极其魁梧健美，步履轻盈、矫健而极富弹性。他头顶一顶宽檐加利福尼亚阔帽，身穿一整套剪裁考究、镶边缀珠的华美鹿皮装。他向我们点头致意，父亲便热情邀他坐下歇息，他也爽朗入座。闲谈寒暄了几句后，他转过头对我说道：
“小家伙，我瞧你正忙着摆弄你的小马驹呢。性子还野得很哩。”
在父亲唤我过去端详这位加州来客前，我一直在轻抚多莉并尝试驯服它。
“是啊，”我脆生生应道，“而且另一匹马还从来没人骑过呢。”
“那好办，我来替你骑它！”他轻巧敏捷地一跃而起，接着说：“走着，马在哪儿？”
于是我们一行人来到拴着“王子”的草地上。这位陌生汉子熟练地解开拴马桩上的套马绳，在小马鼻梁上轻巧绕了一个半扣结套，随即纵身飞跃跨上了光秃秃的马背。
刹那间，一人一马如离弦之箭般在大平原上疾驰飞奔！尚未驯化的烈马连连人立而起、狂暴地连踢带颠，使出浑身解数想把背上的骑手甩下马来；然而汉子双腿如铁钳般紧锁，稳如泰山纹丝不动。他显然是一位阅历深厚、身经百战的驭马大师。我目不转睛凝视着他的一举一动，在潜移默化中再次汲取了一堂宝贵的实践操演大课，而这门绝技在日后的岁月里令我终生受益无穷。
这位加利福尼亚客一直把这匹烈马驯得服服帖帖、彻底没了脾气，才纵马兜回到我跟前，飘身跃下地面，将缰绳递到我手中笑道：
“小马归你了。这下它老实啦。”
我牵着“王子”退到一旁，父亲与这位陌生人则并肩坐在帐篷阴凉处，就着汉子方才那手令父亲赞不绝口的惊艳骑术热切攀谈起来。

===Original===

“Oh, that’s nothing; I was raised on horseback,” said the Californian; “I ran away from home when a boy, went to sea, and finally landed in the Sandwich Islands, where I fell in with a circus, with which I remained two years. During that time I became a celebrated bareback rider. I then went to California, being attracted there by the gold excitement, the news of which had reached the Islands. I did not go to mining, however, but went to work as a *bocarro*﻿—catching and breaking wild horses, great numbers of which were roaming through California. Last summer we caught this herd that we have brought with us across the plains, and are taking it to the States to sell. I came with the outfit, as it gave me a good opportunity to visit my relatives, who live at Cleveland, Ohio. I also had an uncle over at Weston, across the river, when I ran away, and tomorrow I am going to visit the town to see if he is there yet.”
“I am acquainted in Weston,” said father, “and perhaps I can tell you about your uncle. What is his name?”
“Elijah Cody,” said the Californian.
“Elijah Cody!” exclaimed father, in great surprise; “why Elijah Cody is my brother. I am Isaac Cody. Who are you?”
“My name is Horace Billings,” was the reply.
“And you are my nephew. You are the son of my sister Sophia.”
Both men sprang to their feet and began shaking hands in the heartiest manner possible.
The next moment father called me, and said: “Come here, my son. Here is someone you want to know.”
As I approached he introduced us. “Horace, this is my only son. We call him little Billy;” and turning to me said: “Billy, my boy, this is a cousin of yours, Horace Billings, whom you’ve often heard me speak of.”
Horace Billings had never been heard of from the day he ran away from home, and his relatives had frequently wondered what had become of him. His appearance, therefore, in our camp in the guise of a Californian was somewhat of a mystery to me, and I could hardly comprehend it until I had heard his adventurous story and learned the accidental manner in which he and father had made themselves known to each other.
Neither father nor myself would be satisfied until he had given us a full account of his wanderings and adventures, which were very exciting to me.
Late in the afternoon and just before the sun sank to rest, the conversation again turned upon horses and horsemanship.

===Chinese===

“嗨，这算不得什么，我打小就是在马背上泡大的，”这位加州汉子爽朗笑道，“我小时候离家出走跑去当水手航海，最后漂洋过海流落到了三明治群岛（Sandwich Islands），在那儿入伙了一家马戏团，一干就是2年。也就是在那段日子里，我练成了一手名噪一时的无鞍骑术绝活。后来加利福尼亚淘金热的消息传到了群岛，我按捺不住也奔赴加州。不过我没去淘金下矿，而是当了一名套马牛仔（bocarro）——专事抓捕并驯化成群游荡在加州广袤荒原上的野马。去年夏天，我们抓获了这批如今正横穿大平原赶往各州发售的大马群。我之所以跟着马队同行，是为了借机探望定居在俄亥俄州克利夫兰（Cleveland, Ohio）的亲族。另外，当年我逃家出走时，大河对岸的韦斯顿（Weston）还住着我的一位舅舅，我正打算明天动身进城去探探虚实，看他是否依然健在呢。”
“韦斯顿的人我大多相识，”父亲插话道，“说不定我认得你那位亲戚。他尊姓大名？”
“以利亚·科迪（Elijah Cody）。”汉子答道。
“以利亚·科迪？！”父亲惊喜交加失声惊呼，“以利亚·科迪正是我亲哥哥呀！我就是艾萨克·科迪（Isaac Cody）。你到底是哪一位？”
“我叫霍勒斯·比林斯（Horace Billings）。”对方答道。
“那你就是我的亲外甥！你是我姐姐索菲亚（Sophia）的孩子！”
两位壮汉霍然起身，情不自禁紧紧握手相拥，激动喜悦之情溢于言表。
紧接着父亲把我唤到身旁说道：“快过来，孩子。来见见你的亲人。”
待我走上前，父亲向他介绍：“霍勒斯，这是我唯一的儿子。我们平时唤他小比利（little Billy）。”接着又扭头对我说道：“比利，好孩子，这是你的表兄霍勒斯·比林斯，你平素常听我念叨起他的大名。”
霍勒斯·比林斯自打当年负气逃家之后便杳无音讯，族里的亲朋好友常常忧心忡忡揣测他的下落。因此，他此番以一身加利福尼亚骑手装束赫然现身我们的营地，对我而言不啻为一个天大的传奇谜团；直到我完整聆听了他惊心动魄的历险经历，并见证了他与父亲戏剧般相认的奇妙巧合，才真正回过神来。
父亲与我听得津津有味，非缠着他把这些年来天涯漂泊的惊险历程和盘托出方才罢休，那些传奇故事听得我热血沸腾。
日头偏西，就在夕阳即将敛尽最后一抹余晖之际，营地里的话题再度回到了战马与骑术之上。

===Original===

Father told Billings all about Little Gray, and his great fault of running away. Billings laughed and said Little Gray could not run away with him.
After supper he went out to look at the horse, which was picketed in the grass. Surveying the animal carefully, he untied the lariat and slipped a running noose over his nose; then giving a light bound, he was on his back in a second, and away went the horse and his rider, circling round and round on the prairie. Billings managed him by the rope alone, and convinced him that he was his master. When half a mile away, the horse started for camp at the top of his speed. Billings stood straight up on his back, and thus rode him into camp. As he passed us he jumped to the ground, allowed the horse to run to the full length of the lariat, when he threw him a complete somersault.
“That’s a pretty good horse,” said Billings.
“Yes, he’s a California horse; he was captured there wild,” replied father. The exhibition of horsemanship given by Billings on this occasion was really wonderful, and was the most skillful and daring feat of the kind that I ever witnessed. The remainder of the evening was spent around the camp, and Horace, who remained there, entertained us with several interesting chapters of his experiences.
Next morning he walked over to his own camp, but soon returned, mounted on a beautiful horse, with a handsome saddle, bridle and lariat. I thought he was a magnificent looking man. I envied his appearance, and my ambition just then was to become as skillful a horseman as he was. He had rigged himself out in his best style in order to make a good impression on his uncle at Weston, whither father and I accompanied him on horseback.
He was cordially received by Uncle Elijah, who paid him every possible attention, and gave me a handsome saddle and bridle for my pony, and in the evening when we rode out to the farm to see my mother and sisters, I started ahead to show them my present, as well as to tell them who was coming. They were delighted to see the long-lost Horace, and invited him to remain with us. When we returned to camp next day, Horace settled up with the proprietor of the horses, having concluded to make his home with us for that summer at least.

===Chinese===

父亲将烈马“小灰（Little Gray）”的脾性原原本本告诉了比林斯，尤其是它极其容易惊厥脱缰狂奔的致命恶习。比林斯听罢哈哈大笑，自信满满地断言：小灰在自己胯下休想撒野逃脱。
晚饭后，他踱步来到草地上察看拴在桩子上的战马。仔细打量了一番牲口后，他解开套马索，在马鼻梁上利索挽了个活套滑结；紧接着身形轻轻一纵，眨眼工夫便已稳坐马背之上。只见战马载着骑手长嘶一声狂冲而出，在辽阔草滩上风驰电掣般一圈又一圈疾旋狂奔。比林斯仅仅单凭一根绳索便将烈马拿捏得死死的，让它彻底领教了谁才是真正的主宰。冲到约半英里开外时，烈马撒开四蹄以雷霆万钧之势全速朝大营狂奔折返。比林斯索性双足并拢笔挺伫立在狂奔的马背之上，就这般迎风踏马冲入了营盘。就在飞掠过我们身侧的刹那，他飘身轻巧落地，任由马匹冲至套索全长尽头猛然一较劲，硬生生借力将疾驰的战马凌空掀了一个结结实实的大跟头。
“这马倒真算得上是一匹神驹！”比林斯赞道。
“可不是嘛，它是地地道道的加州马，当年在荒原上活捉的野物，”父亲回道。比林斯此番所展现的惊世骇俗驭马绝技当真令人叹为观止，是我平生亲眼目睹过最精绝纯熟、最具惊人胆魄的马术奇观。当晚的剩余时光在篝火营地旁悄然流逝，留宿大营的霍勒斯用他诸多妙趣横生的跌宕历险轶事逗得我们开怀尽兴。
次日破晓，他步行赶回自家营房，未几便跨着一匹神骏非凡的纯种良驹折返回来，通体配着华美鞍具、水勒与盘绕得整整齐齐的套马长索。在我的眼里，他真是一位威风凛凛的西部大丈夫。我无比羡慕崇拜他的英姿与做派，心头登时燃起了无穷雄心壮志，誓要练就同他一般出神入化的绝顶骑术。他特意换上了自己最体面的行头，只为给远在韦斯顿的以利亚叔父留下上佳的初见印象；父亲与我则策马同行，一道护送他前往。
以利亚叔父热情洋溢地款待了他，对其关怀备至体贴入微，还慷慨赠予我一副做工考究的精美马鞍与嚼子用于打扮我的小马驹。傍晚时分，当我们策马奔赴农庄探望母亲与妹妹们时，我一马当先飞奔在前，既急不可耐向她们炫耀新得的珍贵行头，又迫不及待向全家人通报这位贵客的降临。听闻失散多年的霍勒斯平安归来，全家人欣喜若狂，热情挽留他同我们共同生活。待次日折返营地后，霍勒斯便同原马队的东家结清了报酬，下定决心至少在那个夏天里留在我们身边同甘共苦。

===Original===

Father employed him in cutting house logs and building houses, but this work not being adapted to his tastes, he soon gave it up, and obtained government employment in catching United States horses. During the previous spring the government herd had stampeded from Fort Leavenworth, and between two and three hundred of the horses were running at large over the Kansas prairies, and had become quite wild. A reward of ten dollars was offered for every one of the horses that was captured and delivered to the quartermaster at Fort Leavenworth. This kind of work of course just suited the roaming disposition of Billings, especially as it was similar to that in which he had been engaged in California. The horses had to be caught with a lasso, with which he was very expert. He borrowed Little Gray, who was fleet enough for the wildest of the runaways, and then he at once began his horse hunting.
Everything that he did, I wanted to do. He was a sort of hero in my eyes, and I wished to follow in his footsteps. At my request and with father’s consent, he took me with him, and many a wild and perilous chase he led me over the prairie. I made rapid advances in the art of horsemanship, for I could have had no better teacher than Horace Billings. He also taught me how to throw the lasso, which, though it was a difficult thing to learn, I finally became, quite skillful in.
Whenever Horace caught one of the horses which acted obstinately, and would not be led, he immediately threw him to the ground, put a saddle and bridle on him, and gave me Little Gray to take care of. He would then mount the captive horse and ride him into Fort Leavenworth. I spent two months with Horace in this way, until at last no more of the horses were to be found. By this time I had become a remarkably good rider for a youth, and had brought both of my ponies under easy control.
Horace returned to assist father in hauling logs, which were being used in building a dwelling for the family who had moved over from Missouri. One day a team did not work to suit him, and he gave the horses a cruel beating. This greatly displeased father, who took him to task for it. Horace’s anger flew up in a moment; throwing down the lines he hurried to the house, and began packing up his traps. That same day he hired out to a Mormon train, and bidding us all goodbye started for Salt Lake, driving six yokes of oxen.

===Chinese===

父亲起初雇佣他砍伐建筑原木并营造木屋，但这种枯燥的粗苯苦力显然不合他的狂野脾胃；没过多久他便撂挑子不干，转而谋得了一份替政府抓捕逃逸军马的美差。在前一年的春季里，莱文沃思堡的一大群军马因受惊发生严重炸群，约有200到300匹军马散落游荡在辽阔的堪萨斯大草原上，早已沦为桀骜难驯的纯野马。军方悬赏重金：凡活捉一匹逃马并送交莱文沃思堡军需官者，一律赏赐10美元。这桩差事自然恰好契合比林斯浪迹荒原的天性，况且与他此前在加州操持的本行如出一辙。追捕这群野马必须动用套索，而这恰恰是他炉火纯青的看家本领。他向父亲借调了战马“小灰”——它的飞毛腿足以匹敌大草原上最狂暴的奔马——随即风风火火拉开了猎马大戏的序幕。
凡是他做的事情，我都渴望依样画葫芦。在我的少年心目中，他就是顶天立地的真正大英雄，我做梦都渴望步其后尘。在我的苦苦央求以及父亲的欣然应允下，他答应带我一同驰骋荒野；他率领着我在广袤大平原上展开了一场又一场凶险万分却又扣人心弦的狂暴追逐。得益于霍勒斯·比林斯这位不可多得的天才良师倾囊相授，我的马术水平突飞猛进。他还手把手教我如何甩出抛掷套索；尽管这项技艺极难领悟，但经年累月的摔打终使我掌握得相当娴熟精湛。
每逢霍勒斯捕获一匹性情桀骜、死活不肯顺从被牵着走的大烈马时，他便毫不客气地当场将其掀翻在地，强行套上马鞍和嚼子，随后把“小灰”交由我照看照料。紧接着他飞身跨上新俘获的桀骜战马，一路风驰电掣直接骑进莱文沃思堡交差。就这样，我与霍勒斯在大草原上形影不离地度过了整整2个月，直到荒原上再也搜寻不到一匹游荡的逃马为止。到了彼时，我以一名少年的年纪，已然成长为一名出类拔萃的少年骑术好手，亦能随心所欲驾轻就熟地彻底掌控我的那两匹小马了。
霍勒斯折返回营地协助父亲拖运原木，这些木料正用于为刚刚从密苏里州（Missouri）举家迁居过来的亲人们建造永久居所。有一回，一队挽马干活不顺他的心意，他便挥动皮鞭对牲口施以无情痛打。这让父亲极度不悦并当场严厉训斥责备了他。霍勒斯的暴脾气刹那间被点燃；他一把将缰绳狠狠摔在地上，气冲冲快步奔回木屋收拾随身行囊。就在同一天，他受雇投奔了一支摩门教徒的大篷车队，向我们草草道别后，赶着6轭耕牛，径直踏上了前往盐湖城（Salt Lake）的漫漫征程。

## XIV Earning a Title / 第十四章 赢得名号
===Original===

It was about this time that the end of the Kansas Pacific track was in the heart of the buffalo country, and the company was employing about twelve hundred men in the construction of the road. As the Indians were very troublesome, it was difficult to obtain fresh meat for the workmen, and the company therefore concluded to engage the services of hunters to kill buffaloes. Having heard of my experience and success as a buffalo hunter, Messrs. Goddard Brothers, who had the contract for boarding the employees of the road, met me in Hays City one day and made me a good offer to become their hunter, and I at once entered into a contract with them. They said that they would require about twelve buffaloes per day; that would be twenty-four hams, as we took only the hindquarters and hump of each buffalo. As this was to be dangerous work, on account of the Indians, who were riding all over that section of the country, and as I would be obliged to go from five to ten miles from the road each day to hunt the buffaloes, accompanied by only one man with a light wagon for the transportation of the meat, I of course demanded a large salary. They could afford to remunerate me well, because the meat would not cost them anything. They agreed to give me five hundred dollars per month, provided I furnished them all the fresh meat required.
Leaving my partner, Rose, to complete our grading contract, I immediately began my career as a buffalo hunter for the Kansas Pacific Railroad, and it was not long before I acquired considerable notoriety. It was at this time that the very appropriate name of “Buffalo Bill,” was conferred upon me by the road-hands. It has stuck to me ever since, and I have never been ashamed of it.
During my engagement as hunter for the company﻿—a period of less than eighteen mouths﻿—I killed 4,280 buffaloes; and I had many exciting adventures with the Indians, as well as hair-breadth escapes, some of which are well worth relating.
One day in the spring of 1868, I mounted Brigham and started for Smoky Hill River. After galloping about twenty miles I reached the top of a small hill overlooking the valley of that beautiful stream.
As I was gazing on the landscape, I suddenly saw a band of about thirty Indians nearly half a mile distant; I knew by the way they jumped on their horses that they had seen me as soon as I came into sight.

===Chinese===

正是在这段时间里，堪萨斯太平洋铁路（Kansas Pacific）的铺轨尽头已深入到野牛群的核心腹地，铁路公司当时雇佣了约1200名劳工投身修筑工程。鉴于印第安人（Indians）频频骚扰滋事，铁路工人极难吃上新鲜肉食；因此，公司决定出资聘请专业猎手捕杀野牛。承包了铁路员工伙食的戈达德兄弟（Messrs. Goddard Brothers）久闻我作为野牛猎手的丰富经验与辉煌斩获，有一日特意在海斯城（Hays City）与我会面，开出优渥的条件聘请我出任他们的专属猎手，我当即便同他们签订了契约。他们提出每天大约需要12头野牛；这相当于24块牛腿肉，因为我们每头野牛通常只割取后腿和肉峰。鉴于印第安骑兵在这一带四处游弋，这项差事实在凶险万分，而且我每天必须离开铁路线深入荒原5到10英里去猎杀野牛，身边仅有一名随从赶着一辆轻型大车协助运肉，我自然开出了高额报酬。由于野牛肉本身无需成本，他们完全付得起丰厚报酬。双方最终敲定：只要我能全额保障所需的新鲜肉食供应，他们每月支付我500美元。
我将先前的路基修筑承包工程全权移交给搭档罗斯（Rose）收尾，随即正式开启了作为堪萨斯太平洋铁路野牛猎手的全新职业生涯。没过多久，我便名声大噪。正是在这段时期，工地上修路的弟兄们送给了我一个极其贴切的响亮绰号——“野牛比尔（Buffalo Bill）”。从那时起，这个名字便与我形影不离相伴终生，而我也始终以此为傲。
在受雇担任公司猎手的不到18个月的时间里，我总共射杀了4280头野牛；在此期间，我同印第安人打过无数场惊心动魄的遭遇战，多次死里逃生险象环生，其中颇有几段历险极具玩味。
1868年春天的一天，我跨上爱驹布里格姆（Brigham），策马奔赴斯莫基希尔河（Smoky Hill River）。狂奔了约20英里后，我登上一座俯瞰这片明丽水系河谷的小山冈。
正当我极目远眺四方美景之际，猛然瞅见距离我不到半英里开外，有一伙约30人的印第安人队伍；单凭他们飞身上马的架势，我便断定早在我们打照面的刹那，对方就已经盯上我了。

===Original===

The only chance I had for my life was to make a run for it, and I immediately wheeled and started back towards the railroad. Brigham seemed to understand what was up, and he struck out as if he comprehended that it was to be a run for life. He crossed a ravine in a few jumps, and on reaching a ridge beyond, I drew rein, looked back and saw the Indians coming for me at full speed and evidently well-mounted. I would have had little or no fear of being overtaken if Brigham had been fresh; but as he was not, I felt uncertain as to how he would stand a long chase.
My pursuers seemed to be gaining on me a little, and I let Brigham shoot ahead again; when we had run about three miles farther, some eight or nine of the Indians were not over two hundred yards behind, and five or six of these seemed to be shortening the gap at every jump. Brigham now exerted himself more than ever, and for the next three or four miles he got “right down to business,” and did some of the prettiest running I ever saw. But the Indians were about as well-mounted as I was, and one of their horses in particular﻿—a spotted animal﻿—was gaining on me all the time. Nearly all the other horses were strung out behind for a distance of two miles, but still chasing after me.
The Indian who was riding the spotted horse was armed with a rifle, and would occasionally send a bullet whistling along, sometimes striking the ground ahead of me. I saw that this fellow must be checked, or a stray bullet from his gun might hit me or my horse; so, suddenly stopping Brigham, and quickly wheeling him around, I raised old “Lucretia” to my shoulder, took deliberate aim at the Indian and his horse, hoping to hit one or the other, and fired. He was not over eighty yards from me at this time, and at the crack of my rifle down went his horse. Not waiting to see if he recovered, I turned Brigham, and in a moment we were again fairly flying towards our destination; we had urgent business about that time, and were in a hurry to get there.
The other Indians had gained on us while I was engaged in shooting at their leader, and they sent several shots whizzing past me, but fortunately none of them hit the intended mark.

===Chinese===

我保住性命的唯一生路唯有绝尘狂奔，于是我当即调转马头，拼命朝铁路方向折返狂奔。布里格姆似乎心领神会当下的险局，撒开四蹄奋力飞奔，宛如深知这是一场关乎生死的夺命竞速。它三两下纵跃便跨越了一道深沟；登上对面的山脊后，我勒缰回望，只见印第安人正全速朝我猛扑过来，胯下坐骑显然脚力非凡。倘若布里格姆体力充沛，我断无惧色；然而它刚跑了长途早已疲惫，我心里委实没底它能否经受住这番漫长的亡命追击。
身后的追兵似乎在一点点缩小差距，我只得催促布里格姆再次全速前冲；待我们狂奔了约3英里后，追赶在最前头的8或9名印第安人已咬到我身后不足200码处，其中5或6人每一次纵跃都在拉近距离。布里格姆此时爆发出了前所未有的潜能，在接下来的3或4英里征程中彻底拿出了看家真本领，跑出了我平生所见最惊艳绝伦的奔腾步伐。然而印第安人的坐骑同样精良无比，尤其是其中一人骑乘的花斑斑纹马，一刻不停地向我逼近。其余的大多数马匹已被远远拉开，稀稀拉拉拖延在2英里开外的后方，但依然穷追不舍。
骑花斑马的印第安人手持步枪，时不时抬手朝我射出一枪，呼啸的流弹数度激射在我的前头地面上。我深知必须立刻刹住这小子的嚣张气焰，否则不知哪颗冷枪子弹就会夺去我或坐骑的性命；于是我猛地勒停布里格姆，迅疾拨转马头，将老伙计“卢克丽霞（Lucretia）”号步枪抵上肩窝，屏息凝神瞄准那名印第安人及其坐骑——只求能击中其中之一——扣动了扳机。彼时他距离我不过80码光景，随着一声清脆枪响，他的坐骑应声轰然栽倒在地。我根本顾不上去看他是否爬将起来，当即策转布里格姆，转瞬间我们再度向着终点绝尘飞奔；当时我们有要紧大事在身，正急着赶路呢。
趁着我驻足射击领头敌人的空当，其余印第安人已然追近，数颗枪弹呼啸着从我耳畔飞掠而过，万幸均未击中要害。

===Original===

To return their compliment I occasionally wheeled myself in the saddle and fired back at them, and one of my shots broke the leg of one of their horses, which left its rider *hors(e) de combat*, as the French would say.
Only seven or eight Indians now remained in dangerous proximity to me, and as their horses were beginning to lag somewhat, I checked my faithful old steed a little, to allow him an opportunity to draw an extra breath or two. I had determined, if it should come to the worst, to drop into a buffalo wallow, where I could stand the Indians off for a while; but I was not compelled to do this, as Brigham carried me through most nobly.
The chase was kept up until we came within three miles of the end of the railroad track, where two companies of soldiers were stationed for the purpose of protecting the workmen from the Indians. One of the outposts saw the Indians chasing me across the prairie, and gave the alarm. In a few minutes I saw, greatly to my delight, men coming on foot, and cavalrymen, too, came galloping to our rescue as soon as they could mount their horses. When the Indians observed this, they turned and ran in the direction from which they had come. In a very few minutes I was met by some of the infantrymen and trackmen, and jumping to the ground and pulling the blanket and saddle off of Brigham, I told them what he had done for me; they at once took him in charge, led him around, and rubbed him down so vigorously that I thought they would rub him to death.
Captain Nolan, of the Tenth Cavalry, now came up with forty of his men, and upon learning what had happened he determined to pursue the Indians. He kindly offered me one of the cavalry horses, and after putting my own saddle and bridle on the animal, we started out after the flying Indians, who only a few minutes before had been making it so uncomfortably lively for me. Our horses were all fresh and of excellent stock, and we soon began shortening the distance between ourselves and the redskins. Before they had gone five miles we overtook and killed eight of their number. The others succeeded in making their escape. On coming up to the place where I had killed the first horse﻿—the spotted one﻿—on my “home run,” I found that my bullet had struck him in the forehead and killed him instantly. He was a noble animal, and ought to have been engaged in better business.

===Chinese===

为了礼尚往来还以颜色，我时不时在马鞍上扭转身躯向后还击，其中一枪打断了对方一匹坐骑的长腿，借用法国人的俏皮话来说，这下让那个骑手彻底丧失了战斗力（hors de combat）。
此时只有7或8名印第安人仍死死咬在离我极近的危险距离内；察觉到对方的马匹也渐渐露出疲态，我便稍稍收紧缰绳放慢步调，让我那忠心耿耿的老伙计喘上一两口匀气。我暗自打定主意，倘若局面真恶化到不可收拾的绝境，我便跳进沿途的野牛泥水坑里据险死守，与这帮印第安人周旋抗衡一阵；不过事态并未走到那一步，因为布里格姆以不可思议的高尚韧性硬生生带我突围脱险。
这场狂暴的追逐战一直持续到距离铁路铺轨终点约3英里处，当地驻扎着两个连的守军，专门防范印第安人侵袭筑路工。一处前哨哨兵远远望见印第安人在大草原上穷追我不舍，当即拉响了警报。不过几分钟工夫，令我欣喜若狂的是，大批步兵徒步奔涌而出，骑兵们亦在跨上马鞍后疾驰策马赶来救援。印第安人见状大惊，急忙勒转马头按原路仓皇溃逃。眨眼间我便与几名步兵和铺轨工碰头；我翻身跃下马背，一把扯下布里格姆身上的马鞍与垫毯，向大伙讲述了它是如何舍命救我脱险的伟绩；弟兄们立即将它牵过去细心照料，又是遛马又是使劲揉搓擦拭，用力之猛险些没把它给搓散架。
第十骑兵团（Tenth Cavalry）的诺兰上尉（Captain Nolan）此时率领麾下40名精骑赶抵现场；听闻事情始末后，他毅然决定挥师追剿这伙印第安人。他慷慨地拨给我一匹优质军马，在我将自己的马鞍嚼子换上后，我们便呼啸着展开了对溃逃印第安人的大追击——而就在几分钟前，这帮家伙还追得我狼狈万分呢。我们的战马皆体魄健壮且毫无疲态，很快便急速缩短了同这群红皮肤（redskins）仇敌的距离。尚未追出5英里，我们便迎头赶上并当场击毙了其中的8名敌手，其余残敌则侥幸逃脱。当我们行抵我先前在绝尘逃命途中击倒第一匹花斑马的地点时，我发现我的子弹正中它的额心一击毙命。那真是一匹神骏健硕的好牲口，本该配得上更有出息的正经营生才是。

===Original===

When we got back to camp I found old Brigham grazing quietly and contentedly on the grass. He looked up at me as if to ask if we had got away with any of those fellows who had chased us. I believe he read the answer in my eyes.
Another very exciting hunting adventure of mine which deserves a place in these reminiscences occurred near Saline River. My companion at the time was a man called Scotty, a butcher, who generally accompanied me on these hunting expeditions to cut up the buffaloes and load the meat into a light wagon which he brought to carry it in. He was a brave little fellow and a most excellent shot. I had killed some fifteen buffaloes, and we had started for home with a wagon-load of meat. When within about eight miles of our destination, we suddenly ran on to a party of at least thirty Indians who came riding out of the head of a ravine.
On this occasion I was mounted on a most excellent horse belonging to the railroad company, and could easily have made my escape; but of course I could not leave Scotty who was driving a pair of mules hitched to the wagon. To think was to act, in those days; and as Scotty and I had often talked over a plan of defense in case we were ever surprised by Indians, we instantly proceeded to carry it out. We jumped to the ground, unhitched the mules quicker than it had ever been done before, and tied them and my horse to the wagon. We threw the buffalo hams upon the ground, and piled them around the wheels in such a shape as to form a breastwork. All this was done in a shorter time than it takes to tell it; and then, with our extra box of ammunition and three or four extra revolvers, which we always carried along with us, we crept under the wagon and were fully prepared to give our visitors the warmest kind of a reception.
The Indians came on pell-mell, but when they were within one hundred yards of us we opened such a sudden and galling fire upon them, that they held up and began to circle around the wagon instead of riding up to take tea with us. They however charged back and forth upon us several times, and their shots killed the two mules and my horse; but we gave it to them right and left, and had the satisfaction of seeing three of them fall to the ground not more than fifty yards away.

===Chinese===

待我们班师返回大营时，只见老布里格姆正悠闲惬意地在草地上啃食嫩草。它抬头凝望着我，目光仿佛在关切探问：我们到底有没有替它收拾那帮追兵。我相信它在我眼中读到了肯定的答卷。
在这些回忆纪事中，还值得大书特书我另一桩发生在萨林河（Saline River）畔凶险刺激的猎狩历险。我当时的随行搭档是一位人称斯科蒂（Scotty）的屠夫；在此类狩猎远征中，他通常赶着一辆轻型运货马车同我做伴，负责宰割野牛并将新鲜肉块装车搬运。这是个胆识过人的硬朗小个子，枪法更是百发百中极其刁钻。那天我大约猎杀了15头野牛，我们二人拉着满满一车鲜肉踏上返程归途。在距离大营目的地尚有约8英里处，我们猝不及防迎面撞上了一伙至少有30名凶悍骑手的印第安人，正从一处深壑裂谷的源头呼啸策马杀出。
当时我骑乘着一匹隶属于铁路公司的顶级良驹，单凭一人一骑本可轻而易举脱身逃命；然而我绝不可能抛弃正赶着双骡大车的斯科蒂独自逃生。在那个动荡的年代，心念一转便是生死行动；由于斯科蒂与我此前曾反复推演过倘若遭遇印第安人伏击时的防卫预案，我们当机立断立刻着手付诸实施。我们飞身跃下地面，以风卷残云般前所未有的神速卸下双骡，将骡子和我的战马死死拴在车架上。我们把沉重的野牛后腿肉狠狠砸摔在地上，严严实实码堆在车轮周遭，眨眼间筑起了一道肉墙胸墙掩体。这一切都在电光石火间一气呵成，动作之利索远胜于用言语来描述；紧接着，我们带上随身备足的备用弹药箱以及3或4支多余的左轮手枪，敏捷钻入大车底盘之下，全神贯注做好了给这批不速之客奉上一场最滚烫“热烈迎候”的万全准备。
印第安人乱哄哄蜂拥而上；待冲到距离我们仅100码处，我们冷不丁掀起了一阵猛烈凶狠的迎头齐射，打得他们猝不及防纷纷勒马，再也不敢贸然冲上来同我们“喝茶闲叙”，只得在车阵周围兜圈盘旋。然而他们依然接连数度向我们发起冲锋袭扰，密集的枪火先后夺去了两头挽骡和我的战马的性命；但我们二人从车底左右开弓猛烈还击，欣慰地亲眼目睹3名敌人在不到50码开外中弹扑倒在地。

===Original===

On seeing how well we were fortified and protected by our breastwork of hams, they probably came to the conclusion that it would be a difficult undertaking to dislodge us, for they drew off and gave us a rest, but only a short one.
This was the kind of fighting we had been expecting for a long time, as we knew that sooner or later we would be “jumped” by Indians while we were out buffalo hunting. I had an understanding with the officers who commanded the troops at the end of the track, that in case their pickets should at any time notice a smoke in the direction of our hunting ground, they were to give the alarm, so that assistance might be sent to us for the smoke was to indicate that we were in danger.
I now resolved to signal to the troops in the manner agreed on, and at the first opportunity set fire to the grass on the windward side of the wagon. The fire spread over the prairie at a rapid rate, causing a dense smoke which I knew would be seen at the camp. The Indians did not seem to understand this strategic movement. They got off from their horses, and from behind a bank or knoll, again peppered away at us; but we were well fortified, and whenever they showed their heads we let them know that we could shoot as well as they.
After we had been cooped up in our little fort, for about an hour, we discovered cavalry coming toward us at full gallop over the prairie. Our signal of distress had proved a success. The Indians saw the soldiers at about the same time that we did, and thinking that it would not be healthy for them to remain much longer in that vicinity, they mounted their horses and disappeared down the canyons of the creek. When the soldiers came up we had the satisfaction of showing them five “*good*” Indians, that is dead ones.
Two hours later we pulled into camp with our load of meat, which was found to be all right, except that it had a few bullets and arrows sticking in it.
While I was hunting for the Kansas Pacific railway, I had the pleasure, in the fall of 1867, of meeting the celebrated Kit Carson, one of, if not the oldest and most noted scout, guide, and hunter that our western country has ever produced. He was on his way to Washington. I also met him on his return from the East, and invited him to be my guest for a few days at Hays City, which invitation he accepted.

===Chinese===

眼见我们在这座野牛肉腿堆砌的肉墙掩体护卫下固若金汤，他们大概意识到要强行将我们拔除将是一场极其惨重的苦战，于是纷纷后撤拉开距离，让我们得以喘上一口粗气，不过这平静也仅仅持续了极短片刻。
此类血腥交火早已在我们意料之中，因为我们心知肚明，在荒原上捕猎野牛迟早会被印第安人撞破突袭。此前我便与铁路铺轨终点的守军军官们达成过默契约定：一旦前哨哨兵发现我们的狩猎荒原方向升起滚滚浓烟，必须立刻拉响警报派兵增援，因为狼烟正是我们身陷险境的求救信标。
我当即决意按照约定向大军发信号，抓住间歇的第一时间，在大车的迎风面纵火点燃了干枯青草。烈火在大草原上以惊人的速度蔓延翻滚，腾起阵阵遮天蔽日的滚滚浓烟，我笃信大营守军定能尽收眼底。印第安人似乎并未看透这一精妙战术意图。他们纷纷跳下战马，依托土坡与小山包的掩护，再度朝我们劈头盖脸胡乱扫射；然而我们工事坚固，每当敌手稍稍探头，我们便用精准的还击让他们领教我们的枪法绝不在他们之下。
在被困在这座简易肉垒小堡垒约莫1小时后，我们蓦地眺见骑兵大队正沿着大草原全速策马飞驰赶来增援。我们的狼烟求救大获全胜。印第安人几乎在同一时刻发现了飞驰而来的骑兵，心知再逗留此地绝无好下场，急忙纷纷翻身上马，一溜烟消失在溪谷深处的峡谷裂隙之中。待官兵们赶到跟前时，我们欣慰自豪地向他们展示了5个“好”印第安人——也就是毙命的死敌。
2小时后，我们拉着满满一车猎获的鲜肉昂首进驻大营；除了肉块上扎着几根断箭和嵌着几颗弹头外，整车野牛肉完好无损。
在受雇为堪萨斯太平洋铁路捕猎野牛期间，我有幸于1867年秋季结识了名满天下的基特·卡森（Kit Carson）——倘若他不算我国西部有史以来最年长、最负盛名的传奇斥候、向导兼猎手，那也绝对是其中数一数二的大宗师。当时他正取道前往华盛顿（Washington）。待他从东部折返途中，我们再次不期而遇，我便诚挚盛邀他作为我的尊贵宾客在海斯城盘桓数日，他欣然赏光应允。

===Original===

He then proceeded to Fort Lyon, Colorado, near which place his son-in-law, Mr. Boggs, and family, resided. At this time his health was failing, and shortly afterwards he died at Mr. Boggs’ residence on the Picket Wire Creek.

===Chinese===

随后他动身启程赶赴科罗拉多州里昂堡（Fort Lyon, Colorado），其女婿鲍格斯先生（Mr. Boggs）一家便定居在要塞附近。彼时他的身体每况愈下已现颓势；离开海斯城不久之后，这位传奇英雄便在皮克特怀尔溪（Picket Wire Creek）畔鲍格斯先生的府邸中溘然长逝。

## Endnotes / 尾注
===Original===

1. Poor Billy Comstock was afterwards treacherously murdered by the Indians. He and Sharpe Grover visited a village of Indians, supposed to be peaceably inclined, near Big Spring Station, in Western Kansas; and after spending several hours with the redskins in friendly conversation, they prepared to depart, having declined an invitation to pass the night there. It appears that Comstock’s beautiful white-handled revolver had attracted the attention of the Indians, who overtook him and his companion when they had gone about half a mile. After surrounding the two men they suddenly attacked them. They killed, scalped and robbed Comstock; but Grover, although severely wounded, made his escape, owing to the fleetness of the excellent horse which he was riding. This sad event occurred August 27, 1868. ↩︎
2. Major North is now my partner in a cattle ranch in Nebraska. ↩︎
3. Since traveled with me in my Dramatic Combination as interpreter for Sioux Indians. ↩︎
4. Near the lonely camp where I had so long been laid up with a broken leg, when trapping years before with Dave Harrington. ↩︎
5. A favorite expression of the Professor’s. ↩︎

===Chinese===

1. 可怜的比利·康姆斯托克（Billy Comstock）后来不幸惨遭印第安人（Indians）背信弃义地杀害。他与夏普·格罗弗（Sharpe Grover）曾一同造访堪萨斯州西部（Western Kansas）大泉驿站（Big Spring Station）附近一处据信倾向和平的印第安村落；在同这群红皮肤和睦攀谈数小时后，两人婉拒了留宿过夜的挽留，整装准备离去。据悉，康姆斯托克随身佩带的一支精美绝伦的白柄左轮手枪引来了印第安人的觊觎；待两人走出约半英里时，印第安人从后方追赶了上来。将两人合围之后，他们猝不及防发难猛攻。他们残忍杀害了康姆斯托克，剥去其头皮并洗劫了财物；格罗弗虽身负重伤，却凭借胯下神骏良驹的飞奔之速侥幸杀出重围逃得一命。这桩惨案发生于1868年8月27日。 ↩︎
2. 诺斯少校（Major North）如今是我在内布拉斯加州（Nebraska）一处畜牧牧场的合伙人。 ↩︎
3. 此后曾随同我加入“巡回戏剧联合剧团（Dramatic Combination）”，担任苏族（Sioux）印第安人的随团翻译。 ↩︎
4. 位于那处荒凉孤寂的旧营地附近；多年前我与戴夫·哈灵顿（Dave Harrington）一道在荒原设陷阱捕兽时，我曾因摔断一条腿而在那里卧床养伤了许久。 ↩︎
5. 这是教授（Professor）生平最钟爱的口头禅。 ↩︎

## IX Fast Driving / 第九章 飞马狂飙
===Original===

It was in the spring of 1861, while I was at Horseshoe, that the eastern-bound coach came in one day loaded down with passengers and baggage, and stopped for dinner; Horseshoe being a regular dinner station as well as a home station. The passengers consisted of six Englishmen, and they had been continually grumbling about the slow time that was being made by the stages, saying that the farther they got East the slower they went.
“These blarsted ’eathens don’t know hanything habout staging, hany-’ow,” remarked one of them.
“Blarst me bloody heyes! they cawn’t stage in this country as we do in Hingland, you know,” said another.
Their remarks were overheard by Bob Scott, who was to drive the coach from Horseshoe to Fort Laramie, and he determined to give them satisfaction before they got over his route. Scott was known to be the best reinsman and the most expert driver on the whole line of the road. He was a very gentlemanly fellow in his general appearance and conduct, but at times he would become a reckless daredevil, and would take more desperate chances than any other driver. He delighted in driving wild teams on the darkest nights, over a mountain road, and had thus become the hero of many a thrilling adventure.
It happened on this day he was to drive a team of six pony express horses, which had been only partially broken in as a stage team. As the stock-tenders were hitching them up, Bob, who was standing by, said, “I’ll show them Englishmen that we ‘blarsted heathens’ do know something about staging in this country.” We all knew from Bob’s looks that something was up.
It required several men to hitch up this frisky team, as a man had to hold on to each one of the horses by the bits, while they were stringing them out. The Englishmen came out from dinner, and were delighted to see the horses prancing and pawing as if anxious to start.
“Ha! my deah fellah, now we will ’ave a fine ride this hafternoon,” said one of them.
“By Jove! those are the kind of ’orses they hought to ’ave on hall the teams,” remarked another.
“Are you the lad who is going to drive today?” asked another of Bob.
“Yes, gentlemen,” answered Bob, “I’ll show you how we stage it in this country.”
Bob mounted the box, gathered the lines, and pulling the horses strongly by the bits, he sang out to the Englishmen, “All aboard!” Bob’s companion on the box was Capt.

===Chinese===

1861年春，正当我驻留马蹄铁站（Horseshoe）期间，有一日一辆满载乘客与行李的东行长途驿马车驶入车站停歇用午餐；马蹄铁站既是沿途固定的正餐补给站，也是一处中心大站。这趟车的乘客是6名英国绅士，一路上他们喋喋不休抱怨驿马车脚程奇慢无比，嚷嚷着越往东走车速倒越慢吞吞了。
“这帮该死的乡巴佬生番，压根就不懂怎么赶车！”其中一人傲慢地嘟囔道。
“真是活见鬼！在这鬼地方赶车，哪能跟咱们英格兰本土的体面马车相提并论嘛！”另一人随声附和。
这番风凉话恰好被鲍勃·斯科特（Bob Scott）听了个正着。鲍勃正是负责将这辆驿车从马蹄铁站一路驱赶至拉勒米堡（Fort Laramie）的值班车夫，他暗下决心，在车轮跑完自己的这段辖区之前，非得让这帮洋绅士好好“过过瘾”不可。在整条驿道线上，斯科特是公认手法最精绝、技艺最超群的驭马头把交椅。平日里他言谈举止颇具绅士风度，可骨子里一旦野性发作，便会化身为彻头彻尾亡命狂徒，比任何车夫都敢拿命去搏险。他生平最痴迷在漆黑如墨的深夜里，赶着桀骜难驯的生荒烈马拉车在崇山峻岭间飞奔，因而成了无数惊险传奇中的风云英雄。
偏巧在这一天，他奉命驱赶的是一组由6匹小马快递战马组成的驭马队，这批烈马仅仅接受过粗浅的拉车调教。趁着马夫们忙着套车之际，伫立一旁的鲍勃冷笑道：“我倒要让这帮英国佬好好瞧瞧，咱们这群‘该死的生番’在西部到底懂不懂怎么赶车！”大伙一瞧鲍勃的神情，心里便全明白了：今天准有好戏看了。
为了把这组狂躁乱跳的烈马套上辕架，足足动用了好几条壮汉，甚至不得不安排专人死死攥住每匹战马的嚼子才能将马队逐一排开。英国绅士们用罢午餐踱步而出，眼见马匹四蹄腾空、急躁刨地直欲狂奔的架势，顿时喜上眉梢。
“哈！亲爱的伙计，看来咱们今天下午能舒舒服服跑上一程痛快路了！”其中一人眉飞色舞。
“可不是嘛！见鬼，所有车队都早该换上这种神骏骏马才是！”另一人附和道。
“年轻人，今天是由你来给我们当车夫吗？”又有一人询问鲍勃。
“没错，先生们，”鲍勃彬彬有礼回道，“我这就让各位好好领教领教，咱们在这片土地上究竟是怎么赶马车的！”
鲍勃身手利索翻上驭手座，收紧缰绳，死死勒住马嚼子，朝着英国人大喊一声：“全员上车！”当时与鲍勃同坐在驭手座上的旅伴是“蟋蟀上尉”（Capt. Cricket）——

===Original===

Cricket; a little fellow who was the messenger of the coach. After everybody was seated, Bob told the stock-tenders to “turn ’em loose.”
We, who were standing around to see the stage start out, expected it would go off at a lively rate. We were considerably surprised, therefore, when, after the horses had made a few lively jumps, Bob put on the big California brakes and brought them down to a walk. The road, for a distance of four miles, gradually rose to the top of a hill, and all the way up this ascent, Bob held the impatient team in check.
“Blarst your heyes, driver, why don’t you let them go?” exclaimed one of the passengers, who had all along been expecting a very brisk ride. Every once in a while they would ask him some such question, but he paid no attention to them. At last he reached the top of the hill, and then he suddenly flung three of the lines on the left side of the team, and the other three on the right side. He then began “playing the silk to them,”﻿—that is to say, he began to lash them unmercifully. The team started off like a streak of lightning, so to speak, without a single rein being held by the driver. Bob cried out to the Englishmen, saying, “Hold on, gentlemen, and I’ll give you a lively ride, and show you how to stage it in the Rocky Mountains.”
His next movement was to pull the lamps out of the sockets and throw them at the leaders. The glass broke upon their backs and nearly set them wild, but being so accustomed to running the road, they never once left the track, and went flying on down the grade towards the next station, eight miles distant, the coach bouncing over the loose stones and small obstacles, and surging from side to side, as an eggshell would in the rapids of Niagara. Not satisfied with the breakneck rate at which they were traveling, Bob pulled out his revolver and fired in rapid succession, at the same time yelling in a demoniacal manner.
By this time the Englishmen had become thoroughly frightened, as they saw the lines flying wildly in every direction and the team running away. They did not know whether to jump out or remain in the coach. Bob would occasionally look down from his seat, and, seeing their frightened faces, would ask, “Well, how do you like staging in this country now?”

===Chinese===

一个身材矮小精干、专事沿途押运公文的随车押运员。待所有乘客各就各位坐定后，鲍勃向马夫们断喝一声：“撒手放行！”
