I rode on for a few miles further, and then hiding my horse in a low ravine, I crawled up a high hill, where I obtained a good view of the country. I could see for four or five miles up the creek, and got sight of a village and of two or three hundred ponies in its vicinity. I waited until night came and then I succeeded in riding into the Indian camp unobserved.
I had seen Spotted Tail’s camp when he came from the north and I knew the kind of lodge he was living in. As I entered the village I wrapped a blanket around my head so that the Indians could not tell whether I was a white or a red man. In this way I rode around until I found Spotted Tail’s lodge. Dismounting from my horse I opened his tent door and looked in and saw the old chief lying on some robes. I spoke to him and he recognized me at once and invited me to enter. Inside the lodge I found a white man, an old frontiersman, Todd Randall, who was Spotted Tail’s agent and who had lived a great many years with the Indians. He understood their language perfectly and did all the interpreting for Spotted Tail. Through him I readily communicated with the chief and informed him of my errand. I told him that the warriors and chiefs would greatly please General Sheridan if they would meet him in about ten sleeps at the old Government crossing of the Red Willow. I further informed him that there was a great chief from across the water who was coming there to visit him. Spotted Tail replied that he would be very glad to go; that the next morning he would call his people together and select those who would accompany him. I told Spotted Tail how I had entered his camp.

===Chinese===

在茫茫荒原上，我随时可能一头撞上一伙正在外出游猎的年轻印第安武士；由于我在苏族（Sioux）人里结怨甚多、宿敌无数，一旦狭路相逢，势必招致杀身之祸。
行至第一天傍晚，我在弗伦奇曼岔河的支流恶臭水溪（Stinking Water）畔露宿，在茂密的树林深处点起了一小堆营火；但由于天寒地冻、奇寒刺骨，我整夜冻得难以合眼。天刚蒙蒙亮我便提早赶路，沿着弗伦奇曼岔河逆流而上；到了傍晚时分，看着沿途杂乱新鲜的马蹄印以及四处散落、刚被猎杀不久的野牛残骸，我断定自己已极其接近斑尾的大营了。我策马继续前行了数里，随后将坐骑小心拴在一处背风深沟里，自己则徒步匍匐爬上一座高耸的土丘极目远眺。放眼望去，顺着溪流方向可以清晰俯瞰四五英里外的地界，一座规模庞大的印第安村落赫然映入眼帘，周边散牧着两三百匹健壮的小马。我耐着性子隐蔽潜伏到夜幕彻底笼罩荒野，随后借着夜色掩护，悄无声息地策马摸进了印第安大营，未曾惊动任何人。
此前斑尾从北方（North）率部南下时我曾目睹过他的营帐，因而对老酋长下榻的大帐形制了然于胸。踏入村落时，我将厚毛毯紧紧包裹住头部，叫营里的印第安人根本辨不清我究竟是白人还是红皮肤同胞。我就这般骑着马四处搜寻，直到准确找到了斑尾酋长的专属大帐。我翻身下马，掀开门帘向内窥视，只见老酋长正神态安详地侧卧在几张厚兽皮褥子上。我轻声唤了他一句，他当场便辨认出了我的声音，立刻热情招手邀我进帐。营帐之内还坐着一名白人老开拓者托德·兰德尔（Todd Randall），此人常年担任斑尾酋长的代理人，与印第安人同吃同住数十载，能说一口极其纯正流利的印第安土语，斑尾平日对外的一切交涉翻译全由他一手操办。借助兰德尔的翻译通译，我与老酋长毫无窒碍地坦诚交谈，向他说明了我此行的重大使命。我转达道，若各位勇士与部落首领能在十个落日（约10天）后移步红柳溪的官道老渡口迎候，谢里登将军将不胜感激。我进一步向他透露，届时将有一位远涉重洋而来的异国无上大统领（大公）亲临荒原拜会于他。斑尾爽快答应，表示十分乐意前往；并约定次日一早召集全体部众，亲自挑选随行出访的精兵强将。随后我向斑尾坦陈了自己刚才潜入大营的经过。

===Original===

He replied that I had acted wisely; that although his people were friendly, yet some of his young men had a grudge against me, and I might have had difficulty with them had I met them away from the village. He directed his squaw to get me something to eat, and ordered that my horse be taken care of, and upon his invitation I spent the remainder of the night in his lodge.
Next morning the chiefs and warriors assembled according to orders, and to them was stated the object of my visit. They were asked:
“Do you know who this man is?”
“Yes, we know him well,” replied one, “that is Pa-he-haska,” (that being my name among the Sioux, which translated means “Long Hair”) “that is our old enemy,” a great many of the Indians, who were with Spotted Tail at this time, had been driven out of the Republican country.
“That is he,” said Spotted Tail. “I want all my people to be kind to him and treat him as my friend.”
I noticed that several of them were looking daggers at me. They appeared as if they wished to raise my hair then and there. Spotted Tail motioned and I followed him into his lodge, and thereupon the Indians dispersed. Having the assurance of Spotted Tail that none of the young men would follow me I started back for the Red Willow, arriving the second night.
There I found Captain Egan with a company of the Second Cavalry and a wagon train loaded with tents, grain, provisions, etc. The men were leveling off the ground and were making preparations to put up large wall tents for the Grand Duke Alexis and his suite, and for General Sheridan, his staff and other officers, and invited guests of the party. Proceeding to Fort McPherson I reported what had been done. Thereupon Quartermaster Hays selected from the five or six hundred horses in his charge, seventy-five of the very best, which were sent to the Red Willow, to be used by Alexis and his party at the coming hunt. In a day or two a large supply of provisions, liquors, etc., arrived from Chicago, together with bedding and furniture for the tents; all of which were sent over to Camp Alexis.
At last, on the morning of the 12th of January, 1872, the Grand Duke and party arrived at North Platte by special train; in charge of a Mr. Francis Thompson.

===Chinese===

老酋长听罢点头称许，说我处置得极为明智；尽管部落主流与官军和睦修好，但族里几个愣头青年轻武士私下一直对我怀恨在心，倘若在村落外头荒郊野岭狭路相逢，免不了要爆发一场你死我活的流血火拼。他随即吩咐身边的印第安妇人为我张罗热腾腾的饭食，命人将我的坐骑牵去悉心喂饮；在老酋长的盛情相邀下，我便在他温暖宽敞的兽皮大帐内踏踏实实歇下了后半夜。
翌日清晨，部落各级酋长与剽悍勇士奉命齐聚大帐前，老酋长当众向全族郑重宣布了我此行的来意。老酋长指着我高声发问：
“你们可认得眼前这汉子是谁？”
“认得，化成灰也认得他！”一名勇士高声答道，“他就是帕赫哈斯卡（Pa-he-haska）！”——这正是我在苏族人中间家喻户晓的绰号，译意为“长发”——“他是咱们的老冤家宿敌！”彼时伴随在斑尾身边的众多武士中，有相当一部分当年正是被我随军扫荡逐出共和河流域的溃兵。
“没错，正是他，”斑尾酋长神色威严地朗声道，“但我要求全族上下必须善待于他，奉他为我斑尾的至交挚友！”
我冷眼旁观，注意到人群中有好几个狠角色正咬牙切齿拿眼睛直剜我，那副凶神恶煞的模样恨不得当场拔刀割下我的头皮。斑尾酋长朝我使了个眼色，我便随他转身退入大帐，门外的印第安武士们这才怏怏散去。在得到斑尾酋长的铁保、确信绝不会有不开眼的年轻人暗中尾随放冷枪之后，我当即动身拔营折返红柳溪，并于第二天深夜安然返回。
回到红柳溪时，第二骑兵团（Second Cavalry）的伊根上尉（Captain Egan）早已率部赶到，大车队满载着帐篷、谷物粮食、给养物资等辎重正热火朝天施工。士兵们正忙着平整地面，紧锣密鼓搭设供俄国阿列克谢大公及其高贵随从下榻的高规格大壁帐，以及供谢里登将军、参谋军官及受邀各界名流下榻的行军大营。随后我快马加鞭赶回麦克弗森堡复命。军需官海斯上尉当即从自己经管的五六百匹战马中精挑细选出75匹万里挑一的顶级纯血战驹，先行调拨运往红柳溪大营，专供阿列克谢大公及其随行贵族围猎御用。一两天后，由芝加哥火速调拨空运而来的海量山珍海味、珍馐佳酿以及高档床榻被褥等豪奢家具源源不断抵达成营地，全数送抵阿列克谢大营。
1872年1月12日上午，俄国阿列克谢大公一行搭乘由弗朗西斯·汤普森先生（Mr. Francis Thompson）专程调度护送的皇家专列，顺利抵达成北普拉特（North Platte）车站。

===Original===

Captain Hays and myself, with five or six ambulances, fifteen or twenty extra saddle-horses and a company of cavalry under Captain Egan, were at the depot in time to receive them. Presently General Sheridan and a large, fine-looking young man, whom we at once concluded to be the Grand Duke came out of the cars and approached us. General Sheridan at once introduced me to the Grand Duke as Buffalo Bill, for he it was, and said that I was to take charge of him and show him how to kill buffalo.
In less than half an hour the whole party were dashing away towards the south, across the South Platte and towards the Medicine; upon reaching which point we halted for a change of horses and a lunch. Resuming our ride we reached Camp Alexis in the afternoon. General Sheridan was well pleased with the arrangements that had been made and was delighted to find that Spotted Tail and his Indians had arrived on time. They were objects of great curiosity to the Grand Duke, who spent considerable time in looking at them, and watching their exhibitions of horsemanship, sham fights, etc. That evening the Indians gave the grand war dance, which I had arranged for.
General Custer, who was one of the hunting party, carried on a mild flirtation with one of Spotted Tail’s daughters, who had accompanied her father thither, and it was noticed also that the Duke Alexis paid considerable attention to another handsome redskin maiden. The night passed pleasantly, and all retired with great expectations of having a most enjoyable and successful buffalo hunt. The Duke Alexis asked me a great many questions as to how we shot buffaloes, and what kind of a gun or pistol we used, and if he was going to have a good horse. I told him that he was to have my celebrated buffalo horse Buckskin Joe, and when we went into a buffalo herd all he would have to do was to sit on the horse’s back and fire away.
At nine o’clock next morning we were all in our saddles, and in a few minutes were galloping over the prairies in search of a buffalo herd. We had not gone far before we observed a herd some distance ahead of us crossing our way; after that we proceeded cautiously, so as to keep out of sight until we were ready to make a charge.

===Chinese===

海斯上尉同我早早率领五六辆双蓬客运救护车、十五至二十匹精良的备用乘马，并在伊根上尉统领的一个精锐骑兵连护卫下，准时肃立在站台恭候圣驾。不多时，谢里登将军陪同着一位身材高大魁梧、相貌堂堂英挺的年轻贵胄走出车厢朝我们款步走来，大伙儿一眼便断定这位必定是阿列克谢大公本尊无疑。谢里登将军当即郑重将我引荐给大公，直呼我的大名——野牛比尔（Buffalo Bill）；将军爽朗地告知大公，此番将由我全权负责他的安全与向导，亲自传授并示范如何在这片莽原上围猎野牛。
半小时不到，全队人马便浩浩荡荡朝南方开拔疾驰，风驰电掣横渡南普拉特河，一路直扑梅迪辛溪；行抵梅迪辛溪后，大队稍作休整，换乘了生力战马并享用了简便午餐。随后全军重整旗鼓纵马前行，于下午时分平安抵达阿列克谢营。谢里登将军对营地的周密布防与奢华安排赞不绝口，尤为欣慰的是斑尾酋长及其部族勇士已分秒不差准时抵达到位。这群原生态的美洲印第安人引发了大公极大的好奇心，他兴致盎然地围着他们打量了良久，饶有兴致地观摩了印第安骑手的神乎骑术表演与模拟战阵拼杀。当晚，印第安人燃起熊熊烈火，献上了由我事先操办安排的盛大传统战舞盛典。
作为狩猎团重要贵宾之一的卡斯特将军（General Custer），当晚同随父随行的斑尾酋长爱女相谈甚欢、含情脉脉；而旁人也敏锐注意到，阿列克谢大公本人对另一位体态婀娜、容貌姣好的印第安红皮肤少女也表现出了相当热络的殷勤关切。大伙儿在欢声笑语中度过了一个无比美妙的良宵，怀揣着对次日荒原大猎的大获全胜与无限憧憬安然入睡。睡前，大公好奇地向我打听了数不清的问题：我们究竟是如何追射野牛的、用什么枪械、手枪管不管用、分派给他的战马脚力如何等等。我胸有成竹地宽慰大公，说为他御用准备的是大名鼎鼎的猎牛神驹“鹿皮乔”，一旦纵马杀入野牛阵中，他所要做的不过是稳稳端坐在马鞍上、扣动扳机尽情倾泻子弹即可。
次日上午9点整，全员已全副武装翻身上鞍，几分钟内便纵马疾驰在无垠草原上，四处搜寻野牛大群。大队前行未几，便遥遥望见前方地平线上一大群野牛正横穿我们的去路；我们随即压低马步谨慎借地形隐蔽推进，力求在发起冲锋前绝不打草惊蛇。

===Original===

Of course the main thing was to give Alexis the first chance and the best shot at the buffaloes, and when all was in readiness we dashed over a little knoll that had hidden us from view, and in a few minutes we were among them. Alexis at first preferred to use his pistol instead of a gun. He fired six shots from this weapon at buffaloes only twenty feet away from him, but as he shot wildly, not one of his bullets took effect. Riding up to his side and seeing that his weapon was empty, I exchanged pistols with him. He again fired six shots, without dropping a buffalo.
Seeing that the animals were bound to make their escape without his killing one of them, unless he had a better weapon, I rode up to him, gave him my old reliable “Lucretia,” and told him to urge his horse close to the buffaloes, and I would then give him the word when to shoot. At the same time I gave old Buckskin Joe a blow with my whip, and with a few jumps the horse carried the Grand Duke to within about ten feet of a big buffalo bull.
“Now is your time,” said I. He fired, and down went the buffalo. The Grand Duke stopped his horse, dropped his gun on the ground, and commenced waving his hat. When his suite came galloping up, he began talking to them in a tongue which I could not understand. Presently General Sheridan joined the group, and the ambulances were brought up. Very soon the corks began to fly from the champagne bottles, in honor of the Grand Duke Alexis, who had killed the first buffalo.
It was reported in a great many of the newspapers that I shot the first buffalo for Alexis, while in some it was stated that I held the buffalo while His Royal Highness killed it. But the way I have related the affair is the correct version.
It was thought that we had had about sport enough for one day, and accordingly I was directed by General Sheridan to guide the party back to camp, and we were soon on our way thither. Several of the party, however, concluded to have a little hunt on their own account, and presently we saw them galloping over the prairie in different directions in pursuit of buffaloes.
While we were crossing a deep ravine, on our way to camp, we ran into a small band of buffaloes that had been frightened by some of the hunters. As they rushed past us, not more than thirty yards distant, Alexis raised his pistol, fired and killed a buffalo cow.

===Chinese===

毫无疑问，全队的核心默契是将第一枪的无上荣耀与最佳射击身位拱手礼让给阿列克谢大公；一切准备停当之后，我们纵马猛然翻过先前遮挡视线的一座向阳小土岗，数分钟内便全速杀入了狂奔的野牛群核心。起初大公偏爱使用随身佩戴的短轮手枪而非长步枪。他策马逼近到距野牛仅20英尺的极近距离连开6枪，但由于马上颠簸、瞄准全无章法，6发飞弹无一命中猎物。我快马并驾齐驱驰到他身侧，见他枪膛已空，二话不说拔出自己的备用手枪与他调换。大公接枪再度连发6弹，依然未能撂倒任何一头狂奔的野牛。
眼见牛群死命狂奔即将四散突围，若无重型利器大公今日势必落得空手而归的尴尬窘境，我当即策马紧贴上去，将我手中那杆百发百中、最值得信赖的老伙计“卢克丽霞”硬塞入他手中，叮嘱他狠狠踢马紧贴野牛，听我号令再行开火。与此同时我甩手一马鞭狠狠抽在老战马“鹿皮乔”屁股上，神驹心领神会腾空几个纵跃，瞬间将阿列克谢大公送到了距一头狂奔的巨型野牛公牛不到10英尺的绝杀距离！
“就是现在，开火！”我扯开嗓子厉声大喊。大公应声扣动扳机，只听砰的一声巨响，狂奔的巨兽轰然栽倒在地！大公兴奋得猛地勒住缰绳，随手将步枪往地上一扔，挥舞着头顶的帽子放声狂呼。当随行贵族侍从们拍马狂奔赶来护驾时，他满面通红手舞足蹈，用一连串我根本听不懂的俄语母语向他们激动滔滔不绝讲述。谢里登将军随后也策马赶来相贺，后方的客运救护车迅速疾驰跟进。顷刻间，庆祝阿列克谢大公首开纪录猎杀第一头野牛的香槟酒瓶塞砰砰乱飞，泡沫四溅！
当时各大报纸曾谣传是我替大公打死了第一头野牛，甚至有小报杜撰说是我死死按住野牛让大公尊驾从容开枪；在此我必须郑重辟谣，我上面所据实记录的方是绝无半点虚构的真实历史原貌。
大伙儿认为大公初试锋芒已尽兴尽致，今日的开门红战果已足够圆满，谢里登将军遂指示我护送大队返营，队伍随即便踏上归途。然而同行的几位宾客却意犹未尽，决定各自开溜展开私人围猎；不多时，便见他们在草原上三三两两策马追逐野牛的身影。
正在我们横穿一条深邃峡谷返回大营的半道上，冷不丁迎面撞上了一小群被散乱猎手惊吓四处逃窜的野牛。正当牛群贴着我们身侧不足30码飞奔呼啸而过之际，阿列克谢大公眼疾手快拔出手枪抬手便是一枪，竟不可思议地当场轰翻了一头健壮的母野牛！

===Original===

It was either an extraordinary good shot or a “scratch”﻿—probably the latter, for it surprised the Grand Duke as well as everybody else. We gave him three cheers, and when the ambulance came up we took a pull at the champagne in honor of the Grand Duke’s success. I was in hopes that he would kill five or six more buffaloes before we reached camp, especially if a basket of champagne was to be opened every time he dropped one.
General Sheridan directed me to take care of the hides and heads of the buffaloes which Alexis had killed, as the Duke wished to keep them as souvenirs of the hunt. I also cut out the choice meat from the cow and brought it into camp, and that night at supper Alexis had the pleasure of dining on broiled buffalo steak obtained from the animal which he had shot himself.
We remained at this camp two or three days, during which we hunted most of the time, the Grand Duke himself killing eight buffaloes.
One day Alexis desired to see how the Indians hunted buffaloes and killed them with bow and arrow; so Spotted Tail, selecting some of his best hunters, had them surround a herd, and bring the animals down, not only with arrows, but with lances. The Grand Duke was told to follow upon the heels of one celebrated Indian hunter, whose name was “Two Lance,” and watch him bring down the game; for this chief had the reputation of being able to send an arrow through and through the body of a buffalo. Upon this occasion he did not belie his reputation, for he sent an arrow *through* a buffalo, which fell dead at the shot, and the arrow was given to Alexis as a souvenir of his hunt on the American Plains.
When the Grand Duke was satisfied with the sport, orders were given for the return to the railroad. The conveyance provided for the Grand Duke and General Sheridan was a heavy double-seated open carriage, or rather an Irish dogcart, and it was drawn by four spirited cavalry horses which were not much used to the harness. The driver was Bill Reed, an old overland stage driver and wagon master; on our way in, the Grand Duke frequently expressed his admiration of the skillful manner in which Reed handled the reins.
General Sheridan informed the Duke that I also had been a stage-driver in the Rocky Mountains, and thereupon His Royal Highness expressed a desire to see me drive.

===Chinese===

这一枪究竟是大公枪法通神还是纯属瞎猫碰上死耗子的“撞大运”——依我看恐怕多半是后者，因为这一枪不仅惊呆了全场所有人，连大公自己都瞠目结舌愣在当场。大伙儿齐声为他爆发出了三声雷鸣般的欢呼，待后方补给车跟上后，全营再度开怀痛饮香槟为大公的惊人战绩庆功。我心头暗忖，照这架势，若每猎杀一头野牛就要报销一整筐上等香槟，那我真恨不得大公在回营前能一口气再打死个五六头才好！
谢里登将军责成我专门经管大公所击毙野牛的皮张与头骨，大公执意要将这些战利品完好带回俄国本土永久珍藏留念。我亲手割下了那头母牛身上最细嫩的里脊精华带回大营；当晚的大宴上，阿列克谢大公兴致勃勃品尝到了由自己亲手开枪击毙的野牛制成的鲜嫩烤牛排。
我们在红柳溪大营盘桓了整整两三天，其间大部分时光都在纵马围猎中度过，阿列克谢大公一人便独揽了击毙8头野牛的骄人战绩。
某日，大公心血来潮，提出想亲眼见识一番印第安人如何凭传统弓箭骑射猎杀野牛；于是斑尾酋长亲自点将，抽调了部落中最顶尖的弓箭手，在平原上包抄合围了一群野牛，不仅用弓箭攒射，更挥舞锋利的长矛突刺击杀。大公受邀紧随一位声名远扬的传奇印第安神射手身后策马观摩，这位神箭手名叫“双矛”（Two Lance）；此人在大平原威名赫赫，号称能以硬弓骨箭生生贯穿野牛强韧的身躯。在当天的实战中他果然名不虚传，只见他纵马疾驰一箭射出，利箭竟生生由里向外彻底“贯穿”了狂奔的野牛，巨兽中箭当场毙命倒地！那支建功的穿牛骨箭被郑重馈赠给阿列克谢大公，作为其美洲大平原围猎的无上纪念品。
当大公对荒原围猎彻底心满意足之后，全队奉命拔营返程开赴铁路车站。专为阿列克谢大公与谢里登将军调拨的座驾是一辆沉重宽敞的双排座敞篷马车（确切地说是爱尔兰双排座轻便马车），由4匹性情暴烈、平素极少套拉车具的精壮纯血骑兵战马套拉。驭手是老牌陆路长途大车领队比尔·里德（Bill Reed）；在回程的平原坦途上，大公屡屡对里德娴熟精湛的驭马神技击节赞赏。
谢里登将军借机向大公夸赞，说我早年在落基山脉也曾是一名叱咤风云的顶尖长途驿马车驭手，大公闻言顿时兴致勃勃，强烈要求亲眼见识一下我的赶车本领。

===Original===

I was in advance at the time, and General Sheridan sang out to me:
“Cody, get in here and show the Duke how you can drive. Mr. Reed will exchange places with you and ride your horse.”
“All right, General,” said I, and in a few moments I had the reins and we were rattling away over the prairie. When we were approaching Medicine Creek, General Sheridan said: “Shake ’em up a little, Bill, and give us some old-time stage-driving.” I gave the horses a crack or two of the whip, and they started off at a very rapid gait. They had a light load to pull, and kept increasing their speed at every jump, and I found it difficult to hold them. They fairly flew over the ground, and at last we reached a steep hill, or divide, which, led down into the valley of the Medicine. There was no brake on the wagon, and the horses were not much on the hold-back. I saw that it would be impossible to stop them. All I could do was to keep them straight in the track and let them go it down the hill, for three miles; which distance, I believe, was made in about six minutes. Every once in a while the hind wheels would strike a rut and take a bound, and not touch the ground again for fifteen or twenty feet. The Duke and the General were kept rather busy in holding their positions on the seats, and when they saw that I was keeping the horses straight in the road, they seemed to enjoy the dash which we were making. I was unable to stop the team until they ran into the camp where we were to obtain a fresh relay, and there I succeeded in checking them. The Grand Duke said he didn’t want any more of that kind of driving, as he preferred to go a little slower.
On arriving at the railroad, the Duke invited me into his car, and made me some valuable presents, at the same time giving me a cordial invitation to visit him, if ever I should come to his country.
General Sheridan took occasion to remind me of an invitation to visit New York which I had received from some of the gentlemen who accompanied the General on the hunt from Fort McPherson to Hays City, in September of the previous year. Said he:
“You will never have a better opportunity to accept that invitation than now. I have had a talk with General Ord concerning you, and he will give you a leave of absence whenever you are ready to start.

===Chinese===

当时我正策马担任前驱开路，谢里登将军在后头冲我扯开大嗓门喊道：
“科迪（Cody），快上车来，让大公尊驾见识见识你当年赶马车的绝活！里德先生跟你换个位子，让他骑你的马！”
“遵命，将军！”我朗声应答。不过片刻功夫，我便接过了紧绷的缰绳，沉重马车在辽阔平原上颠簸轰鸣疾驰。当大车快逼近梅迪辛溪时，谢里登将军童心大发大喊道：“比尔，给畜生们提提神，给咱们来一段当年老落基山的亡命赶法！”我抬手挥鞭朝马屁股连甩两声清脆脆响，4匹烈马顿时如同离弦之箭般开足马力狂奔起来。车上载重本来就轻，马匹越跑越癫狂，几个纵跃速度节节飙升，连我都几乎勒不住缰绳了。骏马四蹄翻飞贴地飞行，眨眼间车队冲上了一道通往梅迪辛溪谷地的陡峭高岗分水岭。这辆大车既没有安装刹车制动摇杆，这几匹战马在俯冲时也根本不知收力减速为何物。我心知强行刹车绝无可能，眼下唯一的保命之道，便是死死握紧缰绳确保车身笔直居中咬住车辙，放任大车以狂风暴雨之势顺着陡坡狂冲而下！这段整整长达3英里的惊险大下坡，我敢断言我们全程飞驰不过耗费了6分钟！沿途车轮时不时猛烈磕碰在深坎上高高弹起，后轮腾空飞跃15到20英尺方才重重砸回地面。大公与谢里登将军在座位上被颠得东倒西歪，手忙脚乱死死抓住车把手勉强稳住重心；当他们看清我手中缰绳沉稳如山、始终将疯跑的马车精准控制在车道正中时，反倒放声大笑，尽情享受起这场近乎玩命的生死狂飙来。直到疯跑的马队一头扎进中途换马的预定营地，我才凭借过人手劲强行勒止住了这群狂暴的畜生。阿列克谢大公惊魂未定，连连摆手苦笑着说，往后这种亡命飞车他再也不敢尝试第二回了，自己还是宁愿坐得稍微安稳从容些。
抵达成铁路终点站后，大公特意邀我步入他的皇家专列车厢，向我当面馈赠了多件极其贵重的奢华礼品，并热情洋溢地向我发出亲切邀约，盼我日后若有机会远赴俄国本土，务必亲临圣彼得堡做客。
谢里登将军趁机提醒我，去年9月陪同将军完成麦克弗森堡至海斯城围猎的那批各界名流，曾正式向我发出了前往纽约做客的盛情邀请。将军语重心长地对我说：
“比尔，兑现那份邀约的最佳时机莫过于眼下了。关于你的假期我已经同奥德将军（General Ord）打过招呼，只要你收拾妥当准备动身，他随时会特批给你一段带薪长假。

===Original===

Write a letter to General Stager, of Chicago, that you are now prepared to accept the invitation, and he will send you a pass.”
Thanking the General for his kindness, I then bade him and the Grand Duke goodbye, and soon their train was out of sight.

===Chinese===

你只需给芝加哥的斯泰格将军（General Stager）修书一封，说明你已准备妥当受邀成行，他自会火速为你寄来全程免费铁路通行乘车证。”
我由衷感激谢里登将军对我体贴入微的关怀照拂，随后依依不舍同将军及阿列克谢大公握手惜别；不多时，皇家专列便喷吐着滚滚浓烟，消失在地平线的尽头。

## XXX I Return to the Plains / 第三十章 重返大平原
===Original===

We closed our theatrical season earlier than usual in the spring of 1876, because I was anxious to take part in the Sioux war which was then breaking out. Colonel Hills had written me several letters saying that General Crook was anxious to have me accompany his command, and I promised to do so, intending to overtake him in the Powder River country. But when I arrived at Chicago, on my way West, I learned that my old regiment, the gallant Fifth Cavalry, was on its way back from Arizona to join General Crook, and that my old commander, General Carr, was in command. He had written to military headquarters at Chicago to learn my whereabouts, as he wished to secure me as his guide and chief of scouts. I then gave up the idea of overtaking General Crook, and hastening on to Cheyenne, where the Fifth Cavalry had already arrived, I was met at the depot by Lieutenant King, adjutant of the regiment, he having been sent down from Fort D. A. Russell for that purpose by General Carr, who had learned by a telegram from military headquarters at Chicago that I was on the way. I accompanied the Lieutenant on horseback to the camp, and as we rode up, one of the boys shouted, “Here’s Buffalo Bill!” Soon after there came three hearty cheers from the regiment. Officers and men all were glad to see me, and I was equally delighted to meet them once more. The General at once appointed me his guide and chief of scouts.
The next morning the command pulled out for Fort Laramie, and on reaching that post we found General Sheridan there, accompanied by General Frye and General Forsyth, en route to Red Cloud Agency. As the command was to remain here a few days, I accompanied General Sheridan to Red Cloud and back, taking a company of cavalry as escort.
The Indians having recently committed a great many depredations on the Black Hills road, the Fifth Cavalry was sent out to scout the country between the Indian agencies and the hills. The command operated on the South Fork of the Cheyenne and at the foot of the Black Hills for about two weeks, having several small engagements with roving bands of Indians during the time. General Wesley Merritt﻿—who had lately received his promotion to the Colonelcy of the Fifth Cavalry﻿—now came out and took control of the regiment. I was sorry that the command was taken from General Carr, because under him it had made its fighting reputation.

===Chinese===

1876年春，我们的剧场演出季比往年提前收官，因为我急切地渴望奔赴前线，投身当时正全面爆发的苏族战争（Sioux war）。希尔斯上校（Colonel Hills）此前接连给我写了几封信，说克鲁克将军（General Crook）极盼我能随军参战，我也慨然应允，打算前去粉河一带追赶他的部队。然而在西行途中抵达芝加哥时，我得知我的老部队——英勇无畏的第五骑兵团（Fifth Cavalry）正从亚利桑那兼程赶回，准备前去与克鲁克将军会师，而执掌该团帅印的正是我的老长官卡尔将军（General Carr）。卡尔将军已向芝加哥军区司令部发函打听我的下落，希望招揽我继续出任他的向导兼首席侦察兵。于是我打消了直接去追赶克鲁克将军的念头，日夜兼程赶赴夏延（Cheyenne）。此时第五骑兵团已先期抵达那里，卡尔将军收到芝加哥司令部告知我正在路上的电报后，特派团副金中尉（Lieutenant King）从D·A·拉塞尔堡（Fort D. A. Russell）专程赶往火车站接我。我随中尉策马前往军营，刚一靠近，营里便有名士兵高喊：“野牛比尔（Buffalo Bill）来啦！”紧接着，全团官兵爆发出一阵又一阵由衷的三声欢呼。全体军官与士兵无不为重逢欢欣鼓舞，而能与老战友们再次并肩作战，我也同样喜不自胜。将军当场任命我为他的向导兼首席侦察兵。
翌日清晨，大队人马开拔奔赴拉勒米堡（Fort Laramie）。抵达到该哨所后，我们见到了谢里登将军（General Sheridan），同行的还有弗莱将军（General Frye）和福赛思将军（General Forsyth），他们正取道前往红云代办所（Red Cloud Agency）。由于大部队需在此休整驻留数日，我便率领一个骑兵连护送谢里登将军前往红云代办所，随后又平安返回。
鉴于印第安人近期在黑山（Black Hills）沿线道路上频频劫掠作乱，第五骑兵团奉命开赴印第安各代办所与黑山之间的广大区域执行巡逻侦察。部队在夏延河南汊（South Fork of the Cheyenne）及黑山脚下持续巡弋了约两周时间，其间与游荡流窜的印第安小股武装打了几次小规模遭遇战。不久，晋升为第五骑兵团上校的韦斯利·梅里特将军（General Wesley Merritt）赶赴前线，接掌了全团指挥权。指挥权从卡尔将军手中移交出去，我心中颇感惋惜，毕竟这支部队威震四方的赫赫威名，正是在卡尔将军麾下一刀一枪打出来的。

===Original===

However, upon becoming acquainted with General Merritt, I found him to be an excellent officer.
The regiment, by continued scouting, soon drove the Indians out of that section of the country, as we supposed, and we had started on our way back to Fort Laramie, when a scout arrived at the camp and reported the massacre of General Custer and his band of heroes on the Little Big Horn, on the 25th of June, 1876; and he also brought orders to General Merritt to proceed at once to Fort Fetterman and join General Crook in the Big Horn country.
Colonel Stanton, who was with the Fifth Cavalry on this scout, had been sent to Red Cloud Agency two days before, and that same evening a scout arrived bringing a message from him that eight hundred Cheyenne warriors had that day left the Red Cloud Agency to join Sitting Bull’s hostile forces in the Big Horn region. Notwithstanding the instructions to proceed immediately to join General Crook by the Way of Fort Fetterman, Colonel Merritt took the responsibility of endeavoring to intercept the Cheyennes, and as the sequel shows he performed a very important service.
He selected five hundred men and horses, and in two hours we were making a forced march back to Hat, or War-Bonnet Creek﻿—the intention being to reach the main Indian trail running to the north across that creek before the Cheyennes could get there. We arrived there the next night, and at daylight the following morning, July 17th, 1876, I went out on a scout, and found that the Indians had not yet crossed the creek. On my way back to the command I discovered a large party of Indians, which proved to be the Cheyennes, coming up from the south, and I hurried to the camp with this important information.
The cavalrymen quietly mounted their horses, and were ordered to remain out of sight, while General Merritt, accompanied by two or three aides and myself, went out on a little tour of observation to a neighboring hill, from the summit of which we saw that the Indians were approaching almost directly towards us. Presently fifteen or twenty of them dashed off to the west in the direction from which we had come the night before; and upon closer observation with our field glasses, we discovered two mounted soldiers, evidently carrying dispatches for us, pushing forward on our trail.

===Chinese===

然而在与梅里特将军熟悉相处之后，我发现他着实是一位极其出色的职业军官。
全团经过连日不懈的巡逻搜索，很快便将那一带的印第安人悉数肃清——至少我们当时是这般认为的。我们正启程踏上返回拉勒米堡的归途，一名侦察兵飞驰而至抵营报信，带来了1876年6月25日卡斯特将军（General Custer）及其麾下一众英雄在小大角河（Little Big Horn）全军覆没的惨烈噩耗；信使同时传达了给梅里特将军的指令，命他火速开拔前往费特曼堡（Fort Fetterman），赴大角山区与克鲁克将军会师。
在此次巡逻侦察中随第五骑兵团同行的斯坦顿上校（Colonel Stanton），在两天前已被派往红云代办所。就在这天傍晚，又有一名侦察兵飞马送来他发出的紧急军情：当天有800名夏延勇士（Cheyenne warriors）离开红云代办所，企图北上汇合大角地区坐牛（Sitting Bull）的反叛敌军。尽管上级严令全团立即取道费特曼堡驰援克鲁克将军，梅里特上校却毅然担起重任，当机立断决定全力截击这批夏延人；事后的战局发展充分证明，他这一决断立下了居功至伟的奇功。
他精选了500名精骑，短短两小时内全军便已拔营，顶着夜色强行军杀回帽溪（Hat Creek），即战冠溪（War-Bonnet Creek）——我们的企图是在夏延人抵达前，抢先封堵住向北横跨该溪流的印第安主要迁徙通道。我们在次日夜晚赶到了目的地。1876年7月17日拂晓，我单骑前出侦察，探明印第安人尚未渡溪。在返回营地的途中，我赫然发现一大群印第安人正自南面蜂拥逼近，后经证实这正是夏延大队人马；我火速策马回营通报这一极其关键的情报。
骑兵们悄无声息地翻鞍上马，奉命隐蔽待命；梅里特将军在两三名副官以及我的陪同下，悄悄登上一座临近的山丘进行前哨瞭望。从山顶举目望去，只见大批印第安人正几乎径直向我们的方向迎面扑来。不多时，其中约有15到20名骑手突然调头疾奔向西，朝我们前一晚行军经过的路线猛扑过去；我们举起望远镜凝神细看，只见两名骑马的士兵正沿着我们的马蹄足迹疾驰前行，显而易见是奉命为我们送达紧急军情的通信兵。

===Original===

The Indians were evidently endeavoring to intercept these two men, and General Merritt feared that they would accomplish their object. He did not think it advisable to send out any soldiers to the assistance of the couriers, for fear that would show to the Indians that there were troops in the vicinity who were waiting for them. I finally suggested that the best plan was to wait until the couriers came closer to the command, and then, just as the Indians were about to charge, to let me take the scouts and cut them off from the main body of the Cheyennes, who were coming over the divide.
“All right, Cody,” said the General, “if you can do that, go ahead.”
I rushed back to the command, jumped on my horse, picked out fifteen men, and returned with them to the point of observation. I told General Merritt to give us the word to start out at the proper time, and presently he sang out:
“Go in now, Cody, and be quick about it. They are going to charge on the couriers.”
The two messengers were not over four hundred yards from us, and the Indians were only about two hundred yards behind them. We instantly dashed over the bluffs, and advanced on a gallop towards the Indians. A running fight lasted several minutes, during which we drove the enemy some little distance and killed three of their number. The rest of them rode off towards the main body, which had come into plain sight, and halted, upon seeing the skirmish that was going on. We were about half a mile from General Merritt, and the Indians whom we were chasing suddenly turned upon us, and another lively skirmish took place. One of the Indians, who was handsomely decorated with all the ornaments usually worn by a war chief when engaged in a fight, sang out to me, in his own tongue:
“I know you, Pa-he-haska; if you want to fight, come ahead and fight me.”
The chief was riding his horse back and forth in front of his men, as if to banter me, and I concluded to accept the challenge. I galloped towards him for fifty yards and he advanced towards me about the same distance, both of us riding at full speed, and then, when we were only about thirty yards apart, I raised my rifle and fired; his horse fell to the ground, having been killed by my bullet.
Almost at the same instant my own horse went down, he having stepped into a hole. The fall did not hurt me much, and I instantly sprang to my feet.

===Chinese===

那些印第安人显然企图半路拦截这两名通信兵，梅里特将军深恐他们得逞。然而将军认为派出士兵出营援救并非良策，生怕此举会打草惊蛇，让印第安人察觉附近有大队主力潜伏，一时间左右权衡，委决不下。说话间，印第安人已经逼近到距离两名信使不足300码之处，距离我们也仅有200码之遥；他们猫腰潜入了一条干涸狭窄的小溪河床，伺机守株待兔，只等信使跨过溪流，便一拥而上将其生擒活剥。
“将军，”我请命道，“您只要拨给我15名精兵，由我带队，我保证把他们带到敌人看不见的隐蔽阵位；一旦印第安人准备向信使发起冲锋，我们就突然杀出，切断他们的退路！”
“好，科迪，”将军当机立断，“若是你有把握，立刻行动！”
我如旋风般奔回部队，纵身跃上马背，火速挑出15名精悍战士，带着他们悄然折返到那处瞭望哨位。我严令所有人保持绝对肃静，全员屏息凝神静候冲锋命令。
“出击，科迪，动作要快！他们就要向信使动手了！”
此时那两名信使距离我们已不过400码，而印第安人尾随在他们身后仅约200码。我向部下一声断喝下达冲锋令，大伙儿策马凌空跃入河床沟壑，连连开火射击。印第安人原本以为四周除那两名落单信使外别无敌情，冷不丁遭到这记当头棒喝，顿时被打得魂飞魄散，仓皇调头四散奔逃；但在他们逃出有效射程之前，我们当场干净利落地击毙了3人。
顺着小溪下游行进的其余印第安主力闻声赶到现场，随即向我们倾泻下密集凶猛的弹雨。面对数倍于己的敌群重重包围，我们拼尽全力死守阵地，麾下战士们个个浴血奋战，表现得无比英勇剽悍。一名相貌英俊俊朗的年轻印第安人，头戴象征部落酋长威仪的斑斓羽冠（war-bonnet），手握一柄温彻斯特连发步枪（Winchester rifle），率领几名勇士径直朝我们迅猛冲杀过来，其余印第安人则在远处游弋策应。当这位年轻酋长冲至近前辨认出我时，竟操着颇为流利的英语冲我大喊：
“我认得你，帕赫斯卡（Pa-he-haska，意为‘长发者’）！你要是有胆量决斗，就放马过来同我一战！”
酋长在部众阵前策马疾驰来回逡巡，神态张狂尽显挑衅戏谑，我当即横下一条心接下了这场生死挑战。我大步跨出队伍，朝他迎面怒吼道：
“成全你，红皮魔鬼！放马过来，咱们俩一对一单挑定生死！”
我们双双拍马对冲而上，当逼近到相距仅约30码时，几乎同时端枪怒射。他的子弹呼啸着擦过，击穿了我外套的翻领；而我的枪弹则不偏不倚，将他胯下的坐骑当场击毙。
几乎就在同一瞬间，我自己的坐骑也因前蹄踏入洞穴轰然栽倒在地。这一跤并未让我受什么重伤，我敏捷如豹地一跃而起，双脚稳稳站定在地上。

===Original===

The Indian had also recovered himself, and we were now both on foot, and not more than twenty paces apart. We fired at each other simultaneously. My usual luck did not desert me on this occasion, for his bullet missed me, while mine struck him in the breast. He reeled and fell, but before he had fairly touched the ground I was upon him, knife in hand, and had driven the keen-edged weapon to its hilt in his heart. Jerking his war-bonnet off, I scientifically scalped him in about five seconds.
The whole affair from beginning to end occupied but little time, and the Indians, seeing that I was some little distance from my company, now came charging down upon me from a hill, in hopes of cutting me off. General Merritt had witnessed the duel, and realizing the danger I was in, ordered Colonel Mason with Company K to hurry to my rescue. The order came none too soon, for had it been given one minute later I would have had not less than two hundred Indians upon me. As the soldiers came up I swung the Indian chieftain’s topknot and bonnet in the air, and shouted: “*The first scalp for Custer.*”
General Merritt, seeing that he could not now ambush the Indians, ordered the whole regiment to charge upon them. They made a stubborn resistance for a little while, but it was of no use for any eight hundred, or even sixteen hundred Indians to try and check a charge of the gallant old Fifth Cavalry, and they soon came to that conclusion and began a running retreat towards Red Cloud Agency. For thirty-five miles we drove them; pushing them so hard that they were obliged to abandon their loose horses, their camp equipage and everything else. We drove them into the agency, and followed in ourselves, notwithstanding the possibility of our having to encounter the thousands of Indians at that point. We were uncertain whether or not the other agency Indians had determined to follow the example of the Cheyennes and strike out upon the warpath; but that made no difference with the Fifth Cavalry, for they would have fought them all if necessary. It was dark when we rode into the agency, where we found thousands of Indians collected together; but they manifested no disposition to fight.
While at the agency I learned the name of the Indian Chief whom I had killed in the morning; it was Yellow Hand; a son of old Cut-nose﻿—a leading chief of the Cheyennes.

===Chinese===

那印第安人也已翻身跃起，此时我们双双徒步对峙，彼此相距不过20步之遥。我们几乎在同一弹指间同时扣动扳机。上苍赐予我的一贯好运在这一生死攸关的当口再度眷顾了我：他的子弹射偏掠过，而我的子弹却结结实实洞穿了他的胸膛。他身形摇晃轰然倾倒，然而还没等他完全瘫倒在尘土中，我已飞身扑上前去，拔出雪亮的利刃，将冰冷的锋刃齐根没入了他的心脏。我猛力扯下他的战冠羽饰，手起刀落，仅仅用了大约5秒钟，便纯熟精准地剥下了他的头皮。
这场决斗从头至尾不过瞬息之间，四周的印第安人眼见我孤身一人落单在队伍前沿，纷纷从山丘上狂呼策马向下冲杀，企图将我合围绞杀。梅里特将军在远方亲眼目睹了这场决斗，察觉到我身陷重围命悬一线，当即命令梅森上校（Colonel Mason）率领K连火速驰援接应。这道命令下达得千钧一发，哪怕只要再耽搁哪怕一分钟，我必将被不少于200名印第安人彻底吞没。当大队骑兵如狂潮般策马赶到近前时，我高高扬起那名印第安酋长的发辫与战冠，在风中振臂高呼：“*为卡斯特报仇的第一张头皮！（The first scalp for Custer.）*”
梅里特将军眼见伏击奇袭的战机已过，索性下令全团发动排山倒海般的大冲锋。印第安人起初还负隅顽抗了一阵子，但莫说是800人，纵使有1600名印第安狂徒，也绝无可能阻挡声名赫赫的功勋第五骑兵团的铁蹄洪流；他们很快便痛苦地意识到这一点，开始沿着通往红云代办所的方向一路溃退溃逃。我们乘胜追击，策马足足追杀驱赶了35英里；攻势之凌厉迅猛，迫使他们沿途狼狈丢弃了未套挽具的备用马匹、宿营辎重以及随身携带的一切家当。我们一路将他们狠狠驱赶进代办所辖区，并紧随其后长驱直入，哪怕明知在该据点可能会撞上数以千计的当地印第安人，也毫不畏缩。我们当时尚无法断定代办所辖区内的其余印第安部族是否已决意效仿夏延人走上全面反叛的战争之路；但这对于悍勇绝伦的第五骑兵团来说毫无二致——若有必要，哪怕与全境所有敌寇决一死战，全团官兵也万死不辞。夜幕低垂之际，我们策马驰入代办所，只见成千上万的印第安人黑压压地聚集在一起；然而面对我们的森严铁骑，他们已全然丧失了任何抵抗应战的意志。
在代办所驻留期间，我打听到了上午被我亲手击毙的那位印第安酋长的大名：他名叫黄手（Yellow Hand），是夏延族大酋长——老断鼻（old Cut-nose）的亲生儿子。

===Original===

Cut-nose, having learned that I had killed his son sent a white interpreter to me with a message to the effect that he would give me four mules if I would turn over to him Yellow Hand’s war-bonnet, guns, pistols, ornaments, and other paraphernalia which I had captured. I sent back word to the old gentleman that it would give me pleasure to accommodate him, but I could not do it this time.
The next morning we started to join General Crook, who was camped near the foot of Cloud Peak in the Big Horn Mountains; awaiting the arrival of the Fifth Cavalry, before proceeding against the Sioux, who were somewhere near the head of the Little Big Horn﻿—as his scouts informed him. We made rapid marches and reached General Crook’s camp on Goose Creek about the 3rd of August.
At this camp I met many old friends, among whom was Colonel Royal, who had received his promotion to the Lieutenant Colonelcy of the Third Cavalry. He introduced me to General Crook, whom I had never met before, but of whom I had often heard. He also introduced me to the General’s chief guide, Frank Grouard, a half breed, who had lived six years with Sitting Bull, and knew the country thoroughly.
We remained in this camp only one day, and then the whole troop pulled out for the Tongue River, leaving our wagons behind, but taking with us a large pack train. We marched down the Tongue River for two days, thence in a westerly direction over to the Rosebud, where we struck the main Indian trail, leading down this stream. From the size of the trail, which appeared to be about four days old, we estimated that there must have been in the neighborhood of seven thousand Indians who had made the broad trail.
At this point we were overtaken by Jack Crawford, familiarly known as “Captain Jack, the Poet Scout of the Black Hills,” and right here I will insert the following lines, written by him, just after the “Custer Massacre,” upon receiving from me the following dispatch:
“Jack, old boy, have you heard of the death of Custer?”

> Did I hear the news from Custer?

Well, I reckon I did, old pard;

It came like a streak of lightnin’,

And, you bet, it hit me hard.

I ain’t no hand to blubber,

And the briny ain’t run for years;

But chalk me down for a lubber,

If I didn’t shed regular tears.
> What for? Now look you here, Bill,

You’re a bully boy, that’s true;

As good as e’er wore buckskin,

===Chinese===

老断鼻得知我杀死了他的儿子，派了一名白人翻译官来见我，转达意图说：若我肯将缴获的黄手战冠羽饰、枪支、手枪、佩饰以及其他一应战利品归还于他，他愿赠送我4头骡子。我托人带话给这位老先生：能为他效劳我固然深感欣慰，但这一回实在恕难从命。
翌日清晨，我们启程奔赴大角山脉（Big Horn Mountains）云峰（Cloud Peak）脚下，与驻扎在彼处的克鲁克将军会合；据前沿侦察兵通报，苏族人正集结在小大角河源头附近的某处，克鲁克将军正在等待第五骑兵团的增援，然后再大举进军征剿。我们兼程疾进，大约于8月3日抵达了克鲁克将军驻扎在鹅溪（Goose Creek）的营地。
在这座营地里，我遇到了许多旧雨故知，其中就有刚荣升第三骑兵团中校的罗亚尔上校（Colonel Royal）。他将我引荐给了克鲁克将军；我对将军的大名早有耳闻，却一直缘悭一面。他还向我介绍了将军的首席向导弗兰克·格鲁阿尔（Frank Grouard）——这是一名印白混血儿，曾与坐牛共同生活过6年之久，对当地的山川地理了如指掌。
我们在这处营地仅驻留了1天，全军便拔营奔赴唐河（Tongue River）；我们留下了所有辎重马车，仅随行携带了一支庞大的骡马驮队。我们沿唐河顺流急进2天，随后向西折向玫瑰花蕾溪（Rosebud），在此截获了印第安人沿河而下踏出的一条主要大迁徙通道。从这条大约残留了4天的踪迹规模来推断，我们估计踩出这条开阔大道的印第安人足有7000人之众。
正是在此地，人称“黑山诗人侦察兵杰克船长”的杰克·克劳福德（Jack Crawford）追赶上了我们的部队。恰逢此时，在收到我发给他的那则电报——“杰克，老兄，你听闻卡斯特阵亡的消息了吗？”之后，他当即挥笔写下了下面这些诗行，在此特意将其收录录入：
“杰克，老兄，你听闻卡斯特阵亡的消息了吗？”

> 问我是否听闻卡斯特的噩耗？
>
> 唉，老搭档，我怎能不知晓；
>
> 它犹如一道晴天霹雳横空劈下，
>
> 相信我，将我的心狠狠击倒。
>
> 我向来不是个多愁善感、号啕啜泣之辈，
>
> 泪水早已多年未曾流淌打湿眼眶；
>
> 但若说当时我未曾泪如雨下，
>
> 那大可把我骂作没心没肺的蠢汉荒唐。
>
> 问我为何落泪？比尔，且听我细说端详，
>
> 你是个顶天立地的硬汉，半点不假；
>
> 凡身披鹿皮战袍的边疆男儿，无出你右，

===Original===

Or fought with the boys in blue;

But I’ll bet my bottom dollar

Ye had no trouble to muster

A tear, or perhaps a hundred,

At the news of the death of Custer.
> He always thought well of you, pard,

And had it been heaven’s will,

In a few more days you’d met him,

And he’d welcome his old scout Bill.

For if ye remember at Hat Creek,

I met ye with General Carr;

We talked of the brave young Custer,

And recounted his deeds of war.
> But little we knew even then, pard,

(And that’s just two weeks ago),

How little we dreamed of disaster,

Or that he had met the foe﻿—

That the fearless, reckless hero,

So loved by the whole frontier,

Had died on the field of battle

In this, our centennial year.
> I served with him in the army,

In the darkest days of the war:

And I reckon ye know his record,

For he was our guiding star;

And the boys who gathered round him

To charge in the early morn,

War just like the brave who perished

With him on the Little Horn.
> And where is the satisfaction,

And how will the boys get square?

By giving the reds more rifles?

Invite them to take more hair?

We want no scouts, no trappers,

Nor men who know the frontier;

Phil, old boy, you’re mistaken,

*We must have the volunteer.*
> Never mind that two hundred thousand

But give us a hundred instead;

Send five thousand men towards Reno,

And soon we won’t leave a red.

It will save Uncle Sam lots of money,

In fortress we need not invest,

Jest wollup the devils this summer,

And the miners will do all the rest.
> The Black Hills are filled with miners,

The Big Horn will soon be as full,

And which will show the most danger

To Crazy Horse and old Sitting Bull

A band of ten thousand frontier men,

Or a couple of forts with a few

Of the boys in the East now enlisting﻿—

Friend Cody, I leave it with you.
> They talk of peace with these demons

By feeding and clothing them well:

I’d as soon think an angel from Heaven

Would reign with contentment in H﻿—l

And one day the Quakers will answer

Before the great Judge of us all,

For the death of daring young Custer

And the boys who round him did fall.
> Perhaps I am judging them harshly,

But I mean what I’m telling ye, pard;

I’m letting them down mighty easy,

Perhaps they may think it is hard.

But I tell you the day is approaching﻿—

The boys are beginning to muster﻿—

That day of the great retribution,

===Original===

The day of revenge for our Custer.
> And I will be with you, friend Cody,

My weight will go in with the boys;

I shared all their hardships last winter,

I shared all their sorrows and joys;

Tell them I’m coming, friend William,

I trust I will meet you ere long;

Regards to the boys in the mountains;

Yours, ever; in friendship still strong.

Jack was a new man in the country, but evidently had plenty of nerve and pluck, as he had brought dispatches from Fort Fetterman, a distance of 300 miles through a dangerous Indian country. The dispatches were for General Crook, and notified him that General Terry was to operate with a large command south of the Yellowstone, and that the two commands would probably consolidate somewhere on the Rosebud.
Jack at once hunted me up and gave me a letter from General Sheridan, informing me that he had appointed him (Jack) as one of the scouts.
While we were conversing, Jack informed me that he had brought me a present from Colonel Jones of Cheyenne, and that he had it in his saddle-pockets. Asking the nature of the gift, he replied that it was only a bottle of good whiskey.
I placed my hand over his mouth and told him to keep still, and not to whisper it even to the winds, for there were too many dry men around us; and only when alone with him did I dare to have him take the treasure from his saddle-pockets.
In this connection I may remark that Jack Crawford is the only man I have ever known that could have brought that bottle of whiskey through without *accident* befalling it, for he is one of the very few teetotal scouts I ever met.
Not wishing to have a game of “whiskey solitaire,” I invited General Carr to sample the bottle with me. We soon found a secluded spot, and dismounting, we thought we were going to have a nice little drink all by ourselves, when who should ride up but Mr. Lathrop, the Reporter of the Associated Press of the Pacific slope﻿—to whom we had given the name of the “Death Rattler,”﻿—and who was also known in San Francisco as “the man with the iron jaw,” he having, with the true nose of a Reporter, smelt the whiskey from afar off, and had come to “interview” it. He was a good fellow withal, and we were glad to have him join us.
Now to resume: For two or three days we pushed on, but we did not seem to gain much on the Indians, as they were evidently making about the same marches that we were.

===Chinese===

为我们的卡斯特复仇雪耻的壮烈之日！
> 朋友科迪，我定会与你并肩战在一处，
>
> 我将倾尽全力与兄弟们共赴生死；
>
> 去年严冬我曾与他们同甘共苦，
>
> 饱尝了他们所有的欢欣与苦楚；
>
> 朋友威廉，替我转告兄弟们我已在路上，
>
> 深信不日之后你我自会并肩相聚；
>
> 且替我向群山间奋战的弟兄们致以问候；
>
> 友谊坚逾金石，此致永远。

杰克初来乍到，在这片地界算是个新人，但他显然骨头极硬、胆识过人，因为他刚刚只身横穿危机四伏的印第安险境，从费特曼堡（Fort Fetterman）星夜兼程送达了紧急军情公文，全程足足有300英里之遥。这批急件是呈交克鲁克将军的，通报说特里将军（General Terry）即将率领一支庞大重兵在黄石河南岸展开作战行动，两路大军预计将在玫瑰花蕾溪一带的某处胜利会师。
杰克一进营地便立刻寻到了我，递给我一封谢里登将军亲笔签署的信件，告知将军已正式委任杰克为侦察兵之一。
攀谈之间，杰克悄声告诉我，他特意从夏延的琼斯上校（Colonel Jones）那里给我捎来了一份心意厚礼，此时正严严实实揣在他马鞍两旁的搭谢里。当我追问究竟是何等宝贝时，他咧嘴一笑，悄悄说那不过是一瓶陈年绝品好威士忌。
我赶紧眼疾手快伸手捂住他的嘴，严厉告诫他千万噤声，连半点风声都绝不可向旷野的风透露半分，因为四周到处都是喉咙冒烟、嗜酒如命的饥渴汉子；直到我同他避开众人独处时，我才敢容许他从马鞍搭谢里小心翼翼取出了这份无价珍宝。
说到这里，我不得不由衷感慨一句：杰克·克劳福德是我生平所结识的人当中，唯一一个能在穿越数百里荒原后，还能让这样一瓶琼浆玉液安然无恙、中途未出任何“意外”的人，因为他是我平生罕见碰到的极少数滴酒不沾的绝对戒酒主义侦察兵之一。
我自然不愿落个自斟自饮玩“威士忌接龙（whiskey solitaire）”的吃独食名声，便特意邀请卡尔将军同我一道细细品鉴这瓶美酒。我们很快寻到了一处避人耳目的僻静角落，翻鞍下马，满以为这下终于可以安享属于我们两人的微醺时光，冷不防一阵马蹄声疾驰而至——策马冲上前的不是别人，正是太平洋沿岸美联社的随军特派记者莱斯罗普先生（Mr. Lathrop）！我们平日里都戏称他为“索命符（Death Rattler）”，而在旧金山他更是以“铁颌怪人（the man with the iron jaw）”的大名威震四方。此君凭借着职业记者那条神犬般灵敏的鼻子，隔着老远就闻出了烈酒的醇香，大摇大摆跑来要对美酒进行一番“专访独家报道”。不过话说回来，他倒是个为人极仗义的好哥们儿，我们便欣然邀他入座同饮。
言归正传：随后两三天里，我们全速挥师急进，但似乎并未同前方的印第安人拉近多少距离，因为敌人显然每天也在以同等的速度日夜兼程行军。

===Original===

On the fourth or fifth morning of our pursuit, I rode ahead of the command about ten miles, and mounting a hill I scanned the country far and wide with my field glass, and discovered an immense column of dust rising about ten miles further down the creek, and soon I noticed a body of men marching towards me, that at first I believed to be the Indians of whom we were in pursuit; but subsequently they proved to be General Terry’s command. I sent back word to that effect to General Crook, by a scout who had accompanied me, but after he had departed I observed a band of Indians on the opposite side of the creek, and also another party directly in front of me. This led me to believe that I had made a mistake.
But shortly afterwards my attention was attracted by the appearance of a body of soldiers, who were forming into a skirmish line, and then I became convinced that it was General Terry’s command after all, and that the redskins whom I had seen were some of his friendly Indian scouts, who had mistaken me for a Sioux, and fled back to their command terribly excited, shouting, “The Sioux are coming!”
General Terry at once came to the post, and ordered the Seventh Cavalry to form line of battle across the Rosebud; he also ordered up his artillery and had them prepare for action, doubtless dreading another “Custer massacre.” I afterwards learned the Indians had seen the dust raised by General Crook’s forces, and had reported that the Sioux were coming.
These manoeuvres I witnessed from my position with considerable amusement, thinking the command must be badly demoralized, when one man could cause a whole army to form line of battle and prepare for action. Having enjoyed the situation to my heart’s content, I galloped down towards the skirmish line, waving my hat and when within about one hundred yards of the troops, Colonel Weir, of the Seventh Cavalry, galloped out and met me. He recognized me at once, and accompanied me inside the line; then he sang out, “Boys, here’s Buffalo Bill. Some of you old soldiers know him; give him a cheer!” Thereupon the regiment gave three rousing cheers, and it was followed up all along the line.
Colonel Weir presented me to General Terry, and in answer to his questions I informed him that the alarm of Indians which had been given was a false one, as the dust seen by his scouts was caused by General Crook’s troops.

===Chinese===

追击进行到第四或第五天清晨，我单骑突前在全军前方约十英里处侦察；登上一处高耸的山岗后，我举起望远镜对四面八方的原野仔细巡视，赫然发现顺着小溪下游约十英里处升腾起遮天蔽日的滚滚尘柱。不多时，我便辨认出一队行军队列正朝我迎面开来，起初我还以为这就是我们苦苦追击的那伙印第安大军；但随后查明，那其实是特里将军统领的大军。我立刻通过身旁随行的一名斥候给后方的克鲁克将军带口信通报情况；然而就在他离去之后，我猛然瞥见小溪对岸晃动着一队印第安人，正前方也有一小股印第安武装若隐若现。这一景象让我不由得心生疑窦，暗忖自己方才恐怕是判断失误。
然而片刻之后，我的注意力被一支正在徐徐展开为散兵线的正规军部队所吸引，我这才彻底笃定那千真万确就是特里将军的部队，而我先前瞥见的那群印第安红种人，其实是他麾下的友方印第安侦察兵；这帮斥候在远处将我误认作了凶悍的苏族敌骑，吓得惊慌失措屁滚尿流一路奔逃回大营，声嘶力竭狂喊：“苏族人杀过来啦！”
特里将军闻讯立刻亲临前沿阵地，急令第七骑兵团横跨玫瑰花蕾溪摆开严整阵型展开决战架势；他还调集了麾下的全部炮兵，严令架炮备战，显而易见是深恐再次重演“卡斯特大屠杀”的灭顶惨剧。事后我才弄明白，那批印第安向导其实是远远看见了克鲁克将军大军行军扬起的遮天尘土，便想当然地惊呼是苏族大军汹涌扑来。
我高高伫立在山头，怀着极大的兴味打量着眼前这出令人忍俊不禁的调兵遣将：区区单枪匹马的一介凡人，竟能让一整支装备精良的正规大军全军肃立、严阵以待摆开阵地决战，这支部队的士气必定已被惊吓到了草木皆兵的地步。待我居高临下欣赏够了这出好戏之后，我挥舞着帽子策马飞驰疾奔下山，直扑他们的散兵线。当冲至距大军约一百码处时，第七骑兵团的韦尔上校（Colonel Weir）飞马出阵前来迎候。他一眼便认出了我，陪同我穿过严密的警戒防线；随后他扯开嗓门冲全军放声大呼：“弟兄们，这位就是野牛比尔！咱们队伍里的老兵没有不认识他的，大伙儿给他欢呼喝彩！”话音未落，全团官兵爆发出响彻云霄的三声雷鸣欢呼，欢呼声随即如巨浪般沿着整条战线此起彼伏经久不息。
韦尔上校将我郑重引荐给特里将军。在回答将军的焦急询问时，我向他明确报告说，此前所谓遭遇印第安大军的警报纯属虚惊一场，他的斥候所看见的滚滚烟尘，其实是克鲁克将军麾下的正规大军在开进。

===Original===

General Terry thereupon rode forward to meet General Crook, and I accompanied him at his request. That night both commands went into camp on the Rosebud. General Terry had his wagon train with him, and everything to make life comfortable on an Indian campaign. He had large wall tents and portable beds to sleep in, and large hospital tents for dining-rooms. His camp looked very comfortable and attractive, and presented a great contrast to that of General Crook, who had for his headquarters only one small fly tent; and whose cooking utensils consisted of a quart cup﻿—in which he made his coffee himself﻿—and a stick, upon which he broiled his bacon. When I compared the two camps, I came to the conclusion that General Crook was an Indian fighter; for it was evident that he had learned that, to follow and fight Indians, a body of men must travel lightly and not be detained by a wagon train or heavy luggage of any kind.
That evening General Terry ordered General Miles to take his regiment, the Fifth Infantry, and return by a forced march to the Yellowstone, and proceed down that river by steamboat to the mouth of Powder River, to intercept the Indians, in case they attempted to cross the Yellowstone. General Mills made a forced march that night of thirty-five miles, which was splendid traveling for an infantry regiment through a mountainous country.
Generals Crook and Terry spent that evening and the next day in council, and on the following morning both commands moved out on the Indian trail. Although General Terry was the senior officer, he did not assume command of both expeditions, but left General Crook in command of his own troops, although they operated together. We crossed the Tongue River to Powder River, and proceeded down the latter stream to a point twenty miles from its junction with the Yellowstone, where the Indian trail turned to the southeast in the direction of the Black Hills. The two commands now being nearly out of supplies, the trail was abandoned, and the troops kept on down Powder River to its confluence with the Yellowstone, and remained there several days. Here we met General Mills, who reported that no Indians had as yet crossed the Yellowstone. Several steamboats soon arrived with a large quantity of supplies, and once more the “Boys in Blue” were made happy.

===Chinese===

特里将军随即策马向前与克鲁克将军会晤，并应其邀请由我随行陪同。当晚，两路大军在玫瑰花蕾溪畔各自安营扎寨。特里将军随军带着庞大的辎重车队，凡征讨印第安人战役中能让行军生活舒坦惬意的一应用度，无不一应俱全。他配备了宽敞高大的直壁帐篷和可折叠的行军便携床铺，甚至用巨大的野战医院帐篷作为官兵就餐的餐厅。他的营地看起来舒适优雅、赏心悦目，与克鲁克将军的简陋营地形成了极其强烈的鲜明反差：克鲁克将军的司令部仅仅是一顶狭小的遮阳单棚篷；他的全部炊具不过是一个一品脱装的小铁杯——他总是在里面亲手烹煮自己的咖啡——外加一根用来插着培根在篝火上炙烤的树枝。每当我把这两座营地暗自作比时，我便深切认定克鲁克将军才是真正千锤百炼的征剿印第安人行家里手；显而易见，他早已用血与火的经验领悟到：若想在这片荒原上追猎并彻底击溃印第安人，一支部队必须轻装简从、迅捷如风，绝不可被沉重的辎重车队或任何繁重行李所拖累羁绊。
当天傍晚，特里将军命令迈尔斯将军（General Miles，原文亦作 Mills）率领他的第五步兵团（Fifth Infantry）以强行军杀回黄石河，随后搭乘蒸汽轮船顺流而下直奔粉河口，以切断并拦截可能企图横渡黄石河逃遁的印第安人。迈尔斯将军在当夜创下了强行军35英里的骄人战绩，对于一支跋涉在崇山峻岭崎岖地形中的步兵团而言，这样的推进速度堪称奇迹神速。
克鲁克将军与特里将军在当晚及次日闭门举行了多次军事会议；到了第三天清晨，两路大军再度沿着印第安人留下的踪迹大举开拔追击。特里将军资历与军衔虽高，却并未统揽两军的总指挥大权，而是让克鲁克将军继续独立统辖自己的本部人马，双方保持协同作战。我们跨过唐河挺进粉河，并沿着粉河顺流而下，推进至距离其与黄石河交汇处约20英里的地界；在此处，印第安人的足迹突然折向东南，朝黑山方向延伸而去。此时两支大军的粮草给养几乎消耗殆尽，不得不放弃顺藤摸瓜的追踪，大部队继续沿粉河下行直至与黄石河的交汇处，并在彼处驻扎休整了数日。在这里我们见到了迈尔斯将军，他汇报称迄今尚无任何印第安人能够渡过黄石河。不多时，几艘满载大批补给物资的蒸汽轮船相继靠岸，饱受艰辛的“蓝衫军健儿们（Boys in Blue）”终于再次欢欣鼓舞、喜笑颜开。

## V In Business / 第五章 初入商海
===Original===

In the summer of 1857, Russell, Majors & Waddell were sending a great many trains across the plains to Salt Lake with supplies for General Johnston’s army. Men were in great demand, and the company was paying teamsters forty dollars per month in gold. An old and reliable wagon master, named Lewis Simpson﻿—who had taken a great fancy to me, and who, by the way, was one of the best wagon-masters that ever ran a bull train﻿—was loading a train for the company, and was about to start out with it for Salt Lake. He asked me to go along as an “extra hand.” The high wages that were being paid were a great inducement to me, and the position of an “extra hand” was a pleasant one. All that I would have to do would be to take the place of any man who became sick, and drive his wagon until he recovered. I would have my own mule to ride, and to a certain extent I would be a minor boss.
My mother was very much opposed to my taking this long trip, as I would be absent nearly a year, and there was a possibility that something might arise to prevent me from ever coming back, as we could not often tell how the Mormon difficulty would terminate. Then again, owing to the Indians, a journey over the plains in those days was a perilous undertaking. She said that as I had recently returned from the plains, and had had a narrow escape from death at the hands of the Indians, she did not want me to risk my life a second time. I told her that inasmuch as I had determined to follow the plains for an occupation, nothing could now stop me from going on this trip, and if it became necessary I would run away.
Seeing that it was impossible to keep me at home, she reluctantly gave her consent, but not until she had called upon Mr. Russell and Mr. Simpson in regard to the matter, and had obtained from the latter gentleman his promise that I should be well taken care of, if we had to winter in the mountains. She did not like the appearance of Simpson, and upon inquiry she learned, to her dismay, that he was a desperate character, and that on nearly every trip he had made across the plains he had killed someone. Such a man, she thought, was not a fit master or companion for her son, and she was very anxious to have me go with some other wagon master; but I still insisted upon remaining with Simpson.

===Chinese===

1857年夏，罗素、梅杰斯与沃德尔公司（Russell, Majors & Waddell）正向盐湖城（Salt Lake）派出大批车队，横穿大平原为约翰斯顿将军（General Johnston）的西征大军运送补给物资。当时车队极度紧缺人手，公司开出的待遇十分诱人，付给赶车驭手的月薪高达40美元黄金。一位名叫刘易斯·辛普森（Lewis Simpson）的资深车队队长对我青睐有加——顺便说一句，他堪称大平原上统领牛车队最顶尖的车长之一——当时他正为公司装载车队，准备启程开赴盐湖城。他力邀我以“机动帮手（extra hand）”的身份随队同行。丰厚的高额薪酬对我而言有着莫大的吸引力，而且机动帮手这个职位也颇为惬意体面：我平时无需日日挥鞭赶车，只需在有人生病告假时代其顶班，替他驾车直至康复即可；我还能配有一头属于自己的坐骑骡子，在某种程度上也算是个不大不小的小工头了。
然而，母亲却极力反对我踏上这趟遥远的远征。毕竟我一走便将近一年，加之谁也料不准那场摩门教危机（Mormon difficulty）究竟会以何种惨烈局势收场，稍有不测恐有去无回；加之沿途印第安人出没无常，在那年月横穿大平原着实是一项九死一生的凶险行当。母亲直言，我前不久才刚从大平原历险归来，在印第安人的刀口下侥幸死里逃生，她实在不愿看我再去冒第二次生命危险。可我对她说，既然我已经铁了心把纵横大平原当作毕生的志向事业，如今就没有任何力量能阻拦我成行；若是家里执意不允，我哪怕离家出走也决不回头。
母亲深知无论如何也留不住我这颗渴望远行的野心，只得勉强点头应允；但在此之前，她特意专程拜访了罗素先生与辛普森先生商榷此事，并从辛普森先生那里得到了郑重承诺：倘若车队不得不滞留在茫茫深山中过冬，他定会将我照料得周全妥当。母亲初见辛普森时便对他的相貌举止心存芥蒂；私下一打听，更令她惊骇万分的是，此人竟是个杀人不眨眼的亡命之徒，据说他几乎每一次带队横穿大平原，手里都会沾染上人命血案。母亲认定这样凶悍的人物绝非自己年幼儿子的合适管教者或同行者，极力催促我换一位温和的车队长同行；但我却横下一条心，认准了非要跟定辛普森不可。

===Original===

“Madam, I can assure you that Lew Simpson is one of the most reliable wagon-masters on the plains,” said Mr. Russell, “and he has taken a great fancy to Billy. If your boy is bound to go, he can go with no better man. No one will dare to impose on him while he is with Lew Simpson, whom I will instruct to take good care of the boy. Upon reaching Fort Laramie, Billy can, if he wishes, exchange places with some fresh man coming back on a returning train, and thus come home without making the whole trip.”
This seemed to satisfy mother, and then she had a long talk with Simpson himself, imploring him not to forget his promise to take good care of her precious boy. He promised everything that she asked. Thus, after much trouble, I became one of the members of Simpson’s train. Before taking our departure, I arranged with Russell, Majors & Waddell that when my pay should fall due it should be paid over to mother.
As a matter of interest to the general reader, it may be well in this connection to give a brief description of a freight train. The wagons used in those days by Russell, Majors & Waddell were known as the “J. Murphy wagons,” made at St. Louis specially for the plains business. They were very large and were strongly built, being capable of carrying seven thousand pounds of freight each. The wagon-boxes were very commodious﻿—being as large as the rooms of an ordinary house﻿—and were covered with two heavy canvas sheets to protect the merchandise from the rain. These wagons were generally sent out from Leavenworth, each loaded with six thousand pounds of freight, and each drawn by several yokes of oxen in charge of one driver. A train consisted of twenty-five wagons, all in charge of one man, who was known as the wagon master. The second man in command was the assistant wagon master; then came the “extra hand,” next the night herder; and lastly, the cavallard driver, whose duty it was to drive the lame and loose cattle. There were thirty-one men all told in a train. The men did their own cooking, being divided into messes of seven. One man cooked, another brought wood and water, another stood guard, and so on, each having some duty to perform while getting meals. All were heavily armed with Colt’s pistols and Mississippi yagers, and everyone always had his weapons handy so as to be prepared for any emergency.

===Chinese===

“夫人，我可以向您担保，卢·辛普森（Lew Simpson）是大平原上最靠得住的车队长之一，”罗素先生宽慰母亲道，“而且他打心眼里喜欢小比利。如果您的孩子执意要闯荡一番，跟着他绝对是最好的选择。只要有卢·辛普森罩着，这片平原上就没人敢动他一根汗毛；我也自会严令他全力照顾好孩子。更何况等车队抵达拉勒米堡后，若是比利想家了，他完全可以同返程车队里那些换防的新手对调位置，这样便无需走完全程，便能平平安安折返回家。”
这番话总算让母亲宽了心。随后她又拉着辛普森长谈了一番，苦苦恳求他切莫忘记照料她心肝宝贝的诺言。对于母亲的每一句托付，辛普森无不一口应承下来。就这样，在一波三折之后，我终于正式成为了辛普森车队的一员。在整装启程之前，我还特意与罗素、梅杰斯与沃德尔公司立下协定：日后凡我应得的薪饷结余，皆全额直接支取转交给母亲。
为了满足广大读者的兴味，不妨在此对当年的大平原货运车队作一番简要介绍。当年罗素、梅杰斯与沃德尔公司所使用的货车，通称“J·墨菲货车（J. Murphy wagons）”，全由圣路易斯专为平原货运特制。这些大车体型极其庞大、结构异常坚固，单车额定载重便高达7000磅之巨。大车的木制车厢宽敞阔绰，足有寻常人家的屋舍大小，车顶覆盖着两层厚重的厚帆布油布，以防风雨侵蚀货物。这些车厢通常自莱文沃思（Leavenworth）发运，每车满载6000磅物资，各自套挽着数副重型牛轭、由多头耕牛拉拽，并由一名专职驭手照料掌控。一支标准车队由25辆大车编组而成，统归一名总掌全局的负责人差遣，此人便被称为车队长（wagon master）。车队里位列第二的副手则是助理车长；往下依次是“机动帮手”、夜间放牧工（night herder），最后则是散畜驭手（cavallard driver）——其职责在于驱赶队伍里掉队的跛牛与未套挽具的散牛。整支车队合计算来共有31人。伙食全由伙计们自行料理，全队被划分为每组7人的伙食小班：一人司厨掌勺，一人运水打柴，一人荷枪放哨，余者各司其职，在操办饭食时人人有责。全员皆重装配备柯尔特转轮手枪（Colt’s pistols）以及密西西比猎枪（Mississippi yagers，一种重型大口径步枪），武器时刻揣在顺手处，以便随时应对突发凶险。

===Original===

The wagon master, in the language of the plains, was called the “bull-wagon boss”; the teamsters were known as “bull-whackers”; and the whole train was denominated a “bull-outfit.” Everything at that time was called an “outfit.” The men of the plains were always full of droll humor and exciting stories of their own experiences, and many an hour I spent in listening to the recitals of thrilling adventures and hair-breadth escapes.
Russell, Majors & Waddell had in their employ two hundred and fifty trains, composed of 6,250 wagons, 75,000 oxen, and about eight thousand men; their business reaching to all the government frontier posts in the north and west, to which they transported supplies, and they also carried freight as far south as New Mexico.
The trail to Salt Lake ran through Kansas to the northwest, crossing the Big Blue River, then over the Big and Little Sandy, coming into Nebraska near the Big Sandy. The next stream of any importance was the Little Blue, along which the trail ran for sixty miles; then crossed a range of sand-hills and struck the Platte River ten miles below Old Fort Kearney; thence the course lay up the South Platte to the old Ash Hollow Crossing, thence eighteen miles across to the North Platte﻿—near the mouth of the Blue Water, where General Harney had his great battle in 1855 with the Sioux and Cheyenne Indians. From this point the North Platte was followed, passing Court House Rock, Chimney Rock and Scott’s Bluffs, and then on to Fort Laramie, where the Laramie River was crossed. Still following the North Platte for some considerable distance, the trail crossed this river at old Richard’s Bridge, and followed it up to the celebrated Red Buttes﻿—crossing the Willow creeks to the Sweet Water, passing the great Independence Rock and the Devil’s gate, up to the Three Crossings of the Sweet Water, thence past the Cold Springs, where, three feet under the sod, on the hottest day of summer, ice can be found; thence to the Hot Springs and the Rocky Ridge, and through the Rocky Mountains and Echo Canyon, and thence on to the Great Salt Lake valley.
We had started on our trip with everything in good shape, following the above described trail. During the first week or two out, I became well acquainted with most of the train men, and with one in particular, who became a lifelong and intimate friend of mine. His real name was James B.

===Chinese===

用大平原上的行话来说，车队长被称为“牛车头领（bull-wagon boss）”；赶车人通称为“甩鞭手（bull-whackers）”；而整支车队则统称为“牛车队（bull-outfit）”。在那个年头，人们习惯把万事万物都唤作“outfit（队伍/行头）”。平原上的汉子们个个风趣诙谐，肚子里装满了各自亲历的惊险故事；我不知度过了多少个日夜，如痴如醉地聆听着他们口中那些扣人心弦的冒险传奇与死里逃生的惊险历险。
罗素、梅杰斯与沃德尔公司旗下共雇用了250支车队，坐拥6,250辆货车、75,000头耕牛以及约8000名雇员；他们的货运网络纵横四方，触角延伸至北部与西部所有政府边疆哨所，源源不断地运送各类补给物资，甚至连南至新墨西哥（New Mexico）的边陲重镇都有他们的货运车队奔忙穿梭。
通往盐湖城的古道穿过堪萨斯一路斜向西北延伸，横跨大蓝河（Big Blue River），继而跨越大小桑迪溪（Big and Little Sandy），在靠近大桑迪溪处进入内布拉斯加（Nebraska）境内。此后遇到的第一条重要水系是小蓝河（Little Blue River），古道顺着该河沿岸蜿蜒延伸了60英里；随后穿过一片连绵起伏的沙丘地带，在老卡尼堡（Old Fort Kearney）下游10英里处汇入普拉特河（Platte River）；自此古道逆南普拉特河（South Platte）而上，延伸至著名的灰烬凹地渡口（Ash Hollow Crossing），再横穿18英里荒原抵达北普拉特河（North Platte）——此地靠近蓝水河（Blue Water）河口，1855年哈尼将军（General Harney）曾在此同苏族与夏延族印第安人爆发过一场名震四方的大决战。自此之后，车队沿着北普拉特河继续前行，相继途经法院岩（Court House Rock）、烟囱岩（Chimney Rock）与斯科特崖（Scott’s Bluffs），随后挺进拉勒米堡（Fort Laramie），并在该哨所横渡拉勒米河。车队随后继续沿着北普拉特河纵深跋涉相当长一段路程，在古老的理查德桥（Richard’s Bridge）横渡该河，溯流而上直抵闻名遐迩的红孤峰群（Red Buttes）——在此跨过柳树溪（Willow creeks）挺进甜水河（Sweet Water），途经巍峨的独立岩（Independence Rock）与魔鬼门（Devil’s Gate），一路挺进至甜水河的三重渡口（Three Crossings of the Sweet Water）；由此再经由寒泉（Cold Springs）——哪怕在烈日炎炎的三伏盛夏，在草皮下掘地3英尺深处依然能刨出晶莹的坚冰；随后再经温泉（Hot Springs）与岩石脊（Rocky Ridge），翻越落基山脉（Rocky Mountains）并穿过回音峡谷（Echo Canyon），最终长驱直入大盐湖谷地（Great Salt Lake valley）。
我们在整顿一新、状态绝佳的气氛中拔营启程，沿着上述那条漫长的古道坚实迈进。出发后的头一两周里，我同车队里的大多数弟兄都熟络了起来；尤其是其中有一位老哥，日后成为了我相交一生、亲密无间的挚友。他的真实名字叫詹姆斯·B·（James B.）

===Original===

Hickok; he afterwards became famous as “Wild Bill, the Scout of the Plains”﻿—though why he was so called I never could ascertain﻿—and from this time forward I shall refer to him by his popular nickname. He was ten years my senior﻿—a tall, handsome, magnificently built and powerful young fellow, who could outrun, out-jump and outfight any man in the train. He was generally admitted to be the best man physically, in the employ of Russell, Majors & Waddell; and of his bravery there was not a doubt. General Custer, in his *Life on the Plains*, thus speaks of Wild Bill:

===Chinese===

希科克（Hickok）；日后他名震天下，被誉为“大平原侦察兵狂野比尔（Wild Bill, the Scout of the Plains）”——尽管他因何得此绰号我始终未曾探明——从今往后，我将一律以这一家喻户晓的绰号指代他。他比我年长10岁，生得高大伟岸、仪表堂堂、体魄雄健强悍，奔跑、跳跃与格斗拳脚无不冠绝全队，堪称车队里难逢敌手的头号好汉。在罗素、梅杰斯与沃德尔公司的全体雇员当中，大家公认他的体魄与体能最为出众；至于他的胆魄与英勇，更是毋庸置疑。卡斯特将军在所著的《大平原生活》（Life on the Plains）一书中，曾对狂野比尔作过如下绘声绘色的描述：

===Original===

> “Among the white scouts were numbered some of the most noted of their class. The most prominent man among them was ‘Wild Bill,’ whose highly varied career was made the subject of an illustrated sketch in one of the popular monthly periodicals a few years ago. ‘Wild Bill’ was a strange character, just the one which a novelist might gloat over. He was a plainsman in every sense of the word, yet unlike any other of his class. In person he was about six feet and one inch in height, straight as the straightest of the warriors whose implacable foe he was. He had broad shoulders, well-formed chest and limbs, and a face strikingly handsome; a sharp, clear blue eye, which stared you straight in the face when in conversation; a finely shaped nose, inclined to be aquiline; a well-turned mouth, with lips only partially concealed by a handsome moustache. His hair and complexion were those of the perfect blonde. The former was worn in uncut ringlets, falling carelessly over his powerfully formed shoulders. Add to this figure a costume blending the immaculate neatness of the dandy with the extravagant taste and style of the frontiersman, and you have Wild Bill.﻿ ﻿… Whether on foot or on horseback, he was one of the most perfect types of physical manhood I ever saw.
> “Of his courage there could be no question; it had been brought to the test on too many occasions to admit of a doubt. His skill in the use of the pistol and rifle was unerring; while his deportment was exactly the opposite of what might be expected from a man of his surroundings. It was entirely free from all bluster or bravado. He seldom spoke himself unless requested to do so. His conversation, strange to say, never bordered either on the vulgar or blasphemous. His influence among the frontiersmen was unbounded, his word was law; and many are the personal quarrels and disturbances which he has checked among his comrades by his simple announcement that ‘This has gone far enough,’﻿—if need be followed by the ominous warning that when persisted in or renewed the quarreler ‘must settle it with me.’
> “Wild Bill was anything but a quarrelsome man; yet no one but him could enumerate the many conflicts in which he had been engaged, and which had almost always resulted in the death of his adversary.

===Chinese===

> “在白人侦察兵群体中，涌现出不少名震边疆的传奇人物。而其中最为耀眼夺目的当属‘狂野比尔’；几年前，一家深受欢迎的知名月刊还曾专门为他那跌宕起伏的传奇生涯配图刊文。‘狂野比尔’是一个极富传奇色彩的怪杰，恰是小说家们最梦寐以求的绝佳题材。他是彻头彻尾、不折不扣的大平原之子，却又与他同侪中的任何人都截然不同。他身长6英尺1英寸，身姿挺拔如松，比他平生最顽强的死敌印第安勇士还要挺直昂扬。他肩膀宽阔，胸膛厚实，四肢修长健美，面庞俊美异常；生着一双锐利清澈的湛蓝眼眸，每与人交谈对视，那目光便直视你的双眼深处；鼻梁高挺秀美，线条微带鹰钩；嘴唇轮廓分明，修剪考究的八字美髭掩映其上。他的金发与肤色呈现出纯正金发碧眼者的极致色泽，那一头未经修剪的金发烫成波浪卷曲，随意垂落在宽阔厚实的肩头。再加上一身兼具花花公子般一尘不染的考究利落，与荒原游侠那般不羁奢靡之风的独特装束，这便是‘狂野比尔’的传神剪影。……无论他是徒步跋涉还是跨马驰骋，皆是我生平所见最具男儿阳刚气概的完美化身。
>
> “至于他的过人胆识，更是毋庸置疑，那早已在无数次命悬一线的血火考验中得到了铁一般的印证。他对转轮手枪与来福枪的驾驭技法百步穿杨、算无遗策；而他平素的言谈举止，却与人们在边疆蛮荒恶劣环境中司空见惯的粗鲁做派大相径庭。他身上全然没有半分吹嘘浮夸或耀武扬威的戾气。除非旁人主动垂询，他极少夸夸其谈。令人称奇的是，他的话语间从未沾染过半句污言秽语或轻慢渎神的市井脏话。他在边疆同胞中享有崇高威望，他的话语就是不容置疑的金科玉律；每逢营地同袍间发生意气之争或流血纠纷，他只需平静地说上一句‘到此为止吧’——若有必要，再补上一句令人不寒而栗的严正警告：若谁再执迷不悟或挑起事端，‘就由我来亲自跟他清算’，一切争端便会在瞬息间烟消云散。
>
> “狂野比尔绝非嗜血好斗、无端生事之徒；然而除他本人之外，恐怕无人能数得清他究竟经历过多少场惊心动魄的殊死决斗，而这些生死对决的结果，几乎无一例外皆是以对手血溅黄沙、命丧黄泉而告终。

===Original===

I have a personal knowledge of at least half a dozen men whom he had at various times killed, one of these being at the time a member of my command. Others had been severely wounded, yet he always escaped unhurt.
> “On the plains every man openly carries his belt with its invariable appendages, knife and revolver﻿—often two of the latter. Wild Bill always carried two handsome ivory-handled revolvers of the large size; he was never seen without them.﻿ ﻿… Yet in all the many affairs of this kind in which Wild Bill has performed a part, and which have come to my knowledge, there was not a single instance in which the verdict of twelve fair-minded men would not have been pronounced in his favor.”

===Chinese===

我亲眼熟知的、在不同岁月里被他击毙的亡魂便至少有半打（6人），其中有一位在当时甚至还是我麾下部队的一员。至于被他重创伤残者更是不计其数，而他自己却总能在弹雨刀光中毫发无损地全身而退。
>
> “在大平原上，人人腰间都明晃晃地挂着一条佩带，上面必然携带着永不离身的刀刃与转轮手枪——往往还是两把手枪。狂野比尔随身佩戴的始终是两把大号象牙手柄的华美转轮手枪；无论何时何地，从未见他放下过双枪。……然而，在我所知悉的狂野比尔卷入的所有此类流血纷争中，若交由12位秉持公允的陪审员来审断裁决，无一例外都会一致认定他是出于自卫而宣判无罪。”

===Original===

Such is the faithful picture of Wild Bill as drawn by General Custer, who was a close observer and student of personal character, and under whom Wild Bill served as a scout.
The circumstances under which I first made his acquaintance and learned to know him well and to appreciate his manly character and kindheartedness, were these. One of the teamsters in Lew Simpson’s train was a surly, overbearing fellow, and took particular delight in bullying and tyrannizing over me, and one day while we were at dinner he asked me to do something for him. I did not start at once, and he gave me a slap in the face with the back of his hand﻿—knocking me off an ox-yoke on which I was sitting, and sending me sprawling on the ground. Jumping to my feet I picked up a camp kettle full of boiling coffee which was setting on the fire, and threw it at him. I hit him in the face, and the hot coffee gave him a severe scalding. He sprang for me with the ferocity of a tiger, and would undoubtedly have torn me to pieces, had it not been for the timely interference of my newfound friend, Wild Bill, who knocked the man down. As soon as he recovered himself, he demanded of Wild Bill what business it was of his that he should “put in his oar.” “It’s my business to protect that boy, or anybody else, from being unmercifully abused, kicked and cuffed, and I’ll whip any man who tries it on,” said Wild Bill; “and if you ever again lay a hand on that boy﻿—little Billy there﻿—I’ll give you such a pounding that you won’t get over it for a month of Sundays.” From that time forward Wild Bill was my protector and intimate friend, and the friendship thus begun continued until his death.
Nothing transpired on the trip to delay or give us any trouble whatever, until the train struck the South Platte River. One day we camped on the same ground where the Indians had surprised the cattle herd, in charge of the McCarty brothers. It was with difficulty that we discovered any traces of anybody ever having camped there before, the only landmark being the single grave, now covered with grass, in which we had buried the three men who had been killed. The country was alive with buffaloes. Vast herds of these monarchs of the plains were roaming all around us, and we laid over one day for a grand hunt.

===Chinese===

以上便是卡斯特将军笔下对狂野比尔的真实写照。卡斯特将军素来善于观察剖析人物性情，而狂野比尔正是在他麾下效力的得力侦察兵。
至于我最初与他相识相知、并由衷敬重他的男子汉气概与侠骨柔肠的经过，则是一段颇有戏剧性的往事。在卢·辛普森的车队里，有一名性格乖戾、蛮横跋扈的赶车驭手，平日里尤其以欺凌作践我为乐。有一天我们正围坐着用午餐，他盛气凌人地支使我替他跑腿干活。我没有立刻起身遵命，他反手便是一记重重的耳光抽在我脸上——将我从正坐着的一具牛轭上一头打翻在地，摔得我满地打滚。我一跃而起，一把抓起火堆上正烧得滚烫、咕嘟咕嘟冒着热气的一整壶热咖啡，劈头盖脸朝他狠狠砸了过去。滚烫的咖啡不偏不倚砸中了他的脸庞，将他烫得皮开肉绽、鬼哭狼嚎。他如恶虎扑食般红着眼向我猛扑过来，若非我新结识的挚友狂野比尔在千钧一发之际挺身而出、一拳将那恶汉轰倒在地，我当时势必会被他活活撕成碎片。那恶汉挣扎着从地上爬起来，气急败坏地质问狂野比尔凭什么多管闲事插手横栏。“我的闲事就是专管护着这个孩子，或者任何无辜的人免受残忍虐待、拳打脚踢；谁要是敢动他一根汗毛，我就把他打得满地找牙！”狂野比尔冷冷喝道，“要是你敢再碰这孩子——我们的小比利一下，我就把你揍得一个月都爬不下床！”自那以后，狂野比尔便成了我形影不离的庇护者与莫逆之交，这份在患难中结下的深厚友谊一直维系到他生命的最后一刻。
此后的长途跋涉中一路风平浪静，未曾发生任何耽搁行程或滋生事端的波折，直至车队推进到南普拉特河畔。那一天，我们恰好在当年印第安人袭击麦卡蒂兄弟（McCarty brothers）看管的牛群的旧址上扎营宿夜。岁月变迁，我们已很难在荒草丛中寻觅到当年曾有人在此扎营的蛛丝马迹，唯一醒目的地标是一座如今已被青草掩映的孤坟，当年我们正是把罹难的那三名伙伴（3人）安葬在此处。荒原上到处都是成群结队的野牛，这群大平原上的霸主浩浩荡荡漫山遍野游弋，于是我们特意休整驻留了一天，尽情展开了一场盛大的狩猎。

===Original===

Besides killing quite a number of buffaloes, and having a day of rare sport, we captured ten or twelve head of cattle, they being a portion of the herd which had been stampeded by the Indians, two months before. The next day we pulled out of camp, and the train was strung out to a considerable length along the road which ran near the foot of the sand-hills, two miles from the river. Between the road and the river we saw a large herd of buffaloes grazing quietly, they having been down to the stream for a drink.
Just at this time we observed a party of returning Californians coming from the West. They, too, noticed the buffalo herd, and in another moment they were dashing down upon them, urging their steeds to the greatest speed. The buffalo herd stampeded at once, and broke for the hills; so hotly were they pursued by the hunters that about five hundred of them rushed through our train pell-mell, frightening both men and oxen. Some of the wagons were turned clear round, and many of the terrified oxen attempted to run to the hills, with the heavy wagons attached to them. Others turned around so short that they broke the wagon tongues off. Nearly all the teams got entangled in their gearing, and became wild and unruly, so that the perplexed drivers were unable to manage them.
The buffaloes, the cattle, and the drivers, were soon running in every direction, and the excitement upset nearly everybody and everything. Many of the cattle broke their yokes and stampeded. One big buffalo bull became entangled in one of the heavy wagon-chains, and it is a fact that in his desperate efforts to free himself, he not only actually snapped the strong chain in two, but broke the ox-yoke to which it was attached, and the last seen of him he was running towards the hills with it hanging from his horns. A dozen other equally remarkable incidents happened during the short time that the frantic buffaloes were playing havoc with our train, and when they had got through and left us, our outfit was very badly crippled and scattered. This caused us to go into camp and spend a day in replacing the broken tongues, and repairing other damages, and gathering up our scattered ox-teams.
The next day we rolled out of camp, and proceeded on our way towards the setting sun.

===Chinese===

除了猎获相当数量的野牛、痛痛快快享受了一整天难得的狩猎乐趣之外，我们还意外捕获了10到12头散落的耕牛——经辨认，它们正是两个月前被印第安人冲散的那群牛群中的失散牲畜。次日清晨，大队人马拔营启程，整支车队在靠近沙丘脚下的古道上逶迤拉开了相当长的阵线，此地距河岸约有两英里（2英里）。在道路与河流之间的开阔草甸上，我们望见一大群野牛刚刚下河饮水归来，正悠然自得地在草地上低头觅食。
就在这当口，我们瞥见一群自西向东而来的加利福尼亚归乡客。他们同样敏锐地盯上了这群野牛，眨眼之间便策马扬鞭直扑牛群，催动坐骑狂飙突进。野牛群瞬间惊炸受惊，发疯般朝沙丘方向亡命奔逃；在后方猎手们穷追不舍的狂轰滥炸下，大约有500头惊恐万状的野牛如决堤洪流般没头没脑地直直冲入我们的车队阵中，将赶车的驭手与拉车的耕牛吓得魂飞魄散。好几辆大车被硬生生撞得原地打转掉头，许多受惊发狂的耕牛套着沉重的大车盲目向山丘方向狂奔；还有的牲口在剧烈冲撞中猛然急转弯，竟将结实的辕木车辕当场生生别断。几乎所有牛队的套具与挽具都被冲撞得乱作一团、死死缠绕在一起，耕牛彻底陷入了癫狂狂躁，急得焦头烂额的驭手们根本无法控制驾驭局面。
荒原上刹那间乱成一团，野牛、耕牛与驭手在四面八方惊惶乱窜，整座车队被这场突如其来的浩劫搅得天翻地覆。大批耕牛挣断了牛轭四散奔逃。一头庞大如山的野牛公牛不慎被一条沉重粗壮的货车铁链死死缠住；千真万确的是，在拼死挣脱束缚的狂暴挣扎中，这头巨兽不仅硬生生把粗大的铁链当场崩断成两截，甚至将连着铁链的坚固牛轭也彻底扯断；等我们最后瞥见它时，它正顶着挂在牛角上的断轭向着山丘亡命狂奔。在陷入癫狂的野牛群大闹车队的短短片刻之内，还发生了十多起（12起）同样匪夷所思的惊险插曲；待野牛群狂奔呼啸而过、扬长而去之时，我们的车队早已被摧残得七零八落、残破不堪。这场飞来横祸迫使我们不得不当场就地扎营，整整耗费了一整天时间来更换断裂的车辕、抢修损毁的部件，并四出搜寻聚拢那些惊散在旷野各处的耕牛队。
直到第三天清晨，我们才重新整饬车队拔营开拔，沿着古道继续向着落日西沉的方向坚定挺进。

===Original===

Everything ran along smoothly with us from that point until we came within about eighteen miles of Green River, in the Rocky Mountains﻿—where we camped at noon. At this place we had to drive our cattle about a mile and a half to a creek to water them. Simpson, his assistant, George Woods and myself, accompanied by the usual number of guards, drove the cattle over to the creek, and while on our way back to camp, we suddenly observed a party of twenty horsemen rapidly approaching us. We were not yet in view of our wagons, as a rise of ground intervened, and therefore we could not signal the trainmen in case of any unexpected danger befalling us. We had no suspicion, however, that we were about to be trapped, as the strangers were white men. When they had come up to us, one of the party, who evidently was the leader, rode out in front and said:
“How are you, Mr. Simpson?”
“You’ve got the best of me, sir,” said Simpson, who did not know him.
“Well, I rather think I have,” coolly replied the stranger, whose words conveyed a double meaning, as we soon learned. We had all come to a halt by this time, and the strange horsemen had surrounded us. They were all armed with double-barreled shot guns, rifles and revolvers. We also were armed with revolvers, but we had had no idea of danger, and these men, much to our surprise, had “got the drop” on us, and had covered us with their weapons, so that we were completely at their mercy. The whole movement of corraling us was done so quietly and quickly that it was accomplished before we knew it.
“I’ll trouble you for your six shooters, gentlemen,” now said the leader.
“I’ll give ’em to you in a way you don’t want,” replied Simpson.
The next moment three guns were leveled at Simpson. “If you make a move you’re a dead man,” said the leader.
Simpson saw that he was taken at a great disadvantage, and thinking it advisable not to risk the lives of the party by any rash act on his part, he said: “I see now that you have the best of me, but who are you, anyhow?”
“I am Joe Smith,” was the reply.
“What! the leader of the Danites?” asked Simpson.
“You are correct,” said Smith, for he it was.
“Yes,” said Simpson, “I know you now; you are a spying scoundrel.”

===Chinese===

从那一处起，我们的行程一直十分顺遂平稳，直至推进到落基山脉腹地、距离绿河（Green River）大约还有18英里的一片高地——我们在正午时分在此扎营歇脚。在这处宿营地，我们必须把牛群驱赶到大约一英里半（1.5英里）开外的一条小溪边去饮水。辛普森、他的副手乔治·伍兹（George Woods）以及我本人，在常规数量的警卫护送下，赶着大批耕牛前往小溪饮水。就在我们饮完牲口折返回营的途中，突然赫然发现一队约有20名骑手的武装马队正风驰电掣般向我们急速逼近。由于中间隔着一道隆起的起伏地势山梁，我们此时尚未进入车队大营的视线范围之内，因而一旦遭遇突发险情，我们根本无法向留守大营的弟兄们鸣枪示警。然而，我们起初丝毫没有料到自己即将踏入精心布置的圈套陷阱，因为迎面而来的陌生人全是一清二楚的白人。当他们策马冲到我们近前时，其中显然是领头头目的一人拍马出阵，皮笑肉不笑地打招呼道：
“辛普森先生，近来可好啊？”
“这位先生，您可把我问住了，不知阁下是哪位？”辛普森完全认不出此人，坦然答道。
“哼，我想我确实是占了你的上风。”陌生人冷酷从容地答道。我们很快便心惊肉跳地领会到了他话里有话的双重含义。此时我们所有人已被迫勒马止步，那帮陌生的武装骑手已将我们团团包围在中央。他们个个荷枪实弹，配备了双管猎枪、来福枪与转轮手枪。我们腰间虽也佩有转轮手枪，但此前毫无临战戒备之心，这帮歹徒出其不意抢先“拔枪在手（got the drop）”，黑洞洞的枪口瞬间死死封锁住了我们全员的要害，叫我们彻底沦为任人宰割的俎上鱼肉。这整场将我们合围缴械的突袭行动干得如此悄无声息、迅雷不及掩耳，以至于还没等我们回过神来，局面便已大局已定。
“诸位先生，劳驾把你们身上的六连发转轮手枪都交出来吧。”头目冷冷发话道。
“老子给你枪子儿的方式，保管不是你想要的！”辛普森怒目圆睁回敬道。
话音未落，三支枪口（3支枪）齐刷刷顶在了辛普森的脑袋上。“你只要敢动一动，立刻叫你脑袋开花。”头目阴森森地警告道。
辛普森深知自己眼下身处极端劣势，自忖若是一时鲁莽冲动必将连累全队同伴白白送命，便强压怒火道：“我承认眼下是你占了上风，但你究竟是何方神圣？”
“行不更名坐不改姓，乔·史密斯（Joe Smith）是也。”对方冷笑答道。
“什么！你就是摩门教达尼特杀手团（Danites）的头子？”辛普森失声惊呼。
“算你有眼力见儿。”史密斯傲然答道，来者正是此人。
“哼，”辛普森咬牙切齿道，“老子现在认出你来了；你就是那个刺探军情的下三滥密探！”

===Original===

Simpson had good reason for calling him this and applying to him a much more opprobrious epithet, for only a short time before this, Joe Smith had visited our train in the disguise of a teamster, and had remained with us two days. He suddenly disappeared, no one knowing where he had gone or why he had come among us. But it was all explained to us now that he had returned with his Mormon Danites. After they had disarmed us, Simpson asked, “Well, Smith, what are you going to do with us?”
“Ride back with us and I’ll soon show you,” said Smith.
We had no idea of the surprise which awaited us. As we came upon the top of the ridge, from which we could view our camp, we were astonished to see the remainder of the train men disarmed and stationed in a group and surrounded by another squad of Danites, while other Mormons were searching our wagons for such articles as they wanted.
“How is this?” inquired Simpson. “How did you surprise my camp without a struggle? I can’t understand it.”
“Easily enough,” said Smith; “your men were all asleep under the wagons, except the cooks, who saw us coming and took us for returning Californians or emigrants, and paid no attention to us until we rode up and surrounded your train. With our arms covering the men, we woke them up, and told them that all they had to do was to walk out and drop their pistols﻿—which they saw was the best thing they could do under circumstances over which they had no control﻿—and you can just bet they did it.”
“And what do you propose to do with us now?” asked Simpson.
“I intend to burn your train,” said he; “you are loaded with supplies and ammunition for Sidney Johnson, and as I have no way to convey the stuff to my own people, I’ll see that it does not reach the United States troops.”
“Are you going to turn us adrift here?” asked Simpson, who was anxious to learn what was to become of himself and his men.
“No; I hardly am as bad as that. I’ll give you enough provisions to last you until you can reach Fort Bridger,” replied Smith; “and as soon as your cooks can get the stuff out of the wagons, you can start.”
“On foot?” was the laconic inquiry of Simpson.
“Yes sir,” was the equally short reply.
“Smith, that’s too rough on us men.

===Chinese===

辛普森用这般尖刻的字眼痛斥他，甚至哪怕用上更加污秽刺耳的蔑称也绝不为过，因为就在此战不久之前，乔·史密斯曾乔装打扮成一名普通赶车驭手混入我们的车队，在我们队伍里足足厮混潜伏了两天（2天）。随后他又神秘莫测地人间蒸发，当时谁也不知道他去往何方，更不知他混入我们车队究竟意欲何为。直到此时此刻，他率领着摩门教达尼特杀手团卷土重来，先前的谜团才终于真相大白。在被他们彻底缴械搜身之后，辛普森沉着脸质问道：“好你个史密斯，你打算把我们怎么样？”
“跟我们一道骑马回大营看看，你很快便会心领神会。”史密斯冷冷哂笑道。
我们万万没有料到等待着我们的竟是这样一幅景象。当我们策马登上那道山梁俯瞰营地时，眼前的一幕令我们目瞪口呆、惊骇欲绝：留守在大营里的车队弟兄们早已被全数缴械，被三五成群押解在一处，周围由另一队达尼特杀手持枪严密看押；而其他摩门教徒则正大肆翻箱倒柜，在我们的货车里疯狂搜刮洗劫他们看中的一应物资。
“这到底是怎么回事？”辛普森又惊又怒地喝问，“你们怎能在毫无激烈交火搏杀的情况下，就把我的大营悄无声息地端了？我实在无法理解！”
“那可太轻而易举了，”史密斯得意洋洋地嘲讽道，“除那几名厨子之外，你的这帮手下当时全钻在货车底下呼呼大睡；而厨子们远远望见我们人马靠近，误以为我们是从加州归乡的淘金客或寻常移民，根本没往心里去，任由我们大摇大摆策马杀到跟前将整个车队围得水泄不通。我们举着枪把这群睡眼惺忪的汉子一个个从车底唤醒，明白无误地告诉他们：摆在他们面前唯一的活路，就是乖乖钻出来扔掉手枪——在那种人为刀俎我为鱼肉的绝境下，他们自然懂得识时务者为俊杰——老兄，你大可相信，他们干得可比谁都顺从干脆。”
“那么，你现在打算如何处置我们？”辛普森急切追问道。
“我打算把你的整座车队一把火烧成灰烬，”史密斯冷酷地宣布，“你们满载着运给西德尼·约翰斯顿（Sidney Johnston）大军的军需补给与军火弹药；既然我手头的人马无法将这批庞大辎重全数运回供给我的教友同胞，那我无论如何也绝不会容许它落入美利坚合众国军队的手中。”
“难道你打算把我们这帮大活人赤手空拳丢在这荒郊野岭等死？”辛普森心急如焚地质问道，迫切想探明自己与麾下三十多号弟兄的最终生路。
“不，我倒还不至于丧心病狂到那个地步。我会留给你们足够维持到你们徒步跋涉至布里杰堡（Fort Bridger）的口粮，”史密斯答道，“只要你们的伙夫把那些干粮物资从车厢里搬运出来，你们便可以立刻启程上路。”
“徒步？用两条腿走？”辛普森言简意赅、冷若冰霜地从牙缝里挤出这几个字。
“没错，先生，”对方同样干脆利落地回应。
“史密斯，你这对咱们这帮弟兄未免也太狠绝毒辣了！

===Original===

Put yourself in our place and see how you would like it,” said Simpson; “you can well afford to give us at least one wagon and six yokes of oxen to convey us and our clothing and provisions to Fort Bridger. You’re a brute if you don’t do this.”
“Well,” said Smith, after consulting a minute or two with some of his company, “I’ll do that much for you.”
The cattle and the wagon were brought up according to his orders, and the clothing and provisions were loaded on.
“Now you can go,” said Smith, after everything had been arranged.
“Joe Smith, I think you are a mean coward to set us afloat in a hostile country, without giving us our arms,” said Simpson, who had once before asked for the weapons, and had had his request denied.
Smith, after further consultation with his comrades, said: “Simpson, you are too brave a man to be turned adrift here without any means of defense. You shall have your revolvers and guns.” Our weapons were accordingly handed over to Simpson, and we at once started for Fort Bridger, knowing that it would be useless to attempt the recapture of our train.
When we had traveled about two miles we saw the smoke arising from our old camp. The Mormons after taking what goods they wanted and could carry off, had set fire to the wagons, many of which were loaded with bacon, lard, hardtack, and other provisions, which made a very hot, fierce fire, and the smoke to roll up in dense clouds. Some of the wagons were loaded with ammunition, and it was not long before loud explosions followed in rapid succession. We waited and witnessed the burning of the train, and then pushed on to Fort Bridger. Arriving at this post, we learned that two other trains had been captured and destroyed in the same way, by the Mormons. This made seventy-five wagon loads, or 450,000 pounds of supplies, mostly provisions, which never reached General Johnson’s command, to which they had been consigned.

===Chinese===

换作是你处在我们的境地，看看你自个儿心里会是什么滋味！”辛普森据理力争怒斥道，“凭你劫掠的这笔横财，你完全有余力至少给我们留下一辆大车和6副牛轭（六副牛轭/12头耕牛），好运送我们的人马、衣物和口粮前往布里杰堡。倘若你连这点生路都不给，那你简直连畜生都不如！”
“好吧，”史密斯同身旁的几名副手低头耳语商议了一两分钟后，勉强松口道，“看在这一层的份上，我便成全你这一遭。”
遵照他的吩咐，耕牛与一辆大车被牵运上前，我们被准许装载上了必要的过冬衣物与行军口粮。
“现在，你们可以滚蛋了。”一应交接停当之后，史密斯冷冷下达了逐客令。
“乔·史密斯，把我们赤手空拳扔在强敌环伺的凶险绝境中，连防身的防卫武器都不给，你这分明就是个卑鄙下流的懦夫！”辛普森毫不退让，在此前曾索要武器被拒的情况下，再度义正辞严地据理力争。
史密斯同他的同伙再度窃窃私语权衡了一番，转过头来说道：“辛普森，不得不承认，你是条真正的汉子，把你毫无防卫能力地丢在这片蛮荒地界自生自灭，确实有失体面。你们的转轮手枪和枪支可以还给你们。”于是我们的武器被原封不动交还给了辛普森，我们深知凭这几条枪绝无可能夺回庞大的车队，便片刻不敢耽搁，立即启程大步迈向布里杰堡。
