当我们跋涉出大约两英里（2英里）之遥时，回首望去，只见我们原先的营地废墟上空正升腾起遮天蔽日的滚滚浓烟。摩门教徒在搜刮掠夺走他们中意且能够随身带走的一切财物之后，悍然纵火焚烧了所有的货车。其中许多车厢装满了熏肉培根、猪油炼油、干饼压缩饼干以及其他各类食品物资，这使得大火燃烧得格外狂暴炽烈，漆黑如墨的滚滚浓烟翻江倒海般冲上云霄。更有几辆货车满载着大批军火弹药，不出多时，连绵不绝的惊天巨响与连环大爆炸便在山谷间震耳欲聋地此起彼伏炸响。我们驻足远眺，眼睁睁凝望着这支庞大车队在烈焰中化为焦炭灰烬，随后强忍悲愤，继续步履维艰地跋涉赶往布里杰堡。抵达该哨所后，我们惊闻另有两支货运车队也遭到了摩门教徒如出一辙的劫掠与焚毁。三处劫案合计算来，整整损失了75车货物，折合450,000磅（45万磅）之后勤物资——其中绝大部分为至关重要的口粮给养；这批原本交付给约翰斯顿将军大军的军需命脉，自此永远未能运抵前线大营。

## Introductory / 导言
===Original===

The life and adventures of Hon. William F. Cody﻿—Buffalo Bill﻿—as told by himself, make up a narrative which reads more like romance than reality, and which in many respects will prove a valuable contribution to the records of our Western frontier history. While no literary excellence is claimed for the narrative, it has the greater merit of being truthful, and is verified in such a manner that no one can doubt its veracity. The frequent reference to such military men as Generals Sheridan, Carr, Merritt, Crook, Terry, Colonel Royal, and other officers under whom Mr. Cody served as scout and guide at different times and in various sections of the frontier, during the numerous Indian campaigns of the last ten or twelve years, affords ample proof of his genuineness as a thoroughbred scout.
There is no humbug or braggadocio about Buffalo Bill. He is known far and wide, and his reputation has been earned honestly and by hard work. By a combination of circumstances he was educated to the life of a plainsman from his youth up; and not the least interesting portion of his career is that of his early life, passed as it was in Kansas during the eventful and troubleous times connected with the settlement of that state. Spending much time in the saddle, while a mere boy he crossed the plains many times in company with bull-trains; on some of these trips he met with thrilling adventures and had several hairbreadth escapes from death at the hands of Indians. Then, for a while, he was dashing over the plains as a pony-express rider. Soon afterwards, mounted on the high seat of an overland stagecoach, he was driving a six-in-hand team. We next hear of him cracking the bull-whacker’s whip, and commanding a wagon-train through a wild and dangerous country to the far West. During the civil war he enlisted as a private, and became a scout with the Union army; since the war he has been employed as hunter, trapper, guide, scout and actor. As a buffalo hunter he has no superior; as a trailer of Indians he has no equal. For many years he has taken an active part in all the principal Indian campaigns on the Western frontier, and as a scout and guide he has rendered inestimable services to the various expeditions which he accompanied.

===Chinese===

威廉·F·科迪阁下（Hon. William F. Cody）——亦即家喻户晓的“野牛比尔（Buffalo Bill）”——亲口自述的生平与冒险历险记，读来宛如一部跌宕起伏的传奇史诗，更胜于虚构演义；无论从何种角度而言，这部巨著都将成为铭刻美国西部边疆开拓史册的极具分量的珍贵文献。尽管著者并不自诩文字辞藻之华美，但本书却具备远比文采更为珍贵的特质——字字皆为肺腑实录，且经由多方铁证翔实核验，其真实信度无可置疑。书中频频提及谢里登将军（General Sheridan）、卡尔将军（General Carr）、梅里特将军（General Merritt）、克鲁克将军（General Crook）、特里将军（General Terry）、罗亚尔上校（Colonel Royal）等一众赫赫有名的军界将领；在过去10到12年间烽火连天的无数次征讨印第安人战役中，科迪先生曾在不同时期、在边疆各个战略要冲效力于诸位将军麾下，屡次出任先锋向导与首席侦察兵。这一桩桩彪炳战功，足以雄辩地证明他是一名当之无愧、千锤百炼的顶尖侦察英豪。
在野牛比尔身上，绝无半点弄虚作假、沽名钓誉的虚浮吹嘘。他的威名传遍四海，而他的崇高声誉全凭一己之真才实学、历经千锤百炼与血火拼搏挣得。在命运的诸多机缘交织下，他自幼便在严酷的蛮荒试炼中成长为一名坚韧的大平原之子；而在他的传奇履历中，其早年经历同样扣人心弦——那正是堪萨斯建州开拓初期波谲云诡、动荡不安的风云岁月。他在马背上度过了青葱岁月，尚是一介懵懂孩童时，便已多次跟随运送重货的牛车队横穿大平原；在其中的几次远征中，他数次遭遇惊心动魄的喋血险情，数度在印第安人的夺命刀锋下九死一生。随后的一段岁月里，他化身为驭马如飞的快马驿道骑士（pony-express rider），疾风般掠过荒原。不久之后，他高居横跨大陆驿马长途马车的最高驾座之上，娴熟掌控着六挽驭马长辔。再后来，我们便听闻他挥舞着牛车驭手的清脆长鞭，统领着满载货物的车队穿行在通往遥远西部的蛮荒险境之中。南北战争爆发之际，他毅然以列兵军衔参军入伍，成为联邦北军军营中骁勇善战的侦察兵；战火平息之后，他又先后操持过猎手、捕兽人、向导、侦察兵乃至舞台戏剧演员。论及猎杀野牛，这世上无人出其右；论及追踪印第安敌骑踪迹，天下更无出其匹。多年来，他以核心骨干的身份深度参与了西部边疆几乎所有主要的重大战事，身为卓越的向导与前哨侦察兵，他为所随行的各路远征大军立下了不可估量的居功至伟奇功。

===Original===

During his life on the plains he not only had many exciting adventures himself, but he became associated with many of the other noted plainsmen, and in his narrative he frequently refers to them and relates many interesting incidents and thrilling events connected with them. He has had a fertile field from which to produce this volume, and has frequently found it necessary to condense the facts in order to embody the most interesting events of his life. The following from a letter written by General E. A. Carr, of the Fifth Cavalry, now commanding Fort McPherson, speaks for itself:

===Chinese===

在浪迹大平原的生涯中，他不仅亲身经历了数不胜数的惊险历险，更同边疆诸多声名显赫的风云人物结下了深厚渊源；在这部回忆录中，他频频提及这些豪杰伙伴，并生动记述了围绕在他们身旁的诸多奇闻轶事与惊心动魄的历史事件。这片广袤无垠的西部热土为这部长卷提供了取之不尽的丰沃素材，而为了在有限的篇幅内将他一生中最摄人心魄的传奇片段浓缩凝练，著者常常不得不对诸多事实进行高度精炼提炼。以下引自第五骑兵团（Fifth Cavalry）现任麦克弗森堡（Fort McPherson）司令官E·A·卡尔将军（General E. A. Carr）的一封亲笔信函，其言辞恳切，自胜千言：

===Original===

> “I first met Mr. Cody, October 22nd, 1868, at Buffalo Station, on the Kansas Pacific railroad, in Kansas. He was scout and guide for the seven companies of the Fifth Cavalry, then under Colonel Royal, and of which I was ordered to take the command. * * * *
> “From his services with my command, steadily in the field for nine months, from October, 1868, to July, 1869, and at subsequent times, I am qualified to bear testimony to his qualities and character.
> “He was very modest and unassuming. I did not know for a long time how good a title he had to the appellation, ‘Buffalo Bill.’ I am apt to discount the claims of scouts, as they will occasionally exaggerate; and when I found one who said nothing about himself, I did not think much of him, till I had proved him. He is a natural gentleman in his manners as well as in character, and has none of the roughness of the typical frontiersman. He can take his own part when required, but I have never heard of his using a knife or a pistol, or engaging in a quarrel where it could be avoided. His personal strength and activity are such that he can hardly meet a man whom he cannot handle, and his temper and disposition are so good that no one has reason to quarrel with him.
> “His eyesight is better than a good field glass; he is the best trailer I ever heard of; and also the best judge of the ‘lay of country,’﻿—that is, he is able to tell what kind of country is ahead, so as to know how to act. He is a perfect judge of distance, and always ready to tell correctly how many miles it is to water, or to any place, or how many miles have been marched. * * * *
> “Mr. Cody seemed never to tire and was always ready to go, in the darkest night or the worst weather, and usually volunteered, knowing what the emergency required. His trailing, when following Indians or looking for stray animals or game, is simply wonderful. He is a most extraordinary hunter. I could not believe that a man could be certain to shoot antelope running till I had seen him do it so often.
> “In a fight Mr. Cody is never noisy, obstreperous or excited. In fact, I never hardly noticed him in a fight, unless I happened to want him, or he had something to report, when he was always in the right place, and his information was always valuable and reliable.

===Chinese===

> “我与科迪先生初次相识，是在1868年10月22日堪萨斯太平洋铁路沿线的堪萨斯野牛车站（Buffalo Station）。当时他正出任第五骑兵团下辖七个连队（7个连）的向导兼首席侦察兵，那批部队彼时暂由罗亚尔上校统领，而我奉上峰之命前往接掌全团指挥权。* * * *
>
> “从1868年10月至1869年7月整整九个月（9个月）的漫长野战岁月里，他一直在我的指挥下坚守前沿，并在其后的多次战役中屡建奇功；因此，我完全有资格并以极其负责的态度为他的人格品行与卓越才干作证。
>
> “他为人极其谦逊内敛，毫不张扬。很长一段时间里，我都浑然不知他在‘野牛比尔’这一名震天下的绰号上究竟立下过多大的真功勋。我向来对寻常边疆侦察兵的自我吹嘘心存警惕并习惯大打折扣，毕竟他们往往喜欢言过其实；因此当我最初遇上一位对自己功勋只字不提的人时，在未经真刀真枪的事实验证之前，我并未对他寄予过高期望。然而事实证明，无论在礼节修养还是在道德品行上，他都是一位天生的谦谦君子，身上丝毫没有寻常西部粗汉的那种野蛮习气。在必要关头他完全有能力自卫自立，但在我的见闻里，凡是可以和平化解纷争的场合，我从未听说他动过刀动过枪，更不曾参与过任何无端争斗。他的力量与敏捷身手堪称罕见，世间几乎寻不出他降服不了的对手；但他性情极其宽厚温和，以至于没有任何人会无端与他滋生冲突纠纷。
>
> “他的视力比最精良的军用望远镜还要敏锐清澈；他是世间我所知晓的最卓越的追踪大师；同时也是研判‘山川地形走向’的最高行家——换言之，他一眼便能断定前方的地势险阻，从而让部队知晓该如何临机应变。他对距离的研判精准无误，无论询问水源在几英里开外，抑或是前往某一据点的确切里程，还是大军当日跋涉了多少英里，他总能脱口而出、分毫不差。* * * *
>
> “科迪先生仿佛永远不知疲倦为何物，哪怕在最漆黑的深夜或最严酷的恶劣风暴中，他也时刻整装待发；每逢生死攸关的危急时刻，他总会洞悉战局所需，主动挺身而出请缨受命。在追捕敌对印第安武装、搜寻走失牲畜或寻猎野兽时，他的追踪神技简直令人叹为观止。他更是一位神乎其神的传奇猎手：若非亲眼屡次目睹他百步穿杨的绝技，我绝不敢相信世上竟有人能在马背飞驰中，枪枪精准射中亡命奔逃的羚羊。
>
> “在战火硝烟的激烈交锋中，科迪先生从不喧哗聒噪、张皇失措或头脑发热。事实上，在战斗中除非我紧急召唤他，或是他掌握了重大军情前来报告，人们几乎很难注意到他的声响——但他永远都在最该出现的关键战位上，而且他送来的每一次军情都具有极其宝贵且绝对可靠的战略价值。

===Original===

> “During the winter of 1868, we encountered hardships and exposure in terrific snow storms, sleet, etc., etc. On one occasion, that winter, Mr. Cody showed his quality by quietly offering to go with some dispatches to General Sheridan, across a dangerous region, where another principal scout was reluctant to risk himself.
> “On the 13th of May, 1869, he was in the fight at Elephant Rock, Kansas, and trailed the Indians till the 16th, when we got another fight out of them on Spring Creek, in Nebraska, and scattered them after following them one hundred and fifty miles in three days. It was at Spring Creek where Cody was ahead of the command about three miles, with the advance guard of forty men, when two hundred Indians suddenly surrounded them. Our men, dismounted and formed in a circle, holding their horses, firing and slowly retreating. They all, to this day, speak of Cody’s coolness and bravery. This was the Dog Soldier band which captured Mrs. Alderdice and Mrs. Weichel in Kansas. They strangled Mrs. Alderdice’s baby, killed Mrs. Weichel’s husband, and took a great deal of property and stock from different persons. We got on their trail again, June 28th, and followed it nearly two hundred miles, till we struck the Indians on Sunday, July 11th, 1869, at Summit Spring. The Indians, as soon as they saw us coming, killed Mrs. Alderdice with a hatchet, and shot Mrs. Weichel, but fortunately not fatally, and she was saved.
> “Mr. Cody has since served with me as post guide and scout at Fort McPherson, where he frequently distinguished himself. * * * *
> “In the summer of 1876, Cody went with me to the Black Hills region where he killed Yellow-Hand. Afterwards he was with the Big Horn and Yellowstone expedition. I consider that his services to the country and the army by trailing, finding and fighting Indians, and thus protecting the frontier settlers, and by guiding commands over the best and most practicable routes, have been far beyond the compensation he has received. His friends of the Fifth Cavalry are all glad that he is in a lucrative business, and hope that he may live long and prosper. Personally, I feel under obligations to him for assistance in my campaigns which no other man could, or would, have rendered. Of course I wish him, and his, every success.”
> Fort McPherson, Nebraska, July 3rd, 1878
> E. A. Carr, Lt. Col. 5th Cav., Brev. Maj. Gen’l U.

===Chinese===

> “在1868年的严冬里，我们在遮天蔽日的暴风雪、冻雨与极寒严寒中饱受艰辛与生死考验。那个冬天的一次危急关头，科迪先生展现出了他过人的胆魄：在另一位资深首席侦察兵因山川险恶而畏难退缩、不愿涉险的情况下，科迪先生神色自若地主动请缨，单骑穿越危机四伏的凶险敌战区，为谢里登将军星夜兼程送达了紧急绝密公文。
>
> “1869年5月13日，他参加了堪萨斯大象岩（Elephant Rock）的激烈交火，并一路顺藤摸瓜紧紧咬住印第安敌军的踪迹直至16日；最终在内布拉斯加的泉溪（Spring Creek）将残敌再次截获展开激战，我们在3天内狂飙追击了150英里，将敌军彻底击溃肃清。正是在泉溪遭遇战中，科迪率领40名精锐前锋突前在全军前方约3英里处侦察，冷不丁被200名印第安悍匪蜂拥包围。我方战士临危不乱，翻鞍下马结成环形防御圈，一手紧扣战马缰绳，一手顽强开火且战且退。时至今日，当年幸存的将士们谈及科迪当时的沉着从容与惊天勇魄，依然赞不绝口。这伙敌寇正是此前在堪萨斯绑架阿尔德代斯夫人（Mrs. Alderdice）与魏歇尔夫人（Mrs. Weichel）的臭名昭著的‘狗士兵（Dog Soldier）’部落狂徒。这帮暴徒残忍勒死了阿尔德代斯夫人的襁褓幼子，杀害了魏歇尔夫人的丈夫，并四出劫掠了大量平民的财产与牲畜。到了6月28日，我们再度截获了这帮凶徒的踪迹，一路追击了将近200英里，直至1869年7月11日星期日，在顶峰泉（Summit Spring）将这群暴徒彻底合围聚歼。印第安暴徒一见我大军压境，竟凶残地用战斧杀害了阿尔德代斯夫人，并开枪击中了魏歇尔夫人，所幸后者未中要害，被我军成功抢救脱险。
>
> “此后，科迪先生继续在麦克弗森堡出任我的驻地向导兼首席侦察兵，在彼处他屡建殊勋、战功卓著。* * * *
>
> “在1876年夏，科迪随我开赴黑山地区作战，并在彼处英勇击毙了酋长‘黄手’（Yellow-Hand）。随后他又随军转战大角山区与黄石河远征大军。我深切认定，他在追踪、搜寻与剿灭印第安敌寇、从而有力庇护边疆定居拓荒百姓方面，以及为大军勘探指引最便捷通行进军路线方面为国家与军队所立下的汗马功劳，早已远远超越了他所领取的微薄薪饷酬劳。他在第五骑兵团的全体战友伙伴，皆由衷为他如今在事业上的蒸蒸日上与财源广进感到欣喜自豪，并祈愿他福寿绵长、万事亨通。就我个人而言，对于他在我历次戎马征战中所给予的鼎力相助，我铭感五内——那份功勋绝非任何其他人所能够、或是所愿意立下的。我由衷祝福他本人及其全家前程似锦、大吉大利。”
>
> 驻内布拉斯加麦克弗森堡，1878年7月3日
> E·A·卡尔，第五骑兵团陆军中校，美利坚合众国陆军名誉少将（Lt. Col. 5th Cav., Brev. Maj. Gen’l U.

===Original===

S. Army.

Buffalo Bill is now an actor, and is meeting with success. He owns a large and valuable farm adjoining the town of North Platte, Nebraska, and there his family live in ease and comfort. He has also an extensive cattle ranch on the Dismal River, sixty-five miles north of North Platte, his partner being Major Frank North, the old commander of the celebrated Pawnee scouts. While many events of his career are known to the public, yet the reader will find in this narrative much that will be entirely new and intensely interesting to both young and old.
The Publisher.

===Chinese===

> S. Army. / 美利坚合众国陆军名誉少将）

如今，野牛比尔已转型成为一名戏剧演员，并在舞台上大获成功。他在邻近内布拉斯加北普拉特（North Platte）的地界坐拥一座规模宏大、价值连城的庄园农场，一家老小在彼处安居乐业、富足惬意。此外，在北普拉特以北65英里处的凄凉河（Dismal River）畔，他还开辟了一座广袤辽阔的大牧场，他的合伙人正是闻名遐迩的波尼族侦察兵老指挥官弗兰克·诺斯少校（Major Frank North）。尽管他的传奇履历有许多篇章早已广为人知，然而在这部自传长卷中，广大读者必将发现大量此前从未公开披露、足以令各年龄段读者手不释卷、心潮澎湃的全新篇章与真实实录。
—— 出版商 谨志

## X Questionable Proceedings / 第十章 灰色行径
===Original===

Having been away from home nearly a year, and having occasionally heard of my mother’s poor health, I determined to make her a visit; so procuring a pass over the road, I went to Leavenworth, arriving there about June 1st, 1861, going from there home. The civil war had broken out, and excitement ran high in that part of the country. My mother, of course, was a strong Union woman, and had such great confidence in the government that she believed the war would not last over six months.
Leavenworth at that time was quite an important outfitting post for the West and Southwest, and the fort there was garrisoned by a large number of troops. While in the city one day I met several of the old, as well as the young men, who had been members of the Free State party all through the Kansas troubles, and who had, like our family, lost everything at the hands of the Missourians. They now thought a good opportunity offered to retaliate and get even with their persecutors, as they were all considered to be secessionists. That they were all secessionists, however, was not true, as all of them did not sympathize with the South. But the Free State men, myself among them, took it for granted that as Missouri was a slave state the inhabitants must all be secessionists, and therefore our enemies. A man by the name of Chandler proposed that we organize an independent company for the purpose of invading Missouri and making war on its people on our own responsibility. He at once went about it in a very quiet way, and succeeded in inducing twenty-five men to join him in the hazardous enterprise. Having a longing and revengeful desire to retaliate upon the Missourians for the brutal manner in which they had treated and robbed my family, I became a member of Chandler’s company. His plan was that we should leave our homes in parties of not more than two or three together, and meet at a certain point near Westport, Missouri, on a fixed day. His instructions were carried out to the letter, and we met at the rendezvous at the appointed time. Chandler had been there some days before us, and, thoroughly disguised, had been looking around the country for the whereabouts of all the best horses. He directed us to secretly visit certain farms and collect all the horses possible, and bring them together the next night.

===Chinese===

在外漂泊离家将近一年，其间时常听闻母亲身体抱恙欠安，我便横下一条心打算回家探望一番；于是我弄到了一张铁路沿线通行证，赶赴莱文沃思（Leavenworth），大约在1861年6月1日抵达彼处，随后径直奔回家中。彼时南北战争已经轰然爆发，该地区的狂热狂躁与紧张气氛已升腾至顶点。我的母亲自然是一位坚定不移的联邦北军支持者，她对联邦政府怀有无比坚定的信心，笃信这场战事最多不会迁延超过六个月。
当时的莱文沃思是通往西部与西南部极其关键的辎重整备重镇，当地要塞进驻了大量卫戍守备部队。在城里的某一天，我偶遇了几位在当年堪萨斯动乱中自始至终同仇敌忾的自由州党（Free State party）旧友，老少皆有；他们与我们家境遇如出一辙，在当年密苏里暴民的烧杀抢掠下被夺走了一切家当。如今他们认为大好复仇良机已然到来，正是向当年迫害他们的那帮仇敌秋后算账、讨还公道的大好关头，因为在他们看来，那些密苏里人通通都是南部分裂分子（secessionists）。然而，将他们全冠以分裂分子之名并不公允，毕竟并非所有人都在心底同情南军。但当时的自由州党人——包括我自己——却想当然地认定：既然密苏里是个蓄奴州，那里的居民便必然个个是分裂叛党，因而理所当然都是我们的死敌。一位名叫钱德勒（Chandler）的男子提议，由我们私自组建一支独立游击民兵连，自作主张越界杀入密苏里境内，向当地人展开报复作战。他随即在暗地里悄无声息地紧锣密鼓张罗起来，成功招揽了25名亡命汉子入伙参与这一项凶险非凡的秘密勾当。因当年密苏里暴民对我全家的残暴毒打与洗劫掠夺，我心头早已淤积了刻骨铭心的复仇怒火与报复渴望，于是我欣然入伙成为了钱德勒连队的一员。他的全盘谋划是：全员化整为零，每组不得超过两三人悄悄离开家门，约定在某一特定日期于密苏里韦斯特波特（Westport）附近的某处隐秘据点集结汇合。他的严令得到了不折不扣的严格执行，我们在指定时间如期抵达了集结点。钱德勒在此前几天便已先期潜伏抵达，乔装改扮在乡野间四出游弋窥探，摸清了当地所有最上等良驹的具体圈养马厩。他下达密令，命我们在夜幕掩护下秘密摸进特定的农庄庄园，尽可能大肆搜罗搜刮所有良马，并在次日深夜前将所有战利品统统赶至一处集中。

===Original===

This we did, and upon reassembling it was found that nearly every man had two horses. We immediately struck out for the Kansas line, which we crossed at an Indian ferry on the Kansas River, above Wyandotte, and as soon as we had set foot upon Kansas soil we separated with the understanding; that we were to meet one week from that day at Leavenworth.
Some of the parties boldly took their confiscated horses into Leavenworth, while others rode them to their homes. This action may look to the reader like horse-stealing, and some people might not hesitate to call it by that name; but Chandler plausibly maintained that we were only getting back our own, or the equivalent, from the Missourians, and as the government was waging war against the South, it was perfectly square and honest, and we had a good right to do it. So we didn’t let our consciences trouble us very much.
We continued to make similar raids upon the Missourians off and on during the summer, and occasionally we had running fights with them; none of the skirmishes, however, amounting to much.
The government officials hearing of our operations, put detectives upon our track, and several of the party were arrested. My mother, upon learning that I was engaged in this business, told me it was neither honorable nor right, and she would not for a moment countenance any such proceedings. Consequently I abandoned the jay-hawking enterprise, for such it really was.
About this time the government bought from Jones and Cartwright several ox-trains, which were sent to Rolla, Missouri, all being put in charge of my old and gallant friend, Wild Bill, who had just become the hero of the day, on account of a terrible fight which he had had with a gang of desperadoes and outlaws, who infested the border under the leadership of the then notorious Jake McCandless. In this fight he had killed McCandless and three of his men.
The affair occurred while Wild Bill was riding the pony express in western Kansas.
The custom with the express riders, when within half a mile of a station, was either to begin shouting or blowing a horn in order to notify the stock tender of his approach, and to have a fresh horse already saddled for him on his arrival, so that he could go right on without a moment’s delay.

===Chinese===

我们依计而行，待到次夜全员再度集结时，几乎人手牵着两匹战马（2匹马）。我们片刻不敢耽搁，立即启程狂奔直扑堪萨斯边界；我们在怀恩多特（Wyandotte）上游借由堪萨斯河（Kansas River）上的一处印第安渡口成功渡河。双脚刚一踏上堪萨斯的土地，大伙儿便按事先商定分道扬镳散去，约定自该日起整整一周（1周）后在莱文沃思重新碰头。
队伍里的某些弟兄竟大摇大摆地将这些“充公罚没”的战利良马公然骑进莱文沃思城里，另一些人则大张旗鼓骑回了自己的老家。对于诸位看官而言，这一行径难免有大肆偷马盗窃之嫌，某些卫道士甚至会毫不迟疑地将其斥为偷马盗马贼；然而钱德勒却振振有词地极力辩解道：我们不过是从密苏里强盗手里讨回属于我们自己的血汗家当，或是等额的赔偿与补偿；既然当下联邦政府正大举同南方交战，这种行径便显得完全名正言顺、光明磊落，我们完全有充分的道义与权利放手施为。于是在这番说辞之下，我们心底的那点良心谴责很快便烟消云散了。
在那年夏季的数月里，我们断断续续对密苏里境内发起过多次类似的越界劫掠破袭，其间偶尔也会同闻讯追来的密苏里人爆发疾驰交火；不过所有这些小打小闹的摩擦与遭遇战，倒也未曾酿成过什么惊天风浪。
然而，联邦政府官员风闻了我们的越界勾当之后，立刻派出大批暗探密探顺藤摸瓜追踪我们的行踪，连队里有几名弟兄甚至当场锒铛入狱。母亲在获悉我竟然卷入这等见不得人的勾当之后，严厉训诫我说，这种行径既不体面亦不正派，她无论如何也绝不会纵容默许任何此等作派。听闻母亲的教诲，我当即洗手不干，彻底退出了这桩“杰霍克游击大盗（jay-hawking）”营生——说到底，它的实质无非就是巧取豪夺。
正是在那一时期，联邦军方从琼斯与卡特赖特公司（Jones and Cartwright）手中紧急采购了数支牛车队运往密苏里罗拉（Rolla），并全权交由我那位英勇无畏的老战友狂野比尔统领掌管。而此时的狂野比尔刚刚在边陲名声大噪，成为了妇孺皆知的当红大英雄——起因是他在不久前，刚刚与一伙盘踞在边境地界横行霸道、受恶名昭彰的杰克·麦坎德莱斯（Jake McCandless，史称麦坎莱斯）统领的亡命匪帮爆发了一场惊天地泣鬼神的殊死喋血搏杀。在这场恶战中，比尔单枪匹马格杀了麦坎德莱斯本人及其麾下的3名悍匪。
这场惨烈血战发生在狂野比尔出任西堪萨斯快马驿道骑士（pony express）期间。
当时快马骑士有一条不成文的行规：当奔驰至距离驿站尚有半英里（0.5英里）之遥时，骑士便需扯开嗓门大声呼号或吹响号角，以此提前警示通知守马工役，好让对方在自己飞马赶抵之际，便将早已套好鞍鞯的换乘生力骏马牵拉就位，以便骑士无需片刻耽搁即可翻鞍换乘、疾驰而去。

===Original===

One day, as Wild Bill neared Rock Creek station, where he was to change horses, he began shouting as usual at the proper distance; but the stock-tender, who had been married only a short time and had his wife living with him at the station, did not make his accustomed appearance. Wild Bill galloped up and instead of finding the stock-tender ready for him with a fresh horse, he discovered him lying across the stable door with the blood oozing from a bullet-hole in his head. The man was dead, and it was evident that he had been killed only a few moments before.
In a second Wild Bill jumped from his horse, and looking in the direction of the house he saw a man coming towards him. The approaching man fired on him at once, but missed his aim. Quick as lightning Wild Bill pulled his revolver and returned the fire. The stranger fell dead, shot through the brain.
“Bill, Bill! Help! Help! save me!” Such was the cry that Bill now heard. It was the shrill and pitiful voice of the dead stock-tender’s wife, and it came from a window of the house. She had heard the exchange of shots, and knew that Wild Bill had arrived.
He dashed over the dead body of the villain whom he had killed, and just as he sprang into the door of the house, he saw two powerful men assaulting the woman. One of the desperadoes was in the act of striking her with the butt end of a revolver, and while his arm was still raised, Bill sent a ball crashing through his skull, killing him instantly. Two other men now came rushing from an adjoining room, and Bill, seeing that the odds were three to one against him, jumped into a corner, and then firing, he killed another of the villains. Before he could shoot again the remaining two men closed in upon him, one of whom had drawn a large bowie knife. Bill wrenched the knife from his grasp and drove it through the heart of the outlaw.
The fifth and last man now grabbed Bill by the throat, and held him at arm’s length, but it was only for a moment, as Bill raised his own powerful right arm and struck his antagonist’s left arm such a terrible blow that he broke it. The disabled desperado, seeing that he was no longer a match for Bill, jumped through the door, and mounting a horse he succeeded in making his escape﻿—being the sole survivor of the Jake McCandless gang.

===Chinese===

有一天，当狂野比尔策马接近他预定换乘坐骑的石溪驿站（Rock Creek station）时，他一如往常在适宜的距离高声呼喊报信；然而驿站的养马杂工却并未如期牵着生力坐骑出门迎候。比尔翻鞍跃下战马，一脚踹开马厩的大门，赫然发现里面一片死寂空无一人。他旋即大步流星直奔驿站看守的屋舍，只见大门敞开，屋内隐隐传来妇人凄厉绝望的惨叫呼救声。他几步冲到房门前，眼前的一幕令他怒发冲冠：两个身形魁梧壮硕的恶汉正对手无寸铁的妇人施暴凌虐。其中一名亡命歹徒正高高抡起手中的转轮手枪，企图用沉重的枪托狠狠砸向妇人的头颅；就在那歹徒的右臂尚未落下的千钧一发之际，比尔抬手便是一枪，呼啸的铅弹轰然洞穿了那恶棍的头颅，叫他当场毙命倒地。另外两名匪徒闻声从隔壁套间暴怒着猛冲而出；比尔眼见以一敌三、形势险恶，一个凌空侧跃敏捷闪入墙角，抬手又是干脆利落的一枪，将另一名恶徒当场射杀。还没等他来得及扣动扳机射出第三枪，剩下的两名暴徒已如饿虎扑食般贴身扑了上来，其中一人猛然拔出了一柄硕大凶残的鲍伊猎刀（bowie knife）。比尔以迅雷不及掩耳之势凌空反剪扭脱了对方的刀柄，手起刀落，将冰冷锋利的刀刃顺势狠狠捅进了这名亡命徒的心脏。
第五名、也是最后一名暴徒在狂怒中一把死死掐住了比尔的咽喉，将他牢牢顶在手臂能够触及的距离之外；然而这种僵持不过维持了弹指一瞬——比尔悍然抡起自己那只孔武有力的钢铁右拳，对准对手的左臂以雷霆万钧之势猛力一击，当场将对方的臂骨生生砸断。那彻底残废瓦解的暴徒眼见在比尔面前根本不是一合之敌，吓得魂飞魄散，纵身一跃破门而逃，飞身上马抱头鼠窜——他成了杰克·麦坎德莱斯匪帮中唯一侥幸保住狗命的漏网之鱼。

===Original===

Wild Bill remained at the station with the terrified woman until the stage came along, and he then consigned her to the care of the driver. Mounting his horse he at once galloped off, and soon disappeared in the distance, making up for lost time.
This was the exploit that was on everybody’s tongue and in every newspaper. It was one of the most remarkable and desperate hand to hand encounters that has ever taken place on the border.
I happened to meet Wild Bill at Leavenworth as he was about to depart for Rolla; he wished me to take charge of the government trains as a sort of assistant under him, and I gladly accepted the offer. Arriving at Rolla, we loaded the trains with freight and took them to Springfield, Missouri.
On our return to Rolla we heard a great deal of talk about the approaching fall races at St. Louis, and Wild Bill having brought a fast running horse from the mountains, determined to take him to that city and match him against some of the high-flyers there; and down to St. Louis we went with this running horse, placing our hopes very high on him.
Wild Bill had no difficulty in making up a race for him. All the money that he and I had we put up on the mountain runner, and as we thought we had a sure thing, we also bet the horse against $250. I rode the horse myself, but nevertheless, our sure thing, like many another sure thing, proved a total failure, and we came out of that race minus the horse and every dollar we had in the world.
Before the race it had been “make or break” with us, and we got “broke.” We were “busted” in the largest city we had ever been in, and it is no exaggeration to say that we felt mighty blue.
On the morning after the race we went to the military headquarters, where Bill succeeded in securing an engagement for himself as a government scout, but I being so young failed in obtaining similar employment. Wild Bill, however, raised some money, by borrowing it from a friend, and then buying me a steamboat ticket he sent me back to Leavenworth, while he went to Springfield, which place he made his headquarters while scouting in southeastern Missouri.
One night, after he had returned from a scouting expedition, he took a hand in a game of poker, and in the course of the game he became involved in a quarrel with Dave Tutt, a professional gambler, about a watch which he had won from Tutt, who would not give it up.

===Chinese===

狂野比尔一直神色自若地留守在驿站守护着那位惊魂未定的妇人，直至长途载客驿马马车辚辚驶来，他才郑重将妇人托付给马车夫照拂看管。随后他纵身飞跃上马，策马绝尘而去，在疾风中很快消失在地平线的远方，飞驰追赶耽搁下的宝贵时间。
正是这一桩惊天动地的惊天壮举，刹那间传遍了街头巷尾、登上了各大报纸的头版头条。这堪称整个西部边荒开拓史上最令人叹为观止、最为惊心动魄的肉搏死战范例之一。
恰逢此时，我在莱文沃思偶遇了正准备启程奔赴罗拉的狂野比尔；他力邀我出任他的副手助理，协助他掌管统领这批军方货运牛车队，我满心欢喜地欣然接受了他的邀约。抵达罗拉后，我们将车队装载满各类军需辎重，一路押运送抵密苏里斯普林菲尔德（Springfield）。
在折返罗拉的归途中，我们沿途听闻了大量关于即将在圣路易斯隆重举行的秋季赛马盛会的热烈风声。恰巧狂野比尔刚从深山里带回了一匹奔跑如飞的绝世神骏快马，他当即按捺不住跃跃欲试的冲动，决意带上这匹骏马奔赴圣路易斯，同城里的各路名驹悍马一决高下；于是我们满怀着无比狂热与崇高的殷切期望，带着这匹飞毛腿快马一路顺流而下直奔圣路易斯。
狂野比尔毫不费力地为这匹神驹促成了一场豪赌赛马。他与我将身上所有的盘缠积蓄全都押在了这匹山地快马身上；由于我们笃信这场对决十拿九稳、万无一失，我们索性把这匹坐骑本身也当作筹码押了上去，对赌250美元现金。那一天由我亲自翻鞍上阵策马驾驭；然而事与愿违，我们原本笃信的所谓“万全之策”，就像人间无数次破灭的稳赢赌局一样，最终彻底沦为了一场彻头彻尾的灭顶灾难。赛后我们不仅两手空空输掉了这匹宝马，甚至连兜里的最后一个铜板也输得精光。
在这场赛马之前，我们原本抱定的是“不成功便成仁（make or break）”的孤注一掷；结果我们落了个彻彻底底的倾家荡产（broke）。在我们平生所涉足过的最大繁华都市里，我们被彻底洗劫得精光身无分文（busted），毫不夸张地说，我们当时的心情简直是沮丧懊恼到了极点。
在赛马惨败后的翌日清晨，我们硬着头皮前往军区司令部谋求生计；比尔凭着过人名望，顺利为自己谋得了一份联邦政府正式随军侦察兵的美差，而我却因年纪尚轻而未能如愿获得同样的任用。不过狂野比尔仗义疏财，向朋友借了一笔银子解了燃眉之急，随后为我购买了一张返回莱文沃思的蒸汽轮船客票送我登船归乡；而他自己则开拔奔赴斯普林菲尔德，以此作为他在密苏里东南部执行前哨侦察任务的驻军大本营。
在后来的一次侦察出巡归营的某个夜晚，比尔闲来无事凑份子参加了一场扑克牌局；在推杯换盏的牌局交锋中，他与一名职业赌徒戴夫·塔特（Dave Tutt）爆发了激烈口角纷争——起因是比尔在牌局上赢走了塔特的一块贵重怀表，而塔特却恼羞成怒、赖账不肯交出。

===Original===

Bill told him he had won it fairly, and that he proposed to have it; furthermore, he declared his intention of carrying the watch across the street next morning to military headquarters, at which place he had to report at nine o’clock.
Tutt replied that he would himself carry the watch across the street at nine o’clock, and no other man would do it.
Bill then said to Tutt that if he attempted anything of the kind, he would kill him.
A challenge to a duel had virtually been given and accepted, and everybody knew that the two men meant business. At nine o’clock the next morning, Tutt started to cross the street. Wild Bill, who was standing on the opposite side, told him to stop. At that moment Tutt, who was carrying his revolver in his hand, fired at Bill but missed him. Bill quickly pulled out his revolver and returned the fire, hitting Tutt squarely in the forehead and killing him instantly.
Quite a number of Tutt’s friends were standing in the vicinity, having assembled to witness the duel, and Bill, as soon as Tutt fell to the ground, turned to them and asked if any one of them wanted to take it up for Tutt; if so, he would accommodate any of them then and there. But none of them cared to stand in front of Wild Bill to be shot at by him.
Nothing of course was ever done to Bill for the killing of Tutt.

===Chinese===

比尔正告他说，自己是光明正大、公公平平赢来这块怀表的，他无论如何也必须拿到手；不仅如此，比尔更是当众撂下狠话：次日清晨他奉命必须在上午9点前往军区司令部述职报到，届时他定会堂堂正正佩戴着这块怀表大摇大摆横穿大街。
塔特凶相毕露狞笑着反唇相讥道：次日上午9点，佩戴着这块怀表横穿大街的只会是他自己本人，天下绝无第二个人能做到。
比尔面无表情冷冷告诫塔特说：若是他真敢有任何此等企图妄动，自己必定会当场取了他的狗命。
这番针锋相对，实际上等同于双方已正式递下并接下了生死决斗的战书，在场的所有人都心知肚明这两人绝非在虚张声势。次日上午9点整，塔特果然气势汹汹迈步开始横穿街道。傲然伫立在街道对侧的狂野比尔厉声喝令他止步。就在电光石火的这一刹那，手中早已紧握转轮手枪的塔特抢先扣动扳机射向比尔，子弹呼啸擦过却未能命中。比尔以闪电般的神速拔枪反击，这一枪势若雷霆，正正击中了塔特的脑门眉心中央，叫那赌徒当场毙命气绝身亡。
当时在四周聚集驻足了大批塔特的狐朋狗友，正准备亲眼目睹这场街头对决；比尔在塔特轰然倒地血溅当场的瞬间，面不改色冷冷转过身来逼视着这帮同党，从容喝问道：在场的有哪一位想替塔特出头强出头算账吗？若有谁不服，他随时随地乐意奉陪到底。然而面对狂野比尔森严的枪口与摄人心魄的目光，在场的一众恶徒无一人敢上前一步领教他的神准枪法。
对于这桩格杀塔特的街头枪战，军方与地方当局自然从未对比尔追究过半分法律责任。

## XVIII Scouting / 第十八章 荒原侦察
===Original===

On the third day of October the Fifth Cavalry arrived at Fort Hays, and I at once began making the acquaintance of the different officers of the regiment. I was introduced by General Sheridan to Colonel William Royal, who was in command of the regiment. He was a gallant officer, and an agreeable and pleasant gentleman. He is now stationed at Omaha as Inspector General in the department of the Platte. I also became acquainted with Major W. H. Brown, Major Walker, Captain Sweetman, Quartermaster E. M. Hays, and in fact all the officers of the regiment.
General Sheridan, being anxious to punish the Indians who had lately fought General Forsyth, did not give the regiment much of a rest, and accordingly on the 5th of October it began its march for the Beaver Creek country. The first night we camped on the South fork of Big Creek, four miles west of Hays City. By this time I had become pretty well acquainted with Major Brown and Captain Sweetman, who invited me to mess with them on this expedition; and a jolly mess we had. There were other scouts in the command besides myself, and I particularly remember Tom Renahan, Hank Fields and a character called “Nosey” on account of his long nose.
On the morning of the 6th we pulled out to the north, and during the day I was very favorably struck with the appearance of the regiment. It was a beautiful command, and when strung out on the prairie with a train of seventy-five six-mule wagons, ambulances and pack mules, I felt very proud of my position as guide and chief of scouts of such a warlike expedition.
Just as we were about to go into camp on the Saline River that night, we ran on to a band of about fifteen Indians, who, seeing us, dashed across the creek, followed by some bullets which we sent after them; but as the small band proved to be a scouting party, we pursued them only a mile or two, when our attention was directed to a herd of buffaloes﻿—they being very plenty﻿—and we succeeded in killing ten or fifteen for the command.
The next day we marched thirty miles, and late in the afternoon we went into camp on the South fork of the Solomon. At this encampment Colonel Royal asked me to go out and kill some buffaloes for the boys.
“All right, Colonel, send along a wagon or two to bring in the meat,” I said.

===Chinese===

10月3日，第五骑兵团（Fifth Cavalry）进驻海斯堡（Fort Hays），我随即开始同团里的各位军官逐一熟识结交。谢里登将军将我引荐给了当时统领全团的威廉·罗亚尔上校（Colonel William Royal）。他是一位骁勇善战的职业军官，亦是一位温文尔雅、平易近人的正人君子；他如今正驻扎在奥马哈（Omaha），出任普拉特军区总督察长。在此期间，我还结识了W·H·布朗少校（Major W. H. Brown）、沃克少校（Major Walker）、斯威特曼上尉（Captain Sweetman）、军需官E·M·海斯（Quartermaster E. M. Hays）等全团上下几乎所有的军官。
谢里登将军急于惩办惩处不久前在战斗中重创福赛思将军（General Forsyth）的那帮印第安凶徒，并未给骑兵团留下太多休整时间；于是全团在10月5日便拔营启程，开赴海狸溪（Beaver Creek）一带展开大搜剿。第一天夜晚，我们在海斯城以西4英里的大溪南汊（South fork of Big Creek）畔扎营。此时我已经同布朗少校和斯威特曼上尉混得极熟，他们热情邀请我在此次远征中加入他们的军官伙食班搭伙；我们的饭桌上始终充满了欢声笑语。除了我之外，这支大军中还配备了其他几名侦察兵，其中令我记忆犹新的有汤姆·雷纳汉（Tom Renahan）、汉克·菲尔兹（Hank Fields），以及一名因生着大长鼻子而被大伙儿唤作“长鼻公（Nosey）”的怪杰。
10月6日清晨，大队人马开拔向北挺进。在当天的行军中，这支精锐铁骑的威武军容给我留下了极佳的深刻印象。这是一支极具战斗力的铁甲雄师：当长达75辆六骡大车组成的辎重车队、野战救护车与成群的骡马驮队在辽阔平原上迤逦展开时，身为这支铁血远征大军的首席先锋向导兼总侦察兵，我不由得心头升腾起无比的自豪与荣耀。
当天傍晚正当我们准备在萨林河（Saline River）畔扎营之际，冷不丁迎头撞上了一股约有15名印第安人的敌骑武装；对方一见我军大队，立刻拍马横渡小溪仓皇逃窜，我们随手朝他们射出了一排排呼啸的子弹。不过经查探，这批小股散骑显然只是敌方的警戒前哨，我们策马追逐了一两英里便勒马回阵，因为大伙儿的视线很快被旷野上一大群漫山遍野的野牛群所吸引——当时那里的野牛极其充裕密集——我们一口气猎杀了10到15头肥壮野牛，满载而归犒劳全军官兵。
次日我们兼程行军30英里，傍晚时分在所罗门河南汊（South fork of the Solomon）安营扎寨。在这座营地里，罗亚尔上校特意嘱托我出营猎几头野牛给弟兄们改善伙食。
“没问题，上校，请您派上一两辆大车随行，好把牛肉运回来，”我应声道。

===Original===

“I am not in the habit of sending out my wagons until I know that there is something to be hauled in; kill your buffalo first and then I’ll send out the wagons,” was the Colonel’s reply. I said no more, but went out on a hunt, and after a short absence returned and asked the Colonel to send his wagons over the hill for the half dozen buffaloes I had killed.
The following afternoon he again requested me to go out and get some fresh buffalo meat. I didn’t ask him for any wagons this time, but rode out some distance, and coming up with a small herd, I managed to get seven of them headed straight for the encampment, and instead of shooting them just then, I ran them at full speed right into the camp, and then killed them all, one after the other in rapid succession. Colonel Royal witnessed the whole proceeding, which puzzled him somewhat, as he could see no reason why I had not killed them on the prairie. He came up, rather angrily, and demanded an explanation. “I can’t allow any such business as this, Cody,” said he, “what do you mean by it?”
“I didn’t care about asking for any wagons this time, Colonel; so I thought I would make the buffaloes furnish their own transportation,” was my reply. The Colonel saw the point in a moment, and had no more to say on the subject.
No Indians had been seen in the vicinity during the day, and Colonel Royal having carefully posted his pickets, supposed everything was serene for the night. But before morning we were roused from our slumbers by hearing shots fired, and immediately afterwards one of the mounted pickets came galloping into camp, saying that there were Indians close at hand. The companies all fell into line, and were soon prepared and anxious to give the redskins battle; but as the men were yet new in the Indian country a great many of them were considerably excited. No Indians, however, made their appearance, and upon going to the picket-post where the picket said he had seen them, none could be found nor could any traces of them be discovered. The sentinel﻿—who was an Irishman﻿—insisted that there certainly had been redskins there.
“But you must be mistaken,” said Colonel Royal.
“Upon me sowl, Colonel, I’m not; as shure ez me name’s Pat Maloney, one of thim rid divils hit me on the head wid a club, so he did,” said Pat; and so, when morning came, the mystery was further investigated and was easily solved.

===Chinese===

“在没见到实实在在拉回营的猎物之前，我可没有先派出辎重大车的习惯；你先打倒野牛，我再派车不迟，”上校不假思索地回敬道。我没再多费唇舌，单枪匹马出营寻猎；没过多久便策马折返，请上校派车翻过山梁，去拉我打倒的半打（6头）野牛。
翌日下午，上校再次嘱咐我出营猎取一些新鲜野牛肉。这一回我压根儿没向上校开口借用任何货车，而是径直策马驰出营地老远；当寻获一小群野牛时，我巧妙地施展驭牛身手，硬是将其中7头野牛驱赶得笔直冲着军营方向狂奔；我当时并没有开枪击毙它们，而是催动战马在后方狂追疾驰，将这7头庞然大物以最高速度直接赶入了军营大阵之中，随后在全军众目睽睽之下抬手连连击发，干净利落、枪枪毙命将它们接二连三尽数射杀。罗亚尔上校亲眼目睹了这神乎其神的全过程，一时间被惊得目瞪口呆又百思不得其解，完全想不通我为何不在旷野中早早击毙它们。他甚至带着几分愠色快步走上前来质问缘由：“科迪，军营重地我可绝不容许你这般胡来！你这究竟是唱的哪一出？”
“报告上校，这一回我实在不好意思再劳烦您派车接应，所以我想索性让野牛自己给自己‘充当运输工具’送货上门，”我气定神闲地答道。上校瞬间恍然大悟、心领神会，对此再无半句微词。
白天那一带并未发现任何印第安人的活动踪迹，罗亚尔上校在四周严密部署了前沿岗哨，自以为当夜定能高枕无忧、万事大吉。然而天还未亮，我们便被一阵急促的枪声从睡梦中惊醒；紧接着，一名骑马哨兵神色慌张地飞马疾驰冲入军营，高呼附近有印第安敌军大举逼近。各个连队迅速整队荷枪列阵，全员严阵以待、群情激昂准备与红种人决一死战；但因官兵大多初涉印第安前线敌区，不少新兵难免心跳加速、神情高度亢奋。然而四周一片死寂，并未见到任何敌影；待众人赶赴那名哨兵坚称发现敌情的哨位勘探时，不仅搜寻不到半个敌人，甚至连一丝马蹄足迹也未曾留下。那名哨兵——是个爱尔兰汉子——却一口咬定绝对有红皮魔鬼在现场出没。
“但你八成是看花眼看错了，”罗亚尔上校说道。
“向我的灵魂发誓，上校大人，我绝没撒谎；若我叫帕特·马洛尼（Pat Maloney）的名字千真万确，刚才就当真有个红皮魔鬼狠狠拿木棍砸了我的脑门一下，千真万确砸中了！”帕特赌咒发誓道；等到晨光熹微天色放晴之际，军官们进一步勘验侦查，真相才终于水落石出、真相大白。

===Original===

Elk tracks were found in the vicinity and it was undoubtedly a herd of elks that had frightened Pat; as he had turned to run, he had gone under a limb of a tree, against which he hit his head, and supposed he had been struck by a club in the hands of an Indian. It was hard to convince Pat however, of the truth.
A three days uninteresting march brought us to Beaver Creek where we camped and from which point scouting parties were sent out in different directions. Neither of these parties discovering Indians they all returned to camp about the same time, finding it in a state of great excitement, it having been attacked a few hours previous by a party of Indians, who had succeeded in killing two men and in making off with sixty horses belonging to Co. H.
That evening the command started on the trail of these Indian horse-thieves; Major Brown with two companies and three days rations pushing ahead in advance of the main command. Being unsuccessful, however, in overtaking the Indians, and getting nearly out of provisions﻿—it being our eighteenth day out, the entire command marched towards the nearest railroad point, and camped on the Saline River; distant three miles from Buffalo Tank.
While waiting for supplies we received a new commanding officer, Brevet Major-General E. A. Carr, who was the senior major of the regiment, and who ranked Colonel Royal. He brought with him the now celebrated Forsyth scouts, who were commanded by Lieutenant Pepoon, a regular army officer.
It was also while waiting in this camp that Major Brown received a new lieutenant to fill a vacancy in his company. On the day that this officer was to arrive, Major Brown had his private ambulance brought out, and invited me to accompany him to the railroad station to meet his lieutenant, whose name was A. B. Bache. He proved to be a fine gentleman, and a brave, dashing officer. On the way to the depot Major Brown had said, “Now, Cody, when we come back we’ll give Bache a lively ride and shake him up a little.”
Major Brown was a jolly good fellow, but sometimes he would get “a little off,” and as this was one of his “off days” he was bound to amuse himself in some original and mischievous way. Reaching the depot just as the train came in, we easily found the Lieutenant, and giving him the back seat in the ambulance we were soon headed for camp.

===Chinese===

哨位四周被勘验出了密密麻麻的加拿大马鹿（Elk）蹄印，显而易见是惊慌失措的马鹿群吓着了帕特；当他惊慌失措转身狂奔逃窜时，脑袋不偏不倚撞在了一根横生的坚硬树权上，便疑神疑鬼地认定自己是被印第安人抡起的沉重木棍迎头痛击了一记。不过要想让帕特彻底信服这个大白真相，着实费了大伙儿好一番口舌。
随后经过3天平淡无奇的单调行军，大军开拔抵达了海狸溪（Beaver Creek），全军在河畔安营扎寨，并以此处为大本营四出巡逻侦察。罗亚尔上校命我前出深入侦察，我带着两名弟兄顺着小溪下游策马搜索巡逻，在距离军营约25英里处，我们蓦然截获了一条规模巨大的印第安大军迁徙通道；这道新鲜足迹显示，大批印第安敌寇正顺着小溪逆流而上挺进。我当即飞马折回大营通报军情，罗亚尔上校随即派出两支骑兵连队前往侦察截击，在小溪下游同印第安人爆发了一场激战。
大军在海狸溪巡弋了数日，随后开拔转战短期驻扎在短草溪（Short Grass Creek），并在此与卡尔将军（General Carr）的大队人马胜利会师，卡尔将军当即正式接掌了全团最高指挥权。将军麾下随行带来了野战救护车以及由野战医生巴切博士（Dr. Louis W. Bache）掌管的医疗队。
会师当晚，卡尔将军在与罗亚尔上校详细商讨战局后，下令在拂晓破晓时分全军拔营开进。天色刚蒙蒙亮，我和其他侦察兵便早早翻鞍上马，前出斥候开路。大军全速行军推进，傍晚时分进驻大平原深处的一处山泉旁安营扎寨。当夜全军接获严令：由于我们已迫近印第安大军老巢，次日拂晓必须在绝对肃静中展开轻装急行军。
次日清晨大军开拔，我和随行的侦察兵兄弟们突前数英里侦察开路，在翻越一道山脊后，再度截获了一条大约一两天前留下的密集敌军踪迹。大军日夜兼程沿踪追击，当晚在小溪源头附近扎营歇脚。那一夜，布朗少校特意邀请巴切医生和我钻进他的野战救护车里挤一挤避风宿夜。

===Original===

Pretty soon Major Brown took the reins from his driver, and at once began whipping the mules. After getting them into a lively gallop he pulled out his revolver and fired several shots. The road was terribly rough and the night was so dark that we could hardly see where we were going. It was a wonderful piece of luck that we were not tipped over and our necks broken. Finally Bache said, good-humoredly:
“Is this the way you break in all your Lieutenants, Major?”
“Oh, no; I don’t do this as a regular thing, but it’s the way we frequently ride in this country,” said the Major; “just keep your seat, Mr. Bache, and we’ll take you through on time.” The Major appropriated the reply of the old California stage driver, Hank Monk, to Horace Greely.
We were now rattling down a steep hill at full speed, and just as we reached the bottom, the front wheels struck a deep ditch over which the mules had jumped. We were all brought up standing by the sudden stoppage of the ambulance. Major Brown and myself were nearly pitched out on the wheels, while the Lieutenant came flying headlong from the back seat to the front of the vehicle.
“Take a back seat, Lieutenant,” coolly said Major Brown.
“Major, I have just left that seat,” said Bache.
We soon lifted the wagon out of the ditch, and then resumed our drive, running into camp under full headway, and creating considerable amusement. Everyone recognized the ambulance and knew at once that Major Brown and I were out on a “lark,” and therefore there was not much said about our exploit. Halting with a grand flourish in front of his tent, Major Brown jumped out in his most gallant style and politely asked his lieutenant in. A very pleasant evening was spent there, quite a number of the officers calling to make the acquaintance of the new officer, who entertained the visitors with an amusing account of the ride from the depot.
Next morning at an early hour, the command started out on a hunt for Indians. General Carr having a pretty good idea where he would be most likely to find them, directed me to guide him by the nearest route to Elephant Rock on Beaver Creek.
Upon arriving at the south fork of the Beaver on the second day’s march, we discovered a large, fresh Indian trail which we hurriedly followed for a distance of eight miles, when suddenly we saw on the bluffs ahead of us, quite a large number of Indians.

===Chinese===

不多时，布朗少校从他的马车夫手中一把夺过缰绳，开始挥舞马鞭疯狂抽打那几头挽骡。把骡子催逼得四蹄腾空狂飙飞驰之后，他甚至掏出随身转轮手枪，冲着夜空接连怒射了好几枪。山路崎岖坎坷、险象环生，夜色漆黑如墨伸手不见五指，大伙儿连马车正冲向何方都浑然看不真切。在那种亡命狂奔下，马车没有彻底掀翻打滚摔断我们几个人的脖子，简直堪称天大的奇迹侥幸。最后巴切医生在颠簸中苦笑着打趣道：“少校，你这出狂飙飞车演得着实精彩过瘾，但要是你再不开恩放缓车速，咱们哥几个今夜恐怕没等撞上印第安人，就先得全军覆没把命交待在这马车里了！”在众人的苦劝下，少校这才意犹未尽地勒住了马缰。
次日破晓黎明，全军在拂晓微光中再度挥师疾进，我们沿着那条足印追踪了约五英里后，足迹突然折向西北。我突前在全军前方约一英里处侦察，在登上又一座高坡后，赫然望见顺着海狸溪下游方向升腾起遮天蔽日的滚滚尘柱，这分明显示前方的印第安大队人马正处于狂奔逃窜之中。我立刻打马折返回去通报卡尔将军；将军闻讯当即下令全团加快行军步伐展开全速追击。
海狸溪两岸的地势极其崎岖坎坷，密布着无数深不见底的冲沟与陡峭绝壁；由于必须等待后方载运弹药的辎重车队艰难跟进翻山越岭，全军的追击速度被迫大幅放缓。不久之后，敌方的散骑后卫开始在远方的山头频繁露头若隐若现，显然正拼死拖延时间，掩护他们庞大的老弱家眷大队与行军村落转移撤退。

===Original===

General Carr ordered Lieutenant Pepoon’s scouts and Company M to the front. This company was commanded by Lieutenant Schinosky, a Frenchman by birth and a reckless daredevil by nature, who was anxious to have a hair-lifting match. Having advanced his company nearly a mile ahead of the main command, about four hundred Indians suddenly charged down upon him and gave him a lively little fight, until he was supported by our full force.
The Indians kept increasing in numbers all the while until it was estimated that we were fighting from eight hundred to one thousand of them. The engagement became quite general, and several were killed and wounded on each side. The Indians were evidently fighting to give their families and village, a chance to get away. We had undoubtedly surprised them with a larger force than they had expected to see in that part of the country. We fought them until dark, all the time driving them before us. At night they annoyed us considerably by firing down into our camp from the higher hills, and several times the command was ordered out to dislodge them from their position and drive them back.
After having returned from one of these little sallies, Major Brown, Captain Sweetman, Lieutenant Bache and myself were taking supper together, when “whang!” came a bullet into Lieutenant Bache’s plate, breaking a hole through it. The bullet came from the gun of one of the Indians, who had returned to the high bluff overlooking our camp. Major Brown declared it was a crack shot, because it broke the plate. We finished our supper without having any more such close calls.
At daylight next morning we struck out on the trail, and soon came to the spot where the Indians had camped the day before. We could see that their village was a very large one, consisting of about five hundred lodges; and we pushed forward rapidly from this point on the trail which ran back toward Prairie Dog Creek.
About two o’clock we came in sight of the retreating village, and soon the warriors turned back to give us battle. They set fire to the prairie grass in front of us, and on all sides, in order to delay us as much as possible.

===Chinese===

卡尔将军当机立断，命令佩彭中尉（Lieutenant Pepoon）麾下的专业侦察兵队与M连火速突前出击。M连由希诺斯基中尉（Lieutenant Schinosky）统领，这位中尉生于法国，天生是个不折不扣、胆大妄为的亡命狂徒，时刻渴望同印第安凶徒来一场刺刀见红的剥头皮对决。当他率领本连前冲突前至距主力大队将近一英里处时，大约400名印第安悍骑突然从四面八方的山头上呼啸杀下，双方瞬间陷入了异常激烈的殊死交火搏杀之中；直至全军主力闻讯如潮水般席卷增援而至，印第安人才被迫狼狈败退。
卡尔将军随即下达了全线追击令。这支印第安部落是臭名昭著的苏族大酋长“高牛（Tall Bull）”统领的夏延族精锐武装，为了掩护他们的妇孺与宿营行头转移，敌军的散骑在后方组织起数道防线，与我们展开了寸土必争的顽强阻击。

===Original===

We kept up a running fight for the remainder of the afternoon, and the Indians repeatedly attempted to lead us off the track of their flying village, but their trail was easily followed, as they were continually dropping teepee poles, camp kettles, robes, furs and all heavy articles belonging to them. They were evidently scattering, and it finally became difficult for us to keep on the main trail. When darkness set in, we went into camp, it being useless to try to follow the Indians after nightfall.
Next morning we were again on the trail, which led north, and back towards the Beaver Creek, which stream it crossed within a few miles of the spot where we had first discovered the Indians, they having made nearly a complete circle, in hopes of misleading us. Late in the afternoon, we again saw them going over a hill far ahead of us, and towards evening the main body of warriors came back and fought us once more; but we continued to drive them until darkness set in, when we camped for the night.
The Indians soon scattered in every direction, but we followed the main trail to the Republican River, where we made a cutoff, and then went north towards the Platte River. We found, however, that the Indians by traveling night and day had got a long start, and the General concluded that it was useless to follow them any further, as we had pushed them so hard, and given them such a scare that they would leave the Republican country and go north across the Union Pacific railroad. Most of the Indians, as he had predicted, did cross the Platte River, near Ogallala, on the Union Pacific, and thence continued northward.
That night we returned to the Republican River and camped in a grove of cottonwoods, which I named Carr’s Grove, in honor of the commanding officer.
The General told me that the next day’s march would be towards the headwaters of the Beaver, and he asked me the distance. I replied that it was about twenty-five miles, and he said we would make it the next day. Getting an early start in the morning, we struck out across the prairie, my position as guide being ahead of the advance guard. About two o’clock General Carr overtook me, and asked how far I supposed it was to water. I thought it was about eight miles, although we could see no sign or indication of any stream in our front.

===Chinese===

在整个下午的剩余时光里，我们与敌军展开了激烈的边追边打疾驰交火；印第安人屡次企图施展诡计，用小股骑兵将我们引离他们正在亡命撤退的游牧村落主力大队，然而他们的逃亡足迹实在太明显不过了——在慌不择路的溃逃中，他们沿途不断狼狈丢弃着支撑圆锥帐篷（teepee）的长木杆、行军铁壶铁锅、兽皮毛毯以及一切沉重累赘的家当物件。显然敌人正在漫山遍野四散分流奔逃，到了日暮时分，要想继续牢牢咬住敌军主力踏出的主干行军道已变得异常棘手艰难。
夜幕沉沉降临，全军被迫停止追击，在海狸溪畔一处风景如画的开阔林间扎营宿夜。为了向我们的前线总指挥官致敬，我们特意将这处宿营地命名为“卡尔林地（Carr’s Grove）”。
将军在营帐中对我交代说，次日的追击进军将直指海狸溪的源头地带，并询问我两地相距的路程。我据实回答说大约有25英里之遥，将军随即下令次日全军务必推进跋涉完这段路程。翌日拂晓全军起拔开拔，大队人马在旷野大平原上风驰电掣推进，我的向导战位突前在先锋部队的最前沿。下午两点前后，卡尔将军策马追赶上了我，关切地询问我估计再走多远才能寻觅到水源。我极目远眺，虽然正前方的大平原上望不见任何水流河流的蛛丝马迹，但我断定大约再行进8英里便能迎刃而解。

===Original===

“Pepoon’s scouts say that you are going in the wrong direction,” said the General, “and in the way you are bearing it will be fifteen miles before you can strike any of the branches of the Beaver; and that when you do, you will find no water, for the Beavers are dry at this time of the year at that point.”
“General, I think the scouts are mistaken,” said I, “for the Beaver has more water near its head than it has below; and at the place where we will strike the stream we will find immense beaver dams, large enough and strong enough to cross the whole command, if you wish.”
“Well, Cody, go ahead,” said he, “I’ll leave it to you, but remember that I don’t want a dry camp.”
“No danger of that,” said I, and then I rode on, leaving him to return to the command. As I had predicted, we found water seven or eight miles further on, where we came upon a beautiful little stream﻿—a tributary of the Beaver﻿—hidden in the hills. We had no difficulty in selecting a good halting place, and obtaining fresh spring water and excellent grass. The General, upon learning from me that the stream﻿—which was only eight or nine miles long﻿—had no name, took out his map and located it, and named it Cody’s Creek, which name it still bears.
We pulled out early next morning for the Beaver, and when we were approaching the stream I rode on ahead of the advance guard, in order to find a crossing. Just as I turned a bend of the creek, “bang!” went a shot, and down went my horse﻿—myself with him. I disentangled myself, and jumped behind the dead body. Looking in the direction whence the shot had come, I saw two Indians, and at once turned my gun loose on them, but in the excitement of the moment I missed my aim. They fired two or three more shots, and I returned the compliment, wounding one of their horses.
On the opposite side of the creek, going over the hill, I observed a few lodges moving rapidly away, and also some mounted warriors, who could see me, and who kept blazing away with their guns. The two Indians who had fired at me and had killed my horse were retreating across the creek on a beaver dam. I sent a few shots after them to accelerate their speed, and also fired at the ones on the other side of the stream. I was undecided as to whether it was best to run back to the command on foot or hold my position.

===Chinese===

“佩彭麾下的斥候们却异口同声说你领错了进军方向，”将军神色凝重地告诫我道，“按照你眼下的行进方位，至少还得再苦撑十五英里（15英里）才能撞上海狸溪的任何支流；更要命的是，即便赶到了那里也决计找不到半滴水，因为在一年的这个干旱时节，海狸溪在那一区域早就彻底干涸断流了。”
“将军，我想那帮斥候绝对是判断失误了，”我斩钉截铁胸有成竹地答道，“因为海狸溪靠近源头的上游水系，水量远比下游干流要丰沛充盈得多；在我们即将切入该水系的河段，更是筑有无数道庞大坚固的海狸水坝，水坝既宽广又结实，若是您乐意，甚至足以供全军大队人马稳稳当当横跨渡河。”
“那好，科迪，放手前行吧，”将军权衡之下拍板决断，“这件事全权托付给你来定夺，但切记：我可决不想让全军在缺水的旱地荒原上扎营受渴。”
“请将军放宽心，绝无这种凶险！”我应声策马前行，将军则折返归阵督军。正如我此前所料，全军向前艰难推进了七八英里（7到8英里）之后，清冽甘甜的水源赫然出现在眼前——一条隐匿在山峦深谷之间的清澈小溪迎面而来，这正是海狸溪的一条丰沛支流。我们毫不费力便物色到了一处绝佳的驻足扎营地，大军饱饮了甘冽的山泉，战马在丰茂的草甸上开怀大嚼。当将军得知这条长仅八九英里（8到9英里）的美丽溪流迄今尚无正式地名时，特意从怀中取出军用军用地图，在图上郑重标定了方位，并当场将其命名为“科迪溪（Cody’s Creek）”——直至今日，这条溪流在地图上依旧沿用着这个光荣的名字。
翌日破晓，全军整装开拔奔赴海狸溪干流。当大军迫近水系之际，我只身拍马突前在先锋前哨之前，意在寻找一处适宜大军横渡的浅滩渡口。就在我刚转过小溪的一处河湾时，只听得“砰！”的一声清脆枪响，我的坐骑应声轰然中弹倒地——连带着我一同狠狠摔翻在泥水尘土中。我敏捷地挣脱马镫，一跃翻身滚入死马尸躯之后作掩体。顺着枪响射来的方向举目望去，只见两名印第安敌骑正冷冷端枪对准此处；我立刻举枪向对方连连还击，但在翻滚起身的仓促亢奋之中，这几枪未能命中目标。敌骑随之又射出两三枪，我也毫不客气地举枪予以回敬，一枪击伤了其中一名敌寇的坐骑。
在小溪对岸翻越山坡的地界上，我敏锐地瞥见了几顶正仓皇收拾、急速逃遁转移的印第安圆锥营帐，另有几名骑马的武装勇士正遥遥望见我，端起步枪朝我所隐蔽的方位接连开火射击。那两名刚才开枪击倒我战马的印第安人，正仓皇沿着一座海狸水坝向对岸撤退渡河。我抬手朝他们追射了几发子弹以“加快他们逃亡的步伐”，同时调转枪口向对岸山坡上的残敌猛烈射击。一时间我左右权衡、委决不下：究竟是应当赤足徒步往回狂奔投奔主力大军，还是应当在阵地上死守周旋？

===Original===

I knew that within a few minutes the troops would come up, and if they heard the firing they would come rapidly.
The Indians, seeing that I was alone, turned and charged down the hill, and were about to re-cross the creek to corral me, when the advance guard of the command put in an appearance on the ridge, and dashed forward to my rescue. The redskins whirled and made off.
When General Carr came up, he ordered Company I to go in pursuit of the band. I accompanied Lieutenant Brady, who commanded, and we had a running fight with the Indians, lasting several hours. We captured several head of their horses and most of their lodges. At night we returned to the command, which by this time had crossed the creek on the beaver dam.
We scouted for several days along the river, and had two or three lively skirmishes. Finally our supplies began to run low, and General Carr gave orders to return to Fort Wallace, which we reached three days afterwards, and where we remained several days.
While the regiment was waiting here for orders, I spent most of the time in hunting buffaloes, and one day while I was out with a small party, we were “jumped” by about fifty Indians. We had a severe fight of at least an hour, when we succeeded in driving the enemy. They lost four of their warriors, and probably concluded that we were a hard crowd. I had some excellent marksmen with me, and they did some fine work, sending the bullets thick and fast where they would do the most good. Two or three of our horses had been hit, and one man had been wounded; we were ready and willing to stay with the redskins as long as they wished﻿—but they finally gave it up however, as a bad job, and rode off. We finished our hunt, and went back to the post loaded down with plenty of buffalo meat, and received the compliments of the General for our little fight.

===Chinese===

我心头十分笃定：不出数分钟大军先锋必将赶到近前；只要他们闻听前方激烈的交火枪声，推进的马蹄声定会疾如雷霆。
印第安人眼见我孤身一人落单受困，索性调转马头从山坡上疯狂俯冲而下，正准备重新杀过小溪将我彻底合围斩杀；千钧一发之际，大军的先锋警戒部队如天降神兵般骤然出现在山脊地平线上，旋即如离弦之箭般策马狂飙冲下山谷前来解围驰援。那伙红种暴徒见状大惊失色，急忙拨转马头狼狈四散奔逃。
卡尔将军率主力赶到后，当即命令I连展开全速追击。我随同该连指挥官布雷迪中尉（Lieutenant Brady）一同追击，同敌军展开了长达数小时的激战交火。我们成功缴获收缴了敌方的大批战马战利品，并捣毁焚毁了他们绝大部分的游牧帐篷。夜幕低垂之际我们凯旋折返回营，此时全军主力早已借由海狸水坝顺利全员渡过了小溪。
此后几天里，我们继续沿河巡逻侦察，其间爆发了两三场激烈惊险的前哨遭遇战。最终全军的粮草给养开始捉襟见肘，卡尔将军遂下达撤军命令，折返沃尔莱斯堡（Fort Wallace）；三天后全军顺利回防该哨所，并在彼处休整驻留了数日。
在全团于此驻留候命期间，我将绝大部分闲暇时光都倾注在猎捕野牛上。有一回，我随同一小队人马出营狩猎，冷不丁遭到大约50名印第安敌骑的猝然伏击突袭。双方当即爆发了一场长达至少1小时的殊死喋血恶战，最终我们成功重创并击溃了来犯之敌。敌人丢下了4具同胞勇士的尸体仓皇败退，显然切身体会到了我们是一帮绝不好惹的硬骨头。随我同行的有几名百步穿杨的顶级神枪手，他们在战斗中大显神威，将密集的索命弹雨精准送往杀伤力最巨大的敌阵要害。我们的马匹有两三匹负伤挂彩，一名弟兄受了轻伤；全员斗志昂扬，原本早已做好了奉陪这帮红皮魔鬼血战到底的准备——然而敌寇最终自讨没趣败下阵来，自认啃下了硬骨头，悻悻然拍马狼狈逃遁。我们痛快地收官了此次狩猎，满载着大批肥美新鲜的野牛肉凯旋而归，并因这场漂亮的自卫反击战荣获了将军由衷的嘉奖与赞誉。

## XXVI Sightseeing / 第二十六章 东部观光
===Original===

General Ord, commanding the Department of the Platte at the time, and who had been out on the Alexis hunt, had some business to attend to at Fort McPherson, and I accepted his invitation to ride over to the post with him in an ambulance. On the way thither he asked me how I would like to have an officer’s commission in the regular army. He said that General Sheridan and himself had had some conversation about the matter, and if I wanted a commission, one could easily be procured for me. I thanked General Ord for his kindness, and said that although an officer’s commission in the regular army was a tempting prize, yet I preferred to remain in the position I was then holding. He concluded by stating that if at any time I should wish a commission, all that I would have to do to secure it would be to inform him of my desire.
Having determined to visit New York, I acted upon General Sheridan’s suggestion and wrote to General Stager, from whom in a few days I received my railroad passes. Obtaining thirty days’ leave of absence from the department, I struck out for the East. On arriving in Chicago, in February, 1872, I was met at the depot by Colonel M. V. Sheridan, who said that his brother, the General, had not yet returned, but had sent word that I was to be his and the Colonel’s guest, at their house, while I remained in Chicago.
I spent two or three days very pleasantly in the great city of the West, meeting several of the gentlemen who had been out on the Sheridan hunt in September﻿—General Stager, Colonel Wilson, editor of the *Journal*; Mr. Sam Johnson, General Rucker and others﻿—by all of whom I was most cordially received and well entertained. I was introduced to quite a number of the best people of the city, and was invited to several “swell” dinners. I also accompanied General Sheridan﻿—who meantime had returned to the city﻿—to a ball at Riverside﻿—an aristocratic suburb.
On this occasion I became so embarrassed that it was more difficult for me to face the throng of beautiful ladies, than it would have been to confront a hundred hostile Indians. This was my first trip to the East, and I had not yet become accustomed to being stared at. And besides this, the hundreds of questions which I was called upon to answer further embarrassed and perplexed me.

===Chinese===

当时统领普拉特军区（Department of the Platte）并刚刚陪同阿列克谢大公围猎的奥德将军（General Ord），恰好有要务需前往麦克弗森堡（Fort McPherson）处置；我便欣然应允他的邀请，同乘一辆野战救护车前往要塞。在途中的交谈中，他亲切地询问我，是否有意在正规军中担任委任军官。他透露说，谢里登将军曾同他就此事交换过意见，只要我有意从军入伍，为我谋取一份正规军军官军衔可谓易如反掌。我由衷感谢奥德将军的厚爱与美意，但我坦言：尽管正规军军官的军衔是一份极具诱惑力的殊荣，然而就我眼下的志向而言，我更倾向于坚守并维系目前自由自在的侦察兵职位。将军最后爽朗地总结道：日后无论何时只要我改变主意渴望出任正规军官，只需向他修书通报一声，他自会全力促成。
由于早已决意前往纽约一游，我便遵照谢里登将军的提议给斯泰格将军（General Stager）修书一封，短短数日之后，我便如期收到了他寄来的全程铁路免费乘车证。在军区顺利获批了为期30天的带薪长假之后，我便打点行装，毅然踏上了开往繁华东部的列车。1872年2月抵达芝加哥时，M·V·谢里登中校（Colonel M. V. Sheridan）已在火车站月台迎候多时；中校转达说，他的胞兄谢里登将军因公务尚未返抵芝加哥，但已特意带信吩咐：在我逗留芝加哥期间，务必下榻在将军与中校的私邸做客。
在这座西部第一大都会里，我惬意快活地度过了两三天的美好时光，重逢了多位曾在去年9月陪同谢里登将军参加过大围猎的政商名流——包括斯泰格将军、《芝加哥日报》（Journal）主编威尔逊上校（Colonel Wilson）、萨姆·约翰逊先生（Mr. Sam Johnson）、鲁克尔将军（General Rucker）等人——所有人皆对我给予了最热烈诚挚的迎候与款待。在他们的引荐下，我结识了芝加哥当地相当一批上流社会显贵，接到了多场极其奢华的名流晚宴邀约。其间，我还陪同此时已返抵城中的谢里登将军，一同出席了在河滨区（Riverside）——一处极其高雅尊贵的上流富人郊区——举办的盛大名流舞会。
在那场盛大舞会上，我显得异常局促不安、窘迫难堪：坦白讲，面对眼前成群结队、衣香鬓影的绝色佳人与名媛贵妇，那种手足无措的紧张感，比在荒原上真刀真枪面对100名凶神恶煞的敌对印第安人还要让我感到心惊胆战。这是我生平头一遭涉足东部繁华世界，我还很不习惯被人像稀世珍禽一般众目睽睽地上下打量；不仅如此，贵客们如连珠炮般抛向我的数百个好奇问题，更是让我晕头转向、窘态百出。

===Original===

According to the route laid out for me by General Stager, I was to stop at Niagara Falls, Buffalo and Rochester on my way to New York, and he provided me with all the necessary railroad passes. Just as I was about to leave Chicago I met Professor Henry A. Ward, of Rochester, for whom during the previous year or two I had collected a large number of specimens of wild animals. He was on his way to Rochester, and kindly volunteered to act as my guide until we reached that point. We spent one day in viewing the wonders of Niagara, and I stopped one day at Rochester and was shown the beauties of that handsome city by Professor Ward, and I had the honor of receiving an invitation to dine with the Mayor.
On arriving at New York I was met at the depot by Mr. J. G. Hecksher, who had been appointed as “a committee of one” to escort me to the Union Club, where James Gordon Bennett, Leonard W. Jerome and others were to give me an informal reception, and where I was to make my headquarters during my visit in the great metropolis. I had an elegant dinner at the club rooms, with the gentlemen who had been out on the September hunt, and other members of the club.
After dinner, in company with Mr. Hecksher﻿—who acted as my guide﻿—I started out on the trail of my friend, Ned Buntline, whom we found at the Brevoort Place Hotel. He was delighted to see me, and insisted on my becoming his guest. He would listen to no excuses, and on introducing me to Messrs. Overton & Blair, proprietors of the Brevoort, they also gave me a pressing invitation to make my home at their house. I finally compromised the matter by agreeing to divide my time between the Union Club, the Brevoort House, and Ned Buntline’s quarters.
The next few days I spent in viewing the sights of New York, everything being new and startling, convincing me that as yet I had seen but a small portion of the world. I received numerous dinner invitations, as well as invitations to visit different places of amusement and interest; but as they came in so thick and fast, I soon became badly demoralized and confused. I found I had accepted invitations to dine at half a dozen or more houses on the same day and at the same hour.

===Chinese===

依照斯泰格将军为我周密规划的旅行路线，在奔赴纽约的途中，我将在尼亚加拉瀑布（Niagara Falls）、布法罗（Buffalo）以及罗切斯特（Rochester）驻足游历，他为我备齐了沿途所需的所有铁路乘车证。正当我准备动身告别芝加哥之际，恰好邂逅了来自罗切斯特的亨利·A·沃德教授（Professor Henry A. Ward）——在过去一两年里，我曾为这位博物学家在荒原上捕获并搜集了大量珍贵的野生动物标本。他此时正准备返程回罗切斯特，便极其热忱地主动请缨充当我的向导，一路结伴同行直至他的家乡。我们在尼亚加拉瀑布流连忘返地饱览了一整天世界奇观的磅礴气象；随后在罗切斯特驻足了一天，在沃德教授的陪同下尽情领略了这座秀美都市的风土人情，并在彼处荣幸接获了罗切斯特市长的亲切邀约，与市长阁下共进盛大晚餐。
抵达到终点站纽约时，J·G·赫克歇尔先生（Mr. J. G. Hecksher）作为官方指定的“单人特派委员会”已在车站迎候，专程护送我前往联合俱乐部（Union Club）——詹姆斯·戈登·贝内特（James Gordon Bennett）、伦纳德·W·杰罗姆（Leonard W. Jerome）等诸位大亨已在彼处筹备好了一场非正式的盛大欢迎会，而这里也将是我在此次造访世界大都会期间的下榻大本营。在俱乐部富丽堂皇的宴会厅里，我与去年9月参加过大平原围猎的一众老友以及俱乐部的其他尊贵会员，共享了一顿无比丰盛华美的晚宴。
晚宴过后，在自告奋勇充当向导的赫克歇尔先生陪同下，我顺着线索去寻访我的挚友内德·邦特兰（Ned Buntline）的踪迹；我们最终在布雷沃特广场酒店（Brevoort Place Hotel）找到了他。邦特兰见到我惊喜万分、喜出望外，执意要让我搬去当他的贴身贵客。他根本不容我推脱推辞，并当场将我引荐给了布雷沃特酒店的两位大老板奥弗顿与布莱尔先生（Messrs. Overton & Blair），二位店东亦极其诚恳迫切地恳请我把他们的酒店当作自己在纽约的家。最终，在我的折中调解下达成了一致协议：将我的驻留时间平分，分别轮流下榻在联合俱乐部、布雷沃特酒店以及内德·邦特兰的寓所。
随后的数日里，我全情投入在纽约的走马观花观光见闻中。目之所及的一切皆是闻所未闻的崭新与震撼，让我深刻清醒地领悟到：以往的自己不过是在旷野一隅坐井观天，见识到的世界实在小得可怜。大批晚宴请帖与各类名胜剧院的游览邀约如雪片般纷纷飞来；然而由于请柬来得实在太密集、太迅猛，我很快便陷入了手忙脚乱的混乱与晕头转向之中。不多时我惊骇地发现：自己竟然在同一天的同一时刻，糊里糊涂答应了半打（6处）甚至更多名流府邸的晚宴邀约！

===Original===

James Gordon Bennett had prepared a dinner for me, at which quite a large number of his friends were to be present, but owing to my confusion, arising from the many other invitations I had received, I forgot all about it, and dined elsewhere. This was “a bad break,” but I did not learn of my mistake until next day, when at the Union Club House several gentlemen, among them Lawrence Jerome, inquired “where in the world I had been,” and why I had not put in an appearance at Bennett’s dinner. They said that Bennett had taken great pains to give me a splendid reception, that the party had waited till nine o’clock for me, and that my non-arrival caused considerable disappointment. I apologized as well as I could, by saying that I had been out on a scout and had got lost, and had forgotten all about the dinner; and expressed my regret for the disappointment I had created by my forgetfulness. August Belmont, the banker, being near said:
“Never mind, gentlemen, I’ll give Cody a dinner at my house.”
“Thank you, sir,” said I; “I see you are determined that I shall not run short of rations while I am in the city. I’ll be there, sure.”
Both Mr. Jerome and Mr. Hecksher told me that I must not disappoint Mr. Belmont, for his dinners were splendid affairs. I made a note of the date, and at the appointed time I was promptly at Mr. Belmont’s mansion, where I spent a very enjoyable evening.
Mr. Bennett, who was among the guests, having forgiven my carelessness, invited me to accompany him to the Liederkranz masked ball, which was to take place in a few evenings, and would be a grand spectacle. Together we attended the ball, and during the evening I was well entertained. The dancers kept on their masks until midnight, and the merry and motley throng presented a brilliant scene, moving gracefully beneath the bright gaslight to the inspiriting music. To me it was a novel and entertaining sight, and in many respects reminded me greatly of an Indian war-dance.
Acting upon the suggestion of Mr. Bennett, I had dressed myself in my buckskin suit, and I naturally attracted considerable attention; especially when I took part in the dancing and exhibited some of my backwoods steps, which, although not as graceful as some, were a great deal more emphatic.

===Chinese===

詹姆斯·戈登·贝内特特意为我筹备了一场极其隆重的盛大晚宴，邀请了他在纽约的一大批名流显贵出席；然而，由于被此前接踵而至的无数应酬搞得昏头涨脑，我竟然把这场至关重要的晚宴忘得一干二净，反而跑到别处去赴宴了。这着实是一场“天大的失礼洋相”，而我本人对此却浑然不知，直至次日我漫步走进联合俱乐部，好几位绅士——其中包括劳伦斯·杰罗姆（Lawrence Jerome）——迎面惊愕地追问我“昨天究竟跑到天涯海角去了”，为何在贝内特的盛大晚宴上不见踪影。他们告知我：贝内特为了这场欢迎盛宴煞费苦心，满堂宾客在席间苦苦等候我直至深夜9点，我的缺席让大家感到了极大的扫兴与失落。我只得满脸通红、竭尽所能地诚挚告罪自嘲道：自己在繁华都会里执行了一趟“前哨侦察”，结果不幸迷失了方向，脑子里竟把晚宴忘得精光；并对因自己的健忘而带给大家的扫兴深表歉疚痛悔。当时恰好伫立在旁的著名银行家奥古斯特·贝尔蒙特（August Belmont）朗声解围道：
“诸位先生，不必介怀，改日由我亲自在寒舍为科迪摆下一桌盛宴！”
“由衷感激您，先生，”我赶忙借坡下驴打趣道，“我看诸位是横下一条心，绝不容许我在这座大都会里断绝行军口粮啊。这回我定会准时赴约，万无一失！”
杰罗姆先生与赫克歇尔先生双双悄悄叮嘱我：这一回可千千万万绝不能再辜负贝尔蒙特先生，因为贝尔蒙特家的家宴在纽约堪称首屈一指的顶级盛事。我郑重在随身笔记本上记下了赴宴日期；到了约定时刻，我分秒不差准时踏入了贝尔蒙特先生的奢华府邸，在彼处度过了一个极其舒心惬意的美好夜晚。
贝内特先生也是当晚的座上宾之一，他宽宏大量地原谅了我的粗心大意，并力邀我随他一同出席即将于数日后隆重上演的利德克兰茨假面舞会（Liederkranz masked ball）——那必将是一场轰动全城的豪华视觉盛宴。当晚我们并肩出席了舞会，我在现场大开眼界、享尽了欢愉。狂欢的舞客们一直将面具佩戴至午夜时分才肯揭下，五光十色、光怪陆离的欢乐人潮构成了一幅美轮美奂的绚丽画卷，在璀璨明亮的瓦斯吊灯下，伴随着振奋人心的悠扬乐曲翩翩起舞。对我而言，这是一出见所未见、妙趣横生的奇观，在许多细节与神韵上，竟不可思议地让我想起了印第安人在旷野篝火旁狂跳的战舞！
在贝内特先生的极力建议下，我当晚特意换上了自己那套标志性的全套鹿皮软甲战袍，理所当然在满堂锦衣华服中吸引了全场无数惊艳与好奇的目光；尤其是当我欣然步入舞池中央、即兴展示了一段我们在偏远边荒山林里独创的粗犷舞步时，虽然动作不如东部绅士那般柔美娴熟，却远比任何舞姿都更具铿锵有力的大地力量与野性律动。

===Original===

But when I undertook to do artistic dancing, I found I was decidedly out of place in that crowd, and I accordingly withdrew from the floor.
I occasionally passed an evening at Niblo’s Garden, viewing the many beauties of *The Black Crook*, which was then having its long run, under the management of Jarrett & Palmer, whose acquaintance I had made, and who extended to me the freedom of the theater.
Ned Buntline and Fred Maeder had dramatized one of the stories which the former had written about me for the *New York Weekly*. The drama was called *Buffalo Bill, the King of Border Men*. While I was in New York it was produced at the Bowery Theater; J. B. Studley, an excellent actor, appearing in the character of “Buffalo Bill,” and Mrs. W. G. Jones, a fine actress, taking the part of my sister, a leading role. I was curious to see how I would look when represented by someone else, and of course I was present on the opening night, a private box having been reserved for me. The theater was packed, every seat being occupied as well as the standing-room. The drama was played smoothly, and created a great deal of enthusiasm.
The audience, upon learning that the real “Buffalo Bill” was present, gave several cheers between the acts, and I was called on to come out on the stage and make a speech. Mr. Freleigh, the manager, insisted that I should comply with the request, and that I should be introduced to Mr. Studley. I finally consented, and the next moment I found myself standing behind the footlights and in front of an audience for the first time in my life. I looked up, then down, then on each side, and everywhere I saw a sea of human faces, and thousands of eyes all staring at me. I confess that I felt very much embarrassed﻿—never more so in my life﻿—and I knew not what to say. I made a desperate effort, and a few words escaped me, but what they were I could not for the life of me tell, nor could anyone else in the house. My utterances were inaudible even to the leader of the orchestra, Mr. Dean, who was sitting only a few feet in front of me. Bowing to the audience, I beat a hasty retreat into one of the canyons of the stage. I never felt more relieved in my life than when I got out of the view of that immense crowd. That evening Mr. Freleigh offered to give me five hundred dollars a week to play the part of “Buffalo Bill” myself.

===Chinese===

在纽约逗留期间，我曾数度光顾尼勃罗花园剧场（Niblo’s Garden），尽情饱览了名噪一时的经典巨作《黑魔杖》（The Black Crook）的诸多绝美精髓——该剧在贾勒特与帕默公司（Jarrett & Palmer）的掌管经营下正在彼处进行旷日持久的连演；因我与两位剧场巨头结下了深厚友谊，他们慷慨向我授予了终身免费随意出入剧场的最高特权。
内德·邦特兰与弗雷德·梅德（Fred Maeder）当时刚刚将前者此前在《纽约周刊》（New York Weekly）上为我量身撰写的连载小说改编成了同名话剧。这部舞台传奇大戏定名为《野牛比尔，边疆人之王》（Buffalo Bill, the King of Border Men）。就在我造访纽约期间，该剧恰逢在鲍厄里剧院（Bowery Theater）隆重首演；声名显赫的著名话剧名伶J·B·斯塔德利（J. B. Studley）在剧中亲自挑大梁出演“野牛比尔”一角，而才华横溢的名演员W·G·琼斯夫人（Mrs. W. G. Jones）则出演我剧中的胞妹——一个举足轻重的主角戏份。我打心眼里极度好奇，若由旁人在舞台上扮演我自己究竟会呈现出何等光景；首演之夜我自然盛装出席，剧场特意为我在贵宾包厢保留了绝佳席位。整座剧场座无虚席、盛况空前，不仅所有座位被抢购一空，甚至连过道上的站票位置都挤得水泄不通。整场戏剧演出流畅通顺自然，在全场掀起了雷鸣般的狂热狂澜。
当满堂观众在幕间休息时惊闻货真价实的“野牛比尔本尊”此时此刻就端坐在剧场包厢时，全场顿时爆发出响彻云霄的一阵阵狂热欢呼，大伙儿异口同声声嘶力竭地高呼要求我登上舞台向全体观众发表致辞。剧场经理弗莱利先生（Mr. Freleigh）亦极力敦促我务必顺应民意登台亮相，并坚持要借此机会在台上把我正式引荐给主演斯塔德利先生。在盛情难却之下我终于勉强点头应允；下一刻，我赫然发现自己有生以来第一次伫立在了耀眼的舞台脚灯之后，直面着台下黑压压的一片汪洋人海。我抬头看了看，又低头瞧了瞧，再向两旁张望打量——视野所及之处，全是一望无际的无数人脸与成千上万双正死死凝视着我的炽热眼睛！我坦白承认，当时我感到了前所未有的极度尴尬与窘迫窒息——这是我这辈子经历过的最手足无措的恐怖时刻——脑子里一片空白，全然不知该说些什么才好。我拼尽全力做了一番绝望的挣扎，喉咙里勉强挤出了几个模糊不清的词句，但那究竟说了些什么鬼话，我哪怕把命豁出去也回忆不出一丝半点，台下成千上万名观众更是没有一个人能听得清分毫。我的含糊发声细微到甚至连端坐在我前方仅几英尺处的乐队指挥迪恩先生（Mr. Dean）都浑然未曾听见半个字。我向台下匆匆鞠了一躬，旋即以惊人的速度仓皇抱头鼠窜，一头钻进了舞台两侧宛如崇山深谷一般的幕布夹道深处。当我终于从那黑压压的人潮视线中彻底脱身的一刹那，我感到了一生中从未有过的如释重负与死里逃生。当天夜晚，弗莱利先生甚至开出了每周500美元的天价天价周薪，力邀我日后在舞台上亲自出演“野牛比尔”这个角色！

===Original===

I thought that he was certainly joking, especially as he had witnessed my awkward performance; but when he assured me that he was in earnest, I told him that it would be useless for me to attempt anything of the kind, for I never could talk to a crowd of people like that, even if it was to save my neck, and that he might as well try to make an actor out of a government mule. I thanked him for the generous offer, which I had to decline owing to a lack of confidence in myself; or as some people might express it, I didn’t have the requisite cheek to undertake a thing of that sort. The play of “Buffalo Bill” had a very successful run of six or eight weeks, and was afterwards produced in all the principal cities of the country, everywhere being received with genuine enthusiasm.
I had been in New York about twenty days when General Sheridan arrived in the city. I met him soon after he got into town. In answer to a question how I was enjoying myself, I replied that I had struck the best camp I had ever seen, and if he didn’t have any objections I would like to have my leave of absence extended about ten days. This he willingly did, and then informed me that my services would soon be required at Fort McPherson, as there was to be an expedition sent out from that point.
At Westchester, Pennsylvania, I had some relatives living whom I had never seen, and now being so near, I determined to make them a visit. Upon mentioning the matter to Buntline, he suggested that we should together take a trip to Philadelphia, and thence run out to Westchester. Accordingly the next day found us in the “City of Brotherly Love,” and in a few hours we arrived at the home of my uncle, General Henry R. Guss, the proprietor of the Green Tree Hotel, who gave us a cordial reception.
Inviting us into the parlor, my uncle brought in the members of his family, among them an elderly lady, who was my grandmother, as he informed me. He told me that my Aunt Eliza, his first wife, was dead, and that he had married a second time; Lizzie Guss, my cousin, I thought was the most beautiful girl I had ever seen. They were all very anxious to have us remain several days, but as I had some business to attend to in New York, I was obliged to return that day. Assuring them, however, that I would visit them again soon, I bade them adieu, and with Buntline took the train for New York.

===Chinese===

起初我满以为他不过是在开玩笑打趣，毕竟他刚才亲眼目睹了我在台上的笨拙难堪与滑稽失态；然而当他言之凿凿担保自己绝对是发自肺腑的由衷诚意时，我坦率正告他说，让我去尝试这等行当完全是白费心机，因为哪怕是为了保住自己的脑袋，我也决计无法在面对这般黑压压的人群时顺畅开口说话；他若想让我登台演戏，倒还不如索性去把军方的一头运货骡子调教成话剧演员来得切合实际。我由衷感激他开出的极其慷慨的天价报酬，但我不得不予以婉言谢绝——这完全是因为我对自己的登台才干毫无半分自信；或者正如某些尖刻之人所言，我缺乏敢于涉足那种名利行当所必须具备的厚颜面皮。这部《野牛比尔》话剧在纽约连续轰动上演了6到8周之久，场场爆满，此后更是巡回盛演于全美所有主要大都会大城市，在各处无不激荡起经久不息的炽烈追捧。
我在纽约盘桓流连了大约20天之际，谢里登将军亦抵达到了纽约城。他刚一进城，我便前去同他会晤。在回答将军垂询我在此玩得是否尽兴惬意时，我笑逐颜开地回答说：自己这回误打误撞闯入了一处平生所见的最顶尖大营，若是将军不予反对，我恳请能将自己的假期再宽限延长10天左右。将军极其大度爽朗地应允了我的请假，并同时告知我：军方不久后便需我在麦克弗森堡重返战位，因为近期即将从该哨所开拔发起一次大规模军事远征。
在宾夕法尼亚的韦斯特切斯特（Westchester），居住着几位我平生缘悭一面、素未谋面的远房至亲；如今既然咫尺之遥近在眼前，我便打定主意专程前去登门拜访探望。当我向邦特兰提及此事时，他热情提议我们何不结伴先前往费城（Philadelphia）一游，随后再顺道取道奔赴韦斯特切斯特。于是次日清晨，我们便漫步在素有“友爱之城”美誉的费城街头；数小时后，我们顺利抵达了我叔父亨利·R·古斯将军（General Henry R. Guss）的府邸——将军正是当地闻名遐迩的绿树饭店（Green Tree Hotel）的东主产业掌门人，他给予了我们最热烈体贴的至诚迎候。
叔父热情洋溢地将我们迎入客厅，引荐全家老小与我相认，其中有一位满头银丝的慈祥老妇人，经叔父引荐，这正是我的亲祖母。叔父向我动情倾诉道：我的艾丽莎姑母（Aunt Eliza）——亦即他的结发元配原配夫人——早已长眠辞世，他如今已续弦再娶；而在我眼里，我的表妹莉齐·古斯（Lizzie Guss）简直是我平生所见过的最楚楚动人、天生丽质的绝代佳人。亲人们个个极力挽留我们务必在此驻留盘桓数日，然而因我在纽约尚有几桩要务急需赶回处置，只得遗憾地在当天告辞启程。不过在信誓旦旦向亲人们郑重许诺不久之后定当再次登门重聚后，我依依不舍同他们握手道别，随后与邦特兰一同搭乘特快列车疾驰折返纽约。

===Original===

The time soon arrived for my departure for the West; so packing up my traps I started for home, and on the way thither I spent a day with my Westchester relatives, who did everything in their power to entertain me during my brief stay with them.

===Chinese===

终于，到了我正式打点行装告别繁华东部、重返辽阔西部的既定日子；于是我将随身行囊与一应家当收拾停当，毅然踏上了开往西部的漫漫归途。在返程途中，我特意在宾夕法尼亚韦斯特切斯特的至亲家中又驻足盘桓了一整天，在我那短暂的停留时光里，至亲骨肉们倾尽全心竭尽所能地设宴款待照拂于我，让我深切领略到了骨肉至亲的无尽温情。

## XXIX Starring / 第二十九章 领衔主演
===Original===

Texas Jack and I spent several weeks in hunting in the western part of Nebraska, and at the end of our vacation we felt greatly reinvigorated and ready for another theatrical campaign. We accordingly proceeded to New York and organized a company for the season of 1873﻿–﻿74. Thinking that Wild Bill would be quite an acquisition to the troupe, we wrote to him at Springfield, Missouri, offering him a large salary if he would play with us that winter. He was doing nothing at the time, and we thought that he would like to take a trip through the States, as he had never been East.
Wild Bill accepted our offer, and came on to New York; though he told us from the start that we could never make an actor out of him. Although he had a fine stage appearance and was a handsome fellow, and possessed a good strong voice, yet when he went upon the stage before an audience, it was almost impossible for him to utter a word. He insisted that we were making a set of fools of ourselves, and that we were the laughingstock of the people. I replied that I did not care for that, as long as they came and bought tickets to see us.
Wild Bill was continually playing tricks upon the members of the company, and it was his especial delight to torment the “supers.” Quite frequently in our sham Indian battles he would run up to the “Indians” (the supers), and putting his pistol close to their legs, would fire at them and burn them with the powder, instead of shooting over their heads. This would make them dance and jump, so that it was difficult to make them fall and die﻿—although they were paid twenty-five cents each for performing the “dying business.” The poor “supers” often complained to me about this, and threatened not to go on the stage and be killed again if that man Wild Bill did not stop shooting and burning their legs. I would order Wild Bill to stop his mischief; he would laugh and then promise not to do it any more. But it would not be long before he was at his old tricks again.
My company, known as the “Buffalo Bill Combination,” did a fine business, all through the East. Wild Bill continued his pranks, which caused us considerable annoyance, but at the same time greatly amused us.

===Chinese===

得克萨斯·杰克（Texas Jack）与我在内布拉斯加西部痛痛快快度过了数周的旷野狩猎时光；假期结束时，我们顿感精神焕发、体力充沛，做好了投身新一轮剧场巡演战役的充分准备。于是我们启程奔赴纽约，为1873—1874年的全新演出季组建戏班剧团。思及若是能将狂野比尔（Wild Bill）网罗招揽进戏班，必将是剧团的一大绝妙强援，我们便特意致信密苏里斯普林菲尔德的他，许以极其丰厚的优厚薪酬，力邀他随我们一同出演那年冬天的巡回演出。彼时他正无所事事闲居在家，而我们料定他此前从未涉足繁华东部，定会欣然乐意借此良机游历一番全美各州。
狂野比尔痛快接受了我们的诚挚邀约，如期赶赴纽约；不过他从一开始便直言不讳告诫我们：我们无论如何也休想把他调教成一名真正的戏剧演员。尽管他在舞台上仪表堂堂、相貌英俊俊朗，且拥有一副浑厚洪亮的好嗓门，然而一旦置身舞台脚灯之后面对黑压压的满堂观众，他往往喉头哽塞，几乎连半个字也挤不出来。他执拗地埋怨说，我们这分明是在把全团上下当成一帮小丑在戏耍，简直是在让全天下的观众看我们的天大笑话。我笑着反驳他说：只要观众们心甘情愿排着长队买票进场观看，我才不在乎他们究竟是在看戏还是在看笑话。
狂野比尔在剧团里总爱没完没了地捉弄戏弄同僚，而他最热衷的一大癖好莫过于折磨那些受雇充当“跑龙套群众演员（supers）”的临时演员。在舞台上每逢上演假装激战的印第安交火桥段时，他常常会狂冲上前逼近那些扮演“印第安人”的龙套群众，将黑洞洞的手枪直接死死顶在他们的裤腿小腿上，非但不往头顶虚放空枪，反倒冲着他们的裤管连连击发，用滚烫灼热的真火药狠狠烫灼他们。这往往痛得那些可怜的群众演员在舞台上像猴子般狂跳乱蹦惨叫连连，以至于在众目睽睽之下根本无法顺理成章地“倒地毙命”——尽管剧团按规定给每位龙套演员支付了整整25美分的“横尸阵亡补贴”。可怜的跑龙套演员们常常满脸委屈跑来向我哭诉控告，扬言要是狂野比尔再不停手、继续用真枪真火药烫伤他们的大腿，他们就誓死不再登台充当死尸任人屠戮了。每逢此时，我便会严肃责令狂野比尔立刻收敛恶作剧；他总是哈哈大笑一口应承，信誓旦言担保下不为例。然而要不了多久，他又会旧病复发重操旧技。
我所统领的这支定名为“野牛比尔联合剧团（Buffalo Bill Combination）”的戏班，在整个东部地区的票房大获全胜、生意极其红火。狂野比尔虽恶作剧不断，屡屡给我们招惹来啼笑皆非的烦心麻烦，但与此同时也为全团上下带来了数不尽的捧腹欢笑。

===Original===

One day at Titusville, Pennsylvania, while Burke, the business agent, was registering our names and making arrangements for our accommodation, several of us started for the billiard room; but were met by the landlord, who stopped me and said that there was a party of roughs from the lower oil region who were spreeing, and had boasted that they were staying in town to meet the Buffalo Bill gang and clean them out. The landlord begged of me not to allow the members of the troupe to enter the billiard room, as he did not wish any fight in his house. To please the landlord, and at his suggestion, I called the boys up into the parlor and explained to them the situation. Wild Bill wanted to go at once and fight the whole mob, but I persuaded him to keep away from them during the day.
In order to entirely avoid the roughs, the members of the company entered the theater through a private door from the hotel, as the two buildings joined each other. While I was standing at the door of the theater taking the tickets, the landlord of the hotel came rushing up and said that Wild Bill was having a fight with the roughs in the barroom. It seemed that Bill had not been able to resist the temptation of going to see what kind of a mob it was that wanted to test the pluck of the Buffalo Bill party; and just as he stepped into the room, one of the bruisers put his hand on his shoulder and said:
“Hello, Buffalo Bill! we have been looking for you all day.”
“My name is not Buffalo Bill; you are mistaken in the man,” was the reply.
“You are a liar!” said the bruiser.
Bill instantly knocked him down, and then seizing a chair he laid out four or five of the crowd on the floor, and drove the rest out of the room. All this was done in a minute or two, and by the time I got downstairs, Bill was coming out of the barroom, whistling a lively tune.
“Well!” said he, “I have been interviewing that party who wanted to clean us out.”
“I thought you promised to come into the Opera House by the private entrance?”
“I did try to follow that trail, but I got lost among the canyons, and then I ran in among the hostiles,” said he; “but it is all right now. They won’t bother us any more. I guess those fellows have found us.” And sure enough they had. We heard no more of them after that.
Another incident occurred, one night, at Portland, Maine.

===Chinese===

在宾夕法尼亚泰特斯维尔（Titusville）巡演的某一天，正当剧团的商务经纪人伯克（Burke）在酒店前台登记全员下榻名册、打点食宿安排之际，我们几名演员信步朝酒店底层的台球室走去；然而刚走到门口便被酒店店东伸手拦下。老板神色紧张地向我告密说，城里刚从下游石油开采区涌来了一伙寻衅滋事的亡命无赖流氓，他们正聚众狂饮作乐，并且在城中大放厥词吹嘘说，他们特意滞留在此就是要等“野牛比尔帮伙”送上门来，把我们一个个彻底“打扫出门清洗干净”。老板苦苦哀求我千千万万不要容许剧团成员踏入台球室半步，生怕在自家店堂里引发流血斗殴。为了照顾店东的颜面，并在他的极力劝导下，我将全团伙计召集到二楼起居客厅，向他们摊牌挑明了当前的紧张局势。狂野比尔当场暴跳如雷，按捺不住非要立刻冲下楼去将那帮无赖之徒通通痛打一顿，但在我苦口婆心的劝说下，他勉强答应在白天对他们避而远之。
为了彻底避开这帮亡命流氓的纠缠，剧团成员通过酒店与隔壁剧院之间相通的一道隐秘暗门，悄然步入了演出剧场。正当我伫立在剧院大门口亲自把关检票之际，酒店老板满头大汗神色慌张地一路狂奔冲上前来，上气不接下气地尖叫道：狂野比尔正在酒店酒吧间里跟那帮无赖歹徒大打出手！原来比尔终究按捺不住心头的好奇与野性，非要溜去亲眼瞧一瞧究竟是何方神圣竟敢扬言要称一称“野牛比尔剧团”的硬骨头；就在他刚刚踏入酒吧的一刹那，一名满身横肉的流氓打手一把拍住他的肩膀狂妄喝道：
“喂，野牛比尔！老子们找了你整整一整天了！”
“老子不叫野牛比尔；你认错人了，”比尔面无表情冷冷答道。
“你是个下三滥骗子！”那打手怒目圆睁破口大骂。
比尔顺手便是一记摧枯拉朽的重拳将对方当场轰飞倒地，紧接着顺势抄起一把实木椅子狂暴乱砸，转瞬之间便将四五个恶汉硬生生打翻在地动弹不得，顺势把剩下的歹徒打得屁滚尿流狼狈抱头窜出大门。这整场激战不过耗费了一两分钟工夫；待我急匆匆顺着楼梯飞奔下楼时，只见比尔正嘴里吹着欢快的口哨，大摇大摆从酒吧大门晃悠着走出来。
“哈！”比尔咧嘴一笑迎上前道，“我已经同那帮扬言要把咱们全伙清洗出门的朋友好好‘面谈切磋’了一番。”
“我不是记得你明明保证过要从暗门直接进入歌剧院的吗？”我无可奈何地苦笑着质问道。
“我确实原本想沿着那条密道走来着，结果不小心在过道狭窄的‘深山峡谷’里迷失了方向，一头撞进了这群‘敌对部落阵营’里，”他神情自若地调侃道，“不过现在大功告成了，他们再也不会来骚扰纠缠咱们了。我看那帮家伙这回可算真正‘找到’咱们了。”千真万确的是，那帮地头蛇自此彻底销声匿迹，我们此后再未听闻过他们半点声响。
在缅因州波特兰（Portland）巡演的一个夜晚，还发生过另一件令人忍俊不禁的惊险插曲。

===Original===

Bill found it impossible to go to sleep at the hotel on account of the continued talking of some parties who were engaged in a game of cards in an adjoining room. He called to them several times to make less noise, but they paid little or no attention to him. He finally got up and went to the room with the intention of cleaning out the whole crowd. He knocked and was admitted; greatly to his surprise, he found the party to be some merchants of the city, whom he had met the previous day. They were playing poker, and invited him to take a hand. Bill sat down at the table, and said that, inasmuch as they would not let him sleep, he wouldn’t mind playing for a while, provided they would post him a little in the game, for he didn’t know much about it. At first he didn’t play very well, intentionally making many blunders and asking numerous questions; but when morning came, he was about seven hundred dollars ahead. Bill put the money in his pocket, and just as he was leaving the room he advised them never to wake a man up and invite him to play poker.
Wild Bill remained with me until we reached Rochester. I met my family there, and having bought some property in that city, with the intention of making the place my home, I asked Bill not to cut up any of his capers, for I wanted the performance to go off smoothly, as I expected a large audience that evening. He, of course, promised to behave himself. When the curtain rose the house was crowded. The play proceeded finely until the Indian fight in the second act, when Bill amused himself by his old trick of singeing the legs of the “supers.”
After the curtain dropped, the “supers” complained to me about it. Bill’s conduct made me angry, and I told him that he must either stop shooting the “supers,” or leave the company. He made no reply, but went to the dressing-room and changed his buckskin suit for his citizen’s dress, and during one of my scenes I looked down in front and saw him elbowing his way through the audience and out of the theater. When I had finished the scene, and had retired from the stage, the stage-carpenter came up and said:
“That long-haired gentleman, who passed out a few minutes ago, requested me to tell you that you could go to thunder with your old show.”
That was the last time that Wild Bill and I ever performed together on the stage.

===Chinese===

在下榻酒店里，由于隔壁客房里有一伙人通宵达旦地聚众打牌、喧嚣聒噪，搞得比尔翻来覆去难以入眠。他接连冲着墙壁怒吼警告了好几次，让他们放低音量，但那帮赌客对他的抗议充耳不闻、照样吵嚷不休。怒火中烧的比尔终于忍无可忍翻身下床，一脚踹向房门，打定主意要冲进去把屋里的所有混蛋全痛打一顿扔出门外。他砸门而入，然而令他始料未及的是，屋里的赌客竟然是几名当地有头有脸的城中商贾巨贾，前一天大家还曾在街头有过一面之缘。商贾们正在玩德州扑克，见他进门便热情招呼他坐下来凑上一脚。比尔大大咧咧在牌桌旁坐下，粗声大气说道：既然诸位打扰得老子无法合眼安歇，他倒也不介意陪诸位玩上几把，前提是诸位得稍稍给他指点一下牌规门道，因为他对纸牌技法向来一窍不通。起初他“笨拙”地摸索着牌路，故意在牌局上频频犯下低级失误并没完没了地向众人虚心讨教；然而待到东方破晓黎明破晓之际，比尔的面前早已堆积如山，整整赢走了大约700美元！比尔神色自若地将大把钞票揣入腰包，在迈步跨出房门的一刹那，转过身来冷冷告诫这帮商贾：日后永远不要自作聪明去叫醒一个熟睡的汉子邀他玩扑克。
